# GST Case Law Compendium

**Sangareddy CGST Division · compiled 14 Oct 2026 · 364 judgments across all GST domains**

All judgments in one place: Supreme Court, High Courts (Telangana HC flagged as binding), GST Appellate Tribunal, appellate authorities and advance rulings, organised by domain, with the points on which courts disagree. It is part of the project's GST knowledge base (`/mnt/project-files/gst-law/`), alongside the Acts, Rules, notifications, circulars and Council minutes.

## Part 1. How to use this compendium

**Reliability.** On 14-15 Oct 2026 every entry was checked against the judgment text on Indian Kanoon (and the Telangana HC and SC sites). An entry marked *Primary - read on Indian Kanoon* has been read at its operative part; its first source link is the judgment, and a "Key words" line quotes the holding with the paragraph number. Entries marked *Secondary* (mostly GSTAT orders, advance rulings and very recent interim orders, which are not on Indian Kanoon) are still from reports only: **read those before quoting them.** Entries marked **[DOUBTFUL]** or "do not cite" should not be relied on. Each domain file ends with a verification log listing what was corrected. Each entry gives the citation, holding, binding status, later history (appeal, SLP, reversal, or overtaken by amendment), a one-line "use in orders" note, source links and its verification status.

**What binds you.**
- **Supreme Court:** binding on all authorities (Art. 141). This compendium has 52 SC entries.
- **Telangana High Court:** binding on officers in Telangana; a lower authority must follow its jurisdictional High Court (*Union of India v. Kamlakshi Finance*, SC). This compendium has 74 entries marked BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- **Other High Courts:** persuasive. Where they conflict and the SC and Telangana HC have not spoken, follow the CBIC position and record the conflict in the order.
- **GSTAT:** followed by the appellate and adjudicating authorities below it; a High Court or SC ruling prevails over it.
- **AAR/AAAR:** binding only on the applicant and its jurisdictional officer (s.103).
- **CBIC circulars:** bind officers, not courts or taxpayers (*Ratan Melting & Wire*, SC).

**Structure.** Part 2 lists where courts disagree and what is pending in the Supreme Court. Part 3 is a one-line index of every judgment by domain. Part 4 has the full entries, with "Quick answers" for each issue at the top of each file. The same entries are in `case-law-index.csv` for filtering in Excel.

## Part 2. Where courts disagree, and what is still pending

| Issue | One view | Other view | Status / what to do |
|----------|------------|------------|--------------|
| Limitation-extension Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 (s.168A) | Upheld through ratification: Patna HC (*Barhonia Engicon*, 27.11.2024). Telangana HC (*Brunda Infra* batch, 02.01.2025) decided on the COVID exclusion and left validity academic, while observing that a Council recommendation is a sine qua non and ratification cannot cure its absence (paras 94-97) | Struck down or held illegal: Gauhati HC (*Barkataki Print*, 2024), Madras HC (*Tata Play*, 12.06.2025) | **Pending in SC**, SLP(C) 4240/2025; no judgment by 14 Oct 2026. Record the pendency in every order relying on the extended dates. |
| Two-year limit for refund, s.54(1) | Directory: Madras HC (*Lenovo*, obiter: the claim was in time). Tax paid without authority of law falls outside s.54 altogether: AP HC (*Nspira*, 26.09.2025) | Mandatory; only a court can condone: Karnataka HC DB (*Merck Life Science*, 2026) | No Telangana or SC ruling found. Treat as mandatory at officer level; COVID exclusion (Notf. 13/2022) applies. |
| One SCN covering several financial years | Allowed: **Telangana HC DB (*Goldstone Infra*, 27.01.2026), binding here**; Delhi HC (*Ambika Traders*); Karnataka DB (23.04.2026), reversing *Bangalore Golf Club* | Not allowed: Madras (*Titan*, 18.12.2023: proceedings split by year), Bombay (*Milroc*) | Bombay Larger Bench reference (*Rollmet*) still not constituted (9.7.2026). Lawful in Telangana; **one SCN per year remains the safer practice** (separate limitation per year). |
| ITC where supplier did not pay, s.16(2)(c) | Valid, no reading down: SC *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (24.07.2026) | Read down for bona fide buyers: Calcutta (*Suncraft*), Tripura (*Sahil Enterprises*) | SC has stayed *Sahil Enterprises* (01.09.2026, SLP(C) 31573/2026); the SC's dismissal in *Suncraft* rested on the small demand, not the law. *Bhandari* governs. Still record a verified finding that the supplier did not pay, and pursue the supplier. |
| Notices uploaded only under the portal's "Additional Notices" tab | Ex parte orders set aside: Delhi HC (*ACE Cardiopathy* and later cases) | Telangana HC sends the taxpayer to appeal with sympathetic condonation | Serve by more than one mode (portal and e-mail/RPAD) and record it. |
| Seizure of cash in a search, s.67(2) | Not permitted: Delhi (*Deepak Khandelwal*), Bombay (*Smurti Waghdhare*, 2026), Gauhati | Permitted: MP HC (*Kanishka Matta*, 2020) | SC notice issued, not decided. No Telangana ruling found. Avoid seizing cash unless it is itself goods liable to confiscation. |
| Interest on wrongly availed ITC, s.50(3) | Only if availed **and** utilised, tested on the total ledger balance: Calcutta (*Utpal Das*), Orissa (*Manoja Kumar Nayak*) | Madras HC (Jun 2026; judgment not found, from reports only) reads s.50(3) more widely | Compute ledger balance month by month and show utilisation in the order. |
| Penalty on a non-taxable person, s.122(1A) | Only a taxable person, and not for transactions before 01.01.2021: Bombay DB (*Amit Manilal Haria*, 25.02.2026) | Any person retaining the benefit, including pre-2021 transactions: Gauhati (*Mayank Bansal*, 08.06.2026). Delhi (*Gurudas Mallik Thakur*, 2025) only sent the directors to appeal | **SC has granted leave on both questions** (*Mukesh Kumar Garg*, 04.08.2025), pending. Record the person's role and benefit retained. |
| Leasehold-right assignment and TDR/JDA | Leasehold assignment not a supply: Gujarat (*Gujarat Chamber*), Bombay (*Aerocom*); SC dismissed the *Aerocom* SLP on 22.05.2026 (*Gujarat Chamber* SC outcome unconfirmed) | TDR under JDA is taxable: Telangana HC (*Prahitha Constructions*, 2024), binding here | *Prahitha*: SC notice issued, no stay. Apply *Prahitha* in Telangana. |
| Retrospective cancellation of registration | Must be proposed in the SCN with objective reasons: Delhi (*Ridhi Sidhi*), Telangana (*JK Technology*) | No contrary view found | Put the proposed effective date and reasons in REG-17. |
| Restoration after cancellation for non-filing | Restore once returns and dues are cleared: Madras (*Suguna Cutpiece*), Gauhati, Telangana 2026 orders | Gujarat pointed to the amnesty instead | Telangana orders allow a physical revocation application beyond the portal's 270 days. |
| Refund of ITC on input services (inverted duty) | Not allowed: SC *VKC Footsteps* (2021) | 57th Council (8 Oct 2026) recommended allowing it for ITC from 1 Nov 2026 | **Not law yet**: no amendment or notification found. *VKC Footsteps* still governs. The Finance Act 2026 did not amend s.54(3). |
| Arrest power, s.69 | Upheld with safeguards: SC *Radhika Agarwal* (2025), *Sunil Biyani* (2026) | 57th Council recommended omitting s.69 | Not law yet; s.69 runs, with the SC safeguards. |

**Other matters pending in the Supreme Court** (check before relying on the High Court view): GST on mining royalty; anti-profiteering validity (*Excel Rasayan* / *Reckitt Benckiser* appeals); s.7(1)(aa) mutuality (*IMA*, Kerala HC); *Prahitha Constructions* (TDR); the one-third land deduction (*Munjaal Bhatt*, Gujarat HC read it down; Union's SLP(C) 21703/2022); *HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV* (from Telangana HC; notice issued). Details are in the full entries.

**Recent rulings that change drafting.**
- *G.R. Infra Projects* (SC, 19.08.2026): a s.74 SCN must state the specific facts of fraud or suppression in the notice itself.
- *Sterling & Wilson* (GSTAT Principal Bench, 11.02.2026; order not yet read, from reports): a return mismatch alone does not support s.74; re-working under s.75(2) is for the proper officer.
- *Armour Security* (SC, 14.08.2025): a summons or search is not "initiation of proceedings" under s.6(2)(b); an SCN on the same subject matter is.
- *Goodluck India* (SC, 06.08.2026): omission of rule 96(10) applies to all proceedings pending on 08.10.2024.
- *Gudla Vanaja* (**Telangana HC Full Bench, 30.09.2026**, W.P. 20397/2025 batch): an SCN or order electronically authenticated and uploaded on the portal is valid without a visible signature, but **a notice or order without a DIN/RFN is invalid**; limitation runs from upload. It overrules *Bigleap Technologies* (2025), which must no longer be cited.
- *Devi Electronics* (Delhi HC, 01.10.2026): a refund may be withheld under s.54(11) where an anti-evasion investigation is already pending; no court stay is needed.
- *A1 Adil Traders* (Telangana HC, 04.03.2025): the grounds must be stated in the SCN itself, not only in an annexed letter.
- *Augustan Textile Colours* (GSTAT Ernakulam, 2026; from reports): ITC eligibility cannot be decided inside a refund proceeding without a s.73/74 order.


## Part 3. Master index of judgments by domain

Binding level: **SC** = Supreme Court, binding on all; **TG** = Telangana HC, binding in Telangana; **P** = other High Court, persuasive; **T** = tribunal (GSTAT/CESTAT); **AAR** = advance ruling; **X** = overruled, do not cite. Full entries are in Part 4 under the file named in the last column.

## Part 4. Full entries, file by file

## File 01: GST Refunds - Case-Law Digest

**Scope.** Case law on refunds under s.54-56 CGST Act, 2017 / s.16 IGST Act and rules 89-97 CGST Rules: inverted duty structure (IDS), rule 89(5) formula, Circulars 135/2020 and 173/2022, limitation under s.54(1) (directory vs mandatory, COVID exclusion), tax paid under the wrong head (s.77 / rule 89(1A)), natural justice before rejection (rule 92(3), RFD-08), deficiency memos (rule 90(3), RFD-03), interest under s.56, IGST export refunds and rule 96(10)/96(4), intermediary services, SEZ supplies, unjust enrichment, pre-deposits and amounts paid during investigation, deemed exports, withholding under s.54(10)/(11), and rule 89(4)/(4C) turnover. Prepared for the Assistant Commissioner, CGST, Sangareddy Division (Telangana).

**Date compiled:** 13 Oct 2026

> **Update 14 Oct 2026:** 44 of 47 entries have now been checked against the judgment text on Indian Kanoon (see each entry's "Verified" line and the Verification log at the end). Several summaries were corrected. Entries still marked "Secondary" must be verified before citing.
>
> **Originally compiled from web-search summaries (13 Oct 2026).**
> Court and case-law websites (Indian Kanoon, SCI, HC sites, TaxGuru, Taxscan full text) could not be opened from this environment. Every holding below is taken from search-result summaries (news sites, law-firm alerts, commentary). Citations marked "not found" were not found. The web-search budget ran out before every issue could be checked; see "Gaps and doubts".

**Binding status key.** Supreme Court: binding on all (Art. 141). Telangana HC: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**. Other HCs: persuasive in Telangana. Tribunal/AAR: persuasive only.

---

### 1. Quick answers

**Inverted duty: input services.** The Supreme Court in *VKC Footsteps* (13.09.2021) upheld rule 89(5): IDS refund under s.54(3)(ii) covers ITC on **inputs** (goods) only, not input services or capital goods. It reversed Gujarat HC and affirmed Madras HC (*Transtonnelstroy Afcons*). *Takeaway:* deny the input-services part of an IDS claim by citing VKC Footsteps. A Finance Bill 2026 proposal to rewrite s.54(3) and add input services was reported, but **its enactment could not be confirmed**. Check the enacted Finance Act 2026 text before relying on the pre-2026 position for periods after 30.03.2026.

**Rule 89(5) formula amendment (Notf. 14/2022-CT, 05.07.2022).** Gujarat HC (*Ascent Meditech*) held the amended formula curative and clarificatory, so it applies retrospectively to claims filed within the s.54(1) period. It quashed Circular 181/13/2022 to that extent. One source reports that the SLP (28.03.2025) and a review petition were dismissed. *Takeaway:* apply the amended formula to all claims that are within time, including those for periods before July 2022. Do not reject a claim by applying the old formula.

**Circular 135/2020 para 3.2 (same input and output goods).** Telangana HC (*Micro Systems*, 2022) held Circular 173/05/2022 to be clarificatory and retrospective to 31.03.2020, and remanded the claim. **This binds Telangana officers.** Karnataka HC (*South Indian Oil Corp.*, 12.12.2025) held that a circular cannot cut down s.54(3), and that para 3.2 applies only where credit built up because the rate on the same goods changed over time. *Takeaway:* do not reject an IDS claim only because input and output are the same goods or carry the same rate, or because the supply is at a concessional rate. Record a finding on why the credit actually accumulated.

**Limitation under s.54(1).** The High Courts are split. Madras HC (*Lenovo*, 06.11.2023) called it directory (obiter: the claim was in time). AP HC (*Nspira*, 26.09.2025) held s.54 limitation inapplicable to tax paid without authority of law (exempt supplies), not that it is directory. A Karnataka HC Division Bench (*Merck Life Science*, 17.03.2026) holds it **mandatory**: the officer cannot condone delay, and only the HC can do so under Art. 226. No Telangana HC refund-limitation ruling was found. *Takeaway:* the officer should treat two years as mandatory, since only a court can condone delay. Before rejecting, (i) compute the relevant date correctly, (ii) **exclude 01.03.2020-28.02.2022 under Notf. 13/2022-CT** (the SC suo motu exclusion was also applied to refunds by Bombay HC in *Saiher*), and (iii) reject only after an RFD-08 notice and a hearing.

**Tax paid under the wrong head (s.77 / rule 89(1A)).** Patna HC (*Sai Steel*, 28.07.2025) and Karnataka HC hold that time runs from the date tax is paid under the **correct** head, following Circular 162/18/2021. AP HC (*Axis Bank*, 29.09.2022) remanded a time-barred rejection for fresh decision in the light of Circular 162/18/2021 and rule 89(1A). *Takeaway:* do not count limitation from the original erroneous payment.

**Rejection without RFD-08 or hearing.** The proviso to rule 92(3) bars rejection without a hearing. Rejections that skip the RFD-08 notice, ignore the reply, or give no reasons are routinely quashed (Telangana HC *DSV Air and Sea*, Gujarat HC *Deccan Fine Chemicals*, Madras HC *Pacific Wood Tech*). *Takeaway:* always issue RFD-08 stating specific grounds, consider the RFD-09 reply, give a personal hearing, and pass a speaking RFD-06 order that deals with every point.

**Deficiency memo (RFD-03).** A rectified application filed after a deficiency memo continues the original application for limitation purposes (Gujarat HC *Varidhi Cotspin*, 07.10.2025, and a Karnataka HC ruling). Time bar is not a "deficiency", so a claim cannot be rejected as time-barred through RFD-03 (Madras HC *Pacific Wood Tech*, 01.09.2026). *Takeaway:* use RFD-03 only for missing documents. Any substantive objection, including limitation, goes through RFD-08.

**Technical errors, second applications.** A refund cannot be refused on a technicality when the substantive conditions are met. A second application for the same period, for an amount or invoice left out, is maintainable within two years (Gujarat HC *Shree Renuka Sugars*, 13.07.2023; Bombay HC *Valmet Flow Control*, 22.04.2026). A clerical error corrected in the RFD-09 reply must be considered (Delhi HC *Shri Shyam Footwear*, 31.01.2023). *Takeaway:* do not reject a supplementary claim merely because an RFD-01 for that period has already been processed. Examine it on merits within the time limit.

**Interest under s.56.** Interest runs from the 61st day after the **original** application, even where the claim was rejected first and allowed later on appeal (Ranbaxy SC under s.11BB; Bombay HC *Altisource* 30.09.2025; Gujarat HC *Kuehne Nagel*). Telangana HC holds interest is automatic and compensatory (*Qualcom India*, 20.03.2024). Interest-only claims need no rule 89(2)(m) CA certificate (*Synchrony International*, 2026). Both are **BINDING IN TELANGANA**. *Takeaway:* when sanctioning a refund late, compute and grant s.56 interest suo motu from 60 days after the original ARN. Do not demand fresh certificates for interest alone.

**IGST export refunds, rule 96(10), rule 96(4).** The SC (*Cosmo Films*, 28.04.2023) upheld the pre-import condition for advance authorisations. Rule 96(10) was omitted by Notf. 20/2024-CT (08.10.2024) without a saving clause. Gujarat HC (*Addwrap*, 13.06.2025) held the omission applies to all pending proceedings, and the SC (*Goodluck India*, 06.08.2026) dismissed the Revenue's appeals. CBIC reportedly accepted this by an Office Memorandum dated 24.08.2026. *Takeaway:* do not deny or recover an export refund under rule 96(10) in any proceeding still pending. Withhold under rule 96(4) only on the grounds the rule lists. A "risky exporter" tag alone is not a ground (Gujarat HC *Choksi Exports*; *Agriex* not verified).

**Intermediary (export of services).** In Bombay HC (*Dharmendra M Jani*), after a split verdict, the referee judge (18.04.2023) upheld s.13(8)(b) IGST provided it is confined to IGST and not used to levy CGST/SGST. The Finance Act 2026 **omitted s.13(8)(b)**, reportedly from 30.03.2026, so the default s.13(2) rule (recipient's location) now applies. Whether the omission affects pending disputes is unsettled. Delhi HC (*Ernst & Young*, 23.03.2023) holds that a person supplying services on its own account is not an intermediary. Telangana HC (*Virtusa*, 05.01.2026; *Commscope*, 15.10.2025) quashed and remanded cryptic intermediary findings. *Takeaway:* an "intermediary" finding needs an analysis of the contract showing the supplier arranges or facilitates a supply between two other persons. A bare label is quashed as non-speaking.

**SEZ supplies.** Delay in the Specified Officer's endorsement, where that delay is attributable to the officer, should not defeat a refund (*Lenovo*, Madras). Madras HC (*Lenovo*) held the "authorised operations" endorsement cannot be insisted on for supplies before 01.10.2023. Bombay HC (*Lupin*, 21.11.2025) held an SEZ unit may itself claim refund of accumulated ITC (following Gujarat HC *Britannia*) and remanded for the endorsement point to be examined. *Takeaway:* if an endorsement is missing, flag it by deficiency memo and allow it to be cured. Do not reject outright.

**Withholding under s.54(10)/(11) and pending appeals.** A department's plan to appeal does not suspend an appellate order (s.107(16)). Withholding under s.54(11) needs a pending appeal, the Commissioner's opinion that the refund would adversely affect revenue because of malfeasance or fraud, and a hearing (Telangana HC *ADV Industrial Services*, Sept 2026). This is **BINDING IN TELANGANA**. *Takeaway:* do not reject a refund that follows an appellate order on the ground that "the department proposes to appeal". If the refund is to be withheld, use the s.54(11) procedure formally.

**Unjust enrichment.** For export of services, s.54(8)(d) does not bar the refund, because the incidence is not passed on to anyone in India (Bombay HC; party name not found). AP HC (*Shapoorji Pallonji*, 18.12.2024) remanded a rejection of a cash-ledger (left-over TDS) refund for reconsideration under Circular 166/22/2021-GST, without itself ruling on unjust enrichment. *Takeaway:* apply the unjust-enrichment test only to categories outside s.54(8)(a)-(e), and base it on evidence.

**Amounts paid during search or investigation, and pre-deposits.** A deposit made under coercion during a search, without a DRC-04 acknowledgement, is involuntary and refundable with interest (Delhi HC *Vallabh Textiles*; Bombay and Karnataka HCs). A pre-deposit refund cannot be withheld on extraneous grounds such as a pending SFIO probe (Odisha HC). *Takeaway:* a refund of a pre-deposit after a favourable appellate order should be processed promptly. Rejection cannot rest on the fact that some unrelated inquiry is pending.

---

### 2. Issue-wise case entries

#### A. Inverted duty structure: input services and rule 89(5)

#### Union of India v. VKC Footsteps India Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court - 13 Sep 2021
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 4810 of 2021 and batch; AIR 2021 SC 4407 (Dr D.Y. Chandrachud & M.R. Shah JJ.; Indian Kanoon header lists B.V. Nagarathna J., but the signed judgment shows M.R. Shah J.)
- Holding: Rule 89(5) is valid (para 70-81, 112-113). IDS refund under s.54(3)(ii) is limited to unutilised ITC on inputs (goods); input services are excluded. The proviso to s.54(3) restricts refund and is not a condition of eligibility. The Court acknowledged anomalies in the formula and urged the GST Council to reconsider it.
- Binding: Binding on all authorities (Art. 141).
- Later history: Formula amended by Notf. 14/2022-CT (05.07.2022). A Finance Bill 2026 proposal reportedly rewrites s.54(3) to include input services and capital goods (TaxGuru), but **enactment is unconfirmed**. Separately, the Finance Act 2026 extended 90% provisional refunds under s.54(6) to IDS claims from a date to be notified.
- Use in orders: Cite it to exclude ITC on input services and capital goods from "Net ITC" in an IDS refund.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/92024267/); [Cleartax news](https://news.cleartax.in/supreme-court-upheld-the-madras-hc-decision-regarding-cgst-rule-895/7009/amp); [iPleaders case analysis](https://blog.ipleaders.in/case-analysis-of-union-of-india-vs-vkc-footsteps-india-private-limited/)
- Key words: "The proviso to Section 54(3) is not a condition of eligibility (as the assessees' Counsel submitted) but a restriction which must govern the grant of refund under Section 54(3)." (para 70)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case number confirmed, bench and AIR citation added. Para 113(iii) refers paras 104-111 (anomalies) to the GST Council.

#### VKC Footsteps India Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 24 Jul 2020
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 2792 of 2019 (per SC judgment, fn. 4); reported citation not found
- Holding: Explanation (a) to rule 89(5), which excluded ITC on input services from "Net ITC", was ultra vires s.54(3). Refund was directed treating ITC on input services as part of Net ITC.
- Binding: **Reversed**. No longer good law.
- Later history: Set aside by the SC on 13.09.2021.
- Use in orders: Do not follow it. Cite it only as the decision the SC reversed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment reversing it, paras 4 and 113](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/92024267/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1085598); [DPNC](https://www.dpncindia.com/gujarat-high-court-allows-refund-of-accumulated-credit-on-input-service-in-case-of-inverted-duty-structure)
- Key words: "Explanation (a) to Rule 89(5) which denies the refund of "unutilised input tax" paid on "input services" ... is ultra vires the provision of Section 54(3)" (Gujarat HC as quoted in the SC judgment, para 4)
- Verified: Primary (indirect) - Gujarat HC judgment itself not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 (3 searches); date (24.07.2020) and holding confirmed from the SC judgment in VKC Footsteps, read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026

#### Transtonnelstroy Afcons JV v. Union of India - Madras HC - 21 Sep 2020
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8596 of 2019 and batch (21 writ petitions of 2019-2020); A.P. Sahi CJ & Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J.; reserved 28.08.2020 and 03.09.2020, delivered 21.09.2020; reported citation not found
- Holding: Upheld s.54(3)(ii) (no breach of Art. 14) and rule 89(5) as amended. Refund of accumulated ITC is confined to credit built up because the rate on input goods exceeds the output rate; credit from input services is validly excluded. All writ petitions dismissed. Declined to follow Gujarat HC.
- Binding: Persuasive; its view was affirmed by the SC in VKC Footsteps.
- Later history: Affirmed by the SC (13.09.2021).
- Use in orders: Cite it together with VKC Footsteps.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/79852307/); [amlegals](https://amlegals.com/refund-of-itc-is-not-available-for-input-services-under-inverted-duty-structure/); [TheTaxTalk](https://thetaxtalk.com/2020/09/gst-controversy-on-inverted-rate-structure-to-continue-madras-high-court-has-ruled-against-the-itc-claim/)
- Key words: "Rule 89(5) of the CGST Rules, as amended, is in conformity with Section 54(3)(ii)." (para 63(5))
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case number, bench and dates added

#### Ascent Meditech Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC - 17 Oct 2024
- Citation / case no.: Special Civil Application No. 18317 of 2023, decided 17.10.2024 (per SC record of proceedings and Gujarat HC in Tirth Agro). Reported citations (2024 (12) TMI 511; 2025 (93) GSTL 85; (2025) 145 GSTR 246) not checked.
- Holding: The amendment to the rule 89(5) formula by Notf. 14/2022-CT is curative and clarificatory, so it applies retrospectively to refund or rectification applications filed within the s.54(1) period. Circular 181/13/2022 was quashed to the extent it treated the amendment as prospective.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana. The SLP was dismissed in limine ("No case for interference"), which does not make the HC's reasoning SC precedent.
- Later history: SLP (C) No. 8134 of 2025 dismissed on 28.03.2025 (Abhay S. Oka & Ujjal Bhuyan JJ.); Review Petition (Diary No. 38626 of 2025) dismissed by circulation on 02.12.2025 (Surya Kant CJI & Ujjal Bhuyan J.). Followed by Gujarat HC in Tirth Agro Technology (SCA 14649/2020, 18.12.2024). Later Gujarat HC (July 2026) and GSTAT applications reported, not checked.
- Use in orders: Apply the amended formula to claims that are within time, including those for pre-July 2022 periods.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC SLP dismissal 28.03.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/108334791/); [Indian Kanoon - SC review dismissal 02.12.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17756837/); [Indian Kanoon - Tirth Agro (Guj HC) quoting paras 45-47](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/31444002/); [TaxGuru - GSTAT Lucknow on rule 89(5)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/rule-89-5-amendment-curative-retrospective-gst-refunds-gstat-lucknow.html); [taxo.online 10.07.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/10-07-2026-deficiency-memos-rejecting-refund-claims-under-the-earlier-interpretation-of-rule-895-are-liable-to-be-reconsidered-in-light-of-the-amended-legal-position-gujarat-high-court/)
- Key words: "Notification No. 14/2022 dated 05.07.2022 cannot be applied prospectively for the refund claim which were made within two years as prescribed under section 54(1)" (para 45, as quoted in Tirth Agro)
- Verified: Primary (partly indirect) - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026: SC orders read in full; HC judgment itself not found on Indian Kanoon, its paras 45-47 read as quoted in Tirth Agro (Guj HC, 18.12.2024). Date and case number added; SLP and review dismissals confirmed

#### B. Inverted duty: Circular 135/2020 (same goods or same rate) and Circular 173/2022

#### Micro Systems and Services v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 05 Sep 2022
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 37465 of 2021 (Ujjal Bhuyan CJ & C.V. Bhaskar Reddy J.); 2022 (9) TMI 420 (not checked)
- Holding: Circular 173/05/2022 (06.07.2022) clarified that para 3.2 of Circular 135/2020 does not cover supplies under a concessional notification where the output rate is lower than the input rate on the same goods at the same time. Being clarificatory, it takes effect from 31.03.2020. The refund rejection (Rs 77,91,857, Apr 2019-Mar 2020, computers supplied to DRDO at 5%) and the appellate order were set aside and the claim remanded for decision within 8 weeks. The prayer to declare Circular 135 ultra vires was not decided.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Do not apply para 3.2 of Circular 135 to concessional-rate or structurally inverted supplies for any period from 31.03.2020.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/166094515/); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/refund-allowed-where-inputs-and-output-goods-are-same-but-supply-made-under-concessional-rate-notification-hc/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/circular-would-be-applicable-retrospectively-refund-in-case-of-inverted-duty-structure-allowed-telangana-high-court/)
- Key words: "Being clarificatory, Circular dated 06.07.2022 inserting the above clarification would have the effect from the date when Circular dated 31.03.2020 came into effect." (para 14)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; date (05.09.2022) and bench confirmed

#### BMG Informatics Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gauhati HC - 02 Sep 2021
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 3878 of 2021 with WP(C) Nos. 3880, 3675 and 4120 of 2021; AIROnline 2021 Gau 779 (Achintya Malla Bujor Barua J., as listed on Indian Kanoon)
- Holding: CORRECTED: the case is about para 3.2 of Circular 135/2020 (same input and output goods), not input services. Para 3.2 conflicts with s.54(3)(ii) and must be ignored; a partial-exemption rate (Notf. 45/2017-CT(R), 2.5% to Govt/PSU/research institutions) is neither nil nor fully exempt. Both the AC's rejection and the appellate order were set aside and the claim (Rs 3,92,594, Oct-Dec 2018) remanded for a factual finding on input vs output rates within 6 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana. Consistent with Telangana HC in Micro Systems (Circular 135 para 3.2 not applied to concessional-rate supplies).
- Later history: none found. It pre-dates VKC Footsteps (SC, 13.09.2021) but does not deal with input services, so VKC does not overrule it.
- Use in orders: Do not reject an IDS claim only because input and output are the same goods; record a finding on the actual input and output rates.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/129124717/); [Cleartax news](https://news.cleartax.in/?p=7009)
- Key words: "the provisions of paragraph 3.2 of the circular No.135/05/2020-GST dated 31.03.2020 ... in cases where the input and output supplies are same, would have to be ignored." (para 28)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; MATERIAL CORRECTION: earlier summary (no distinction between inputs and input services; overtaken by VKC) was wrong; holding, date, case number and use rewritten

#### South Indian Oil Corporation v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax - Karnataka HC - 12 Dec 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 22068 of 2024 (T-RES); NC: 2025:KHC:53009; oral order of S.R. Krishna Kumar J. (single judge); 2025 (12) TMI 1537 (not checked)
- Holding: Edible oil bought in bulk at 5% and sold packed at 5%: ITC accumulated because other inputs (e.g. packing) carried higher rates. Following its earlier ruling in Indian Oil Corporation Ltd (WP 14414/2024, 20.08.2024), which relied on Shivaco Associates (Cal), BMG Informatics (Gau) and others, the court held para 3.2 of Circular 135 applies only where credit accumulated because of different rates at different points of time, and s.54(3)(ii) does not require comparing only the principal input with the output. Rejections and appellate order quashed; refund with applicable interest directed within 3 months.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Before invoking Circular 135, record a finding on why the credit actually accumulated.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/98841608/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/22068-of-2024-t-res.html); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/inverted-duty-refund-allowed-even-when-input-and-output-gst-rates-are-same-hc)
- Key words: "the said Circular applies only to scenarios/situations where the ITC is accumulated on account of different rates being applicable at different points of time" (para 16 of Indian Oil Corporation, quoted and followed at paras 2-3)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary broadly confirmed; corrected: single-judge oral order following Indian Oil Corporation (WP 14414/2024); time for compliance is 3 months

(A Madras HC ruling reported on taxo.online, July 2026, rejected the "principal input" theory and Circular 135 reliance. The party name was not found, so no entry is made. See Gaps.)

#### C. Limitation under s.54(1): directory or mandatory, and the COVID exclusion

#### Lenovo (India) Pvt Ltd v. Joint Commissioner of GST (Appeals-1) - Madras HC - 06 Nov 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 23604, 23605 and 23607 of 2022 (Krishnan Ramasamy J., single judge)
- Holding: (i) s.54(1) uses "may" and the two-year limit was described as directory (para 15.7), but the applications here were filed within two years, so this is obiter (para 15.8). (ii) Delay in the SEZ Authorised Officer's endorsement is the officer's fault and cannot bar the claim; mistakes in the endorsement are technical (paras 14.5-14.6). (iii) The "authorised operations" endorsement cannot be insisted on for supplies before 01.10.2023 (para 14.7). (iv) Where documents were lacking, rule 90(3) required a deficiency memo. Notf. 13/2022 exclusion also applied. Orders set aside; refund to issue within 30 days. (IGST on SEZ supplies, Dec 2019-Feb 2020.)
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Karnataka HC DB (Merck, 2026) takes the contrary view on limitation. No appeal found.
- Use in orders: Be aware of it, but do not condone delay yourself. Rely on rule 90(3) compliance.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/103219958/); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan](https://lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/refund-time-limit-under-section-54-1-is-not-mandatory); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/madras-high-court/2-years-limitation-gst-refund-application-directory-mandatory-madras-high-court-242814)
- Key words: "The time limit fixed under Section 54 (1) is directory in nature and it is not mandatory." (para 15.7)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added that the directory remark is obiter (claim was within time) and the pre-01.10.2023 'authorised operations' finding

#### Assistant Commissioner of Central Taxes v. Merck Life Science Pvt Ltd (batch) - Karnataka HC (Division Bench) - 17 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: Writ Appeal No. 110 of 2026 (T-RES) c/w WA Nos. 119, 122, 126 and 140 of 2026 (S.G. Pandit & K.V. Aravind JJ.); against single-judge order dated 07.11.2025 in WP No. 27259/2024 and batch; 2026 (3) TMI 1200 (not checked)
- Holding: The two-year period in s.54 is **mandatory**; the proper officer has no power to entertain a refund claim filed after two years (paras 9.5-9.8, 12(a)). The single judge's "directory" view was set aside. A claimant may seek condonation under Art. 226; if the court condones delay, the proper officer gets a corresponding extension of time for s.73/74 action (para 12). On the facts (IGST paid for Oct 2017 treating the service as export, later paid CGST/SGST as intra-State; claim filed 30.03.2024, about 6 months beyond the rule 89(1A) window; entitlement not disputed) delay was condoned and the claim directed to be considered within 60 days. Appeals allowed in part.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana. It is the most recent appellate-level view found.
- Later history: none found (SLP status not checked)
- Use in orders: Cite it for the point that the proper officer cannot entertain a time-barred claim. Leave condonation to the court.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/127010713/); [LiveLaw Biz](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst/high-court/gst-refund-limitation-mandatory-delay-can-be-condoned-under-article-226-in-cases-of-genuine-hardship-karnataka-high-court-530592); [taxo.online 21.03.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/21-03-2026-refund-limitation-under-gst-is-mandatory-but-high-courts-can-condon-delay-in-genuine-hardship-cases-karnataka-high-court/)
- Key words: "In the absence of such enabling provisions, the period of two years remains mandatory in so far as the proper officer is concerned." (para 9.5)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case numbers, bench and single-judge date (07.11.2025) added

#### Nspira Management Services Pvt Ltd v. Assistant/Deputy Commissioner of Central Tax - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench) - 26 Sep 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 18287 & 14905 of 2024 (R. Raghunandan Rao & T.C.D. Sekhar JJ.; common order by T.C.D. Sekhar J.)
- Holding: CORRECTED: the facts are GST charged by landlords on residential dwellings rented for student hostels, which are exempt under Entry 12 of Notf. 12/2017-CT(R) (refund of Rs 11.49 crore etc., Jul 2017-Jun 2022). It is not an IGST/CGST wrong-head case and the court did not say s.54 is directory. Following Gujarat HC (Comsol Energy), tax paid without authority of law is not "tax" and s.54 limitation does not apply (Art. 265). Deficiency memos that returned the applications as time-barred were set aside, and the claims were directed to be decided without going into limitation within 4 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana (AP HC; same parent HC before 2019, but this is a post-bifurcation ruling).
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Distinguish it where the tax was legally payable. Note also that the claims were returned as time-barred through deficiency memos, which the court set aside; a limitation objection should go through RFD-08 and a hearing.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/16374299/); [EY alert](https://www.ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2025/11/hc-holds-two-year-period-of-limitation-for-claiming-refund-of-wrongly-paid-tax-is-not-mandatory); [a2ztaxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/?p=19669)
- Key words: "the deficiency memos under challenge are set aside and the respondents are directed to consider the application of the petitioner for refund of tax without going into the question of limitation." (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; MATERIAL CORRECTION: facts were exempt residential renting paid to landlords, not IGST paid on an intra-State supply; ratio is 'amount paid without authority of law is outside s.54 limitation', not 'directory'

#### Saiher Supply Chain Consulting Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Bombay HC (Division Bench) - 10 Jan 2022
- Citation / case no.: WP (L) No. 1275 of 2021 (R.D. Dhanuka & S.M. Modak JJ.)
- Holding: The SC's suo motu orders (23.03.2020 and 23.09.2021) excluding 15.03.2020-02.10.2021 bind the refund officer and apply to the s.54(1) period. The third refund application (30.09.2020, for Jul-Sep 2018, filed after two earlier applications were returned for deficiencies) was within time; its rejection as time-barred was quashed and the application restored for decision on merits. The challenge to rule 90(3) and the circular was left open.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Revenue's SLP (C) No. 12404 of 2022 dismissed on 29.07.2022 (M.R. Shah & B.V. Nagarathna JJ.), directing the third application to be considered on merits. In practice overtaken by Notf. 13/2022-CT, which excludes 01.03.2020-28.02.2022 for s.54/55 refunds. Note the dates differ between the SC orders and Notf. 13/2022.
- Use in orders: Always exclude 01.03.2020-28.02.2022 when computing the two years.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185169568/); [Indian Kanoon - SC SLP dismissal 29.07.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/112777256/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/supreme-court-bombay-high-court-gst-act-refund-covid-sc-order-suspension-of-limitation-189579); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/21-01-2022-refund-applications-will-also-be-entitled-for-the-extended-timelines-as-per-supreme-courts-extension-order-in-view-of-covid-pandemic/)
- Key words: "the Respondent No.2 is also bound by the said Order dated 23rd March 2020 and the Order dated 23rd September 2021 and is require to exclude the period of limitation falling during the said period." (para 14)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary corrected: the HC did not discuss a CBIC view limiting the SC orders to appeals; it expressly left rule 90(3) and the circular undecided. SLP dismissal (29.07.2022) added

(Delhi HC has remanded refund rejections where Notf. 13/2022 was not considered. Search results cite Delhi HC judgment PDFs of May 2025, but no party names were captured. Telangana HC's ruling upholding Notf. 13/2022 concerns s.73 adjudication, not refunds, and is reported to be under challenge in SLP 4240/2025: [JurisHour](https://jurishour.in/gst/gst-council-recommendation-not-a-precondition-for-issuing-s-168a-notification-telangana-high-court).)

#### D. Tax paid under the wrong head (s.77 CGST / s.19 IGST / rule 89(1A))

#### Axis Bank Ltd v. Union of India - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench) - 29 Sep 2022
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 11424 of 2021 (C. Praveen Kumar & A.V. Ravindra Babu JJ.)
- Holding: CORRECTED (overstated before): IGST was paid from the AP registration on services actually supplied from Telangana premises; CGST+SGST was later paid in Telangana and refund of IGST (Rs 16.90 crore, Jul 2017-Aug 2019) was rejected as time-barred under s.54. The court did not itself decide the limitation point. Because Circular 162/18/2021 (25.09.2021) and rule 89(1A) post-dated the rejection, it remanded the claim to the Assistant Commissioner to decide limitation and documents afresh in the light of that circular.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: For s.77 / s.19 IGST claims, apply rule 89(1A) and Circular 162/18/2021 (two years from payment under the correct head; for correct-head payments before 24.09.2021, two years from 24.09.2021), not s.54 counted from the original payment.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/118832787/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/andhra-pradesh-high-court-quashes-rejection-gst-refund-application-axis-bank-210866); [Prime Legal](https://blog.primelegal.in/?p=36509)
- Key words: "to remand the matter back to the authority ... to deal with the refund application in the light of the Circular No.162/18/2021-GST dated 25.09.2021" (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; MATERIAL CORRECTION: it is a remand, not a holding that time runs from the date rule 89(1A) was introduced; date and case number added

#### M/s Sai Steel v. State of Bihar - Patna HC (Division Bench) - 28 Jul 2025
- Citation / case no.: Civil Writ Jurisdiction Case No. 13163 of 2024 (Rajeev Ranjan Prasad & Shailendra Singh JJ.); marked AFR
- Holding: CGST+SGST of Rs 5,08,194 was paid in January 2018 on what was an inter-State supply; IGST was later paid by DRC-03 on 04.03.2023. Under s.77 CGST / s.19 IGST read with Circular 162/18/2021, the two years run from the date tax was paid under the correct head (04.03.2023), not from January 2018. Rejection set aside; refund directed with 6% interest from three months after the application, plus Rs 10,000 costs, within three months.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Compute limitation from the date of payment under the correct head.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/18348820/); [taxo.online 12.08.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/12-08-2025-relevant-date-for-computing-limitation-for-refund-under-section-77-is-the-date-on-which-tax-under-the-correct-head-patna-high-court/)
- Key words: "the relevant date for counting the period of limitation would start from the date when the petitioner had deposited the tax under IGST Act, in the present case, the said date is 04.03.2023." (para 16)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name (Sai Steel), date (28.07.2025) and case number identified; holding confirmed; interest and costs added

#### Celebrity Structures - Karnataka HC - Dec 2025 (exact date not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: In s.77 CGST / s.19 IGST cases, the limitation in s.54 and rule 89(1A) is directory. Remitted for reconsideration on merits.
- Binding: Persuasive. Note possible tension with the later Karnataka DB in *Merck* (Mar 2026).
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Note only; follow the correct-head relevant date.
- Source: [taxo.online 21.01.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/21-01-2026-time-limitation-prescribed-u-s-54-is-directory-where-refund-claim-filed-for-tax-paid-under-an-incorrect-head-karnataka-high-court/)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026, 3 searches)

#### Amidc Automation Technologies Pvt Ltd v. Central Board of Indirect Taxes and Customs - Karnataka HC - 30 Oct 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 16978 of 2025 (T-RES); NC: 2025:KHC:43481; oral order of S.R. Krishna Kumar J. (single judge)
- Holding: Wrong-head refund under s.77 CGST (Jan 2019-Mar 2022). The first application (09.05.2023) and a later one (28.02.2024) were within time, and the officer failed to apply the Notf. 13/2022-CT exclusion (Mar 2020-Feb 2022). Rejection (RFD-06, 21.02.2025) set aside; the court itself held the applications from 09.05.2023 onward within time and directed decision on merits with applicable interest within three months.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Apply the Notf. 13/2022 exclusion to s.77 claims as well.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/66479771/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/16978-of-2025-t-res.html); [Taxheal (2)](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=112039)
- Key words: "It is held that the refund claim of the petitioner vide several refund applications commencing from 09.05.2023 up to 03.02.2025 are not barred by limitation and the same are within time." (para 8(iii))
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; full party name, exact date (30.10.2025) and case number confirmed; holding confirmed with detail added (first application within time)

#### E. Rejection on technicalities, second or supplementary applications, natural justice

#### Shree Renuka Sugars Ltd v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 13 Jul 2023
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 22339 of 2022 (Vipul M. Pancholi & Devan M. Desai JJ.)
- Holding: Refund of accumulated ITC on exports without payment of tax (11 months) was under-claimed through an employee's arithmetical error: of about Rs 110.68 crore, Rs 100.47 crore had been granted, leaving Rs 10,20,28,733. The supplementary applications, filed within the s.54(1) period under the "any other" category because the portal did not allow a second application in the same category, could not be rejected on that technicality. Rejection orders (26.08.2022) quashed; the petitioner was allowed to file manually, subject to scrutiny in accordance with law, within six weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Bombay HC in *Rika Global Impex* (WP 2310/2024), as quoted in *Valmet Flow Control* (22.04.2026, para 12), records that it attained finality on the SC dismissing the SLPs (SLP details not checked). Followed by Bombay HC (*Valmet Flow Control*, 2026) and reportedly Rajasthan HC (2024).
- Use in orders: Do not reject a supplementary claim for a period because an earlier RFD-01 for that period was processed. Examine the claim on merits within time.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/179140349/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gujarat-hc-permits-second-gst-refund-application-period.html); [StudyCafe](https://studycafe.in/gst-refund-cannot-be-rejected-outright-merely-on-technicality-when-substantive-conditions-are-satisfied-238880.html)
- Key words: "the benefit which otherwise a person is entitled to once the substantive conditions are satisfied cannot be denied due to a technical error or lacunae in the electronic system." (para 14)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; bench added. The Rs 100.47 crore figure in StudyCafe is the amount already refunded, so there is no conflict in the facts

#### Valmet Flow Control Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Bombay HC (Division Bench) - 22 Apr 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 14685 of 2025; 2026:BHC-AS:19642-DB (G.S. Kulkarni & Aarti Sathe JJ.)
- Holding: s.54(1) does not bar more than one refund application, particularly where an invoice (August 2022) was inadvertently left out, provided the application is within two years. Res judicata or analogous principles do not apply to such applications. The officer ought to have followed *Shree Renuka Sugars*, which had attained finality (citing Bombay HC in *Rika Global Impex*, WP 2310/2024). Rejection (Rs 1,10,52,474, Jul-Sep 2022, AC order 03.04.2025) quashed; the application of 09.08.2024 restored for fresh decision after a hearing; the interest claim kept open.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: As for Shree Renuka Sugars.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/149952733/); [amlegals](https://amlegals.com/gst-refund-relief-bombay-high-court-upholds-supplementary-refund-claims-within-limitation-period/); [LiveLaw Biz](https://www.livelawbiz.com/amp/gst/high-court/gst-law-does-not-bar-more-than-one-refund-application-especially-in-case-of-inadvertent-lapse-bombay-hc-533363)
- Key words: "sub-section (1) of Section 54 does not provide any bar for a party to maintain more than one application, and more particularly, in a case where there is an inadvertent mistake or lapse." (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; neutral citation added

#### Shri Shyam Footwear v. Commissioner of CGST - Delhi HC (Division Bench) - 31 Jan 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) 5845/2022; 2023/DHC/000843 (Vibhu Bakhru & Amit Mahajan JJ.)
- Holding: Refund of unutilised ITC (Rs 4,28,400, Oct-Dec 2020) was rejected because column 7 of Annexure B wrongly showed "Goods". The petitioner admitted the error in RFD-09 and filed a corrected annexure, which neither the original nor the appellate authority considered. The assessee cannot be penalised for an inadvertent error that has been rectified. Both orders set aside and remanded; the claim to be processed within four weeks, with a hearing before any rejection.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Where an RFD-09 reply corrects a clerical error, consider the corrected data. Do not reject on the original error.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/53182714/); [CAclubindia](https://www.caclubindia.com/judiciary/refund-cannot-be-denied-for-an-inadvertent-error-which-was-subsequently-rectified-5187.asp); [Busy caselaw](https://busy.in/caselaw/shri-shyam-footwear-vs-the-commissioner-of-central-goods-and-services-tax-other-high-court-order-delhi-6951)
- Key words: "Clearly, the petitioner cannot be penalised for the inadvertent error in submitting an erroneous information against Column No. 7 of its form, which has since been rectified." (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (it is a remand, not a direct refund order); neutral citation added. Note: it concerns rectified clerical errors, not second applications

#### Deccan Fine Chemicals (India) Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 14 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 14191 of 2019; oral order (A.S. Supehia & Vaibhavi D. Nanavati JJ.)
- Holding: Refund of accumulated ITC on exports without payment of IGST (Sep and Oct 2018) was partly rejected by the Deputy Commissioner of State Tax (orders of 29.12.2018, rejected portion relating to ITC on capital goods) without reasons and without a hearing; the standing counsel could not controvert this. Orders quashed and remanded: issue a show cause notice, consider the reply, and pass a reasoned order within 12 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: A partial rejection also needs an RFD-08 notice, a hearing and reasons.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/73011674/); [StudyCafe](https://studycafe.in/high-court-remands-gst-refund-matter-for-fresh-decision-after-authority-failed-to-give-reasons-427119.html)
- Key words: "the respondent authority has not assigned any reason for rejecting the part of the refund claim ... the petitioner has not been afforded any opportunity of hearing before rejecting the part of the refund claim." (para 5)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; full name, date (14.09.2026), case number and bench added. Short order on natural justice; no point of law decided on capital goods

#### DSV Air and Sea International Pvt Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 25 Apr 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 12325 & 12447 of 2025; common order (Sujoy Paul ACJ & Renuka Yara J.)
- Holding: Refund rejection orders (06.02.2025 and 13.03.2025, State Tax) did not show application of mind to the detailed replies to the show cause notices. With the State's consent, both were set aside and the petitioner directed to appear for a rehearing and fresh orders, without any opinion on merits.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** only in a limited sense: a consent remand without opinion on merits, so it lays down no ratio. Use it as an illustration, not as authority.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Deal with each point in the RFD-09 reply in the RFD-06 order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17295601/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1087737)
- Key words: "the refund rejection orders, impugned herein, do not reflect any application of mind on the replies and specific stands taken therein by the petitioner." (para 3, petitioner's submission accepted without objection)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; full name, date (25.04.2025), case numbers and bench added. The orders under challenge were State Tax orders

#### ADV Industrial Services Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 08 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 3663 of 2026; CNR HBHC010070742026 (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ & G.M. Mohiuddin J.)
- Holding: After an Order-in-Appeal (16.09.2025) set aside an ITC demand of Rs 19,33,34,320 paid during investigation, the consequential refund (RFD-01 of 28.10.2025) was rejected on 12.01.2026 because the department had decided to appeal; the GSTAT appeal was filed only on 25.03.2026. Under s.107(16) the appellate order was binding until stayed, modified or set aside; the refund officer could not reopen the ITC findings (judicial discipline, Kamalakshi Finance). Withholding is possible only under s.54(11): pending appeal, Commissioner's opinion on malfeasance or fraud, and a hearing, none of which existed. RFD-06 quashed; fresh reasoned order within 4 weeks.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Never reject a consequential refund because "the department proposes to appeal". If the refund must be held back, follow s.54(11) formally (pending appeal, Commissioner's opinion, hearing).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/100761566/); [LiveLaw Biz](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst-vat-cst/gst-refund-cannot-be-rejected-merely-because-department-plans-to-challenge-appellate-order-telangana-high-court-550718); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/departmental-appeal-plan-cannot-defeat-gst-refund-telangana-hc.html)
- Key words: "the mere decision to challenge the appellate order, or the subsequent filing of an appeal, did not amount to a stay or suspension of the operation of the Order-in-Appeal." (para 20)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; date corrected to 08.09.2026 (reports of 18-19.09.2026 were report dates); case number added

#### F. Deficiency memo (RFD-03) and its effect on limitation

#### Varidhi Cotspin Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 07 Oct 2025
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 3595 of 2024 (Bhargav D. Karia & Pranav Trivedi JJ.)
- Holding: Refund of IGST (Rs 83,51,438) on capital goods bought domestically under EPCG was first claimed on 13.11.2018, followed by rectified applications after deficiency memos and an earlier remand. Following its ruling in *La-Gajjar Machineries* (SCA 15782/2022, 27.09.2023), the court held that limitation stops running on filing an application in the prescribed form even if the officer later seeks documents, and the period between RFD-01 and the RFD-03 memo is excluded (proviso to rule 90(3), Notf. 15/2021, treated as clarificatory). The rejection of the rectified application (16.08.2023) as time-barred was quashed and the claim restored for decision on merits within 12 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: For limitation, take the date of the original ARN where a rectified application follows an RFD-03.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/133652694/); [taxo.online 15.10.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/15-10-2025-filing-a-rectified-refund-application-after-a-deficiency-memo-under-rule-903-is-not-a-fresh-refund-claim-it-is-a-continuation-of-the-original-timely-claim-gujarat-high-court/); [CA Sahuja blog](https://www.casahuja.com/2025/10/filing-rectified-refund-application.html)
- Key words: "the period of limitation would stop running notwithstanding that the proper officer required further documents or material to satisfy himself that the refund claimed was due to the petitioner." (para 9, quoting La-Gajjar Machineries)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; holding confirmed in substance and refined (it follows La-Gajjar Machineries and applies the rule 90(3) exclusion); case number and bench added

#### L.S. Pacific Wood Tech Pvt Ltd v. Commercial Tax Officer - Madras HC - 01 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 32615 of 2026 (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J., single judge)
- Holding: A deficiency memo (RFD-03, 16.04.2026) stating that the refund claim was time-barred was set at nought: rule 90(3) is for remediable defects, while a proposal to reject wholly or partly (including on limitation) must go by an RFD-08 notice, reply and hearing under rule 92(3). The officer was left free to issue a rule 92(3) notice within two weeks; no further action on the memo. (The court noted that the proviso to rule 90(3) excludes the RFD-01 to RFD-03 period from limitation.)
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: A limitation objection must go through RFD-08 and a hearing, not RFD-03.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/107775843/); [taxo.online 15.09.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/15-09-2026-time-bar-is-not-a-deficiency-deficiency-memo-cannot-be-issued-for-time-barred-claim-proper-officer-must-follow-rule-923-before-rejection-madras-high-court/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=140782)
- Key words: "As contended by learned counsel for the petitioner, a deficiency memo cannot be issued on the ground that the refund application is time barred." (para 5)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; full name, case number and judge added. Respondent was a State Commercial Tax Officer

(A Karnataka HC ruling, reported by TaxGuru, holds that a fresh application after repeated RFD-03 memos inherits the original filing date. Party name and date not found: [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1048298).)

#### G. Interest on delayed refund (s.56)

#### Ranbaxy Laboratories Ltd v. Union of India - Supreme Court - 21 Oct 2011
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 6823 of 2010 with CA Nos. 7637 of 2009 and 3088 of 2010; (2011) 10 SCC 292; AIR 2012 SC (Supp) 238 (D.K. Jain & Anil R. Dave JJ.). 2011 (273) ELT 3 (SC) not checked.
- Holding: Under s.11BB of the Central Excise Act, interest runs from the expiry of 3 months after the refund **application** is received, not from the date of the refund order. The deeming provision for appellate orders does not postpone the start date.
- Binding: Binding (Art. 141). Under GST it is applied by analogy, since s.54/56 CGST are treated as pari materia with s.11B/11BB.
- Later history: Applied by HCs to s.56 CGST (e.g. Karnataka HC in South Indian Oil, 12.12.2025, citing it). A later SC reaffirmation was reported in search excerpts but not checked.
- Use in orders: Compute s.56 interest from the 61st day after the original application.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/1687838/); [Casemine column](https://www.casemine.com/in/column/interest-on-delayed-excise-refunds-in-india:-a-legal-analysis/view); [HNA LLP article](https://hnallp.com/assets/articles/79fef-interest-on-delayed-refunds.pdf)
- Key words: "the liability of the revenue to pay interest under Section 11BB of the Act commences from the date of expiry of three months from the date of receipt of application for refund" (para 15)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (Explanation to s.11BB held irrelevant to the start date, para 9); date (21.10.2011), case number and bench added

#### Qualcom India Pvt Ltd v. Deputy Commissioner (ST)(FAC) - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 20 Mar 2024
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 8869 and 8871 of 2023, common order (P. Sam Koshy & N. Tukaramji JJ.). WP 8871/2023 is by Microsoft Global Services Center (India) Pvt Ltd. Indian Kanoon titles the case "Qualcomm India Private Limited"; the cause title reads "Qualcom".
- Holding: Refunds of unutilised ITC, rejected and then allowed on appeal, were paid without interest, and interest was refused (orders of 09.05.2022 and 19.02.2023, State Tax). Interest on delayed refund under s.56 is automatic; s.56, its proviso and explanation provide no situation in which a delayed refund does not carry interest. There was no reason or prohibitory order for the delay, so denial of interest is a failure of statutory duty. Following Delhi HC, interest at 6% runs from 60 days after the first application, with the proviso's higher rate for the period after the post-appeal application. Writ petitions allowed; interest to be paid forthwith.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: When a refund follows an appellate order, grant s.56 interest without requiring a separate application.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/165943331/); [Telangana HC order PDF](https://csis.tshc.gov.in/hcorders/2023/wp/wp_8871_2023.pdf); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/interest-allowable-delayed-refunds-gst-input-tax-credit-telangana-hc.html)
- Key words: "interest on the delayed refund is automatic. As soon as there is a delay in refund of the money ... the said amount shall automatically be entitled to carry interest." (para 8)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added that the batch includes Microsoft Global Services Center (WP 8871/2023) and that the court adopted the 6% from first application / 9% after appellate application reading of s.56

#### Synchrony International Services Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 30 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 12047, 12052, 12127, 12128, 12130, 12132, 12133 and 12134 of 2026, common order (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ & G.M. Mohiuddin J.)
- Holding: Interest claims under s.56 on refunds already sanctioned and paid (Jul 2018-Mar 2021, some after appeal) had been pending since 03.06.2025, with repeated deficiency memos (State Tax) seeking the Circular 125/44/2019 'any other ground' documents, including a rule 89(2)(m) CA/CMA certificate. The court directed the proper officer to scrutinise the summary of refunds and decide the interest claim within two weeks after a hearing on 06.08.2026, without insisting on the rule 89(2)(m) certificate, since the claim relates only to interest on refund already sanctioned. It is a direction on the facts; the court did not itself make a broader ruling that interest is automatic (that was the petitioner's argument).
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Do not issue deficiency memos demanding a rule 89(2)(m) certificate for interest-only claims.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/180806353/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/ca-or-cost-accountant-certificate-under-rule-892m-not-required-for-interest-on-already-sanctioned-gst-refund-telangana-hc-1450366); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/12-08-2026-interest-on-delayed-gst-refund-is-automatic-department-cannot-insist-on-unnecessary-certification-for-interest-only-claims-telangana-high-court/)
- Key words: "without insisting upon certification as required under Rule 89(2)(m) of the CGST Rules as the claim relates only to the interest component over the refund already sanctioned in its favour" (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name (Synchrony), date (30.07.2026) and case numbers confirmed; holding narrowed (direction on facts; no criticism of memos beyond noting the third memo lacked particulars)

#### Altisource Business Solutions India Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Bombay HC (Division Bench) - 30 Sep 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 5312 of 2024 with WP(St) Nos. 1807 of 2025 and 37613 of 2024; 2025:BHC-OS:18088-DB (M.S. Sonak & Advait M. Sethna JJ.)
- Holding: Refund for export of software services (Rs 2,85,63,804; application 23.04.2020) was rejected on 14.09.2020, allowed on appeal on 27.10.2023, re-applied for on 28.11.2023 and paid on 05.02.2024 without interest. Following Bombay HC in *Lupin* (which relied on Delhi and Telangana HC rulings), 6% interest under s.56 runs from 60 days after the **original** application; 9% applies only if the refund is not made within 60 days of the post-appeal order or application. Interest at 6% directed from 60 days after 23.04.2020, within 6 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana. Consistent with Qualcom (Telangana).
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: For refunds following appeal, compute interest from the first ARN plus 60 days.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/156382228/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/6-percent-interest-payable-gst-refund-delayed-60-days-bombay-hc.html); [TaxGuru (2)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/interest-gst-refund-appeal-legal-position-section-56.html)
- Key words: "the respondents cannot resist paying statutory interest at the rate of 6% per annum." (para 19)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; connected petitions, neutral citation and bench added

#### Kuehne Nagel Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 02 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 4373 of 2026 with SCA Nos. 4408, 663 and 667 of 2026; common oral judgment (A.S. Supehia & Vaibhavi D. Nanavati JJ.)
- Holding: The original refund application (14.10.2023) was not processed; the HC (SCA 12151/2025, 06.11.2025) held that refusal illegal; a fresh application followed (11.11.2025) and refund of Rs 2,29,32,535 was paid, but interest of Rs 29,51,700 was refused by counting from the fresh application. Interest must be computed from the date of the original application. Denial set aside; interest claims to be decided within 12 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Same as Altisource.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/180104012/); [StudyCafe/aggregated](https://taxo.online/blogs/gujarat-high-courts-ruling-demands-interest-on-delayed-gst-refunds-a-win-for-businesses/)
- Key words: "the respondent authority was required to consider the date of initial refund application filed by the petitioner for calculating the interest instead of the subsequent application" (para 7)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; full name, date (02.07.2026), case numbers and bench added

#### Baba Super Minerals Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Rajasthan HC (Division Bench, Jaipur) - 05 Jan 2024
- Citation / case no.: D.B. Civil Writ Petition No. 13746/2022 with CWP Nos. 13747, 13962 and 14201 of 2022; 2024:RJ-JP:742-DB (Arun Bhansali & Ashutosh Kumar JJ.)
- Holding: Refunds were paid more than 60 days after application but without interest, and the appellate authority did not deal with interest. Interest under s.56 is payable; "date of receipt of the application" means the date of a complete application, i.e. where deficiencies were pointed out, the date they were removed. Interest directed to be calculated and paid accordingly (in one of the four cases there was no deficiency).
- Binding: Persuasive. Note that it is in tension with the "original application" line (Altisource, Varidhi Cotspin) where the deficiency was minor.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Where an application was genuinely incomplete (RFD-03 issued and later cured), the officer may argue that interest runs from completion. Record the deficiency clearly.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/52254613/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/interest-under-section-56-cannot-be-denied-on-the-ground-of-discrepancies-in-the-refund-application-rajasthan-high-court/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=107594)
- Key words: "'the date of receipt of the application', can only be read as date of receipt of a 'complete application' i.e. in case, there are deficiencies, from the date the deficiencies are removed by the applicant." (para 11)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (it is both pro-taxpayer, granting interest, and holds the 60 days run from the complete application); date, case numbers and bench added

#### Anita Agarwal (Shanker International) v. Union of India - Bombay HC (Division Bench) - 14 Nov 2024
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 1474 of 2023; 2024:BHC-AS:43508-DB (M.S. Sonak & Jitendra S. Jain JJ.)
- Holding: An exporter red-flagged as risky got its IGST export refund late because the department did not finish verification within the time set by Circulars 16/2019 and 131/1/2020. The delay was the department's, and s.56 contains no exclusion for investigation periods. Interest runs from 60 days after the shipping bill (deemed refund application) to the date of refund, but the court deducted 30 days as a reasonable verification period under Circular 16/2019. Interest to be paid within eight weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: An investigation alone does not stop interest running; beyond the circulars' verification window the department bears the delay. Use s.54(10)/(11) or rule 96(4) formally if withholding.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/146440984/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/blogs/allowed-interest-on-delayed-igst-refund-to-red-flagged-exporter-due-to-inaction-by-authorities/)
- Key words: "Section 56 of the CGST Act providing for interest on delayed refunds does not provide for excluding the period during which the Respondents are investigating the grant of refund." (para 14)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: the court did exclude 30 days as the Circular 16/2019 verification period (para 16(ii)-(iii)); full name, date, case number and bench added

#### H. IGST export refunds: rule 96(10) and rule 96(4) withholding

#### Union of India v. Cosmo Films Ltd - Supreme Court - 28 Apr 2023
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 290 of 2023 and batch (CA Nos. 292-298, 303 of 2023) (S. Ravindra Bhat & Dipankar Datta JJ.); reported citation not confirmed
- Holding: Reversing Gujarat HC, the Court upheld the "pre-import condition" for IGST and compensation cess exemption on advance-authorisation imports (Notf. 79/2017-Cus) as not arbitrary. Because the respondents had enjoyed interim orders, the Revenue was directed to let them claim refund or input credit of IGST paid by applying to the jurisdictional Commissioner within six weeks, to be examined case by case.
- Binding: Binding (Art. 141).
- Later history: DGFT trade notices 07/2023-24 and 27/2023 and a later policy circular deal with EODC. Rule 96(10) itself was omitted on 08.10.2024.
- Use in orders: It concerns the customs exemption condition, not the validity of rule 96(10). Do not cite it as upholding rule 96(10).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/162988112/); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan](https://www.lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/pre-import-condition-under-advance-authorisation-scheme-upheld-by-supreme-court); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/supreme-court/sc-upholds-the-requirement-of-pre-import-condition-to-claim-igst-and-gst-compensation-cess-on-imports-made-under-advance-authorization-1474319)
- Key words: "this alone cannot lead the court to conclude that such a change is unreasonable or arbitrary." (para 74); "the Revenue's appeals are allowed, subject to the above terms." (para 76)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case number and bench added; refund/credit relief tied to the interim orders the respondents had enjoyed

#### Addwrap Packaging Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 13 Jun 2025
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 22519 of 2019 and batch (incl. SCA 1550, 1851, 2031 of 2024); reserved 24.12.2024 (Bhargav D. Karia & D.N. Ray JJ.)
- Holding: Omission of rule 96(10) by Notf. 20/2024-CT (08.10.2024), without a saving clause, operates prospectively but applies to all proceedings not finally adjudicated on that date, before the court or the adjudicating authority. Show cause notices and orders-in-original under rule 96(10) were quashed; the petitioners may maintain IGST export refund claims under rule 96. The vires challenge was not decided.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana by itself, but **effectively affirmed by the SC** (see Goodluck India).
- Later history: SC dismissed the Revenue's SLPs on 06.08.2026. Followed by Madras HC (31.08.2026).
- Use in orders: Drop pending rule 96(10) proceedings.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/14098607/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/gujarat-high-court/omission-of-rule-9610-of-cgst-rules-operates-prospectively-but-applies-to-all-pending-proceedings-gujarat-high-court-295205); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan](https://lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/export-refund-prospective-omission-of-rule-96-10-of-cgst-rules-and-retro-active-applicability-to-pending-proceedings)
- Key words: "Rule 96(10) would stand omitted prospectively but applicable to pending proceedings/cases where final adjudication has not taken place." (para 205)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; batch and outcome details added. Later history (SC dismissal, Madras HC) not re-checked here except as noted under Goodluck India

#### Goodluck India Ltd v. Union of India (batch) - Supreme Court - 06 Aug 2026
- Citation / case no.: SLP (C) No. 24550 of 2025 with SLP (C) No. 26411 of 2026 and connected civil appeals; 2026 INSC 821 (J.B. Pardiwala & K. Vinod Chandran JJ.); marked Non-Reportable. Other reported citations not checked.
- Holding: The Revenue's appeals against the High Court ruling that the omission of rule 96(10) applies to every proceeding pending on the date of omission were dismissed. Following the Constitution Bench in *Kolhapur Canesugar Works* ((2000) 2 SCC 536), proceedings under an omitted rule cannot continue without a saving clause; the GST Council's "prospective" recommendation is advisory and does not bind the rule-making authority. The assessees' vires challenges were dismissed as infructuous. The Registry was directed to send the order to all High Courts. (The order text does not name the High Court judgment under appeal; secondary sources say it is *Addwrap*.)
- Binding: Binding on all (Art. 141), as a reasoned SC judgment, although marked Non-Reportable.
- Later history: CBIC reportedly accepted it by an Office Memorandum dated 24.08.2026.
- Use in orders: No rule 96(10)-based denial or recovery in any proceeding pending on 08.10.2024.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/92578463/); [taxo.online 11.08.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/11-08-2026-supreme-court-settles-rule-9610-controversy-omission-applies-to-all-pending-refund-proceedings-in-absence-of-saving-clause/); [a2ztaxcorp - CBIC accepts](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/cbic-accepts-supreme-court-ruling-omission-of-rule-9610-applies-to-all-pending-gst-refund-proceedings/)
- Key words: "The recommendation that the omission should be prospective is also advisory in nature and does not bind the rule making authority." (para 7)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; 2026 INSC 821 and bench confirmed; noted Non-Reportable and that the impugned HC judgment is not named in the order. CBIC OM of 24.08.2026 not checked

#### Sance Laboratories Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Kerala HC - 10 Oct 2024
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 17447 of 2023 and connected cases; 2024:KER:79782; 2024 LiveLaw (Ker) 685 (not checked) (Gopinath P. J., single judge)
- Holding: Rule 96(10), as inserted by Notf. 53/2018-CT w.e.f. 23.10.2017, is ultra vires s.16 IGST and manifestly arbitrary. Because the 08.10.2024 omission was prospective, the court declared its validity for the earlier period: show cause notices and orders against the petitioners based on rule 96(10) were quashed, and no recovery may be made under it for 23.10.2017-08.10.2024 (relief limited to the petitioners).
- Binding: Persuasive. The vires question was later treated as infructuous by the SC in Goodluck India.
- Later history: Kerala HC applied it on 30.07.2026 (WP(C) 4778/2026) to quash a s.74 order based solely on rule 96(10). No appeal found.
- Use in orders: Rely on Goodluck India instead.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/32097793/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/kerala-high-court/kerala-high-court-rule-9610-cgst-rules-struck-down-ultravires-273928); [Lawyer e-News](https://lawyerenews.com/legal_detail/kerala-high-court-declares-rule-9610-of-cgst-rules-ultra-vires-exporters-entitled-to-igst-refunds)
- Key words: "Rule 96(10) of the CGST Rules as presently worded is ultra vires the provisions of Section 16 of the IGST Act, it is 'manifestly arbitrary'" (para 14)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; resolved the doubt: the declaration covers 23.10.2017 to 08.10.2024 for the petitioners. Later Kerala HC order of 30.07.2026 not checked

#### Choksi Exports v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 03 Feb 2023
