- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 23798 of 2022 (Sonia Gokani & Sandeep N. Bhatt JJ.)
- Holding: IGST export refund (about Rs 14.80 crore) was withheld because the exporter was flagged risky, its supplier's supplier being an L2 risky supplier. The exporter had reversed the ITC (Rs 11,55,726) with interest and penalty, positive verification reports had been sent, and it was not prosecuted under the Act; the law does not require it to verify its supplier's supplier. Following Telangana HC in *Bhagyanagar Copper*, at least the 90% provisional refund under s.54(6) and rule 91 should have been granted. Petition partly allowed; refund as claimed under s.54(6) r/w rule 91 to be credited within three weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found. It relies on Telangana HC, *Bhagyanagar Copper Pvt Ltd* (para 21 quoted), which is binding in Telangana but not separately verified here.
- Use in orders: A risk flag is not a rule 96(4) ground. Withholding must rest on s.54(10)/(11) or a Customs Act violation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/157107975/); [KPMG Flash News](https://www.in.kpmg.com/taxflashnews/KPMG-Flash-News-Choksi-Exports.pdf); [TaxTMI](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=11108)
- Key words: "the petitioner is not prosecuted for any offence under the Act or under the existing law and has also reversed the ITC, therefore, there is no point for the respondents herein to withheld the refund." (para 9)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed with detail (relief framed under s.54(6)/rule 91; relies on Telangana HC Bhagyanagar Copper); bench added

#### Agriex - Gujarat HC - 02 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: The grounds for withholding an IGST refund under rule 96(4) are exhaustive. The refund must be processed without first deciding a separate shipping-bill amendment under s.149 Customs Act.
- Binding: Persuasive at most. Do not cite until the 02.07.2026 order is seen (see Verified).
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Same as Choksi Exports.
- Source: [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=136112)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026, 3 searches). CAUTION: the only Agriex order found, *Agriex v. Union of India*, Gujarat HC (A.S. Supehia & Vaibhavi D. Nanavati JJ.), SCA No. 5623 of 2024, 27.07.2026 (https://indiankanoon.org/doc/30077943/), is a short consent disposal: refund of IGST on exports (08.05.2017-17.07.2023) largely paid, balance (about 10%) to be paid within six weeks if due, interest left to an application. It contains no holding on rule 96(4) or s.149 Customs Act

#### I. Export of services and "intermediary" (s.13(8)(b) IGST)

#### Dharmendra M. Jani v. Union of India - Bombay HC - split verdict 09/16 Jun 2021; third judge's opinion 18 Apr 2023 (final Division Bench order not found)
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 2031 of 2018 with WP (L) No. 639 of 2020 (A.T.E. Enterprises); split decision Ujjal Bhuyan J. / Abhay Ahuja J. (2021-VIL-458-BOM; 2021-VIL-472-BOM, not checked); referral order 16.06.2021; opinion of G.S. Kulkarni J. as referee judge, 18.04.2023, 2023:BHC-OS:3045-DB
- Holding: Bhuyan J. held s.13(8)(b) unconstitutional and Ahuja J. upheld it. The referee judge (G.S. Kulkarni J.) held ss.13(8)(b) and 8(2) IGST "legal, valid and constitutional" **provided** they are confined to the IGST Act and are not applied to levy CGST/SGST (MGST) on such services. The final Division Bench order disposing of the petitions after the reference was not found on Indian Kanoon; secondary sources (BDO) describe it differently, so cite the referee opinion with its proviso.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: **s.13(8)(b) omitted by s.157 Finance Act 2026, reportedly effective 30.03.2026** (date of assent); intermediary services now fall under s.13(2). The effect on pending disputes is unsettled. Supreme Court appeal status not found.
- Use in orders: For periods up to 29.03.2026, s.13(8)(b) remains valid law, but the "intermediary" finding must be reasoned (see below). For later periods, intermediary services to foreign recipients can qualify as exports.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - third judge's opinion 18.04.2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/114383398/); [Indian Kanoon - 2021 opinion](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/119605824/); [Grant Thornton - Finance Act 2026 reset](https://www.grantthornton.in/en/insights/articles/gst-on-intermediary-services/); [BDO alert](https://www.bdo.in/en-gb/insights/alerts-updates/indirect-tax-alert-bombay-high-court-(division-bench)-rules-on-the-constitutional-validity-of-sect); [Khaitan](https://khaitanco.com/thought-leaderships/Third-judge-of-Bombay-High-Court-pronounces-verdict-on-powers-of-State-to-levy-GST-on-Intermediary-Services-to-foreign-customers)
- Key words: "The provisions of Section 13(8)(b) and Section 8(2) of the IGST Act are legal, valid and constitutional, provided that ... [they] are confined in their operation to the provisions of IGST Act only" (para 113)
- Verified: Primary (partly) - referee judge's opinion read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; resolves the conflict in sources: the third judge DID confine the provisions to the IGST Act. Final DB order and SC appeal status not found (3 searches)

#### Ernst and Young Ltd v. Additional Commissioner, CGST Appeals-II, Delhi - Delhi HC (Division Bench) - 23 Mar 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) 8600/2022; 2023:DHC:2116-DB (Vibhu Bakhru & Amit Mahajan JJ.)
- Holding: Professional services rendered by EY India on its own account to overseas EY entities are not intermediary services; the place of supply is the recipient's location under s.13(2), so they are export of services under s.2(6) IGST. Refund rejections of ITC (Dec 2017-Mar 2020) and the appellate order were set aside and the refund directed to be processed expeditiously.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Followed by Delhi HC in *Fateh Education*. No appeal found.
- Use in orders: An intermediary finding needs three parties and two supplies. Analyse the contract.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/45142193/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/services-provided-by-ey-india-to-overseas-ey-entities-not-intermediary-services-delhi-high-court-directs-igst-refund-to-ey-india-225547); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/03-06-2023-a-person-who-supplies-the-goods-and-services-on-his-own-account-cannot-be-considered-as-intermediary-delhi-high-court/)
- Key words: "the Services rendered by the petitioner are not as an intermediary and therefore, the place of supply ... is required to be determined on basis of the location of the recipient of the Services." (para 33)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed

#### Virtusa Systems (India) Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 05 Jan 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP Nos. 28201, 5621 and 5622 of 2024 (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ & G.M. Mohiuddin J.)
- Holding: CORRECTED (narrower): the appellate orders (12.12.2023) and the refund rejection (09.07.2024) treating services to eTouch Systems Corp., USA as intermediary services recorded no finding on a third party, on whether supplies were principal-to-principal or on own account, or on any arranging or facilitating of another supply; the rejection simply copied earlier appellate findings. The orders were cryptic and non-speaking and were set aside and remanded (to the appellate authority in two petitions and the refund authority in one) for decision within about 12 weeks, expressly without comment on the merits.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: An intermediary finding must record, from the contract, the third party, the two supplies, and how the supplier arranges or facilitates the other supply; do not copy findings from earlier periods.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/82468280/); [TaxGuru (premium)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1026835); [Telangana HC cause list PDF](https://tshc.gov.in/documents/currentsection_2026_02_28T11_21_59.pdf)
- Key words: "the findings of the authorities recorded in the impugned orders are cryptic, not adhering to the requirements under Section 13(2) defining "intermediary", Section 2(6) defining "export of services" ..." (para 20)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (05.01.2026), case numbers and bench confirmed; CORRECTED: it is a remand for non-speaking orders with no view on merits, not a ruling that the services were not intermediary

#### Commscope India Pvt Ltd v. Additional Commissioner (Appeals-I) - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 15 Oct 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 3516 of 2024 (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ & G.M. Mohiuddin J.). Identified on Indian Kanoon as the probable ruling behind the Taxheal report; party name previously not found.
- Holding: On the department's appeal under s.107(2), the appellate authority (29.09.2023) set aside a refund sanction (RFD-06, 07.10.2022, Gachibowli Division; ITC Rs 1,43,97,841 on zero-rated services) saying the original authority had not examined s.13(3)-(13) IGST, without itself analysing the agreement and documents. As the appellate authority has no power of remand under s.107(11), it had to reach its own finding on whether the services were intermediary or export. Order set aside for non-application of mind; remanded to the appellate authority for a fresh order.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Discuss the contract terms and the place-of-supply provision in every intermediary finding.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/165211765/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=110928)
- Key words: "It is trite that the appellate authority does not have the power of remand under Section 107(11) of CGST Act." (para 6)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name, date (15.10.2025), case number and bench identified; holding confirmed. Match to the Taxheal report inferred from the facts (same court, issue and period)

#### J. Rule 89(4)/(4C): turnover of zero-rated supply

#### Tonbo Imaging India Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Karnataka HC - 16 Feb 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 13185 of 2020 (T-RES) (S.R. Krishna Kumar J., single judge)
- Holding: The words "or the value which is 1.5 times the value of like goods domestically supplied by the same or, similarly placed supplier" in the rule 89(4) definition of "turnover of zero-rated supply of goods" (clause (C), inserted by Notf. 16/2020-CT, 23.03.2020) were declared ultra vires the CGST and IGST Acts and violative of Arts. 14 and 19, and quashed. The rejection order (30.06.2020) was quashed and refund with interest directed within three months. Validity of the Explanation to rule 93 left open.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Appeal status not found.
- Use in orders: Be cautious about cutting refunds using the 1.5 times cap. A Telangana taxpayer will cite this.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/69393718/); [EY alert](https://www.ey.com/en_in/alerts-hub/2023/04/karnataka-hc-quashes-amendment-to-rule-89-which-restricts-refund-of-unutilized-itc-under-gst); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/amendment-to-rule-89-4c-of-the-cgst-rules-held-ultra-vires-invalid-and-consequently-quashed-karnataka-high-court/)
- Key words: "the impugned Rule 89(4)(C) is arbitrary and unreasonable, in as much as the possibility of taking undue benefit by inflating the value of the zero-rated supply of goods, cannot be a ground to amend the Rule" (para 26)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case number and single-judge bench added

#### K. Supplies to SEZ

#### Lupin Ltd v. State of Maharashtra - Bombay HC (Nagpur Bench, Division Bench) - 21 Nov 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 4164 of 2024 (Anil L. Pansare & Raj D. Wakode JJ.); reserved 14.11.2025
- Holding: CORRECTED: the SEZ unit itself (a recipient) claimed refund of ITC (ISD credit); State Tax authorities rejected it because only the supplier to an SEZ may claim under rule 89(1), and declined to follow Gujarat HC in *Britannia Industries* because an SLP was pending. The SC had since let Britannia stand (SLP not pressed for low tax effect) and dismissed the Revenue's SLP in *Meghmani Organochem* (SLP (C) 1239/2025, 22.09.2025) keeping the question of law open. Britannia therefore holds the field and binds authorities. Orders quashed; remanded to the Assistant Commissioner (State Tax) to decide afresh in the light of Britannia and after examining whether the services were for authorised operations as endorsed by the Specified Officer (a point first raised by the State in court). The judgment says nothing about 01.10.2023.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: An SEZ unit's own refund claim for accumulated ITC cannot be rejected merely because it is a recipient (follow Britannia unless the SC rules otherwise). Raise any endorsement objection in the RFD-08 notice, not for the first time in court. (The 'authorised operations' requirement applying only from 01.10.2023 comes from Lenovo, Madras HC, para 14.7, not from this case.)
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/139356318/); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=110979); [Masters India](https://www.mastersindia.co/blog/gst-refund-rights-key-court-judgments/)
- Key words: "the status as of now is that the law laid down by the Gujarat High Court in Britannia's case holds the field." (para 8)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; MATERIAL CORRECTION: the case is about an SEZ unit claiming refund of ITC (Britannia), remanded with a direction to also examine the authorised-operations endorsement; the 01.10.2023 reading in secondary sources is not in the judgment; bench added

(See also *Lenovo*, Madras HC, at C above: a delayed SEZ endorsement attributable to the officer does not defeat the claim. The Gujarat HC's *Britannia Industries* ruling (2020 (42) GSTL 3 (Guj)), that an SEZ unit may itself claim refund of accumulated ITC, stands: per Bombay HC in *Lupin* (21.11.2025), the SC let Britannia stand (SLP not pressed for low tax effect) and dismissed the Revenue's SLP in *Meghmani Organochem* (SLP (C) 1239/2025, 22.09.2025) keeping the question of law open. Britannia itself not read.)

#### L. Unjust enrichment, cash ledger, deemed exports

#### Shapoorji Pallonji and Co Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench) - 18 Dec 2024
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 17717 of 2021 (R. Raghunandan Rao & Maheswara Rao Kuncheam JJ.)
- Holding: CORRECTED (overstated before): refund of left-over TDS credit in the electronic cash ledger was rejected (17.10.2019) and the appeal dismissed (26.12.2020) on unjust enrichment and other grounds. The court did not itself rule on unjust enrichment. It set aside both orders and remanded for reconsideration in the light of Circular 166/22/2021-GST (17.11.2021), which allows refund of TDS/TCS balance in the cash ledger under the proviso to s.54(1) read with s.49(6), within three months.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: For refund of excess cash-ledger balance (including TDS/TCS credit), follow Circular 166/22/2021-GST; do not rest a rejection on unjust enrichment without examining that circular.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/182536345/); [TaxGuru - unjust enrichment analysis](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/unjust-enrichment-comprehensive-analysis-judicial-precedents.html)
- Key words: "remanding the matter back to the respective respondents for reconsideration in the light of Circular No.166/22/2021-GST." (para 9)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; MATERIAL CORRECTION: a remand in the light of Circular 166/22/2021, not a holding that unjust enrichment does not apply; date, case number and bench added

#### Bombay HC on s.54(8)(d) and export of services - Bombay HC - date and party name not found
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: The unjust-enrichment bar in s.54(8)(d) applies only where the incidence of tax has been passed on to another person. For export of services to a foreign recipient that concern does not arise. Refund to be processed within 4 weeks.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Zero-rated refunds fall under s.54(8)(a), so no unjust-enrichment test applies.
- Source: [TaxGuru - unjust enrichment analysis](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/unjust-enrichment-comprehensive-analysis-judicial-precedents.html)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026, 3 searches)

#### Shah Paperplast Industries Ltd v. Union of India (batch) - Gujarat HC (Division Bench) - 13 Nov 2025
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 18892 of 2023 with SCA Nos. 17246/2022, 17080/2023, 96/2025 and 8319/2025; reserved 10.10.2025 (Bhargav D. Karia & Pranav Trivedi JJ.)
- Holding: 100% EOUs that actually exported goods without payment of tax and claimed refund of accumulated ITC under rule 89(1)/89(4) were not deemed exporters: their suppliers had not claimed deemed-export refunds under Notf. 48/2017. Para 2.2 of Circular 172/04/2022 and rule 89(4A) therefore did not apply, and refunds could not be withdrawn, recovered or rejected on that basis. Withdrawal, recovery and rejection orders were quashed and refunds directed within 12 weeks. The vires/retrospectivity of the circular and the s.107(2) review / s.73-74 recovery questions were left open.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Apply the deemed-export restrictions only where deemed-export benefits were actually availed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140412757/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/?p=543798)
- Key words: "As the petitioners are exporters of the goods and has never claimed the input tax credit under Notification No.48/2017 as deemed exporter, para no.2.2 of clarificatory Circular No.172/04/2022-GST dated 06.07.2022 would not be applicable" (para 57)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date corrected (13.11.2025, not Dec 2025); holding confirmed; batch, bench and open questions added

#### M. Amounts paid during search or investigation, and pre-deposits

#### Vallabh Textiles v. Senior Intelligence Officer - Delhi HC (Division Bench) - 20 Dec 2022
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) 9834/2022; 2022/DHC/005682 (Rajiv Shakdher & Tara Vitasta Ganju JJ.); reserved 14.09.2022
- Holding: Rs 1,80,10,000 deposited on the petitioner's behalf during a search (17.02.2022), without a proper officer's ascertainment notice or DRC-04 acknowledgement and contrary to Gujarat HC's *Bhumi Associate* directions (no recovery during search; DRC-03 only after the search ends), lacked voluntariness. Refund directed with 6% simple interest from 17.02.2022 within ten days; CBIC directed to align Instruction 01/2022-23 (25.05.2022) with Bhumi Associate.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Payments during search are at risk of refund unless the voluntariness (DRC-03 and DRC-04 trail) is clearly recorded.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/188747488/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/tax-deposited-during-search-not-voluntary-delhi-high-court-directs-gst-dept-return-rs180-crores-217331); [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/delhi-hc-directed-refund-of-amount-deposited-coercively-during-search-operations-at-midnight)
- Key words: "Failure to follow the prescribed procedure will, as in this case, have us conclude that the deposit of tax, interest and penalty was not voluntary." (para 40.1)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; date (20.12.2022), case number, bench and the 6% interest from 17.02.2022 added

#### Union of India v. Bundl Technologies Pvt Ltd - Karnataka HC (Division Bench) - 03 Mar 2022 (affirming single judge, 2021)
- Citation / case no.: W.A. No. 1274 of 2021 (T-RES) in WP No. 4467 of 2021 (Alok Aradhe & M.G.S. Kamal JJ.). Single judge's date not checked.
- Holding: Rs 15 crore (deposited at about 4 a.m. on 30.11.2019 during DGGI investigation) and Rs 12,51,44,157 (27.12.2019) were not self-ascertained deposits under s.74(5), the only provision permitting deposit during investigation; collection breached Arts. 265 and 300A. Refund cannot be made subject to the outcome of the pending investigation. The claim (filed within two years) and writ were not delayed. Revenue's writ appeal dismissed, affirming the single judge's refund direction (findings modified).
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found
- Use in orders: Same as Vallabh Textiles.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - DB judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/124175504/); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan](https://cn.lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/right-of-refund-of-amount-paid-during-investigation)
- Key words: "the contention of the Department that amount under deposit be made subject to the outcome of the pending investigation can not be accepted. The Department, therefore, is liable to refund the amount" (para 31)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (Division Bench judgment); summary confirmed; DB affirmation (03.03.2022) added as the citable ruling

(Odisha HC: s.54 read with s.107 covers refund of pre-deposit, and rejection because an SFIO probe is pending is unsustainable. Refund with interest. Party name not found: [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/refund-with-interest-allowed-as-no-sfio-probe-was-pending-hc/). A further Karnataka HC ruling (party not found) held that no interest is payable on a refund of amounts adjusted from the electronic credit ledger during a search unless a refund claim had been made earlier: [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/no-interest-is-payable-on-refund-of-amount-deposited-during-search-by-way-of-adjustment-of-credit-amount-in-ecl-hc).)

### 3. Statutory developments to check alongside the case law

- **Notf. 14/2022-CT (05.07.2022):** amended the rule 89(5) formula. Treated as retrospective by Gujarat HC (*Ascent Meditech*).
- **Circular 173/05/2022 (06.07.2022):** substituted para 3.2 of Circular 135/2020. Treated as retrospective by Telangana HC (*Micro Systems*).
- **Notf. 13/2022-CT (05.07.2022):** excludes 01.03.2020-28.02.2022 from limitation for s.54/55 refunds.
- **Circular 162/18/2021:** relevant date for s.77 wrong-head refunds.
- **Notf. 20/2024-CT (08.10.2024):** omitted rule 96(10). Applies to pending proceedings (*Goodluck India*, SC, 06.08.2026). CBIC OM dated 24.08.2026 reportedly accepts this.
- **Finance Act 2026 (assent 30.03.2026, per secondary sources):** (i) s.13(8)(b) IGST omitted; (ii) s.54(6) 90% provisional refund extended to IDS claims (date to be notified); (iii) the ₹1,000 floor in s.54(14) no longer applies to export refunds with payment of tax; (iv) a reported rewrite of s.54(3) adding input services and capital goods, **enactment unconfirmed**.

### 4. Gaps and doubts

1. **Search budget exhausted.** The session's web-search limit was reached before these could be searched: refund of tax on supplies later cancelled (advances, flat-buyer cases), refund of excess cash-ledger balance generally, s.54(10) withholding cases, unjust enrichment in IDS claims, Bansal International (Delhi HC), and Microsoft Global Services (Telangana HC). These need a follow-up search.
2. **"Tiger Steel" (Gujarat HC, zero-rated turnover formula under rule 89(4)): not found** in two searches, one of them extended. Do not cite it until the name, court and citation are confirmed. The closest verified authority on the zero-rated turnover cap is *Tonbo Imaging* (Karnataka HC, 16.02.2023).
3. **Finance Act 2026 s.54(3) rewrite (input services in IDS refunds):** only one TaxGuru article, describing the Bill. Enactment not confirmed. Check the gazetted Act before relying on VKC Footsteps for periods after 30.03.2026.
4. **Circular 135/2020 "quashed or read down":** no ruling formally striking down the circular was found. Courts (Telangana, Karnataka, Madras, Calcutta) have refused to apply it or treated Circular 173 as retrospective. A Madras HC ruling (July 2026) rejecting the "principal input" theory was found without a party name.
5. **Limitation split:** Madras (*Lenovo*) and AP (*Nspira*) say directory; Karnataka DB (*Merck*, 2026) says mandatory. **No Telangana HC refund-limitation ruling was found.** SLP status of Merck not checked.
6. **Shree Renuka Sugars:** case number and date (SCA 22339/2022, 13.07.2023) come from one TaxGuru summary. A StudyCafe item mentions different figures (refund ceiling of Rs 100.47 crore), so the facts may be mixed up across two orders. Check the order.
7. **Dates not found or uncertain:** VKC Footsteps (Gujarat HC); Ascent Meditech (citation conflicts; SLP dismissal 28.03.2025 from a single source); Synchrony (30.07.2026 vs 12.08.2026; "Synchron" vs "Synchrony"); Virtusa (date and case number unconfirmed); ADV Industrial Services (exact date); Cosmo Films SC citation.
8. **Dharmendra M Jani:** sources disagree on the third judge's scope. SC appeal status not found. The effect of the Finance Act 2026 omission on pending intermediary disputes is unsettled.
9. **Party names missing:** Patna HC (s.77 relevant date), Bombay HC (s.54(8)(d) and exports), Telangana HC (non-speaking intermediary order), Karnataka HC (RFD-03 inherits original date), Odisha HC (pre-deposit, SFIO). They are kept as entries or notes but should not be cited by name until confirmed.
10. **Interest start date where the application was deficient:** *Baba Super Minerals* (Rajasthan) runs it from completion; *Altisource*, *Kuehne Nagel* and *Qualcom* run it from the original application where the claim was later allowed on appeal. These views do not fully match.
11. **Lupin (Bombay HC, 21.11.2025) on SEZ "authorised operations" endorsement:** two readings in the sources.
12. ~~All entries are secondary.~~ Superseded 14 Oct 2026: items 6 (Shree Renuka facts consistent), 7 (most dates now confirmed), 8 (referee judge did confine s.13(8)(b) to IGST), 9 (Patna = *Sai Steel*; Telangana intermediary = *Commscope*), 10 and 11 (*Lupin* is about SEZ-unit refunds, not the 01.10.2023 point) are resolved in the entries. See the Verification log.

## File 02: GST Case Law - Input Tax Credit (ITC)

**Scope.** Leading Supreme Court and High Court rulings on input tax credit under the CGST Act, 2017, for use by a CGST Assistant Commissioner (Sangareddy Division, Telangana) when drafting show cause notices and orders. Covers: s.16(2)(c) supplier non-payment and the "bona fide purchaser" defence; s.16(2)(aa) and GSTR-2A/2B mismatches; s.16(4) time limit and the 2024 relief in s.16(5)/(6); s.17(5)(d) after Safari Retreats and the Finance Act 2025 amendment; burden of proof on the dealer claiming ITC (Ecom Gill); other blocked credits (CSR, motor vehicles); rule 86A blocking of the electronic credit ledger; retrospective cancellation of a supplier's registration; fake invoices and circular trading; transitional credit (TRAN-1, s.140); and rule 42/43 reversals. Telangana High Court rulings found are flagged.

**Date compiled:** 13 Oct 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries; judgment text not yet read. Verify before citing in an order.** Court and case-law sites could not be opened from the compiling environment, so every entry rests on secondary summaries (law-firm alerts, tax portals). The web-search budget ran out before a few last checks could be made; these are listed under "Gaps and doubts".
>
> **Update 14 Oct 2026:** 40 of the 49 entries have now been checked against the judgment or order text on Indian Kanoon and are marked "Verified: Primary"; the rest remain secondary. See the Verification log at the end for corrections.

---

### Quick answers

**1. Supplier did not pay tax - can the recipient's ITC be denied (s.16(2)(c))?**
The position changed in 2026. The Supreme Court in *Bhandari Scrap Traders v. Union of India* (24 Jul 2026) dismissed the SLP against the Gujarat HC's *Maruti Enterprise* ruling (1 May 2026). It upheld s.16(2)(c) and refused to read it down for bona fide buyers. It also held that Delhi VAT precedents (*Arise India*, *On Quest*, *Shanti Kiran*) cannot be carried over to GST. Earlier pro-recipient rulings (*Suncraft Energy*, Calcutta HC, SLP dismissed Dec 2023; *D.Y. Beathel*, Madras HC 2021) were mainly about procedure: proceed against the supplier first, and take the recipient's ITC only as a last resort. A Tripura HC read-down (*Sahil Enterprises*, Jan 2026) is under SLP (the SC's order in *Bhandari* records that the SLP has been entertained).
*Takeaway:* s.16(2)(c) is valid and binding. Commentators still read *Bhandari* as not allowing mechanical denial. In the order: (a) record a verified finding that the supplier actually failed to deposit the tax, not just an arithmetic 2A/3B gap; (b) record what action was taken or attempted against the supplier; (c) consider the recipient's evidence; (d) note that the recipient can re-avail the credit once the supplier pays. Do not rely on a bare mismatch.

**2. GSTR-2A/2B mismatch (s.16(2)(aa)).**
s.16(2)(aa) has applied only from 1 Jan 2022. For earlier periods, High Courts (Kerala *Diya Agencies* 2023; Karnataka *Biocon*) have set aside denials that rested only on non-reflection in GSTR-2A. They remand so the recipient can prove the transaction was genuine. Circular 183/15/2022-GST (FY 2017-18 and 2018-19) lays down a supplier-certificate / CA-certificate procedure, and courts set aside orders that ignore it.
*Takeaway:* for pre-2022 periods, never deny ITC on 2A non-reflection alone. Call for the Circular 183 documents and examine them in the order. For periods from 2022, s.16(2)(aa) is a statutory condition, but still give a hearing and deal with the documents produced.

**3. Time limit (s.16(4)) and 2024 relief (s.16(5)/(6)).**
The vires of s.16(4) was upheld by the AP HC (*Thirumalakonda Plywoods*, 18 Jul 2023) and the Patna HC (*Gobinda Construction*, 8 Sep 2023; SC issued notice Jan 2024, outcome not found). Paying a late fee does not cure the time-bar. s.16(5) (FY 2017-18 to 2020-21, returns filed by 30 Nov 2021) and s.16(6) (revocation after cancellation) were inserted retrospectively from 1 Jul 2017. Courts set aside s.16(4) denials covered by s.16(5), and Circular 237/31/2024 provided a rectification route.
*Takeaway:* before confirming a s.16(4) demand, check s.16(5) and s.16(6) expressly and record why they do not apply. Any pending s.16(4) demand for FY 2017-21 where GSTR-3B was filed by 30.11.2021 is unsustainable.

**4. Immovable property / "plant or machinery" (s.17(5)(d)).**
*Safari Retreats* (SC, 3 Oct 2024) laid down a functionality test, under which a building can be "plant". The review was dismissed on 20 May 2025. Section 119 of the Finance Act 2025 then retrospectively (from 1 Jul 2017) substituted "plant and machinery" and overrides contrary decisions.
*Takeaway:* apply s.17(5)(d) as amended and cite the Finance Act 2025 amendment and its overriding clause, not the functionality test. Watch for constitutional challenges to the retrospective amendment (none found in results).

**5. Burden of proof on the claimant.**
*State of Karnataka v. Ecom Gill Coffee Trading* (SC, 13 Mar 2023, KVAT s.70) held that the dealer claiming ITC must prove that the transaction and the physical movement of goods are genuine. Invoices and bank payments alone are not enough. CGST s.155 has the same burden rule. HCs (Kerala *Diya Agencies*) cite *Ecom Gill* together with relief on 2A mismatch.
*Takeaway:* in fake-invoice or doubtful-supplier cases, ask for e-way bills, transport and freight proof, delivery records and stock entries. Record what was produced and why it fails. This is the strongest safeguard for an order.

**6. Rule 86A blocking.**
Blocking is limited to the balance actually in the ledger. "Negative blocking" is invalid. This is the view of Gujarat (*Samay Alloys*, 2022), Delhi (*Best Crop Science*, 24 Sep 2024; SLPs reportedly dismissed 2025) and **Telangana (*Laxmi Fine Chem*, 2024 - binding)**. Blocking also needs recorded independent "reasons to believe" and natural justice (**Telangana *Bhavani Oxides*, 26 Jun 2024**). Rule 86A is not a recovery tool; recovery is under s.73/74.
*Takeaway:* block only what is in the ECL on the date of the order. Record your own reasons based on material, not borrowed satisfaction. Give the taxpayer a hearing (the Telangana HC in *Bhavani Oxides* reads natural justice into rule 86A; Gujarat requires at least a post-decisional hearing within two weeks), and note that rule 86A(3) makes the restriction lapse after one year.

**7. Retrospective cancellation of the supplier's registration.**
HCs (Calcutta *LGW Industries* 2021, *Gargo Traders* 2023; Madras *Fathima Traders*; Delhi *Aryan Timber* on cancellation itself) hold that a later retrospective cancellation does not by itself defeat the recipient's ITC. The officer must examine whether the supplier was registered on the transaction date and whether the transaction was genuine (payment through bank, movement of goods, collusion). No Telangana merits ruling was found.
*Takeaway:* never deny ITC only because the supplier's GSTIN was later cancelled from an earlier date. Examine the genuineness of the transaction on evidence.

**8. Fake invoices / circular trading.**
HCs (Calcutta, MP, Delhi, Chhattisgarh; Telangana in *Inam Impex* on a related point) usually decline writ jurisdiction where fraud or fake invoices are alleged and send parties to appeal under s.107. They do insist that relied-upon documents be supplied (Madras *S.S. Metals*).
*Takeaway:* give the noticee every relied-upon document (supplier statements, investigation reports). Make specific findings per supplier. Use s.74 only with a clear fraud finding.

**9. Transitional credit (TRAN-1 / s.140).**
*Union of India v. Filco Trade Centre* (SC, 22 Jul 2022) directed the portal to be opened for TRAN-1/TRAN-2 for two months (later extended), for all assessees, followed by 90-day verification. It left High Court questions of law on s.140 open. Earlier, the SC dismissed the SLP in *Adfert* (28 Feb 2020) and stayed *Brand Equity* (19 Jun 2020). The Telangana HC followed *Filco* (*Parle Agro*, 2022).
*Takeaway:* for claims outside the Filco window, rely on the retrospective s.140 amendment (Finance Act 2020) and Filco's closure. In verification, decide eligibility on merits with a speaking order.

**10. Rule 42/43 reversal.**
The Calcutta HC (*Hemraj Rice Mill*, 16 Jun 2025) set aside a reversal confirmed without applying the rule 42/43 formula and remanded it, holding that the appellate authority should have called for the reconciliation statements.
*Takeaway:* show the formula computation in the order and call for the reconciliation statement. Do not apply rule 42 where there is no exempt turnover (non-GST / no-supply entries in GSTR-9 Table 5 are not automatically "exempt").

---

### Issue 1 - s.16(2)(c): supplier non-payment and the "bona fide purchaser"

#### Bhandari Scrap Traders v. Union of India - Supreme Court - 24 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: SLP(C) No. 23931 of 2026 with SLP(C) Nos. 24088 and 24103 of 2026; order dated 24.07.2026 (Record of Proceedings, reasoned dismissal). Bench: Sanjay Kumar and Sanjeev Sachdeva JJ. Against Gujarat HC judgment dated 01.05.2026 in SCA No. 749 of 2025. Reported as 2026 LiveLaw (SC) 725 (secondary; not checked).
- Holding: dismissed the SLPs and affirmed the Gujarat HC, expressing "complete and respectful agreement" with it. The HC was right not to declare s.16(2)(c) unconstitutional or read it down. Relying on the HC's analysis (para 42 onwards and para 56), there is "no possibility of drawing parity" between the Delhi VAT Act and the CGST Act so as to treat a CGST purchaser as a bona fide purchaser under DVAT. Noted the HC's reliance on s.41 (re-availment once the supplier is made to pay) and ss.73/74. The order notes that an SLP against the Tripura HC in *Sahil Enterprises* has been entertained, but says the Tripura HC did not undertake the Gujarat HC's exercise. It does not mention *Suncraft* or a "pursue the supplier first" rule.
- Key words: "the High Court was fully justified in holding that no grounds were made out to declare Section 16(2)(c) of the CGST Act as unconstitutional or read down the provisions thereof." (order, p.2)
- Binding: **Yes - Supreme Court.** It is a short reasoned order affirming the HC (not a bare dismissal), so the affirmed HC reasoning carries SC approval. It is not a full judgment; cite it with the Gujarat HC judgment.
- Later history: SLP in *Sahil Enterprises* (Tripura) pending per this order. Commentary says it does not authorise automatic or mechanical denial.
- Use in orders: cite as the controlling authority that the supplier's actual payment of tax is a condition for ITC. Pair it with a factual finding of non-payment and an opportunity to the recipient.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 24.07.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/161729461/); [LiveLaw - SC upholds validity of s.16(2)(c)](https://www.livelaw.in/supreme-court/gst-itc-available-to-buyer-only-if-supplier-paid-tax-supreme-court-upholds-validity-of-s-162c-of-cgst-act-543149); [KS&K newsletter](https://ksandk.com/newsletter/section-162c-cgst-act/); [HNA - s.16(2)(c) and Bhandari Scrap Traders](https://hnallp.com/a/section-162c-and-supreme-court-decision-of-bhandari-scrap-traders)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added companion SLPs, HC case number (SCA 749/2025) and the form of order (short reasoned order); holding confirmed.

#### Maruti Enterprise v. Union of India - Gujarat High Court - 1 May 2026
- Citation / case no.: [2026] 186 taxmann.com 90 (Guj). The SC order in *Bhandari Scrap Traders* confirms a Gujarat HC judgment dated 01.05.2026 in SCA No. 749 of 2025 upholding s.16(2)(c) (paras 42 and 56 discuss DVAT and the ITC scheme). Division Bench (A.S. Supehia and Pranav Trivedi JJ, per one source). One source names the lead matter *Jigneshbhai Bharatbhai Tarpara v. Union of India*. The title is uncertain.
- Holding: upheld s.16(2)(c). ITC is a statutory benefit, not a vested right, and may be made conditional. Actual payment of tax by the supplier is an independent, cumulative condition that is unaffected by bona fides. Refused to read the provision down.
- Binding: affirmed by the SC (above), so the reasoning now carries SC approval.
- Later history: SLP dismissed / affirmed in *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (24 Jul 2026).
- Use in orders: cite with *Bhandari* for the reasoning that ITC is a conditional statutory concession. Confirm the exact title and paragraph numbers from the Gujarat HC text before quoting.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order in Bhandari (confirms date and SCA number)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/161729461/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1072601); [taxo.online - SC affirmed validity](https://taxo.online/latest-news/29-07-2026-bona-fide-buyer-defence-rejected-supreme-court-affirmed-the-constitutional-validity-of-section-162c-of-the-cgst-act/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3 sources agree; case title differs) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; date and SCA No. 749/2025 confirmed from the SC order in *Bhandari*).

#### Sahil Enterprises v. Union of India - Tripura High Court - 6 Jan 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 688 of 2022, judgment 06.01.2026; (2026) 154 GSTR 108 (Tri.) (as cited in the SC order in *Bhandari*). Division Bench: M.S. Ramachandra Rao CJ and S. Datta Purkayastha J.
- Holding: s.16(2)(c) is not violative of Arts. 14, 19(1)(g), 265 or 300A, but is read down: it is not to be applied to deny ITC to a purchaser in a bona fide transaction, only where the transaction is not bona fide, collusive or fraudulent. The Department had invoked only s.73, not s.74, and did not dispute that the petitioner paid Rs 1.12 crore GST to the supplier; the denial order was set aside and ITC directed to be allowed. Relied on the DVAT rulings (*On Quest*, *Arise India*).
- Key words: "it should be read down and applied only where the transaction is found to be not bona fide or is a collusive transaction or fraudulent transaction to defraud the revenue." (para 57(b))
- Binding: Tripura only; persuasive elsewhere. **Its reasoning conflicts with *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (SC, later in time), which rejected the read-down approach.**
- Later history: the SC order in *Bhandari* (24.07.2026) records that an SLP against this judgment has been entertained. Outcome not known.
- Use in orders: if the noticee cites it, answer with *Bhandari Scrap Traders*.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/128910236/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/08-01-2026-a-bona-fide-purchaser-who-has-paid-gst-to-the-supplier-cannot-be-penalised-for-the-suppliers-failure-to-remit-tax-tripura/); [LiveLaw (via KLA library)](https://livelaw.library.keralalawacademy.in/high-court/tripura-high-court/cgst-s-162c-not-unconstitutional-but-itc-cannot-be-denied-to-bona-fide-purchaser-for-supplier-default-tripura-high-court-517128)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added case number, bench and citation; date 6 Jan 2026 confirmed; SLP "entertained" confirmed from the SC order in *Bhandari*.

#### Assistant Commissioner of State Tax v. Suncraft Energy Pvt Ltd - Calcutta HC (2 Aug 2023) / Supreme Court SLP dismissed 14 Dec 2023
- Citation / case no.: Calcutta HC (Division Bench, T.S. Sivagnanam CJ and Hiranmay Bhattacharyya J): MAT 1218 of 2023 with CAN 1 of 2023, judgment 02.08.2023, in appeal from the single-judge order in WPA 12153 of 2023 (21.06.2023). SC: SLP(C) Nos. 27827-27828/2023, order 14.12.2023 (B.V. Nagarathna and Ujjal Bhuyan JJ).
- Holding: (WBGST, FY 2017-18, demand Rs 6.5 lakh) the HC set aside an ITC reversal based on a GSTR-2A/3B mismatch where the buyer had produced invoices and bank proof of payment and the officer had made no enquiry or recovery against the supplier. Relying on the CBIC press release of 04.05.2018, it held the officer must first act against the supplier; reversal from the buyer is only for exceptional cases (collusion, missing supplier, closed business, no assets).
- Key words: "before directing the appellant to reverse the input tax credit and remit the same to the government, the first respondent ought to have taken action against the fourth respondent the selling dealer" (HC, para 9)
- Binding: Calcutta HC. The SC dismissed the SLP "having regard to the facts and circumstances of this case(s) and the extent of demand being on the lower side", so the dismissal does not affirm the law. Its weight on the merits is now limited by *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (2026), but its procedural point (pursue the supplier first) is still cited.
- Later history: SLP dismissed 14.12.2023 on the small-amount ground. Effectively qualified by *Bhandari* (2026) on the substantive question.
- Use in orders: show in the order what steps were taken against the defaulting supplier before reversing from the recipient.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Calcutta HC 02.08.2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/110803637/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 14.12.2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/129861655/); [Taxmann - SLP dismissed](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/slp-dismissed-against-hc-order-where-demand-of-itc-reversal-was-set-aside-holding-that-investigation-against-supplier-is-a-pre-requisite/); [VJM Global](https://www.vjmglobal.com/blog/itc-cant-denied-recipient-recovery-effort-supreme-court-dismissed-departments-slp)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added HC case number, date (2 Aug 2023) and benches; confirmed the SC dismissal expressly rests on the small amount involved.

#### Arise India Ltd v. Commissioner of Trade & Taxes - Delhi High Court - 26 Oct 2017 (SC SLP dismissed 10 Jan 2018)
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 2106 of 2015, order dated 26.10.2017 (S. Muralidhar and Chander Shekhar JJ), disposed of by the common judgment of the same date in *On Quest Merchandising India Pvt Ltd v. GNCTD*, W.P.(C) 6093/2017 and batch. SLP dismissed 10.01.2018 (as recorded by the Calcutta HC in *Suncraft*; SLP number not found).
- Holding (Delhi VAT): the expression "dealer or class of dealers" in s.9(2)(g) DVAT Act was read down to exclude a purchasing dealer who bona fide bought from a validly registered seller on proper tax invoices with no 2A/2B mismatch; otherwise the provision would violate Art. 14. The remedy is against the defaulting seller; s.40A can be used where collusion is shown. Default assessments set aside.
- Key words: "the remedy for the Department would be to proceed against the defaulting selling dealer to recover such tax and not deny the purchasing dealer the ITC." (On Quest common judgment, para 54)
- Binding: VAT law. **The SC in *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (2026) held that DVAT precedents cannot be equated with the CGST Act**, so it is of limited use under GST.
- Later history: SLP dismissed 2018; the SC in *Shanti Kiran* (9 Oct 2025) noted the On Quest SLP was disposed of without interference. Distinguished for GST by *Bhandari* (2026).
- Use in orders: if the noticee relies on it, distinguish it as a VAT decision, citing *Bhandari*.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order and common judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/50881698/); [taxo.online - judgment note](https://taxo.online/judgment/arise-india-ltd-v-ctt-wpc-2106-of-2015-dated-26-10-2017-the-delhi-high-court/); [IndiaLaw - SC upholds ITC rights of bona fide dealers](https://www.indialaw.in/blog/tax/supreme-court-upholds-itc-rights-of-bona-fide-dealers/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; corrected: the reasoning is in the common *On Quest* judgment and rests on Art. 14 only (not Art. 19(1)(g)); added bench.

#### Commissioner, Trade & Tax, Delhi v. Shanti Kiran India (P) Ltd - Supreme Court - 9 Oct 2025
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal Nos. 2042-2047 of 2015 with Civil Appeal No. 9902 of 2017; order dated 09.10.2025 (Manoj Misra and N. Kotiswar Singh JJ); 2025 TAXSCAN (SC) 317 (secondary). Against the Delhi HC judgment of 04.01.2013.
- Holding (DVAT): dismissed the department's appeals. The sellers were registered on the transaction date and neither the transactions nor the invoices had been doubted on any inquiry, so there was no reason to interfere with the HC's direction to grant ITC after due verification. Noted that the On Quest SLP (No. 36750/2017) was disposed of without interfering.
- Key words: "there is no dispute regarding the selling dealer being registered on the date of transaction and neither the transactions nor invoices in questions have been doubted, based on any inquiry into their veracity" (para 7)
- Binding: SC, but on the DVAT Act, and a short fact-based order. Distinguished for GST by *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (2026).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: distinguish as VAT-specific. Note that it turns on the absence of any inquiry into genuineness; where the officer has enquired and recorded findings, it does not help the noticee.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 09.10.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/37020422/); [ICAI CA Journal](https://cajournal.icai.org/article-details/judicial-affirmation-of-bona-fide-credit-entitlement); [Taxscan weekly round-up](https://www.taxscan.in/other-taxations/supreme-court-high-courts-weekly-round-up-1435316)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date 9 Oct 2025 and connected CA 9902/2017 added; holding narrowed to its fact-based ground.

#### D.Y. Beathel Enterprises v. State Tax Officer (Data Cell), Madurai - Madras High Court - 24 Feb 2021
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) No. 2127 of 2021 and batch (17 petitions), Madurai Bench, G.R. Swaminathan J; (2021) 127 taxmann.com 80 (Mad).
- Holding: (TNGST) quashed orders reversing ITC from buyers where the sellers had collected tax and not paid it, for two reasons: the sellers were not examined or confronted in the enquiry (especially as the department alleged no movement of goods), and no recovery action had been taken against the sellers. Remitted for a fresh enquiry with the sellers examined as witnesses and, in parallel, recovery action against them. The court also said the VAT-era rule that ITC cannot be reversed for the seller's default "may not be straight-away applicable" under GST.
- Key words: "if the tax had not reached the kitty of the Government, then the liability may have to be eventually borne by one party, either the seller or the buyer." (para 12)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive elsewhere. Contrary Madras view in *Pinstar Automotive* (primary liability on supplier, but reversal by the recipient allowed as a protective measure). Substantively limited by *Bhandari* (2026).
- Later history: appeal status not found. Cited in *LGW Industries* (Calcutta) and *S.S. Metals* (Madras).
- Use in orders: include the supplier in the enquiry (examine and offer cross-examination where the noticee asks), start recovery against the supplier, and record the outcome.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/64616243/); [VJM Global](https://www.vjmglobal.com/blog/supplier-defaults-payment-taxes-then-primary-liability-lies-supplier-itc-not-reversible-by-recipient); [BDO alert](https://www.bdo.in/en-gb/insights/alerts-updates/indirect-tax-alert-gst-shall-be-recovered-from-the-suppliers-who-failed-to-remit-the-tax-after-col)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; corrected: remand was for a fresh enquiry examining the sellers, with recovery against them in parallel (not "first"); added bench and batch.

#### McLeod Russel India Ltd v. Union of India - Gauhati High Court - 9 Dec 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 5725 of 2022; 2025:GAU-AS:16974-DB; oral judgment of Ashutosh Kumar CJ and Arun Dev Choudhury J.
- Holding: the challenge was to **s.16(2)(aa)** of the CGST and Assam GST Acts (not s.16(2)(c)). The court declined to strike it down but read it down "for the present": where the supplier is at fault, a bona fide purchaser must be given an opportunity to prove bona fides, verifiable from tax invoices and other documents, before ITC is denied. The read-down is to last only until CBIC devises a practical solution.
- Key words: "before denying the ITC benefits to a bona fide purchaser, he ought to be given an opportunity to prove his bona fides, which can be verified by the tax invoices and other documents." (para 20)
- Binding: Assam/NE states; persuasive. Belongs with Issue 2 (s.16(2)(aa)). Read-down reasoning is in tension with *Bhandari* (2026); the "opportunity to prove bona fides" point remains sensible practice.
- Later history: not found. (Indian Kanoon also lists unrelated McLeod Russel v. Union of India orders of 18 Sep 2026 on an industrial-incentive matter; do not confuse.)
- Use in orders: in a s.16(2)(aa) case, give the recipient a specific opportunity to show bona fides with invoices, payment and receipt documents, and deal with them.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/24531388/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/before-denying-itc-to-a-bona-fide-purchaser-on-account-of-the-suppliers-default-the-department-must-provide-an-opportunity-to-the-purchaser-to-establish-his-bona-fides/); [KPMG flash - McLeod Russel](https://www.in.kpmg.com/taxflashnews/KPMG-Flash-News-Mcleod-Russel.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected: the provision read down is s.16(2)(aa), not s.16(2)(c)**; added date, case number and bench.

#### Inam Impex Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Telangana High Court - 31 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 9234 of 2026, order 31.03.2026; Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J. TGST (State Tax, Secunderabad Division), assessment year 2021-22; OIO dated 30.12.2025.
- Holding: declined to entertain the vires challenge to s.16(2)(c) and relegated the taxpayer to statutory appeal (s.107 with pre-deposit). The State's own counsel said the SCN and order contained no allegation or finding that the supplier failed to deposit tax; the proceedings were for ITC in excess of what suppliers declared. Whether tax was paid against the invoices and goods received are factual questions for the authorities.
- Key words: "It is not evident from the order-in-original that the supplier has failed to deposit the taxes paid by the petitioner in lieu of the supplies made against invoices raised by it." (para 5)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (on entertaining writs; no ruling on vires).
- Later history: none found. Consistent with *Bhandari* (SC) on validity.
- Use in orders: support for the appeal remedy. Its facts also warn that the SCN/order must contain a verified finding of the supplier's non-payment if s.16(2)(c) is invoked, not just a figure gap.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/79849375/); [taxheal - challenge dismissed, statutory remedy](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=130408); [thetaxcorp - Telangana HC on s.16(2)(c)](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/itc-reversal-for-supplier-default-under-section-162c-telangana-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added case number and bench; summary confirmed.

#### Rythu Seva Kendram, Bairanpally v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Telangana High Court - 30 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 4967 of 2026, order 30.03.2026; Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J. Respondent: Superintendent of Central Tax, Siddipet GST Range. OIO No. 92/2024-25-Adjn (Supdt) dated 13.02.2025; appeal rejected in APL-02 dated 13.11.2025.
- Holding: the appellate authority rejected the appeal without any hearing because it was time-barred (as CBIC counsel admitted). The rejection was set aside and the appeal remitted for a fresh order after hearing. The vires of ss.16(2)(c) and 16(2)(aa) can be raised after the appeal remedy is exhausted. If the pre-deposit has been paid, the OIO stays pending the appeal.
- Key words: "The matter is remitted to the appellate authority to pass a fresh order in accordance with law after granting opportunity of hearing to the petitioner." (para 7)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (on the hearing point). A CGST matter from a neighbouring range.
- Later history: none found. The order cites a similar remand in W.P. No. 34650 of 2025.
- Use in orders: always grant a personal hearing (s.75(4); s.107 appeals) before deciding ITC disputes, even where the appeal appears time-barred.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/88004754/); [thetaxcorp - personal hearing in GST appeals](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-mandates-personal-hearing-in-gst-appeals-defers-section); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/?p=544426)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 30 Mar 2026 confirmed; added case number, bench, and that the appeal had been rejected as time-barred without hearing.

#### Megha Engineering & Infrastructures and batch - Telangana High Court - 2025 (pending)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 6880, 6883, 6897, 6899, 6903 and 6933 of 2025.
- Holding: no final decision found. Interim order dated 18.03.2025 in W.P. No. 6880 of 2025 and analogous petitions, in a challenge to s.16(2)(c) together with an OIO dated 27.11.2024 (as described by a coordinate Bench in *S.S. Infra v. Union of India*, W.P. No. 31795 of 2025, 23.10.2025).
- Binding: n/a (pending). Likely affected by *Bhandari* (2026).
- Later history: final outcome not found. In *S.S. Infra* (23.10.2025) the Telangana HC refused to entertain a similar s.16(2)(c)/41(2) challenge to a 2023 OIO because of a two-year delay.
- Use in orders: check status before deciding similar Telangana matters.
- Source: [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/itc-reversal-for-supplier-default-under-section-162c-telangana-high-court); [Indian Kanoon - S.S. Infra (mentions interim order)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/76261317/)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; interim order of 18.03.2025 confirmed from *S.S. Infra*, read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026).

### Issue 2 - s.16(2)(aa) and GSTR-2A/2B mismatch

#### Diya Agencies v. State Tax Officer - Kerala High Court - 12 Sep 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 29769 of 2023; 2023 (9) TMI 955; 2023 KHC 9238 (as listed in a later Kerala HC exhibit list). Dinesh Kumar Singh J.
- Holding: for FY 2017-18, ITC cannot be denied only because invoices are not reflected in GSTR-2A (relying on the CBIC press release of 18.10.2018). If the supplier has not remitted the tax, the recipient is not to be blamed, but the recipient must prove the transaction is genuine (relying on *Ecom Gill*). The matter was remanded for evidence, with ITC to be allowed if the claim is found bona fide.
- Binding: Kerala; persuasive. Followed in *Taurus Communication* (Kerala HC 2023). Its supplier-default observation is limited by *Bhandari* (2026); the 2A-only point for pre-2022 periods remains sound.
- Later history: appeal not found.
- Use in orders: for pre-1.1.2022 periods, call for and evaluate evidence; do not rest on 2A alone.
- Source: [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/gstr-2a-by-recipient-is-of-nature-of-taxpayer-facilitation-and-does-not-impact-ability-of-taxpayer-to-avail-itc-on-self-assessment-basis-1496033); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/itc-cant-be-denied-due-to-discrepancies-in-gstr-2a-without-proof-of-collusion-between-assessee-and-seller-hc/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (4 sources agree) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Biocon Ltd v. State of Karnataka - Karnataka High Court - 30 Apr 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 11918 of 2024 (T-RES), judgment 30.04.2026; NC: 2026:KHC:24626; S.R. Krishna Kumar J. KGST/CGST order of DCCT (Audit-4.7) dated 22.02.2024.
- Holding: set aside the demand of Rs 20.01 crore (with penalty and interest) for "excess" ITC in GSTR-3B over GSTR-2A for 2018-19. GSTR-2A then did not capture imports or SEZ supplies; the difference arose from reporting import ITC in the wrong GSTR-3B row, rectified in GSTR-9; the Bill of Entry is the document for import ITC; and before s.16(2)(aa) (1.1.2022) there was no requirement to match with 2A. Other issues in the order were remanded.
- Key words: "Bill of Entry is the relevant document for availing ITC of Tax paid on import of goods and there is no requirement of matching with GSTR-2A as Section 16(2)(aa) is introduced w.e.f., 01.01.2022." (para 9)
- Binding: Karnataka; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: do not apply 2A/2B matching to pre-2022 periods or to imports (verify from Bills of Entry / ICEGATE).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/178180582/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1050667); [taxtmi forum](https://www.taxtmi.com/forum/issue?id=120881)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected date (30 Apr 2026, not 2024) and party name (State of Karnataka)**; holding confirmed.

#### Pattern rulings on Circular 183/15/2022-GST (Kerala *GMA Pinnacle Automotives* 2024; Madras *AP Studio Enterprises* 2024; Madras *Contemporary Leather* 2024)
- Citation / case no.: *GMA Pinnacle Automotives Pvt Ltd v. State Tax Officer*, Kerala HC, WP(C) No. 8727 of 2024, 06.03.2024 (Gopinath P. J); *AP Studio Enterprises v. Assistant Commissioner (ST)(FAC)*, **Madras HC** (not AP HC), W.P. No. 9701 of 2024, 12.04.2024 (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J); *Contemporary Leather Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner (ST)*, Madras HC, W.P. No. 15024 of 2024, 18.06.2024 (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J).
- Holding: (all FY 2017-18) *GMA Pinnacle*: denial of Rs 5.1 lakh ITC on 2A/3B mismatch set aside because the officer never asked the taxpayer to explain the mismatch or produce certificates under Circular 183; taxpayer to seek rectification with documents. *AP Studio*: order set aside and remanded because the supplier's certificate and the supplier's CA certificate on record were not considered. *Contemporary Leather*: the reply cited Circular 183 but enclosed no documents; the court gave one more opportunity on condition of a 15% deposit (not a finding that certificates were ignored).
- Key words: "These documents were not taken into account while issuing the impugned order. For such reason, the order impugned herein calls for interference." (*AP Studio*, para 4)
- Binding: respective States; persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: for FY 2017-18 and 2018-19, call for the Circular 183 certificates, and discuss any certificates produced in the order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - GMA Pinnacle](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/158353830/); [Indian Kanoon - AP Studio Enterprises](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/32577298/); [Indian Kanoon - Contemporary Leather](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/178887815/); [thetaxtalk](https://thetaxtalk.com/2024/06/itc-mismatch-demand-set-aside-by-high-court-because-authorities-failed-to-consider-certificate-obtained-by-buyer-from-the-supplier-and-ca-of-supplier/)
- Verified: Primary - all three read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected: *AP Studio Enterprises* is a Madras HC case, not AP HC**; added case numbers and dates; *Contemporary Leather* re-described (no documents produced; remand on 15% deposit).

#### Vishist Business Solutions Pvt Ltd v. Appellate Joint Commissioner (ST) - Telangana High Court - 22 Jan 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1736 of 2026, order 22.01.2026; Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J. TGST, FY 2018-19; OIO 18.04.2024, appeal rejected 19.11.2025.
- Holding: the demand (ITC on invoices missing from GSTR-2A) was upheld in appeal because no Circular 183 supplier/CA certificate was produced. The taxpayer later obtained a CA certificate (05.12.2025) showing the supplier reported the invoices and paid the tax in its September 2019 returns. On the State's suggestion, the court allowed the taxpayer to seek rectification of the OIO under s.161 with the documents, without comment on the merits.
- Key words: "the proper officer would consider such application in accordance with law after due verification of the records." (para 6)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA**, but a consent-based disposal with no ruling on the law.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: where Circular 183 certificates surface after the order, a s.161 rectification application may be entertained and decided after verifying the supplier's returns.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/2156267/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1039588)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; year (2026), case number and bench added; holding clarified (rectification under s.161 by consent, no ruling on merits).

---

### Issue 3 - Returns, GSTR-2A and the s.16(4) time limit; s.16(5)/(6)

#### Union of India v. Bharti Airtel Ltd - Supreme Court - 28 Oct 2021
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal arising out of SLP(C) No. 8654 of 2020; (2022) 4 SCC 328. Bench: A.M. Khanwilkar, Dinesh Maheshwari and C.T. Ravikumar JJ. Against Delhi HC, W.P.(C) 6345/2018, 05.05.2020.
- Holding: reversed the Delhi HC, which had read down para 4 of Circular 26/26/2017-GST. A taxpayer cannot unilaterally rectify filed GSTR-3B returns for Jul-Sep 2017 (about Rs 923 crore paid in cash). Rectification under s.39(9) is only in the period when the error is noticed. Non-operation of GSTR-2A did not excuse the taxpayer, because the return is self-assessed from the taxpayer's own books. ITC is not denied, only postponed; it stays in the ledger for later use.
- Key words: "Form GSTR-2A is only a facilitator for taking an informed decision while doing such self-assessment." (para 46)
- Binding: **SC - binding on all.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: ITC is self-assessed from the taxpayer's own records. Portal or 2A unavailability is no ground for reopening returns outside the statutory scheme.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/60500851/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/gst-rectification-of-errors-permissible-only-at-initial-stages-supreme-court-dismisses-bharti-airtels-plea-for-refund-of-rs923-crore-184507); [BDO alert](https://www.bdo.in/en-gb/insights/alerts-updates/indirect-tax-alert-hon-ble-apex-court-denied-rectification-of-gstr-3b-and-consequent-refund-of-inr)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date (28 Oct 2021), SLP number, bench and SCC citation added; summary confirmed.

#### Thirumalakonda Plywoods v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Andhra Pradesh High Court - 18 Jul 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 24235 of 2022; 2023 SCC OnLine AP 1476 (as cited by the Kerala HC in *M. Trade Links v. Union of India*, 04.06.2024).
- Holding: s.16(4) of the CGST/APGST Act does not violate Arts. 14, 19(1)(g) or 300A. s.16(2) does not override s.16(4); they operate independently. Accepting a late GSTR-3B with late fee does not cure a claim beyond the s.16(4) limit (March 2020 return filed 27.11.2020; COVID plea rejected).
- Binding: AP; **strongly persuasive in Telangana** (sister State with the same statute).
- Later history: appeal not found. Facts may now be covered by s.16(5) (FY 2019-20 return filed by 30.11.2021) - check. Followed by the Kerala HC in *M. Trade Links* (04.06.2024), which also upheld s.16(4).
- Use in orders: cite for vires of s.16(4), but check s.16(5) applicability first.
- Source: [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/mere-acceptance-of-form-gstr-3b-returns-with-late-fee-will-not-exonerate-delay-in-claiming-itc-beyond-period-specified-1488477); [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/high-court-upholds-time-limit-for-claiming-input-tax-credit-under-cgst-act)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources agree) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; only 2022 interim orders listed; SCC OnLine citation taken from *M. Trade Links*, read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026).

#### Gobinda Construction v. Union of India - Patna High Court - 8 Sep 2023
- Citation / case no.: CWJC No. 9108 of 2021 and batch; MANU/BH/1260/2023. Division Bench: Chakradhari Sharan Singh and Madhuresh Prasad JJ.
- Holding: s.16(4) of the CGST/BGST Act is valid and does not violate Arts. 19(1)(g) or 300A. ITC under s.16(1) becomes a vested right only when the statutory conditions, including s.16(4), are met. The writ petitions were dismissed.
- Key words: "sub-section (4) of Section 16 of the CGST/ BGST Act are constitutionally valid and are not violative of Articles 19(1)(g) and Article 300-A of the Constitution of India." (para 37)
- Binding: Bihar; persuasive. The Chhattisgarh HC (*Jain Brothers*, 2023) and Kerala HC (*M. Trade Links*, 2024) took the same view (per later citations).
- Later history: SC issued notice on 3 Jan 2024 in the appeal (secondary). Outcome not found. s.16(5) has since been inserted.
- Use in orders: cite with *Thirumalakonda* (and *Safari Retreats*, where the SC also rejected the challenge to s.16(4)). Note that the SC appeal outcome is unknown.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment (batch copy)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/82450418/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/patna-high-court/patna-high-court-constitutional-validity-section-164-cgst-act-2017-237919); [VJM Global](https://www.vjmglobal.com/blog/constitutional-validity-section-164-cgst-act-2017-upheld)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; summary confirmed.

#### s.16(5) applied retrospectively - Madras HC (2024-2026); Calcutta HC report unconfirmed
- Citation / case no.: Madras HC common order dated 17.10.2024 in W.P. Nos. 25081 of 2023 etc. (batch), followed in *Srinivasa Pharmacy v. Superintendent of GST and Central Excise*, W.P.(MD) No. 758 of 2026, 09.01.2026 (Krishnan Ramasamy J). Calcutta HC ruling (Raja Basu Chowdhury J per taxheal): name not identified.
- Holding: s.16(5), inserted by the Finance (No.2) Act 2024 with effect from 1.7.2017, overrides s.16(4) for FY 2017-18 to 2020-21 where the GSTR-3B was filed by 30.11.2021. Orders reversing ITC only on s.16(4) limitation, where the claim falls within s.16(5), were quashed and the department restrained from proceeding on limitation.
- Key words: "The orders impugned in all Writ Petitions are quashed insofar as it relates to the claim made by the petitioners for ITC which is barred by limitation in terms of Section 16 (4) of the CGST Act, 2017 but, within the period prescribed in terms of Section 16 (5)" (17.10.2024 batch order, para 11(i), as quoted in *Srinivasa Pharmacy*)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive. The statute itself binds.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: drop s.16(4) objections for FY 2017-21 if GSTR-3B was filed by 30.11.2021. Record this check in every s.16(4) order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Srinivasa Pharmacy (quotes the 17.10.2024 batch order)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/37714454/); [taxheal - retrospective relaxation under s.16(5)](https://www.taxheal.com/raja-basu-chowdhury-j-9.html); [taxo.online 12.01.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/12-01-2026-section-165-cgst-act-has-retrospective-overriding-effect-over-section-164-for-fys-2017-18-to-2020-21-itc-cannot-be-reversed-merely-on-limitation-grounds-if-gstr-3b-returns-were-filed/)
- Verified: Primary (Madras part) - *Srinivasa Pharmacy* read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026, which quotes the 17.10.2024 batch order; Madras case names added. Calcutta ruling not identified (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Venus Infra Projects v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Madras High Court - 9 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21371 of 2026, order 09.07.2026; 2026:MHC:2797; Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J. TNGST/CGST, FY 2020-21; registration cancelled 28.03.2022; DRC-07 dated 10.02.2025.
- Holding: ss.16(4)-(6) are read together. s.16(6) helps only where ITC was not already barred by s.16(4) on the date of the cancellation order; here it was, so revocation did not revive it. Writ dismissed.
- Key words: "the petitioner was not entitled to input tax credit, as per Section 16(4), on the date of cancellation of the GST registration. In view thereof, the petitioner is not eligible to make an ITC claim in terms of sub-section (6) of Section 16." (para 6)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: in post-revocation cases, record whether the s.16(4) window was open on the date of cancellation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/177735418/); [taxtmi article](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=16993); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1061092)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added date, case number, judge and facts; summary confirmed.

#### North Agro Industries Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Punjab & Haryana High Court - 10 Dec 2024
- Citation / case no.: CWP-15400-2024 (O&M), decided 10.12.2024; 2024:PHHC:164471-DB; Sanjeev Prakash Sharma and Sanjay Vashisth JJ.
- Holding: disposed of the writ by allowing the taxpayer to apply for rectification under CBIC Circular 237/31/2024-GST (15.10.2024) and the s.148 special procedure (Notification 22/2024-CT, 08.10.2024) for orders under ss.73/74/107/108 that denied ITC now saved by s.16(5)/(6). The court recorded that applications could be made up to 15.04.2025, and expected them to be decided in light of s.16(5) and (6).
- Key words: "It is expected that if any such application for rectification is moved, the same shall be decided keeping in view the provisions of sub-Sections (5) and (6) of Section 16 of the CGST Act, 2017." (para 3)
- Binding: P&H; persuasive.
- Later history: not found. Extension of the 15.04.2025 deadline not confirmed.
- Use in orders: entertain rectification under Circular 237 where it was filed within time.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/76304636/); [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=100084); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1044658)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added date, case number and bench; summary confirmed.

### Issue 4 - s.17(5)(d): Safari Retreats and the Finance Act 2025

#### Chief Commissioner of CGST v. Safari Retreats Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court - 3 Oct 2024
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 2948 of 2023 (with C.A. No. 2949 of 2023 and W.P.(C) Nos. 804, 1030 and 1036 of 2022, 90, 846 and 847 of 2023); 2024 INSC 756; (2025) 2 SCC 523. Bench: Abhay S. Oka and Sanjay Karol JJ.
- Holding: the challenge to the validity of s.17(5)(c) and (d) **and s.16(4)** failed. "Plant or machinery" in s.17(5)(d) is not the same as the defined "plant and machinery". Whether a mall, warehouse or other building is "plant" is a factual question decided by a functionality test. The appeals were remanded to the Orissa HC to apply the test to the mall.
- Key words: "Functionality test will have to be applied to decide whether a building is a plant." (para 65(c))
- Binding: SC. **Overtaken by s.119 Finance Act 2025**, which retrospectively (from 1.7.2017) substituted "plant and machinery", with a clause overriding contrary decisions. Its upholding of s.16(4) remains good law.
- Later history: review petition dismissed 20 May 2025 (secondary). No constitutional challenge to the 2025 amendment found in results.
- Use in orders: do not apply the functionality test. Apply s.17(5)(d) as amended by the Finance Act 2025 and cite the amendment. Cite para 65(a) for the validity of s.16(4).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/170545247/); [Bar & Bench - legislative overruling](https://www.barandbench.com/view-point/scope-for-legislative-overruling-of-the-safari-retreats-judgment); [taxguru - retrospective amendment nullifies](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/retrospective-gst-amendment-nullifies-sc-itc-relief-construction-costs.html); [StudyCafe - review dismissed](https://studycafe.in/sc-dismisses-review-petition-in-the-safari-retreats-case-reaffirming-itc-can-be-claimed-for-leasing-378207.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added civil appeal number, INSC/SCC citations and bench; added that s.16(4) was also upheld.

### Issue 5 - Burden of proof on the dealer claiming ITC

#### State of Karnataka v. Ecom Gill Coffee Trading Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court - 13 Mar 2023
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 230 of 2023 (arising from SLP(C) No. 2572/2022) with C.A. No. 231 of 2023 and connected appeals; (2023) 18 SCC 809 (per one digest). Bench: M.R. Shah and C.T. Ravikumar JJ.
- Holding (KVAT s.70): the dealer claiming ITC bears the burden of proving the genuineness of the transaction and the actual physical movement of goods, by seller details, vehicle, freight, delivery acknowledgement, invoices and payment particulars. Invoices and cheque payments alone are insufficient, and the burden cannot shift to the revenue. The HC and Tribunal were reversed and the assessment restored. The Court's words are that the dealer must "prove beyond doubt the actual transaction".
- Key words: "Mere production of the invoices or the payment made by cheques is not enough and cannot be said to be discharging the burden of proof cast under section 70 of the KVAT Act, 2003." (para 9.1)
- Binding: **SC - binding.** Decided under the KVAT Act, but routinely applied to GST (CGST s.155 has a similar burden clause). Cited by Kerala HC in *Diya Agencies*.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: list the documents called for and analyse why those produced fail to prove movement of goods.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/122433734/); [SCC Online blog](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2023/03/16/kvat-act-claims-of-input-tax-credit-transaction-must-be-proven-beyond-reasonable-doubt-says-supreme-court-legal-news-legal-research-updates/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/kvat-act-dealer-claiming-input-tax-credit-must-prove-transaction-beyond-reasonable-doubt-supreme-court-223792)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; resolved the commentary point (the text says "prove beyond doubt", not "beyond reasonable doubt"); summary confirmed.

### Issue 6 - Other blocked credits (s.17(5))

#### CSR expenditure - s.17(5)(fa) - statutory position and AAR split
- Citation / case no.: s.17(5)(fa) inserted by the Finance Act 2023, in force from 1.10.2023 (Notification 28/2023-CT, 31.07.2023). Pre-amendment AARs: UP AAR *Dwarikesh Sugar* (allowed); UP AAR *Shriram Pistons* (disallowed); a Telangana AAR followed *Dwarikesh* (name not found).
- Holding: from 1.10.2023, ITC on goods and services used for CSR obligations is blocked. For earlier periods, the position is unsettled. No High Court ruling was found.
- Binding: AARs bind only the applicant. The Telangana AAR is not binding on other taxpayers.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: for periods before 1.10.2023, avoid relying on s.17(5)(fa). Test under s.16(1) ("in the course or furtherance of business") with reasons.
- Source: [taxguru - prospective amendment CSR](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/prospective-amendment-itc-csr-expenditure-gst.html); [IRCCL](https://www.irccl.in/post/disallowance-of-input-tax-credit-on-csr-expenditure-an-unnecessary-dampener-for-companies)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2+ sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Demo vehicles - s.17(5)(a) - AAR/AAAR split
- Citation / case no.: Maharashtra AAR *Chowgule Industries*, GST-ARA-18/2019-20/B-121 dated 26.12.2019 (allowed); Goa AAR *Chowgule* (allowed); MP AAR *Khatwani Sales* and Haryana AAR/AAAR *Platinum Motocorp* (disallowed).
- Holding: conflicting rulings on whether demo cars fall in the "further supply" exception to s.17(5)(a)(A). The allowing rulings require that depreciation is not claimed on the tax component. No High Court ruling found.
- Binding: AAR/AAAR bind only the applicant.
- Later history: a CBIC clarification is reportedly not issued (one commentary). Not confirmed.
- Use in orders: no settled law. Decide on facts (capitalisation, use, resale) with reasons.
- Source: [Taxmann - AAAR demo cars](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/gst-itc-not-available-on-demo-cars-being-not-purchased-with-intention-of-further-supply-aaar); [HNA - ITC on demo vehicles](https://hnallp.com/a/itc-on-vehicles-used-for-demo-by-automobile-dealers-)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)
