### Issue 7 - Rule 86A blocking of the electronic credit ledger

#### Samay Alloys India Pvt Ltd v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat High Court - 3 Feb 2022
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 18059 of 2021, judgment 03.02.2022; J.B. Pardiwala and Nisha M. Thakore JJ.
- Holding: the condition precedent for rule 86A is the availability, in the ECL, of the credit alleged to be ineligible. If a balance is available, debit of an equivalent amount may be disallowed for reasons recorded in writing. There is no power of "negative blocking" of future credit; the negative block was ordered withdrawn and Rs 20 lakh paid to file returns was ordered refunded.
- Key words: "there is no power of negative block for credit to be availed in future." (para 57)
- Binding: Gujarat; persuasive. Followed by the Telangana HC in *Laxmi Fine Chem*.
- Later history: none found. Applied by the same Bench in *New Nalbandh Traders* (23.02.2022).
- Use in orders: block only the existing balance.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185364208/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/gujarat-high-court-power-under-rule-86a-gst-rules-electronic-credit-ledger-192163); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/negative-blocking-not-allowed-when-there-is-no-credit-balance-in-electronic-credit-ledger-gujarat-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date (3 Feb 2022) and bench added; summary confirmed.

#### Best Crop Science Pvt Ltd v. Principal Commissioner, CGST - Delhi High Court - 24 Sep 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 10980/2024 and batch (8 petitions); Vibhu Bakhru and Sachin Datta JJ.
- Holding: rule 86A permits disallowing debit only up to the ITC available in the ECL on the date of the order; blocking beyond that ("negative blocking") would turn it into a recovery order. Rule 86A is an emergent protective measure, not a recovery provision; recovery of utilised credit lies under ss.73/74 (and s.83 attachment if needed). Orders set aside to the extent of the excess. The court also noted that under rule 86A(3) the order operates for at most one year. Followed in *Kings Security Guard Services* (4 Dec 2024) and *Karuna Rajendra Ringshia* (W.P.(C) 7250/2024, 21 Oct 2024) and by the P&H HC in *Mannat Steels* (CWP 34077/2025, 17 Nov 2025) (secondary).
- Key words: "Rule 86A of the Rules is not a machinery provision for recovery of tax or dues under the CGST Act." (para 80)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: SLPs against the Delhi line reportedly dismissed (16/17 May 2025 and 9 Jul 2025). Secondary sources only.
- Use in orders: as above. Move to s.73/74 for utilised credit.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/98046732/); [taxguru - not a tax recovery tool](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/blocking-credit-rule-86a-tool-tax-recovery-delhi-hc.html); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/negative-itc-blocking-under-rule-86a-not-allowed-hc/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; added case number and bench; summary confirmed (SLP dismissals still secondary).

#### Laxmi Fine Chem v. Assistant Commissioner - Telangana High Court - 18 Mar 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 5256 of 2024, order 18.03.2024; P. Sam Koshy and N. Tukaramji JJ.
- Holding: a "negative credit" of Rs 50.06 lakh created in the ECL by order of 06.02.2024 was contrary to rule 86A. Where no credit is available, the rule does not allow a negative balance; only existing credit can be blocked, and there is no power to block future credit. The department's remedy for wrongly availed credit is ss.73/74. The order was quashed and the block ordered recalled, reserving the department's right to act in accordance with law. Followed *Samay Alloys* and the same Bench's order in W.P. No. 31039 of 2023 (20.11.2023).
- Key words: "there is no power conferred upon the authorities for block of the credit to be availed by the petitioner in future." (para 7)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: leading Telangana authority. Do not create a negative balance.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/113848584/); [taxo.online 14.05.2024](https://taxo.online/latest-news/14-05-2024-blocking-the-itc-by-creating-a-negative-credit-in-the-electronic-credit-ledger-not-permissible-under-law-telangana-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date (18 Mar 2024) and bench added; also cites W.P. 31039/2023; summary confirmed.

#### Bhavani Oxides and others v. State of Telangana - Telangana High Court - 26 Jun 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 10390, 10425, 10459 and 12733 of 2024, judgment 26.06.2024; Sujoy Paul and Namavarapu Rajeshwar Rao JJ.
- Holding: the State Tax Officer blocked the ECLs (14.03.2024) noting only "Registration of supplier has been cancelled", without notice or disclosed reasons; in one case s.73/74 proceedings were already pending. Principles of natural justice are to be read into rule 86A (the Calcutta HC's *Basanta Kumar Shaw* distinguished), and reasons based on material must be recorded and, where required, disclosed (supplier name, date of cancellation). The blocking was set aside with liberty to proceed in accordance with law.
- Key words: "The action of blocking the electronic credit ledger of the petitioners without following the principles of natural justice and without assigning adequate reasons cannot sustain judicial scrutiny." (para 34)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: the rule 86A order must state the material and the officer's own reasons, disclose the supplier details relied on, and give the taxpayer an opportunity of hearing.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/100052456/); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-strikes-down-rule-86a-itc-blocking-orders-for-violating); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1073478)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; holding sharpened (natural justice read into rule 86A); summary otherwise confirmed.

#### Creative Enterprises v. State of Telangana (W.P. No. 19357 of 2025) - Telangana High Court - 3 Feb 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 19357 of 2025, order 03.02.2026; Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J. TGST.
- Holding: writ against the re-blocking of the ECL on 30.05.2025 (the day the earlier block was lifted) dismissed. The re-block followed a s.74 OIO of 01.05.2025 (Rs 4.96 crore in all, including ITC disallowed for non-existent suppliers), and one year under rule 86A had not expired. The petitioner had not disclosed the s.74 proceedings. The court did not decide the petitioner's plea that no ITC was available when the ledger was blocked.
- Key words: "In writ proceedings, a party has to come clean before the Court disclosing all relevant material facts germane to the issue in controversy." (para 7)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (fact-specific).
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: a rule 86A block supported by s.74 findings and within the one-year limit was left undisturbed. It is not authority for blocking where the ECL has no balance (see *Laxmi Fine Chem*).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/117664944/); [Telangana HC cause list / judgment PDF](https://tshc.gov.in/documents/currentsection_2026_02_11T13_31_58.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name (Creative Enterprises), date and bench identified; holding corrected (re-block after s.74 order and non-disclosure, not a "properly reasoned block").

#### New Nalbandh Traders v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat High Court - 23 Feb 2022
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 17202 of 2021, judgment 23.02.2022; J.B. Pardiwala and Nisha M. Thakore JJ.
- Holding: a rule 86A order bereft of reasons, with no "reasons to believe" recorded, was quashed as arbitrary, with liberty to pass a fresh order. A post-decisional hearing is to be read into rule 86A and should be given within a reasonable time, not beyond two weeks of the blocking order. The Bombay HC (case name not found) similarly rejected "borrowed satisfaction" (secondary).
- Key words: "such post decisional hearing may be granted within a reasonable period of time which may not be beyond two weeks from the date of the order blocking the ECL." (para 17)
- Binding: Gujarat; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: independent recorded reasons are required, and a prompt hearing should follow.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/100764488/); [a2ztaxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/blocking-of-electronic-credit-ledger-under-rule-86a-requires-recorded-reasons-and-timely-post-decisional-hearing/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (23 Feb 2022), case number and bench added; summary confirmed.

---

### Issue 8 - Retrospective cancellation of the supplier's registration

#### LGW Industries Ltd v. Union of India - Calcutta High Court - 13 Dec 2021
- Citation / case no.: WPA 23512 of 2019 and connected petitions, order 13.12.2021; Md. Nizamuddin J (single judge).
- Holding: ITC reversal cases arising from the suppliers' retrospective cancellation were remanded for fresh consideration. The authority must examine the documents on genuineness, whether the price with GST was actually paid to the suppliers, whether the purchases were before or after cancellation, and whether the buyer verified the suppliers' identity. If the purchases are genuine, documented and before cancellation, ITC is to be allowed. The order does not expressly require proof of movement of goods. Some petitions in the batch were listed for later hearing.
- Key words: "If it is found upon considering the relevant documents that all the purchases and transactions in question are genuine and supported by valid documents and transactions in question were made before the cancellation of registration of those suppliers" (operative part)
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive.
- Later history: followed in *Gargo Traders* (2023). Appeal not found.
- Use in orders: examine the transaction date, payment trail and the buyer's verification of the supplier, and record findings. Do not rely on the cancellation date alone.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109803748/); [gstgyaan](https://gstgyaan.com/ms-lgw-industries-ltd-ors-v-union-of-india-ors-s); [taxheal - remanded](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=99067)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date and judge added; holding restated from the operative part (remand; movement-of-goods point not in the order).

#### Gargo Traders v. Joint Commissioner, Commercial Taxes (State Tax) - Calcutta High Court - 12 Jun 2023
- Citation / case no.: WPA 1009 of 2022, dated 12.06.2023 (number and date also listed in the exhibits in *Mekkunnel Timbers v. STO*, Kerala HC, 09.07.2026).
- Holding: ITC denial resting only on the supplier's retrospective cancellation was set aside. The authority had not examined validity at the time of the transaction or genuineness. The buyer had checked the portal and paid through bank. *LGW* applied.
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: as for *LGW*.
- Source: [taxo.online - judgment note](https://taxo.online/judgment/m-s-gargo-traders-vs-the-joint-commissioner-commercial-taxes-state-tax-ors-in-wpa-1009-of-2022-high-court-calcutta); [taxo.online 26.06.2023](https://taxo.online/latest-news/26-06-2023-claim-of-itc-not-to-be-denied-if-the-registration-of-the-supplier-was-cancelled-subsequent-to-the-transaction-calcutta-high-court/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3 sources agree) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Fathima Traders v. Deputy Commercial Tax Officer - Madras High Court - 12 Jun 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 22419, 22420 and 22422 of 2023, order 12.06.2026; Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J. TNGST; supplier's registration cancelled on 06.12.2022 with effect from 01.07.2017.
- Holding: ITC was denied solely because the supplier's registration was cancelled retrospectively; most supplies predated the cancellation order. Following *Engineering Tools Corporation* (W.P. No. 3505 of 2024, 15.02.2024), the orders were set aside and remanded to examine whether supply was established by invoices, e-way bills, lorry receipts and the like.
- Key words: "without examining as to whether the petitioner had established supply of goods by submitting invoices, e-way bills, lorry receipts and the like, the petitioner's claim should not have been rejected solely on the ground of the retrospective cancellation of the suppliers registration." (para 5)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: call for and examine transport and delivery evidence; do not deny on the retrospective cancellation alone.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/46073699/); [taxguru - Madras HC](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/retrospective-supplier-gst-registration-cancellation-deny-itc-madras-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case numbers and judge added (the Indian Kanoon match is dated 12 Jun 2026; the taxguru report was not re-checked against it); holding confirmed.

#### Aryan Timber Store v. Sales Tax Officer - Delhi High Court - 18 Jan 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 628 of 2024; Sanjeev Sachdeva and Ravinder Dudeja JJ.
- Holding: retrospective cancellation under s.29(2) is possible only where the statutory circumstances exist and on objective criteria; it cannot be done mechanically. The SCN had not proposed retrospective cancellation. If the consequence is that customers lose ITC, the officer must consider that before choosing a retrospective date. Cancellation was modified to operate from 06.05.2019, the date the taxpayer applied for cancellation; recovery of dues left open.
- Key words: "Registration cannot be cancelled with retrospective effect mechanically. It can be cancelled only if the proper officer deems it fit to do so." (para 7)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive. Relevant to registration officers in Telangana.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: a retrospective cancellation order (on which buyers' ITC denial will later rest) must give reasons for the retrospective date and must have been proposed in the SCN.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/74353069/); [VJM Global](https://www.vjmglobal.com/blog/gst-registration-cant-be-cancelled-with-retrospective-effect-mechanically-gst-officer-must-provide-reason-for-such-action)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; summary confirmed.

---

### Issue 9 - Fake invoices / circular trading

#### Roshan Sharma (Prop. ARS Metals) v. Deputy Commissioner of Revenue - Calcutta High Court - 18 Jul 2025
- Citation / case no.: WPA 27101 of 2024 with CAN 1 of 2025, judgment 18.07.2025; 2025:CHC-AS:1408; Raja Basu Chowdhury J. The 24.07.2024 date is that of the challenged DRC-07 (WBGST s.73(9), Aug 2020-Mar 2023), not of the judgment.
- Holding: writ dismissed and the ITC denial upheld. The supplier (Rachna Trading Co.) had been found non-existent and its registration cancelled from inception; the taxpayer had been allowed to cross-examine the supplier's proprietor and a transporter, as an earlier Division Bench order required. No perversity or jurisdictional error; the writ court would not re-appreciate evidence where the appeal was bypassed. The burden to prove transport (e.g., toll plaza reports) lay on the taxpayer.
- Key words: "ordinarily the obligation to prove a fact remains with the person who alleges the same." (para 14)
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: a finding of supplier non-existence, backed by inspection or investigation, plus cross-examination where sought, supports denial; the taxpayer must prove movement of goods.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment 18.07.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/189333850/); [Indian Kanoon - interim order 06.12.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/50591050/); [taxguru - Calcutta HC dismisses plea](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/itc-claim-fake-invoices-calcutta-hc-dismisses-plea-upholds-tax-demand.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected date (judgment 18 Jul 2025; 24 Jul 2024 is the adjudication order)**; case number and judge added; holding expanded.

#### Rahul Steels v. Union of India (circular trading, five firms) - Madhya Pradesh High Court - 17 Dec 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8015 of 2024 (Indore Bench), order 17.12.2024; 2024:MPHC-IND:36255; Vivek Rusia and Binod Kumar Dwivedi JJ.
- Holding: writ against a common SCN of 13.10.2023 under ss.74 and 122 dismissed. A search revealed five firms engaged in circular trading (2017-22) to inflate turnover, obtain bank loans and avail fake ITC. A joint assessment of all noticees for the whole period on a common SCN was proper; the plea that a separate notice was needed for each year was rejected, and the petitioner could not be singled out.
- Key words: "the joint assessment proceedings are liable to be undertaken under Section 74 of the CGST Act, 2017 by the proper officer. Hence, the petitioners cannot be singled out from this assessment proceedings" (para 10)
- Binding: MP; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: in circular-trading cases a common SCN to all participants covering several years may be issued; writ courts generally do not interfere at the SCN stage.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/103342623/); [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=110585); [Taxmann - circular trading appellate remedy](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-directed-assessee-to-avail-appellate-remedy-against-itc-disallowed-by-dept-due-to-allegation-of-circular-trading/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case name, number, date and bench identified; added the common-SCN-for-several-years point.

#### Toshniwal Electricals Pvt Ltd v. Principal Commissioner of Central Tax, Delhi North (fake-invoice chain) - Delhi High Court - 30 Oct 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 16455/2025, decided 30.10.2025; Prathiba M. Singh and Shail Jain JJ.
- Holding: the demand (Rs 9.81 lakh against this petitioner) arose from an investigation into 79 fake entities passing over Rs 122 crore of ITC to 1155 taxpayers. In cases of fraudulent ITC the court ordinarily declines writ jurisdiction. The petitioner (whose reply raised only non-supply of relied-upon documents) was relegated to appeal under s.107, to be filed by 30.11.2025 with pre-deposit and heard on merits without a limitation objection.
- Key words: "in cases involving fraudulent availment of ITC, ordinarily, the Court would not be inclined to exercise its writ jurisdiction." (para 7)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: not found. The order notes that the SC, in the SLP against *Metal Techs* (SLP(C) 27411/2025), only extended time to appeal (22.09.2025).
- Use in orders: a well-reasoned order in a fraud case will normally be left to the appellate route.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/131794333/); [Taxmann - writ not maintainable](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/writ-not-maintainable-in-fraudulent-itc-case-appeal-must-be-filed-hc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; identified W.P.(C) 16455/2025 as *Toshniwal Electricals*. **Its appeal deadline (30.11.2025) differs from the rawlaw-reported case (Gurugram, 31.01.2026), which is a different, unidentified Delhi HC order and is no longer relied on here.**

#### S.S. Metals v. State Tax Officer - Madras High Court - 24 Jun 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 15385 of 2024, order 24.06.2024; Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J. TNGST, FY 2018-19; supplier Royal Steel Traders alleged non-existent.
- Holding: the order recorded that the supplier's invoices were fake but refused to give the taxpayer the documentary basis, so the taxpayer could not respond. On the other hand, the taxpayer had not produced lorry receipts or weighment slips and so failed to establish movement of goods under s.16(2)(b). Order set aside on a 20% deposit; the department must furnish particulars and relied-upon documents if it proceeds on the fake-invoice basis.
- Key words: "Without the respondent stating the basis for the conclusion that fake invoices were issued and submitting relevant documents, it is not possible for the petitioner to respond to the allegation." (para 7)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: serve the relied-upon documents and record findings based on them; also record what transport evidence the taxpayer failed to produce.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/106571480/); [Taxmann - provide particulars](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-directed-department-to-provide-particulars-and-documents-relied-upon-for-fake-invoice-allegation-against-assessee)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and judge added; summary confirmed.

---

### Issue 10 - Transitional credit (TRAN-1 / s.140)

#### Union of India v. Filco Trade Centre Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court - 22 Jul 2022
- Citation / case no.: SLP(C) Nos. 32709-32710/2018 (against Gujarat HC, SCA No. 18433/2017, 29.08.2018) and a large batch of SLPs, order 22.07.2022 (S. Abdul Nazeer and J.K. Maheshwari JJ). Extension order 02.09.2022 (S. Abdul Nazeer and V. Ramasubramanian JJ).
- Holding: GSTN directed to open the portal for filing or revising TRAN-1/TRAN-2 from 01.09.2022 to 31.10.2022, for any aggrieved registered assessee whether or not it had filed a writ or gone to ITGRC. Officers then had 90 days to verify and pass orders on merits after a reasonable opportunity; allowed credit to be reflected in the ECL; the GST Council could issue guidelines. By the order of 02.09.2022 the window was extended by four weeks and the Court clarified that all questions of law decided by High Courts on s.140 are kept open.
- Key words: "any aggrieved registered assessee is directed to file the relevant form or revise the already filed form irrespective of whether the taxpayer has filed writ petition before the High Court" (order 22.07.2022, direction 2)
- Binding: **SC - binding.** Implemented by Circulars 180/12/2022 and 182/14/2022-GST.
- Later history: the batch included the Union's SLPs against the Delhi HC in *Brand Equity* (SLP(C) 7425-7428/2020).
- Use in orders: for claims filed in the Filco window, verify on merits and pass a speaking order after hearing. Claims outside it rely on s.140 as amended by the Finance Act 2020.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 22.07.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/15502592/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 02.09.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/55769490/); [Circular 182/14/2022](https://gstcouncil.gov.in/sites/default/files/2024-06/cir-182-14-2022-cgst.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; lead SLP numbers and benches added; corrected: the "questions of law kept open" clarification is in the 02.09.2022 order, not the 22.07.2022 order.

#### Brand Equity Treaties Ltd v. Union of India - Delhi High Court - 5 May 2020
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 11040/2018 and connected matters (incl. *Micromax Informatics*, W.P.(C) 196/2019), judgment 05.05.2020; Vipin Sanghi and Sanjeev Narula JJ. SC: SLP(C) Nos. 7425-7428/2020.
- Holding: the time limit in rule 117 for TRAN-1 is directory, not mandatory, so credit is not forfeited; but credit cannot be claimed in perpetuity, and three years from the appointed date (by analogy with the Limitation Act) is the outer limit. Petitioners and similarly situated taxpayers could file TRAN-1 by 30.06.2020.
- Key words: "We, therefore, have no hesitation in reading down the said provision [Rule 117] as being directory in nature, insofar as it prescribes the time-limit for transitioning of credit" (para 22)
- Binding: Delhi. **Stayed by the SC on 19 Jun 2020** (A.M. Khanwilkar, Dinesh Maheshwari and Sanjiv Khanna JJ), and overtaken by the retrospective amendment to s.140 (Finance Act 2020) and by *Filco* (2022).
- Later history: SC stay 19.06.2020 ("operation of the impugned order shall remain stayed"). The Union's SLP(C) 7425-7428/2020 is listed in the *Filco* batch disposed of on 22.07.2022.
- Use in orders: do not treat as good law. It was stayed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/31571583/); [Indian Kanoon - SC stay order 19.06.2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/136009876/); [SC order 19.06.2020](https://main.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2020/11526/11526_2020_33_9_22647_Order_19-Jun-2020.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case number, bench and the three-year outer-limit point added; stay confirmed; final disposal in the *Filco* batch confirmed from the batch listing.

#### Adfert Technologies Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Punjab & Haryana High Court (4 Nov 2019); SC SLP dismissed 28 Feb 2020
- Citation / case no.: HC: CWP No. 30949 of 2018 (O&M) and batch (102 petitions), decided 04.11.2019; Jaswant Singh and Lalit Batra JJ. SC: SLP(C) No. 4408/2020, dismissed 28.02.2020 (D.Y. Chandrachud and Sanjiv Khanna JJ), non-speaking.
- Holding: unutilised CENVAT credit is a vested right and cannot be lost for failure to file TRAN-1 by 27.12.2017. Petitioners were allowed to file or revise TRAN-1 electronically or manually by 30.11.2019, subject to verification of genuineness. Relied on *Siddharth Enterprises*.
- Key words: "nobody shall be denied to carry forward legitimate claim of CENVAT / ITC on the ground of non-filing of TRAN-I by 27.12.2017." (operative para)
- Binding: P&H. The SLP dismissal is non-speaking ("In the facts and circumstances of the present case"), so no merger of law. Effect diluted by the retrospective s.140 amendment (Finance Act 2020) and *Filco*.
- Later history: SLP dismissed 28.02.2020.
- Use in orders: historical. Not a basis to accept claims outside the Filco window.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/155452764/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 28.02.2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/162733540/); [taxguru - SC dismisses SLP](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/sc-dismisses-slp-hc-order-adfert-case-allowing-file-revise-tran-1.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; HC date (4 Nov 2019) and bench added; summary confirmed.

#### Siddharth Enterprises v. Nodal Officer - Gujarat High Court - 6 Sep 2019
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 5758 of 2019 with SCA Nos. 5759 and 5760 of 2019 and one more; J.B. Pardiwala and A.C. Rao JJ.
- Holding: CENVAT credit earned under the earlier law is property (Art. 300A) and cannot be taken away by rules alone for failure to file a declaration; the differential treatment offended Art. 14. Petitioners were to be allowed to file TRAN-1/TRAN-2 for credit on stock under s.140(3), and the rule 117 due date is procedural, not mandatory.
- Key words: "CENVAT credit earned under the erstwhile Central Excise Law is the property of the writ-applicants and it cannot be appropriated for merely failing to file a declaration in the absence of Law in this respect." (para 42)
- Binding: Gujarat. Overtaken in part by the retrospective s.140 amendment (Finance Act 2020) and *Filco*.
- Later history: the department's review/recall applications dismissed 14.02.2020. A 2021 order rebuked non-compliance (secondary).
- Use in orders: historical.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17855581/); [Indian Kanoon - review dismissed 14.02.2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17898222/); [Taxmann - TRAN-1 time limit](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/time-limit-for-filing-form-tran-1)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case number and bench added; summary and review dismissal confirmed.

#### Parle Agro Pvt Ltd v. Union of India (W.P. No. 15082 of 2018 and batch) - Telangana High Court - 8 Aug 2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 15082, 15973, 17171 of 2018; 1622, 1638, 8772, 11515, 13882, 20325, 23250 of 2019; 3810, 6522, 11063, 14791 of 2020; 34431, 34437 of 2021; common order 08.08.2022; Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and C.V. Bhaskar Reddy J.
- Holding: petitions seeking time to file or revise TRAN-1/TRAN-2 were disposed of in light of the SC's *Filco* directions; counsel for all parties agreed the directions covered the petitioners' interest.
- Key words: "Learned counsel for the parties are in agreement that the above direction of the Supreme Court sufficiently covers the interest of the petitioners." (para 6)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (consent-based disposal; no ruling on s.140).
- Later history: this is the 08.08.2022 batch decision followed in *Vemulapalli Brothers* (below).
- Use in orders: the Telangana HC treats *Filco* as the governing route.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - common order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/41951453/); [LegitQuest](https://www.legitquest.com/case/parle-agro-private-limited-v-the-union-of-india-and-8-others/76BDEA)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; exact date, batch numbers and bench added; identified as the W.P. 15082/2018 batch.

#### Vemulapalli Brothers v. Assistant Commissioner (Central Taxes) - Telangana High Court - 15 Sep 2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 11307 of 2019, order 15.09.2022; Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and C.V. Bhaskar Reddy J. Both sides agreed it was covered by the decision of 08.08.2022 in W.P. No. 15082 of 2018 and batch (*Parle Agro*, above).
- Holding: challenge to rule 117's 90-day limit and to the closure of TRAN-1 filing from 28.12.2017 (and a prayer to credit Rs 42.26 lakh) disposed of in terms of the *Parle Agro* batch, i.e., the SC's *Filco* directions.
- Key words: "Learned counsel for the parties are in agreement that the present writ petition is covered by a decision of this Court dated 08.08.2022 passed in W.P.No.15082 of 2018" (para 3)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (consent-based disposal).
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: TRAN-1 grievances in Telangana are channelled through the *Filco* window and verification.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/173068347/); [busy.in caselaw](https://busy.in/caselaw/vemulapalli-brothers-vs-assistant-commissioner-central-taxes-telangana-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and bench added; the 08.08.2022 batch identified as *Parle Agro* (in line with *Filco*, now confirmed).

#### BMW India Financial Services Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - 18 Aug 2021
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 9166 of 2020, order 18.08.2021; M.S. Ramachandra Rao and T. Vinod Kumar JJ (per T. Vinod Kumar J).
- Holding: the taxpayer filed TRAN-1 on 27.12.2017 and received an ARN and email acknowledgement, but the VAT transitional credit (Rs 21.08 lakh) never reached its ledger. The department's plea of taxpayer negligence was rejected; following the Bombay HC's decision in the petitioner's own case (WP-LD-VC-85 of 2020, 29.10.2020), the respondents were directed to transition the credit. Eligibility may be examined afterwards on notice, but transition cannot be refused at the threshold.
- Key words: "Once, the petitioner has uploaded the required information by submitting the Form GST TRAN-1 electronically on the portal by using its system, the duty cast on a responsible taxpayer gets complete" (para 26)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (fact-specific).
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: where TRAN-1 was filed in time but not reflected because of portal problems, credit should be transitioned and eligibility then verified on merits.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/61022734/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=129232)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; full party name (BMW India Financial Services), date and case number added; holding clarified.

### Issue 11 - Rule 42/43 reversal for exempt supplies

#### Hemraj Rice Mill v. Assistant Commissioner, CGST & CX - Calcutta High Court - 16 Jun 2025
- Citation / case no.: WPA 3159 of 2025, order 16.06.2025; Raja Basu Chowdhury J. Respondent: Assistant Commissioner, CGST & CX, Bardhaman Division. Tax periods 2017-18 to 2018-19.
- Holding: the adjudicating authority confirmed a reversal of about Rs 55.11 lakh under s.17(2) without applying the rule 42/43 computation. The appellate authority recognised this but dismissed the appeal because no reconciliation was filed; under s.107(12) it should have called for the statements. As the taxpayer was also at fault, the OIO (27.12.2023), the appellate order (11.11.2024) and the DRC-07 (03.01.2024) were set aside and the matter remanded to the adjudicating authority; the taxpayer may file reconciliation statements.
- Key words: "the appellate authority having found that the petitioner had failed to afford appropriate reconciliation statement, ought to have called upon the petitioner to produce such statements to arrive at a correct conclusion" (para 5)
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive. A CGST matter, so directly relevant.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: set out the rule 42/43 computation (T1-T4, C1-C3, D1/D2 etc.) in the order. Call for the reconciliation statement; appellate authorities should call for it under s.107(12).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/142760781/); [taxo.online 01.07.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/01-07-2025-failure-to-follow-rule-42-43-in-itc-reversal-proper-officer-must-apply-rule-42-43-formula-before-disallowing-itc-calcutta-high-court/); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-remands-itc-reversal-case-for-non-compliance-with-rules-42-and-43)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; judge and dates added; holding restated (the order does not use the word "estimation"; remand was to the adjudicating authority).

#### GSTR-9 Table 5 non-GST entries treated as exempt - (court not identified) - reported Jun 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: a reversal under s.17(2)/rule 42 was quashed because the figures in GSTR-9 Table 5 related to non-GST / non-supply transactions and there was no exempt turnover. Remanded.
- Binding: unknown.
- Later history: not found.
- Use in orders: confirm that the turnover is actually "exempt supply" before applying rule 42.
- Source: [JurisHour](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/gst-assessment-quashed-non-gst-transactions-itc-reversal/)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (court not identified) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

### Other Telangana HC ITC rulings found

#### BirlaNu Ltd v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - Dec 2025 (reported Jan 2026)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 14564 of 2024; (2026) 38 Centax 172 (Telangana) (as cited by the AP HC in *Re Sustainability Ltd v. Union of India*, 27.04.2026).
- Holding: rule 39(1)(a) of the CGST Rules is ultra vires s.20 (as it stood before the 2024 amendment) to the extent it mandates that an ISD distribute ITC in the same month. The consequential audit report and SCN (penalty about Rs 8.38 crore) were quashed. The ruling concerns FY 2017-18 and 2018-19. s.20 was amended to allow time limits from 1.4.2025.
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: appeal by the Union not found. Relied on by the AP HC (27.04.2026).
- Use in orders: do not deny ISD credit for pre-1.4.2025 periods only because distribution was not in the same month.
- Source: [LiveLaw Biz](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst/high-court/telangana-high-court-strikes-down-gst-rule-mandating-monthly-itc-distribution-by-isds-518411); [taxo.online 17.01.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/17-01-2026-telangana-high-court-struck-down-rule-391a-to-the-extent-it-mandates-same-month-distribution-of-itc-by-an-isd-as-the-parent-statute-did-not-prescribe-or-permit-any-time-limit/); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/rule-39-itc-norms-invalid-under-section-20-hc)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources agree; one source gives the date as 10 Jan 2026) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; existence and Centax citation confirmed from the AP HC order in *Re Sustainability*, read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026).

Also check the status of the reported SLP against *Sahil Enterprises* (Tripura). One source says it was "entertained", which suggests the issue may not be fully closed.
