## File 03: GST Case Law: Demands, Adjudication, Recovery and Appeals

**Scope.** This file covers show cause notices under ss.73/74/74A CGST Act: whether they are adequate (a DRC-01 summary versus a proper SCN, signatures, vagueness), consolidated multi-year SCNs, the challenges to the s.168A limitation-extension notifications (13/2022, 9/2023, 56/2023), personal hearing under s.75(4), the bar on exceeding the SCN in s.75(7), service through the portal's "Additional Notices" tab, s.74 suppression and s.73/74 conversion, s.128A waiver, s.107 appeal limitation and condonation, GSTAT, writs despite an alternative remedy, provisional attachment under s.83, recovery under ss.78/79 and e-way bill detention under s.129. It is written for an Assistant Commissioner, CGST, Sangareddy Division (Telangana). Telangana High Court rulings are flagged **BINDING IN TELANGANA**.

**Date compiled:** 13 Oct 2026

> **Compiled from web-search summaries; judgment text not yet read. Verify before citing in an order.**
> The search budget ran out before registration cancellation/revocation, s.161 rectification and "vague SCN" could be searched. Those topics are only partly covered (see Gaps and doubts).

---

### Quick answers

**1. DRC-01 summary and unsigned notices or orders.** Courts have held that a summary in DRC-01 does not replace a proper SCN that states the contraventions, for example the Jharkhand HC in NKAS Services. Earlier Telangana DB rulings set aside unsigned SCNs and orders (Silver Oak Villas 2024; Bigleap Technologies 2025), but a **Telangana Full Bench (Gudla Vanaja, 30.09.2026)** has overruled that line: SCNs and orders electronically authenticated and uploaded on the portal are valid without a visible signature, but are invalid without a DIN/RFN. *Takeaway:* issue a full, reasoned SCN under s.73(1)/74(1) with the proposal, grounds and quantification, and attach it to the DRC-01. Make sure each SCN and order carries a DIN/RFN; signing digitally remains good practice.

**2. Consolidated SCN covering several financial years.** The courts are split.
- *Delhi HC (Ambika Traders 2025, followed in Mathur Polymers and Technosys) and now the Karnataka HC DB (23.04.2026):* a consolidated SCN is permissible, and in fraud/ITC cases "necessary". The SLPs were dismissed (Ambika as "not pressed"; Mathur Polymers in limine), so the Supreme Court has laid down no law.
- *Against consolidation:* Madras HC (Titan, affirmed by a DB on 21.03.2025), Kerala HC (Lakshmi Mobile Accessories; Tharayil Medicals) and Bombay HC (Milroc; Rithwik Projects, June 2026). A different Bombay bench referred the question to a Larger Bench in Rollmet LLP (17 Apr 2026). The Karnataka single-judge line (Bangalore Golf Club, Veremax) was **reversed by a Karnataka Division Bench on 23.04.2026** (W.A. 1751/2024 & batch), which held consolidation permissible.
- **Telangana HC (Goldstone Infra, DB, 27.01.2026)** followed Mathur Polymers and upheld a composite s.74 SCN and order for several years, holding there is no prejudice if each year is within its s.74(10) limit.

*Takeaway:* in Telangana a composite s.74 SCN/order is defensible (Goldstone Infra), provided each year is within its own limitation and the figures are shown year-wise. For s.73 cases, a separate SCN and order for each financial year remains the safest course.

**3. Limitation-extension notifications (13/2022, 9/2023, 56/2023).**
- *Upheld or not interfered with:* Telangana HC (Brunda Infra / HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV batch, 02.01.2025: declined to interfere because the Supreme Court's Covid exclusion applies, but observed that 56/2023 lacked the mandatory GST Council recommendation), Allahabad HC on 9/2023 (Graziano) and Patna HC on 56/2023 (Barhonia Engicon, 27.11.2024).
- *Quashed:* Gauhati HC quashed 56/2023 (Barkataki Print, 19.09.2024). Madras HC (single judge, Tata Play batch) held both 9/2023 and 56/2023 illegal (12.06.2025).
- *Supreme Court:* the SLP against the Telangana judgment, **SLP (C) 4240/2025, HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV v. Asst. Commr. of State Tax**, is pending. Notice was issued on 21.02.2025 and the matter was admitted before Pardiwala and Mahadevan JJ. No final verdict was found as of the latest reports.

*Takeaway:* the Telangana ruling binds Telangana officers, so orders passed within the extended dates are not invalid here. Expect writs and remands elsewhere (Delhi HC is remanding such cases pending the Supreme Court). Record in the order the date of the SCN, the notification relied on and the due date. Where possible, act within the un-extended limitation.

**4. Personal hearing under s.75(4).** A hearing is mandatory wherever an adverse decision is contemplated, even if the taxpayer did not ask for one or tick "no".
- Telangana HC: B.K. Steels / WP 18654 & 18766 of 2024 (25.07.2024; order set aside by consent for want of a s.75(4) hearing).
- Followed by the Madras, Madhya Pradesh, Patna, Gujarat, Punjab & Haryana, Allahabad, Calcutta and Uttarakhand HCs.
- A notice that shows "N.A." against the hearing date, or no date, time and venue, does not count as a hearing.

*Takeaway:* always issue a separate hearing notice with the date, time and venue, record the outcome, and offer an adjournment within the s.75(5) limit (three adjournments).

**5. Order cannot exceed the SCN (s.75(7)).** The order cannot demand more tax, interest or penalty than the SCN proposed, and cannot rest on grounds the SCN did not raise. Leading cases: Allahabad HC (Vrinda Automation 2025; Sanjay Construction 2026) and Karnataka HC (WP 18888/2025). *Takeaway:* quantify the interest and penalty in the SCN. If fresh grounds or figures emerge, issue a fresh or supplementary SCN within limitation.

**6. "Additional Notices" tab.** Delhi HC (ACE Cardiopathy, 2024:DHC:4108-DB, and many later cases) set aside ex parte orders where the SCN or hearing notices were uploaded only under the "Additional Notices & Orders" tab, which was not prominent before 16 Jan 2024. *Takeaway:* if the taxpayer did not respond, use another mode of service under s.169 as well (email, RPAD) and record it. Do not pass an ex parte order on portal upload alone.

**7. Section 74 invoked without specific allegations.** The **Supreme Court, G.R. Infra Projects Ratlam v. State of M.P. (19.08.2026, CA 11277/2026)**, held:
- The facts showing fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression must be set out in the SCN itself.
- A bland recital of "fraud or suppression" is not enough. Using "or" between the grounds shows the officer was unsure which applied.
- Deficiencies cannot be cured later through a counter-affidavit.

*Takeaway:* in every s.74/74A(5) SCN, plead the specific suppressed facts, the evidence and the intent. Do not use alternative boilerplate.

**8. Conversion from s.74 to s.73 (s.75(2)).** If fraud is not established, the demand is re-determined as if under s.73. Whether s.73 limitation then bars it is unsettled. The GSTAT (Sterling & Wilson, reported) held that the *appellate* authority cannot itself convert a s.74 order into a s.73 order. Gujarat HC (R B Pandey and Sons, Feb 2026) refused conversion where the findings of fraud stood. *Takeaway:* make clear findings on fraud. Where fraud fails, re-determine under s.75(2) and check s.73 limitation.

**9. Waiver scheme (s.128A).**
- Madras HC: waiver is available for self-assessed tax once the department proceeded under s.73 (Incompressible Fluid Control, 28.07.2026). A rejection citing ITC from non-existent suppliers was set aside because the proceedings had been under s.73, not s.74 (A. Gurusamy, 16.06.2026).
- Karnataka HC: the filing deadline is directory (Sri Laxmi Borewell, 17.04.2026). The Madras HC disagreed and held the Rule 164 time limits mandatory (Incompressible Fluid Control).

No Telangana HC ruling was found. *Takeaway:* decide s.128A applications strictly on the statutory and Rule 164 conditions, and give reasons.

**10. Appeal limitation (s.107) and writs.**
- The Supreme Court (GlaxoSmithKline, 06.05.2020; note: an AP VAT case, not Central Excise or 2024) held that a writ should not be used to bypass a time-barred statutory appeal.
- Several HCs (relying on Singh Enterprises, SC) hold that the appellate authority cannot condone delay beyond 3 months + 1 month. The Supreme Court stayed (30.08.2024) an HC ruling that s.5 of the Limitation Act applies, and in July/Aug 2026 issued notice on SLPs raising the point, so the question is pending.
- The Telangana HC usually remits delay questions to the appellate authority. It also requires the appellate authority to hear the appellant on a condonation application (s.107(8)).

*Takeaway:* for officers acting as appellate authority, give a hearing on condonation and pass a reasoned order. Do not condone beyond the s.107(4) cap.

**11. GSTAT.**
- Formally launched 24.09.2025. Chennai Bench reported operational from 22.01.2026.
- Backlog appeals deadline: originally 30.06.2026 (S.O. 4220(E), 17.09.2025), extended to 31.07.2026 by a 30.06.2026 notification.
- Later orders follow the normal 3-month limit under s.112.
- Madras HC (Revenue Bar Association, 20.09.2019) held the original composition of GSTAT unconstitutional.

*Takeaway:* once an appeal is filed with the pre-deposit, recovery of the balance is stayed (s.112(9) and s.107(7)). Lift attachments once proof is shown.

**12. Writ despite an alternative remedy.** The Supreme Court in Radha Krishan Industries (20.04.2021) held that an alternative remedy is a rule of discretion, not a bar. Writs lie for breach of natural justice, lack of jurisdiction or vires challenges. *Takeaway:* orders that breach natural justice (no hearing, no reasons, defective service) will be quashed in writ even though an appeal lies.

**13. Provisional attachment (s.83).**
- The Commissioner must form an opinion on tangible material that attachment is necessary to protect revenue. Power delegated to a Joint Commissioner is exercised as the Commissioner's delegate (Radha Krishan, SC).
- An attachment lapses automatically after one year (Kesari Nandan Mobile, SC 2025 INSC 983; Krish Overseas, Delhi HC).

*Takeaway:* record the reasons and material in the file. Do not continue a bank freeze beyond one year, and release it on Rule 159(5) objections or on pre-deposit and appeal where the law requires.

**14. Recovery before the appeal period ends (ss.78/79).**
- No recovery within 3 months of service of the order unless the proper officer records written reasons under the s.78 proviso (Cargotec India, Madras HC 2024).
- An overdraft account cannot be attached under s.79 (Madras HC 2026).
- Filing an appeal with the pre-deposit stays recovery (Calcutta and Delhi HCs).

*Takeaway:* do not issue a DRC-13 garnishee notice or debit the ledger early without a recorded, specific reason.

**15. E-way bill detention and penalty (s.129).**
- Telangana HC in Satyam Shivam Papers (02.06.2021) held that where an e-way bill expired for a genuine reason (a road blockade) and there was nothing to suggest evasion, detention and penalty under s.129 were wrongly invoked, and imposed costs on the officer. The Supreme Court dismissed the department's SLP on 12.01.2022. **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Allahabad HC has repeatedly quashed s.129 penalties for technical errors without intent to evade.

*Takeaway:* before a s.129 order, record the explanation offered and a specific finding on intent to evade or on actual tax risk.

---

### 1. SCN requirements: DRC-01 summary, signature, form

#### NKAS Services Pvt. Ltd. v. State of Jharkhand - Jharkhand HC (DB: Aparesh Kumar Singh and Anubha Rawat Choudhary JJ.) - 06.10.2021 (W.P.(T) No. 2444 of 2021); second writ W.P.(T) No. 2659 of 2021 (order of 09.02.2022 as reported)
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(T) No. 2444 of 2021, judgment dated 06.10.2021. A second writ by the same petitioner, W.P.(T) No. 2659 of 2021 (s.73 SCN dated 14.06.2021), was heard on 10.01.2022 "on same principles"; its final order (reported as 09.02.2022) was not found.
- Holding: A s.74 SCN that did not state the foundational allegations (fraud, wilful misstatement, suppression) was vague and violated natural justice; a summary in Form GST DRC-01 under Rule 142(1) "cannot substitute" a proper SCN. Both the SCN and the DRC-01 were quashed, with liberty to start fresh proceedings within four weeks (paras 17-18).
- Key words: "A summary of show-cause notice as issued in Form GST DRC-01 in terms of Rule 142(1) of the JGST Rules, 2017 ... cannot substitute the requirement of a proper show-cause notice." (para 17)
- Binding: Persuasive (Jharkhand HC).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: always issue a self-contained SCN setting out facts, contraventions, provisions and quantification, and attach it to the DRC-01.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Jharkhand HC W.P.(T) 2444/2021, 06.10.2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/9472001/); [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(T) 2659/2021, order 10.01.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/61231496/); [Taxo - Jharkhand HC: mere summary in DRC-01 cannot substitute SCN](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/mere-summary-in-form-drc-01-cannot-substitute-the-requirements-of-a-proper-show-cause-notice-under-section-73-or-74-jharkhand-high-court/); [Taxmann - Summary of SCN in DRC-01 cannot substitute SCN (court not named in snippet)](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/summary-of-scn-in-form-gst-drc-01-cannot-substitute-statutory-requirement-of-scn-under-cgst-act-hc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** the reasoned judgment is W.P.(T) 2444/2021 dated 06.10.2021 (not 09.02.2022); bench added (Aparesh Kumar Singh J., now Chief Justice of the Telangana HC, wrote it). The 09.02.2022 order in W.P.(T) 2659/2021 was not found.

#### Silver Oak Villas LLP v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Telangana HC (Division Bench) - 14.03.2024 - **SUPERSEDED: see Full Bench, Gudla Vanaja (30.09.2026)**
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 6671 of 2024 (as reported); [2025] 137 GSTR 620 (Telangana) (cite as given by the Full Bench).
- Holding: Relying on Rule 26(3) CGST Rules and the Bombay HC in Ramani Suchit Malushite, held that an unsigned order lacks efficacy; the SCN and order were set aside (as described by the Telangana Full Bench, which read it).
- Binding: **No longer reliable.** A Telangana DB in Bigleap Technologies (28.02.2025) held its Rule 26(3) reasoning per incuriam (Rule 26(3) is in the registration chapter), and the Full Bench in Gudla Vanaja (30.09.2026) held that SCNs and orders under Chapter XVIII electronically authenticated on the portal are valid without a visible signature.
- Later history: See Binding. The Full Bench (paras 34-38, 143-146) treated Silver Oak Villas, Sahithi Marketers and Bigleap as conflicting DB decisions, which is why the question was referred.
- Use in orders: do not rely on it. Follow Gudla Vanaja (Full Bench): make sure every SCN and order carries a DIN/RFN and is uploaded through the officer's authenticated portal login; signing digitally remains good practice.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC Full Bench, Gudla Vanaja, 30.09.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140433973/); [TaxHeal - Summary attachment to DRC-01 without duly authenticated SCN invalidates s.73 demand](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=138722)
- Verified: Primary (indirect) - the 14.03.2024 judgment itself was not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; its date, citation and reasoning are confirmed from the Full Bench judgment in Gudla Vanaja (read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026). **Corrected:** now superseded.

#### Bigleap Technologies and Solutions Pvt. Ltd. v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (DB: Acting CJ Sujoy Paul and Renuka Yara J.) - 28.02.2025 - **OVERRULED by Full Bench, Gudla Vanaja (30.09.2026)**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21101 of 2024 and batch; [2025] 142 GSTR 608 (Telangana).
- Holding: SCNs and orders placed on the portal without the physical or digital signature of the proper officer (admitted by the State) "cannot sustain judicial scrutiny", because the statutory DRC forms require a signature; it disagreed with Silver Oak Villas on Rule 26(3) but reached the same result through Chapter XVIII and the forms. Notices and orders set aside, with liberty to issue fresh ones and limitation "will not be a hurdle" (paras 47-48).
- Key words: "the impugned show cause notices and the orders which are not pregnant with the signature of the Proper Officer cannot sustain judicial scrutiny." (para 47)
- Binding: **Overruled.** The Full Bench in Gudla Vanaja (30.09.2026) held that Bigleap and similar decisions "do not lay down the correct position of law" (para 154).
- Later history: An SLP against Bigleap was pending without stay (as recorded in Gudla Vanaja); its outcome was not checked. Overruled by the Full Bench on 30.09.2026.
- Use in orders: do not rely on it. See Gudla Vanaja below.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Bigleap Technologies, 28.02.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/131900579/); [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC Full Bench, Gudla Vanaja, 30.09.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140433973/); [LiveLaw - Telangana HC ruling: GST SCN without digital signature](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/telangana-high-court/telangana-high-court-ruling-gst-show-cause-notice-without-digital-signature-central-goods-and-services-act-285563)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** party name, case number and date identified (previously "party name not found"); the holding has since been overruled by a Full Bench.

#### Gudla Vanaja v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Saroor Nagar-I Circle - Telangana HC (**Full Bench**: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh, N. Tukaramji and G.M. Mohiuddin JJ.) - 30.09.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 20397 of 2025 and a batch of about 200 writ petitions (reference made by a Division Bench on 12.06.2026). Reserved 15.07.2026, pronounced 30.09.2026.
- Holding: SCNs and orders under Chapter XVIII (Demands and Recovery) that are electronically authenticated by the proper officer and uploaded on the common portal are valid even without a visible digital or physical signature on the form, and their upload is valid service under s.169(1)(d). **A notice or order without a DIN or RFN is invalid.** Limitation for appeal runs from the date of upload. Bigleap Technologies and similar decisions do not lay down the correct law. Petitioners were given two weeks to appeal (paras 154-155).
- Key words: "absence of DIN or RFN on any of these documents would render it invalid. The period of limitation to avail statutory remedy or to make other compliances would run from the date on which the show cause notice or order is uploaded". (para 154)
- Binding: Binding on all Telangana officers and on Telangana HC benches of lesser strength.
- Later history: none found (any SLP not checked; the judgment is two weeks old).
- Use in orders: when a taxpayer argues that an SCN or order is void for want of a signature, cite this Full Bench. Make sure every SCN, DRC-01/DRC-07 summary and order has a DIN/RFN and is issued from the officer's own authenticated login. Record the upload date as the date of service.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC Full Bench, 30.09.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140433973/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; new entry added during verification (it overrules the Bigleap line and supersedes Silver Oak Villas).

#### M/s Balaji Filling Station v. State Tax Officer, Bodhan and Nizamabad - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 14.08.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 25574 of 2026 (TGST Act).
- Holding: For FY 2017-18, the SCN dated 28.12.2023 was followed by an order on 30.12.2023. On the State's concession that the SCN was contrary to s.73(2) (SCN at least 3 months before the s.73(10) time limit), the SCN and order were set aside (paras 2-5).
- Key words: "the impugned order dated 30.12.2023 arising out of the show cause notice dated 28.12.2023 ... cannot be sustained in the eye of law being in teeth of the specific mandate of timeline to be adhered under Section 73(2)". (para 4)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana. It turned on a concession, so its precedential weight is limited.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: under s.73, the SCN must issue at least 3 months before the due date for the order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 25574/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/146036745/); [TheTaxCorp - Telangana HC quashes GST order and SCN for FY 2017-18 for violation of s.73(2)](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-quashes-gst-order-and-scn-for-fy-2017-18-due-to-violation)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party, bench, case number and date (14.08.2026) added; summary confirmed. Note the order date (30.12.2023), two days after the SCN.

---

### 2. Consolidated SCN covering several financial years

#### Ambika Traders through Proprietor Gaurav Gupta v. Additional Commissioner, Adjudication DGGSTI, CGST Delhi North - Delhi HC (DB: Prathiba M. Singh and Rajneesh Kumar Gupta JJ.) - 29.07.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 4853 of 2025; 2025 SCC OnLine Del 6913 (as cited by the Delhi HC in Sanskriti Exim, 18.09.2026).
- Holding: Following an earlier Delhi DB order (Vallabh Textile, W.P.(C) 13855/2024, 03.10.2024), a consolidated s.74 SCN/order for FY 2017-18 to 2021-22 is permissible; the provisions do not prohibit it, and where fraudulent ITC (here over Rs 83 crore) is alleged it may be "required". The order set out year-wise amounts, so it was decipherable (paras 52-55). Writ disposed of with Rs 25,000 costs; appeal allowed by 31.08.2025 without limitation objection (paras 67, 69).
- Key words: "A consolidated notice is, therefore, not merely permissible but, in fact, required in such cases in order to establish the illegal modality adopted by such businesses and entities." (para 54)
- Binding: Persuasive outside Delhi.
- Later history: SLP (C) No. 23774 of 2025 (Pardiwala and K.V. Viswanathan JJ.) **dismissed as not pressed** on 01.09.2025 (order read on Indian Kanoon). This creates no Supreme Court precedent. Followed in Sanskriti Exim (Delhi HC, 18.09.2026).
- Use in orders: can be cited outside Delhi only as persuasive. Do not describe it as approved by the Supreme Court.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 4853/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/158945360/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 01.09.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/32081952/); [LiveLaw - Consolidated SCN for multiple FYs necessary to establish wrongful ITC: Delhi HC](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/delhi-high-court/consolidated-scn-for-multiple-financial-years-necessary-to-establish-wrongful-availment-of-itc-delhi-high-court-299763); [TaxHeal - SC order on SLP](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=106069); [Jurishour - consolidated GST SCN jurisdiction](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/consolidated-gst-scn-jurisdiction/)
- Verified: Primary - HC judgment and SC order read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number, citation confirmed; the SLP link is now confirmed from the SC order itself.

#### M/s Mathur Polymers v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Prathiba M. Singh and Shail Jain JJ.) - 26.08.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 2394 of 2025 (challenge to OIO dated 02.02.2025).
- Holding: Following Ambika Traders, in fraudulent-ITC cases spread over several years a consolidated SCN/order "may in fact be required"; the statute does not prevent it. Hearing notices sent to the email registered on the portal (even if it was the CA's) were valid service; the petitioner had concealed material facts. Writ dismissed with Rs 50,000 costs (paras 21-25).
- Key words: "in cases involving allegations of fraudulent availment of ITC, where the transactions are spread across several years, a consolidated notice may in fact be required". (para 22)
- Binding: Persuasive outside Delhi.
- Later history: SLP **dismissed** by the Supreme Court (Sanjay Kumar and Alok Aradhe JJ.) on 07.11.2025: "no good ground and reason to interfere" (a non-speaking dismissal, not a declaration of law). A later miscellaneous application was withdrawn on 05.01.2026. The Bombay HC in Rollmet LLP (2026) reportedly cited the dismissal when referring the issue to a Larger Bench.
- Use in orders: supports a consolidated order in fraud/ITC cases. Rely on it with caution.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 2394/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/61101895/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 07.11.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/187470167/); [TaxGuru - SC dismisses SLP of Mathur Polymers](https://taxguru.in/?p=1000495); [CAClubIndia - Bombay HC refers consolidated SCN question to Larger Bench](https://www.caclubindia.com/judiciary/consolidated-gst-show-cause-notice-for-multiple-years-bombay-hc-refers-matter-to-larger-bench-5969.asp)
- Verified: Primary - HC judgment and SC orders (07.11.2025, 05.01.2026) read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, case number added; the SCN/order was multi-year and fraud-based (confirmed).

#### M/s Technosys Integrated Solutions Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Nitin Wasudeo Sambre and Ajay Digpaul JJ.) - 16.03.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 5581 of 2025.
- Holding: An audit-originated s.74 SCN and OIO (31.01.2025; tax about Rs 19.97 crore on a rate-classification issue, not fraudulent ITC) covering several years was upheld against the consolidation challenge. The Court refused to confine Ambika Traders to fraudulent-ITC cases and read it as accepting consolidation under both s.73 and s.74; the Bombay HC's contrary view did not displace binding Delhi precedent; pendency before the Supreme Court is no reason to defer (paras 21-25). Writ dismissed, leaving other remedies open.
- Key words: "this Court, in the said judgment, contemplated and accepted consolidation of SCNs covering multiple financial years under both Section 73 and Section 74 of the CGST Act." (para 22)
- Binding: Persuasive outside Delhi.
- Later history: none found. (The earlier note of a "later Delhi bench (Aug 2025)" calling the issue res integra was not checked.)
- Use in orders: shows the Delhi view extends to s.73. It conflicts with Madras, Karnataka and Bombay.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 5581/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/128021198/); [TaxGuru (Delhi HC consolidated SCN)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1039290); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=129652)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date is 16.03.2026 (not 2025); case number and bench added; facts confirm a non-fraud (classification) case.

#### Titan Company Ltd. v. Joint Commissioner of GST & Central Excise, Salem - Madras HC (Krishnan Ramasamy J., single judge) - 18.12.2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 33164 of 2023 (with W.P. 33120 of 2023); (2024) 15 Centax 118 (Mad.) (as cited by the Bombay HC in Rollmet).
- Holding: "Bunching" of s.73 SCNs for several years is against the spirit of s.73, because limitation runs separately for each financial year from its annual return; relied on State of J&K v. Caltex (each assessment year can be split up) (paras 14-15). Rather than quashing, the Court directed the authority to decide the petitioner's representation to split the SCN year-wise, extended the 2017-18 adjudication deadline by the 26-day stay period, and deferred proceedings meanwhile (para 16).
- Key words: "issuing bunching of show cause notices is against the spirit of provisions of Section 73 of the Act". (para 15)
- Binding: Persuasive (Madras HC).
- Later history: Affirmed by a Madras Division Bench on 21.03.2025 in W.A. Nos. 2389 and 1397 of 2024 (as recorded by the Madras HC in Balamore Estates, 28.07.2025; the DB order itself was not read). Followed in Madras (R.A. and Co., 21.07.2025), Karnataka (Veremax, 04.09.2024; Bangalore Golf Club) and Kerala (Lakshmi Mobile Accessories; Tharayil Medicals, 08.04.2025). Balamore Estates (Madras single judge, 28.07.2025) noted Titan only felt it "pragmatic" to issue separate notices and that Karnataka had taken it further by quashing outright. The earlier note of a DB disposal "by agreement" with a 4-week gap direction is single-source and unconfirmed.
- Use in orders: the main authority against multi-year s.73 SCNs. Issue separate SCNs for each year.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 33164/2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/93671693/); [Indian Kanoon - Balamore Estates, Madras HC 28.07.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/184302126/); [AMLegals - Madras HC: bunching of SCNs violates law](https://amlegals.com/madras-high-court-ruling-bunching-of-show-cause-notices-under-gst-act-violates-legal-provisions/); [EY alert - SCN cannot be clubbed for more than one FY](https://www.ey.com/content/dam/ey-unified-site/ey-com/en-in/alerts-hub/2025/07/hc-holds-scn-cannot-be-clubbed-and-issued-for-more-than-one-financial-year.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date is 18.12.2023 (single judge), not 2024; the relief was a direction to split, not a quashing; DB affirmance (21.03.2025) taken from a later Madras judgment.

#### M/s Bangalore Golf Club v. Assistant Commissioner of Commercial Taxes (Enforcement) - Karnataka HC (Sachin Shankar Magadum J., single judge) - 07.08.2024 - **reasoning overruled by Karnataka DB, 23.04.2026**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 16500 of 2024 (T-RES); (2024) 166 taxmann.com 642 (Kar.) (as cited).
- Holding: Following Titan and State of J&K v. Caltex, a single s.73 SCN (07.05.2024) and DRC-01 summary for 2019-20 to 2023-24 were quashed as contrary to s.73(10), with liberty to issue separate SCNs for each year (paras 3-7).
- Key words: "the practice of issuing a single, consolidated show cause notice for multiple assessment years contravenes the provisions of the CGST Act" (single judge, para 6); reversed: "we hold that issuance of a consolidated/common show cause notice for multiple financial years is permissible." (DB, para 64)
- Binding: **No longer good law in Karnataka.** A Karnataka Division Bench (S.G. Pandit and K.V. Aravind JJ.) in *Principal Commissioner of Central Tax v. Pramur Homes and Shelters / Commissioner of Central Tax v. Chimney Hills Education Society*, W.A. No. 1751 of 2024 & batch, dated 23.04.2026, held that a consolidated SCN for multiple financial years **is permissible** under ss.73/74, set aside six single-judge orders (including Veremax, W.P. 15810/2024, and Chimney Hills, W.P. 26164/2024) and restored the SCNs (paras 61-66).
- Later history: See Binding. Further appeal against the DB judgment not checked.
- Use in orders: do not cite it. Karnataka now sides with Delhi on consolidation; Madras, Kerala and some Bombay benches remain against.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Karnataka HC W.P. 16500/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/119832609/); [Indian Kanoon - Karnataka DB W.A. 1751/2024 & batch, 23.04.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/712560/); [TaxGuru (Madras HC batch discussing Bangalore Golf Club)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1055468)
- Verified: Primary - single-judge order and the 23.04.2026 Karnataka DB judgment read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date (07.08.2024) and judge added; the Karnataka position has since been reversed by a Division Bench.

#### Milroc Good Earth Developers v. Union of India - Bombay HC (Goa Bench, DB) - 09.10.2025 (date as cited by the Bombay HC in Rollmet)
- Citation / case no.: [2026] 104 GSTL 45 (Bombay) [09-10-2025] (as cited in Rollmet LLP, 2026:BHC-OS:9682-DB). Case number not found.
- Holding: GST works on specific tax periods and returns, so proceedings must be confined to each financial year. The SCN dated 28.03.2025 under ss.74(1)/74A covering FY 2017-18 to 2023-24 was held impermissible. A Taxo report dated 09.03.2026 says the Bombay HC held that s.74 SCNs must be issued separately for each FY; this is probably Milroc, but that is not confirmed.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: followed in **Rithwik Projects** (Bombay HC DB, June 2026; secondary) and, per Rollmet, by the Nagpur Bench (Paras Stone Industries; Rite Water Solutions). **Rollmet LLP v. Union of India, W.P. No. 16848 of 2025 & batch (Bombay HC DB: G.S. Kulkarni and Aarti Sathe JJ.), 17.04.2026 - read on Indian Kanoon:** the Bench disagreed with Milroc, observing that s.73/74(10) limits the time to pass an order, not the issue of a consolidated SCN, and referred five questions to a **Larger Bench**, including whether Milroc lays down the correct law and the effect of the SC order in Mathur Polymers (paras 28-31). Interim orders continue. The Larger Bench outcome was not found.
- Use in orders: the law in Bombay is unsettled. Year-wise SCNs avoid the problem.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Bombay HC Rollmet LLP reference, 17.04.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/75433093/); [LiveLawBiz - Bombay HC quashes demand through single SCN covering multiple years](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst/high-court/bombay-high-court-quashes-gst-demand-raised-through-single-show-cause-notice-covering-multiple-financial-years-537945); [CAClubIndia - Bombay HC refers to Larger Bench](https://www.caclubindia.com/judiciary/consolidated-gst-show-cause-notice-for-multiple-years-bombay-hc-refers-matter-to-larger-bench-5969.asp); [Taxo 09.03.2026](https://taxo.online/?p=546077)
- Verified: Secondary for Milroc's own holding (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026); date and citation confirmed from the Rollmet reference judgment, which was read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026.

#### M/s Goldstone Infra v. Additional Commissioner, Customs & Central Tax, Ranga Reddy CGST Commissionerate - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 27.01.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1498 of 2026 (challenge to SCN dated 21.03.2025 and composite s.74 OIO dated 13.10.2025 covering several years including 2018-19 and 2019-20).
- Holding: Following the Delhi HC in Mathur Polymers (SLP dismissed 07.11.2025), the HC rejected the challenge to a composite s.74 SCN and order for multiple years: ss.73(3)/74(3) speak of "any period", and an assessee is not prejudiced if the composite order is passed within the s.74(10) limit for each year (here 2018-19's limit, as extended, was 31.12.2025). It made no difference that ITC was only one of the issues. It also held that non-issue of DRC-01A for periods before 15.10.2020 causes no prejudice and does not vitiate the order (distinguishing New Morning Star Travels (AP) and DBL-Deco (a consent order)). Writ dismissed; petitioner left to appeal, with delay to be considered.
- Key words: "The assessee cannot claim to be prejudiced if the adjudication proceedings for multiple years have been concluded before the period of limitation prescribed under Section 74(10) of the CGST Act."
- Binding: Binding on Telangana officers.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a composite s.74 SCN/order for several years is defensible in Telangana, provided each year is within its own s.74(10) limit and the order sets out the year-wise figures. Year-wise SCNs remain the safer course for s.73 cases.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 1498/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/138915856/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; new entry added during verification (the file previously said no Telangana ruling had been found).

#### M/s Ennem Excel Engineering Pvt. Ltd. v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Sangareddy-II Circle - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 08.01.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 383 of 2026, following the common order dated 15.10.2025 in W.P. No. 20731 of 2025 and batch.
- Holding: Multiple SCNs and multiple orders for the same tax period under the TGST/CGST/IGST Acts. On the Advocate General's statement that such errors fall under the second proviso to s.161 TGST Act (no six-month limit) and a circular dated 14.10.2025 setting out an SOP, the HC disposed of the writ directing the proper officer to rectify the notices/orders with intimation to the assessee; grievances outside s.161 may be raised before the appropriate forum (paras 2-5, quoting paras 7-10 of the 15.10.2025 order).
- Key words: "the grievances relating to issuance of multiple show cause notices or orders covering same tax period are amenable to rectification under Section 161 of the TGST Act." (para 8 of the 15.10.2025 order, quoted in para 4)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: avoid parallel or duplicate SCNs and orders for the same period (coordinate with the State side). Where duplicates exist, rectify them under s.161.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 383/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/106407809/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC directs GST authorities to rectify multiple SCNs/orders](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-directs-gst-authorities-rectify-multiple-scns-orders.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** it is a Division Bench order, not a single judge; respondent (Sangareddy-II Circle, State Tax), case number and the 15.10.2025 batch order (W.P. 20731/2025) identified.

---

### 3. Limitation-extension notifications under s.168A (13/2022, 9/2023, 56/2023)

#### HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV v. Assistant Commissioner of State Tax (W.P. No. 32883 of 2024, part of the Brunda Infra batch W.P. No. 1154 of 2024 & batch) - Telangana HC (DB: Sujoy Paul and Dr. G. Radha Rani JJ.) - 02.01.2025 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: HC: W.P. No. 32883 of 2024, decided in the common judgment in W.P. No. 1154 of 2024 & batch (lead: Brunda Infra Pvt. Ltd.) dated 02.01.2025. SC: **SLP (C) No. 4240 of 2025**.
- Holding:
  - The batch challenged Notifications 13/2022, 9/2023 and 56/2023. The HC observed that a GST Council recommendation is a sine qua non under s.168A, that 56/2023 was admittedly issued on the Implementation/Law Committee's decision without one, and that later "ratification" cannot cure this (paras 94-97).
  - But it held that the Supreme Court's suo motu order (10.01.2022) excluding 15.03.2020 to 28.02.2022 applies to GST proceedings; once that period is excluded, the proceedings were not time-barred, so the validity of the notifications became "academic" and was not decided (paras 116, 118).
  - It declined to interfere and disposed of the writs, leaving the petitioners to appeal within 45 days; the appellate authority was told to decide on merits and not reject on limitation (paras 119, 121).
- Key words: "Since the period between 15.03.2020 to 28.02.2022 stood excluded for the purpose of counting limitation by an order which became law of the land, the remaining argument relating to validity of notifications became academic in nature." (para 116)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana unless and until the Supreme Court decides otherwise.
- Later history: **Supreme Court issued notice on 21.02.2025** (Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ.), confining the SLP to Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 for FY 2019-20 and recording a "cleavage of opinion amongst different High Courts" (order read on Indian Kanoon). Adjourned on 23.04.2025 to 14.05.2025. A Delhi HC order of 08.10.2026 (Forever Exim) still describes the validity of 9/2023 and 56/2023 as "presently under consideration" in SLP (C) 4240/2025. **No final Supreme Court judgment found.**
- Use in orders: Telangana officers may rely on the Covid exclusion (15.03.2020 to 28.02.2022) as held by this judgment. Do **not** describe it as upholding Notification 56/2023: its observations on 56/2023 are adverse. Note in the order that the issue is pending in SLP (C) 4240/2025.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 1154/2024 & batch, 02.01.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/86705505/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 21.02.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198807505/); [Taxscan - SC defers matter observing varying HC opinions](https://www.taxscan.in/extending-limitation-to-adjudicate-gst-scn-by-notification-u-s-168a-supreme-court-defers-matter-observing-varying-hc-opinions/493034); [Jurishour - GST Council recommendation not a precondition for s.168A notification: Telangana HC](https://jurishour.in/gst/gst-council-recommendation-not-a-precondition-for-issuing-s-168a-notification-telangana-high-court); [TaxGuru - Section 168A: four HCs, four views](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-168a-gst-dispute-four-high-courts-four-views-limitation-extension.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (HC judgment and SC orders of 21.02.2025 and 23.04.2025). **Corrected:** date, case number and bench found; it is the same judgment as Brunda Infra; the HC did not uphold 56/2023 - it said Council recommendation is mandatory and ratification cannot cure its absence, but decided the case on the Covid exclusion.

#### Brunda Infra Pvt. Ltd. v. Additional Commissioner of Central Tax (W.P. No. 1154 of 2024 & batch) - Telangana HC (DB: Sujoy Paul and Dr. G. Radha Rani JJ.) - 02.01.2025 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1154 of 2024 & batch (common judgment; the batch includes W.P. No. 32883 of 2024, HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV, which went to the Supreme Court). Indian Kanoon indexes it under the title "Silver Oak Villas LLP v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax" (a petitioner in the batch).
- Holding:
  - **Same judgment as the HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV entry above.**
  - Read "in respect of actions" in s.168A widely, to cover actions of the Covid period that could not be completed, agreeing with the Allahabad HC (para 86).
  - **Did not uphold Notification 56/2023.** It held that a GST Council recommendation is a condition precedent, that 56/2023 was admittedly issued without one, and that later ratification by the Council cannot cure this (paras 94-97). The Jurishour report that the HC held a recommendation is "not a precondition" is wrong.
  - It nonetheless declined to interfere, because the Supreme Court's suo motu Covid exclusion applied and made the challenge academic (paras 116-119).
- Key words: "The expression on the recommendation of Council leaves no room for any doubt that this is a condition precedent or sine qua non for the Government for taking a decision regarding issuance of notification." (para 94)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana.
- Later history: SLP (C) 4240/2025 (HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV) against this judgment is pending in the Supreme Court.
- Use in orders: cite for the Covid exclusion and the wide reading of s.168A. Do not cite it as upholding 56/2023; if a taxpayer cites paras 94-97, answer that the HC did not strike down the notification and decided the case on the Covid exclusion.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 1154/2024 & batch, 02.01.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/86705505/); [Jurishour - Telangana HC](https://jurishour.in/gst/gst-council-recommendation-not-a-precondition-for-issuing-s-168a-notification-telangana-high-court); [TaxGuru - four HCs, four views](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-168a-gst-dispute-four-high-courts-four-views-limitation-extension.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** it is one judgment with HCC-SEW (W.P. 1154/2024 & batch, DB, 02.01.2025); the claim that it "upheld 56/2023" and that Council recommendation is "not a precondition" is wrong.

#### M/s Barkataki Print and Media Services v. Union of India - Gauhati HC (single judge) - 19.09.2024
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 3585 of 2024 and analogous cases (as cited by the Patna HC in Barhonia Engicon); 2024 (9) TMI 1398 (as cited by the Telangana HC).
- Holding: Notification 56/2023-CT is ultra vires s.168A because it was issued without the mandatory prior recommendation of the GST Council, which is a "sine qua non". Ratification afterwards does not cure the defect.
- Binding: Persuasive outside Assam and the north-east.
- Later history: The Revenue's writ appeal (CBIC v. Barkataki Print, **WA/20/2026**, before Ashutosh Kumar CJ and Arun Dev Choudhury J.) was adjourned on 08.06.2026 to 26.08.2026 "in view of the pendency of" SLP (C) 4240/2025 in the Supreme Court (order read on Indian Kanoon). Outcome after that not found. The Patna HC (27.11.2024) expressly disagreed with it; the Telangana HC (02.01.2025) agreed that a Council recommendation is necessary but decided on the Covid exclusion.
- Use in orders: expect taxpayers to cite it. Answer with the Telangana rulings and the pendency in the Supreme Court.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gauhati HC WA/20/2026 order 08.06.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/59017555/); [GSTGyaan - Barkataki Print v. UoI, Gauhati HC 19.09.2024](https://www.gstgyaan.com/ms-barkataki-print-and-media-services-v-union-of-india-and-others-the-gauhati-high-court-dt-19-09-2024); [Taxo - 56/2023 ultra vires: Gauhati HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/25-09-2024-notification-56-2023-ct-was-ultra-vires-and-thus-invalid-as-the-same-was-issued-without-following-the-mandatory-recommendation-requirement-from-gst-council-gauhati-high-court/); [Gauhati HC cause list 19.02.2025](https://ghconline.gov.in/NewCList/sl-19-02-2025.pdf)
- Verified: Partly primary - the 19.09.2024 judgment itself was not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; its case number, date, single-judge status and holding are confirmed from the Patna and Telangana HC judgments, and the pending writ appeal from the Gauhati HC order of 08.06.2026 (all read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026).

#### M/s Graziano Trasmissioni v. Goods and Services Tax - Allahabad HC (DB: Saumitra Dayal Singh and Donadi Ramesh JJ.) - 31.05.2024
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 1256 of 2023 and connected petitions; Neutral Citation 2024:AHC:100568-DB; (2024) 19 Centax 82 (All.) (as cited by the Telangana HC).
- Holding: Upheld Notification 9/2023-CT (31.03.2023) and the parallel UP notification extending the s.73(10) time limit for FY 2017-18; the hearing was confined to those notifications (para 5). Read "in respect of" in s.168A widely, relying on the Supreme Court's judicial notice of force majeure up to 28.02.2022 (para 126, as quoted by the Telangana HC). Writs dismissed; pending adjudications to resume, excluding the stay period, and 45 days allowed to appeal where orders had been passed (paras 138-139). The Telangana HC records that Graziano treated the Supreme Court's suo motu limitation orders as inapplicable to GST proceedings, and disagreed on that point.
- Key words: "The writ petitions challenging the issuance of the impugned notifications must fail." (para 138)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Relied on by the Patna HC (Barhonia Engicon) and the Telangana HC (02.01.2025). The issue is pending in SLP (C) 4240/2025.
- Use in orders: supports 9/2023 (FY 2017-18 deadline). Do not cite it for 56/2023, which it did not decide.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 1256/2023, 31.05.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/179986448/); [Taxscan - Allahabad HC lists with lead case Graziano](https://www.taxscan.in/second-extension-of-time-period-for-scn-issuance-u-s-73-of-gst-act-allahabad-hc-lists-with-lead-case-graziano-trasmissioni-read-order/373430); [Taxmann - HC strikes down GST notifications (overview)](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-strikes-down-gst-notifications-curtailing-limitation-period)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, case number and neutral citation added; confirmed it decided only 9/2023 (and the parallel State notification).

#### Barhonia Engicon Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India / State of Bihar (CWJC No. 4180 of 2024 & batch) - Patna HC (DB: K. Vinod Chandran CJ and Partha Sarthy J.) - 27.11.2024 (CAV 20.11.2024) - upheld the s.168A notifications including 56/2023
- Citation / case no.: CWJC No. 4180 of 2024 and connected cases; reported as (2024) 25 Centax 55 (Pat.) (cite as given in the Telangana HC judgment). Indian Kanoon lists the judgment under 22.11.2024; the page footers read 27.11.2024.
- Holding: Refused to interfere with Notifications 13/2022, 9/2023 and 56/2023. It held that a GST Council recommendation is required, but that 56/2023 was ratified at the 52nd Council meeting, and expressly disagreed with the Gauhati HC single judge in Barkataki Print. The Supreme Court's Covid exclusion also extended limitation (paras 29-41). Orders passed without a s.75(4) personal hearing were set aside and remitted (para 47).
- Key words: "With due respect, we are unable to agree with the reasoning in the judgment of the learned Single Judge of the Gawhati High Court, which led to setting aside of Notification No. 56 of 2023." (para 29)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Relied on by the Telangana HC (02.01.2025), which agreed with the Covid-exclusion reasoning (para 115) but not with ratification curing the lack of a recommendation. Further history not checked.
- Use in orders: cite as "Patna HC (Barhonia Engicon, 27.11.2024) upheld 56/2023".
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Patna HC CWJC 4180/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/88991116/); [Delhi HC order reciting the HC split, W.P.(C) 14911/2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/75425092025CW149112025_181335.pdf); [TaxGuru - four HCs, four views](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-168a-gst-dispute-four-high-courts-four-views-limitation-extension.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **case name confirmed** (it was given in the brief but previously unconfirmed); court, bench, case number and date added.

#### Tata Play Ltd v. Union of India (W.P. No. 17184 of 2024 & batch) - Madras HC (Mohammed Shaffiq J., single judge) - 12.06.2025 - Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 held illegal
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 17184 of 2024 and a large batch; reserved 25.04.2025, pronounced 12.06.2025.
- Holding: (i) The authorities get the benefit of the Supreme Court's exclusion of 15.03.2020 to 28.02.2022 when reckoning limitation under s.73(2) and (10). (ii) Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 are illegal: they curtail the limitation otherwise available under that exclusion, rest on a misreading of the SC order, were recommended without examining relevant material, and 56/2023 was issued before any GST Council recommendation, on the GST Implementation Committee's decision, which cannot substitute for the Council. Matters were remanded: impugned orders to be treated as SCNs, with objections in 8 weeks and fresh orders after hearing (Conclusion, paras 10-12).
- Key words: "Notification Nos.9 and 56 of 2023 stands vitiated and illegal ... It results in diminishing / curtailing the limitation which was otherwise available in view of the order of the Hon'ble Supreme Court under Article 142". (Conclusion, para ii)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: not checked (presumably appealed).
- Use in orders: the strongest contrary authority on the notifications, but note it also holds the Covid exclusion applies to s.73 limitation, which is the basis on which the Telangana HC decided. Rebut with the Telangana ruling.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 17184/2024 batch, 12.06.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/196417143/); [Taxo - 16.07.2025 Madras HC strikes down GST time-extension notifications](https://taxo.online/latest-news/16-07-2025-madras-high-court-strikes-down-gst-time-extension-notifications-notifications-issued-under-section-168a-cannot-override-supreme-courts-limitation-exclusion/); [SAG Infotech - Madras HC declares s.168A notifications unconstitutional](https://blog.saginfotech.com/madras-hc-declares-gst-extension-notifications-us-168a-unconstitutional)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; lead case name, number, judge and outcome (remand) identified; holding corrected to show it is a single-judge decision that also accepted the Covid exclusion.

#### Delhi HC remand practice pending the Supreme Court - Delhi HC - 2025-2026 (several W.P.(C), e.g. 14911/2025, 19726/2025)
- Citation / case no.: e.g. W.P.(C) 19726/2025
- Holding: Since the foundation of the extended limitation is under challenge in the Supreme Court, Delhi HC sets aside or remands adjudication orders, usually for lack of hearing or service, and leaves the validity of the notifications open, subject to the Supreme Court's outcome. Punjab & Haryana HC disposed of petitions directing that the Supreme Court judgment be followed. No Bombay or Kerala HC ruling on vires was found.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: ongoing. Confirmed example: *Forever Exim India Pvt Ltd v. CBIC* (Delhi HC, 08.10.2026, order read on Indian Kanoon) recorded that the validity of 9/2023 and 56/2023 is "presently under consideration" in SLP (C) 4240/2025 and that "the ultimate judgment passed by the Supreme Court will inure to the benefit of the Petitioner, if applicable to its case" (para 8).
- Use in orders: in final orders relying on 9/2023 or 56/2023, add a line that the order is subject to the outcome of SLP (C) 4240/2025.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC, Forever Exim, 08.10.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/118006809/); [Delhi HC W.P.(C) 19726/2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/75424122025CW197262025_180243.pdf); [TaxHeal - orders remanded pending SC verdict](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=114487)
- Verified: Partly primary - the practice is confirmed by the Delhi HC order of 08.10.2026 (Forever Exim) read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; W.P.(C) 14911/2025 and 19726/2025 themselves were not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026.

### 4. Natural justice: personal hearing (s.75(4)) and service

#### M/s B.K. Steels v. Deputy State Tax Officer - Telangana HC (DB: Sujoy Paul and Namavarapu Rajeshwar Rao JJ.) - 25.07.2024 (common order in W.P. Nos. 18654 and 18766 of 2024) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 18654 and 18766 of 2024 (common order).
- Holding: Counsel for both sides admitted that no personal hearing under s.75(4) had been given before the assessment order dated 30.05.2024. **By consent**, the order was set aside and the matter restored for a personal hearing (fixed for 08.08.2024) and a fresh order; the petitioner could reply to the SCN within 7 days (paras 3-5).
- Key words: "Learned counsel for the parties fairly admitted that a right of personal hearing as envisaged under Section 75(4) ... has not been provided to the petitioner before passing Assessment Order". (para 3)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana as to its directions, but it is a consent order and lays down no principle beyond the admitted breach of s.75(4).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: issue a hearing notice with the date, time and venue even if the reply form shows "no hearing".
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 18654/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/66408885/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC (B.K. Steels)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1056255)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date (25.07.2024), bench and respondent (Deputy State Tax Officer) added; the order was passed on consensus, so it is weaker authority than the summary implied.

#### RSVCPL-CAR JV v. Commissioner of Customs and Central Tax (title as indexed on Indian Kanoon) - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 25.11.2025 - hearing under s.107(8) before rejecting a delayed appeal - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 34650 of 2025 (CGST appeal). Indian Kanoon title: "Rsvcplcar Jv vs The Commissioner Of Customs And Central ...".
- Holding: The CGST appellate authority dismissed an appeal filed 203 days late as time-barred (relying on Singh Enterprises), expressly without a personal hearing because a hearing "would not alter the facts". The appellant had filed a condonation application. Held: s.107(8) ("shall give an opportunity ... of being heard") is mandatory; the Order-in-Appeal was set aside only for that breach and remitted for a fresh order after hearing. The Court did not comment on whether the delay could be condoned (paras 3-7).
- Key words: "The language of the above provision shows that opportunity of hearing is mandatory in view of expression 'shall' used in it." (para 6)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: as appellate authority, always give a hearing (and record it) even where the appeal is plainly beyond the s.107(4) outer limit, then pass a reasoned order on limitation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 34650/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/15983005/); [TaxGuru (Telangana HC delayed appeals cluster)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1059416); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC: taxpayer may file delayed appeal against DRC-07](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-gst-taxpayer-file-delayed-appeal-against-drc-07-order.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party, bench, case number and date identified (previously "party name not found"). The case summarised by TaxGuru (State side, State did not dispute the lack of hearing) may be a different order on the same point; this CGST order is the confirmed authority.

#### M/s Shivtara Developers v. State of Uttarakhand (WPMB No. 702 of 2026, 09.09.2026) and M/s Shivtara Settering Services v. Commissioner, State Tax (WPMB No. 797 of 2026, 24.09.2026) - Uttarakhand HC (DB) - September 2026
- Citation / case no.: Shivtara Settering Services: Writ Petition (MB) No. 797 of 2026, 2026:UHC:8645-DB (Manoj Kumar Gupta CJ and Alok Mahra J.), 24.09.2026. It follows Shivtara Developers, WPMB No. 702 of 2026, judgment dated 09.09.2026 (not itself read). Both are two different petitioners, not one case with an inconsistent name.
- Holding: A s.74 UKGST order of 17.07.2025 (Rs 45.65 lakh including penalty) passed without a personal hearing was quashed. Under s.75(4), even if the assessee did not opt for a hearing, a hearing must be given when an adverse order is proposed; fresh order permitted after hearing (paras 3-6).
- Key words: "even if the assessee had not opted for personal hearing, in case, the revenue proposes to pass an adverse order, it is obligatory on its part to afford personal hearing to the assessee." (para 5)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: reinforces s.75(4).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Uttarakhand HC WPMB 797/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185703051/); [TaxGuru - Uttarakhand HC: personal hearing mandatory when adverse order contemplated](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-personal-hearing-mandatory-adverse-order-contemplated-uttarakhand-hc.html); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=142220)
- Verified: Primary for Shivtara Settering Services (read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026). **Corrected:** the two names are two separate cases (Developers, 09.09.2026; Settering Services, 24.09.2026); case numbers and bench added.

#### Gateway Exim - Gujarat HC (DB, 03.12.2025); Raymond Lifestyle - Patna HC; Pooja Exports - P&H HC; Bharat Mint & Allied Chemicals - Allahabad HC; Goutam Bhowmik - Calcutta HC - various dates (2024-2025)
- Citation / case no.: Gateway Exim v. State of Gujarat: R/SCA No. 7183 of 2025, DB (A.S. Supehia and Pranav Trivedi JJ.), 03.12.2025 (read). Others: not found (Patna: CWJC 15354 of 2024 appears in a TaxHeal URL for a s.75(4) case, but the link to Raymond is not confirmed). Note: Patna HC in Barhonia Engicon (27.11.2024, file 03 s.3) also remitted orders passed without a s.75(4) hearing.
- Holding:
  - A hearing notice must state the date, time and venue (Gujarat).
  - "N.A." in the hearing column, or no hearing date, is not a hearing (Calcutta, Allahabad).
  - An order passed without a hearing must be set aside and remitted (Patna, P&H).
  - MP HC (DB): a hearing is needed whenever an adverse order is passed, whether or not a reply was filed.
  - Madras HC: a written reply and a personal hearing are cumulative requirements, not alternatives.
- Key words: "they are required to intimate the date, time and venue of personal hearing." (Gateway Exim, para 10)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: use the standard "personal hearing" paragraph, recording the date(s) offered and whether the taxpayer attended.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC Gateway Exim, 03.12.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/130706825/); [TaxHeal - Patna CWJC 15354 of 2024](https://www.taxheal.com/civil-writ-jurisdiction-case-no-15354-of-2024.html); [StudyCafe - HC quashes GST orders for lack of personal hearing](https://studycafe.in/high-court-quashes-gst-orders-for-lack-of-personal-hearing-directs-fresh-adjudication-with-proper-notice-402380.html)
- Verified: Gateway Exim: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (orders of 28.08.2024 and the appeal order of 06.03.2025 quashed because no date and time of hearing was intimated; a separate SCN is not needed, but the date, time and venue must be; if the assessee then fails to appear the officer may decide (paras 10-11)). Bharat Mint and Goutam Bhowmik: not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026. Others: secondary.

#### M/s ACE Cardiopathy Solutions Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Sanjeev Sachdeva and Ravinder Dudeja JJ.) - 10.05.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 6758 of 2024; neutral citation 2024:DHC:4108-DB (as reported; not shown on the Indian Kanoon copy).
- Holding: A s.73 order of 13.03.2024 was passed ex parte because the SCN of 08.12.2023 had been placed under the "Additional Notices and Orders" tab, which the taxpayer missed. Relying on Madras HC decisions (including Murugesan Jayalakshmi, 08.02.2024, which noted the portal had since been redesigned to put both tabs under one heading), the order was set aside; the portal was to be opened for a reply within 30 days, with a personal hearing and fresh adjudication (paras 5-10). The remedy was remand, not quashing of the SCN.
- Key words: "petitioner has made out a case that petitioner has missed out the receipt of the notice and accordingly could not respondent to the Show Cause Notice." (para 8)
- Binding: Persuasive outside Delhi.
- Later history: followed in many cases (Kamla Vohra; Neelgiri Machinery 2025; W.P.(C) 10696, 10697, 10704 of 2026). In some cases the remedy was remand rather than quashing.
- Use in orders: where the taxpayer has not responded, serve by email or post as well under s.169, and record the mode of service in the order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 6758/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/10498603/); [TaxGuru - Delhi HC voids tax orders over portal notice glitch](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/delhi-hc-voids-tax-orders-gst-portal-notice-glitch.html); [TaxHeal - Delhi HC quashes ex parte orders over "hidden" Additional Notices tab (2026)](https://www.taxheal.com/10696-10697-and-10704-of-2026.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and bench added. **Corrected:** the statement that the department told the court the tab had been moved is not in this order; the redesign was noted from the Madras HC's Murugesan Jayalakshmi decision.

#### Neelgiri Machinery through its Proprietor Mr. Anil Kumar v. Commissioner, Delhi Goods and Service Tax - Delhi HC (DB: Prathiba M. Singh and Rajneesh Kumar Gupta JJ.) - 20.03.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 13727/2024 (2018-19) and W.P.(C) 13752/2024 (2017-18), common order dated 20.03.2025
- Holding: SCNs were uploaded only under the "Additional Notices and Orders" tab, and the department conceded the portal looks different on the taxpayer's side. The demand orders were set aside and the matter remanded: reply within 30 days, and hearing notices to be emailed as well as uploaded (paras 5-7).
- Key words: "The hearing notices shall now not be merely uploaded on the portal but shall also be e-mailed to the Petitioner" (para 7)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: same as ACE Cardiopathy.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 13727/2024 (20.03.2025)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/82998893/); [Taxscan judgment copy - Neelgiri Machinery](https://images.taxscan.in/feeds/2025/07/31/2071250-neelgirimachinerywatermark1.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected:** date is 20.03.2025 (not July 2025, which was the Taxscan upload date); DB bench and case numbers added; relief was set-aside with remand, not outright quashing.

### 5. Order cannot exceed the SCN (s.75(7))

#### M/s Vrinda Automation v. State of Uttar Pradesh and another - Allahabad HC (DB: Arun Bhansali CJ and Kshitij Shailendra J.) - 14.05.2025
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 2006 of 2025; 2025:AHC:79678-DB
- Holding: An order raising Rs 1,34,94,294 on a s.74 SCN that proposed Rs 66,13,874.78 (tax and penalty, plus interest at 18%) was ex facie contrary to s.75(7). The order was quashed and the matter remanded for a reply and a fresh order after hearing (paras 8-10).
- Key words: "the amount of penalty and interest thereon is beyond the show cause notice, which is ex facie contrary to the provisions of Section 75(7) of the Act" (para 8)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: the confirmed demand, item by item, must stay within the SCN.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 2006/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/57546551/); [TaxGuru - Allahabad HC quashes demand exceeding SCN](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/allahabad-hc-quashes-gst-demand-order-exceeding-show-notice.html); [TaxHeal - Demand quashed for exceeding SCN](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=112206)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number, neutral citation and bench added; **corrected:** relief was quash with remand (not plain quashing), and the court did not use the word "mandatory".

#### Sanjay Construction v. State of U.P. - Allahabad HC - February 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: An interest demand not quantified in the SCN was quashed. Section 75(9) (interest payable even if not specified in the order) does not save an SCN that never proposed the interest.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: quantify the interest, or at least propose it, in the SCN.
- Source: [TaxGuru (Allahabad HC s.75(7) cases)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1059925)
- Verified: Secondary - single source. (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### M/s Prestige Nottinghill Investments v. Union of India - Karnataka HC (S.R. Krishna Kumar J., single judge) - 13.10.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 18888 of 2025 (T-RES); NC: 2025:KHC:40502
- Holding: Confirming Rs 6,93,77,821 when the SCN dated 28.05.2024 demanded only Rs 2,49,63,816 is contrary to s.75(7). The order-in-original and the order rejecting rectification were set aside and the matter remitted for fresh decision, with liberty to file further replies (paras 6-7).
- Key words: "the respondents proceeded to confirm the demand for a sum of Rs.6,93,77,821/- which is contrary to the provisions contained in Section 75(7)" (para 6)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: the confirmed amount must stay within the SCN. If more is found due, issue a fresh SCN within limitation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Karnataka HC W.P. 18888/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/100637964/); [TaxHeal - 18888 of 2025](https://www.taxheal.com/18888-of-2025.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name, date (13.10.2025, not November), bench and neutral citation added; **corrected:** the court decided only on the amount exceeding the SCN, and made no finding on "new legal grounds"; relief was set-aside with remand.

### 6. Section 74 suppression, and conversion between s.74 and s.73

#### G.R. Infra Projects Ltd., Ratlam v. State of Madhya Pradesh - **Supreme Court** (J.B. Pardiwala and K. Vinod Chandran JJ.) - 19.08.2026
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 11277 of 2026 (arising from SLP (C) No. 33594 of 2025). A short signed order (reported on Indian Kanoon under "Supreme Court - Daily Orders"), under the CGST Act read with the MPGST Act.
- Holding:
  - The allegations supporting the extended period under s.74 must emanate from the SCN itself; the Court refused to look at the State's counter-affidavit, since a notice's validity cannot be supplanted by an affidavit (para 5).
  - A bland statement of "fraud or concealment of facts", without saying how fraud was inferred or concealment detected, is not enough; the "or" showed the officer was not sure of the ground (para 10).
  - The SCN dated 13.06.2025 for 2018-19 was beyond the s.73 period, so s.74 had to be independently satisfied (paras 6-8).
  - The MP HC order and the SCN were set aside and the State told not to proceed on it (para 11).
- Key words: "It cannot be a mechanical use of the words 'fraud, willful misstatement or suppression of facts' without listing out the aspects which persuades the assessing officer to conclude...". (para 10)
- Binding: **Binding on all authorities (Art. 141).**
- Later history: an interim stay had been granted in Nov 2025. None after the judgment.
- Use in orders: every s.74/74A(5) SCN must plead the specific facts of fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression, the evidence relied on and the intent. Avoid "fraud or suppression" boilerplate.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 19.08.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109494851/); [TaxGuru - SC quashes vague s.74 SCN](https://taxguru.in/?p=1072800); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1072288); [Taxo - SC strikes down SCN for mechanical use of 'fraud'](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/supreme-court-strikes-down-gst-show-cause-notice-for-mechanical-use-of-fraud/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (it is a short order, not a full reported judgment; the Rs 1.52 crore figure is not in the order text).

#### Neeyamo Enterprise Solutions Pvt. Ltd. v. Commercial Tax Officer, Madurai - Madras HC, Madurai Bench (G.R. Swaminathan J., single judge) - 11.11.2025 - extended limitation strictly conditional
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) Nos. 30453 to 30458 of 2024
- Holding: Non-payment, short payment or even evasion is not enough for s.74: it must be "by reason of" fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression. As neither the SCNs nor the orders charged those elements, they were quashed outright without remand (absence of jurisdictional fact); liberty to proceed under s.73 if available (paras 12, 15, 17).
- Key words: "non- payment or short payment of tax or even evasion of tax by itself is not sufficient. They must be by reason of the elements specifically set out in Section 74." (para 12)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: consistent with G.R. Infra (SC, 2026). The judge declined to follow K. Kumaresh Babu J. (W.P.(MD) 28502/2022, 28.01.2025) and the remand approach in W.P. 3458/2024, so single-bench views in the Madras HC differ.
- Use in orders: the SCN must itself charge fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression with the supporting evidence, and the order must give a reasoned finding on it. Avoid the word "determined" in an SCN; "specify" the amount (para 16).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P.(MD) 30453/2024 batch](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185929718/); [Taxo - 17.11.2025 extended limitation strictly conditional on fraud/suppression](https://taxo.online/latest-news/17-11-2025-extended-limitation-provisions-in-gst-law-are-strictly-conditional-on-fraud-suppression-or-wilful-misstatement-mere-delay-short-payment-or-mechanical-invocation-of-section-74-cannot-s/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party, date, case numbers and judge identified (matched to the Taxo report of 17.11.2025 by subject and date); holding refined (quashed without remand; liberty under s.73).

#### R B Pandey and Sons v. Assistant Commissioner, Central CGST and Central Excise, Division IX (Ankleshwar) - Gujarat HC (DB: A.S. Supehia and Pranav Trivedi JJ.) - 13.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 8054 of 2025 (oral order)
- Holding: The taxpayer sought conversion of a s.74 order into a s.73 order, to claim s.128A waiver. The court refused: the taxpayer admitted it had no supporting documents, and the adjudicating authority had recorded categorical findings of deliberate availment of ineligible ITC. The petition was rejected (paras 4-5, 9-11).
- Key words: "In the absence of documents such as GSTR-3B, GSTR-2A, or other supporting records, the adjudicating authority rightly proceeded under Section 74 of the CGST Act." (para 8)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: record specific findings on fraud. Conversion under s.75(2) is not automatic.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 8054/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/31605658/); [TaxGuru - s.74/73 conversion: judicial position](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-74-73-gst-conversion-limitation-penalty-judicial-position.html); [Taxmann - HC rejects plea to convert s.74 order to s.73](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-rejects-plea-to-convert-section-74-order-to-section-73/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; respondent, date, bench and case number confirmed; summary confirmed (purpose of conversion, s.128A benefit, added).

#### Sterling & Wilson v. Commissioner, Odisha - GSTAT - date not found (2026)
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: The appellate authority cannot convert s.74 proceedings into s.73 proceedings. The ruling is said to be silent on limitation after conversion.
- Binding: Tribunal; persuasive for departmental officers.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: if fraud is not proved, the original adjudicating authority should re-determine under s.75(2), checking s.73 limitation. Circulars 185/17/2022 and 254/11/2025 should be checked; they were not verified here.
- Source: [Mondaq - Appellate authority cannot convert s.74 into s.73: GSTAT](https://webiis10.mondaq.com/india/tax-authorities/1783520/appellate-authority-cannot-convert-section-74-proceedings-into-section-73-gstat)
- Verified: Secondary - single source. (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026); GSTAT orders are not indexed there; date and citation remain unverified

### 7. Waiver of interest and penalty (s.128A)

#### Incompressible Fluid Control System v. Assistant Commissioner, Thiruvanmiyur South III (batch) - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J., single judge) - 28.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21378 of 2026 and batch (28 writ petitions); 2026:MHC:3296
- Holding:
  - Initiation of s.73 proceedings is a sine qua non for a s.128A application. Once the department determines liability under s.73, including self-assessed tax, the waiver is available; if it recovers directly under ss.75(12)/79 without s.73, no application lies (para 38(i)-(ii)).
  - Circular 238/32/2024 cannot curtail s.128A (paras 29, 38(vi)).
  - Once SPL-05 approval issues, it cannot be declared void under s.161 "or otherwise" except within Rule 164(16)/(17); deemed approval applies if no order is passed within Rule 164(13) (para 38(iii)-(iv)).
  - Rule 164 time limits, including sub-rule (6), are mandatory, but substantial compliance can be shown in a proper case (paras 34, 37, 38(vii)).
  - 20 petitions were allowed; 8 were dismissed (para 43).
- Key words: "The initiation of proceedings under Section 73 of applicable GST enactments is a sine qua non for filing an application for waiver." (para 38(i))
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found. The court expressly disagreed with the Karnataka HC in Sri Lakshmi Borewell and Akshaya Borewells on the time limit (para 31).
- Use in orders: decide SPL applications strictly on the s.128A and Rule 164 conditions; do not reject on Circular 238 alone; do not cancel an SPL-05 approval under s.161.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 21378/2026 batch (28.07.2026)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/45893232/); [TaxGuru - Madras HC: GST amnesty waiver covers assessed tax](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/madras-hc-gst-amnesty-waiver-assessed-tax-section-128a.html); [Jurishour - GST amnesty waiver covers self-assessed tax](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/gst-amnesty-waiver-covers-self-assessed-tax/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number, neutral citation and judge added; summary confirmed, with the Circular 238, substantial-compliance and disagreement-with-Karnataka points added.

#### A. Gurusamy (Proprietor, Tvl Best Lab Instruments Furnitures) v. State Tax Officer (ST), Padi Assessment Circle - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J., single judge) - 16.06.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21488 of 2026 (FY 2018-19)
- Holding: A s.128A rejection on the ground that ITC was taken from non-existent taxpayers, cancelled dealers and return defaulters was set aside. If that were so, proceedings should have been under s.74, but they were actually under s.73. Fresh order on the waiver application within 30 days (paras 5-6).
- Key words: "In such event, proceedings should have been initiated under Section 74 of applicable GST enactments, whereas the record shows that proceedings were initiated under Section 73." (para 5)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: reject only on grounds the statute lists, and say which condition fails.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 21488/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/155658442/); [Taxo - 25.06.2026 s.128A benefit tested strictly on statutory conditions](https://taxo.online/latest-news/25-06-2026-benefit-of-section-128a-must-be-tested-strictly-on-statutory-conditions-and-cannot-be-denied-on-extraneous-grounds-not-contemplated-by-the-provisionmadras-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected:** date is 16.06.2026 (25.06.2026 was the date of the Taxo report); party, respondent, case number and judge added; summary otherwise confirmed (the court's reasoning is that the proceedings were under s.73, not that the statute lists disqualifications).

#### M/s Sri Laxmi Borewell Agencies v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax, Hosapete - Karnataka HC, Dharwad Bench (Jyoti M. J., single judge) - 17.04.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 102773 of 2026 (T-RES); NC: 2026:KHC-D:5680; reported as 2026 (5) TMI 122. Companion case: M/s Akshaya Borewells v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax (same date)
- Holding: Rule 164(6) uses "may", so the three-month window for a waiver application (SPL-02 filed 18.07.2025, after the 30.06.2025 deadline) is directory. A rejection solely for delay was quashed, and the CGST authority was directed to consider the application on merits (para 5-6).
- Key words: "The provision in question uses the word 'may,' which is enabling and directory in nature, rather than the mandatory 'shall.'" (para 5)
- Binding: Persuasive. It conflicts with the Madras HC in Incompressible Fluid Control (28.07.2026), which expressly disagreed with it and held the Rule 164 time limits mandatory.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: in Telangana there is no ruling either way. Record the reasons if rejecting on time grounds.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Karnataka HC W.P. 102773/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/21165955/); [TaxGuru (s.128A rulings)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1053211); [TaxTMI article](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=16790)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number, respondent (a CGST authority), judge and neutral citation added; summary confirmed; Madras disagreement recorded.
