### 8. Appeals: s.107 limitation, condonation, writs

#### Assistant Commissioner (CT) LTU, Kakinada v. GlaxoSmithKline Consumer Health Care Ltd. - **Supreme Court** (A.M. Khanwilkar and Dinesh Maheshwari JJ.) - 06.05.2020
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 2413 of 2020 (arising from SLP (C) No. 12892 of 2019); AIR 2020 SC 2819; (2020) 19 SCC 681 (SCC cite as cited by the Telangana HC in Sri Sai Krupa Traders, 02.04.2026). An **AP VAT Act, 2005 (s.31)** case decided in **2020**, not a 2024 Central Excise case.
- Holding: Where the statutory appeal period (60 days under s.31 AP VAT Act, plus the condonable period) has expired, the High Court cannot disregard it and entertain a writ under Art. 226 against the assessment order as a matter of course; that would make the statutory scheme otiose. A writ filed within the appeal period on grounds of lack of jurisdiction or breach of natural justice may still be entertained (para 15).
- Key words: "if the writ petitioner choses to approach the High Court after expiry of the maximum limitation period of 60 days prescribed under Section 31 of the 2005 Act, the High Court cannot disregard the statutory period". (para 15)
- Binding: **Binding (Art. 141).**
- Later history: applied to GST by many HCs (e.g. Gujarat HC 2026). The Telangana HC has noted it but often still permits a delayed appeal with a condonation application and pre-deposit.
- Use in orders: cite when opposing writs filed after the appeal period, and when rejecting appeals beyond 3 + 1 months.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment 06.05.2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/87842451/); [Legal500 - To condone delay or not](https://www.legal500.com/developments/?p=27319); [TaxHeal - writ refused: HC cannot condone delay beyond GST statutory limits](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=116320)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case number and AIR citation added; holding confirmed and the in-time-writ qualification added.

#### Singh Enterprises v. Commissioner of Central Excise, Jamshedpur - **Supreme Court** (Arijit Pasayat and Aftab Alam JJ.) - 14.12.2007 (Central Excise)
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 5949 of 2007 (arising from SLP (C) No. 4720 of 2006), from the Jharkhand HC.
- Holding: Under the proviso to s.35(1) Central Excise Act (60 days + 30 days), the Commissioner (Appeals), a creature of statute, has no power to condone delay beyond the further 30 days; s.5 of the Limitation Act is completely excluded (para 8). HCs apply this to s.107(4) CGST Act: the outer limit is 3 months + 1 month.
- Key words: "the appellate authority has no power to allow the appeal to be presented beyond the period of 30 days... Therefore, there is complete exclusion of Section 5 of the Limitation Act." (para 8)
- Binding: Binding (Art. 141) as to the principle.
- Later history: in GST, the Supreme Court on **30.08.2024** stayed an HC judgment holding that s.5 of the Limitation Act applies to s.107. In **July/Aug 2026** (orders dated 30.07.2026 and 01.08.2026) it issued notice on SLPs about appeals filed beyond the s.107(4) cap. Pending.
- Use in orders: the appellate authority should not condone delay beyond one month after the 3-month period. Note the pending Supreme Court SLPs.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment 14.12.2007](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/819947/); [TaxHeal - SC stays HC order on limitation for GST appeals](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=99907); [TaxHeal - SC issues notice on dismissal beyond s.107(4) cap](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=137501)
- Verified: Primary for the 2007 judgment - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date corrected from "2008" to 14.12.2007, case number and bench added. The 2024/2026 GST SLP history in "Later history" is still secondary.

#### Telangana HC - delayed appeals against DRC-07 orders - 2026 (party names not found) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: Confirmed example: M/s Sri Sai Krupa Traders v. State of Telangana, W.P. No. 9689 of 2026, DB (CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.), 02.04.2026. Other cases in this entry: not found.
- Holding:
  - **Sri Sai Krupa Traders (02.04.2026, read):** writ filed on 31.03.2026 against 2023-24 orders, pleading the "Additional Notices" tab and no hearing. The State relied on GlaxoSmithKline. The HC refused to entertain the FY 2017-18 challenge as grossly belated, but gave liberty to appeal the FY 2018-19 orders within two weeks with a condonation application and pre-deposit; the appellate authority, if satisfied with the reasons, must decide on merits (paras 2-7).
  - In a May 2026 case (secondary), the HC declined to interfere on the merits of a 2023 s.73 order and allowed a s.107 appeal with a condonation application.
  - A chartered accountant's lapse could be "sufficient cause" for condonation (secondary).
- Key words: "Needless to say, the appellate authority would consider the question of delay and if he is satisfied with the reasons explained in the delay condonation application, he shall decide the case on merits." (Sri Sai Krupa Traders, para 7)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana as to the directions given.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: when such directions are issued, the appellate authority must consider condonation on the merits and give a hearing.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Sri Sai Krupa Traders, W.P. 9689/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/75589151/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC: taxpayer may file delayed appeal against DRC-07](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-gst-taxpayer-file-delayed-appeal-against-drc-07-order.html); [TaxHeal - delayed writ disallowed, assessee relegated to s.107 appeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=130643)
- Verified: Primary for Sri Sai Krupa Traders (read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; it is a State-tax case, so the earlier "CBIC relied on GlaxoSmithKline" description may refer to another order). The other bullets remain secondary.

#### Radha Krishan Industries v. State of Himachal Pradesh - **Supreme Court** (D.Y. Chandrachud and M.R. Shah JJ.) - 20.04.2021
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 1155 of 2021 (arising from SLP (C) No. 1688 of 2021); AIR 2021 SC 2114; (2021) 3 SCR 406 (per the digiscr link). Arose under the HPGST Act.
- Holding:
  - A Joint Commissioner ordering provisional attachment acts as the Commissioner's delegate under s.5(3), so no s.107(1) appeal lay and the writ was maintainable; the HC erred in dismissing it (para 72(i)-(iii)).
  - The s.83 power is "draconian"; the Commissioner must form an opinion on tangible material that the assessee is likely to defeat the demand and that attachment is necessary to protect revenue (para 72(iv)-(vii)). It is not to be used to harass or cause irreversible harm to the business.
  - Rule 159(5) gives two mandatory safeguards: objections and a personal hearing; the objections must be decided by a reasoned order (para 72(ix)-(x)).
  - Once a final s.74(9) order is passed, the attachment ends; on appeal, s.107(6)-(7) govern stay of recovery (para 72(xi)-(xii)).
  - Alternative remedy is a rule of discretion; exceptions are enforcement of a fundamental right, breach of natural justice, proceedings wholly without jurisdiction, or a vires challenge (para 27(iii)).
  - Attachment orders dated 28.10.2020 set aside (para 74).
- Key words: "Before ordering a provisional attachment the Commissioner must form an opinion on the basis of tangible material that the assessee is likely to defeat the demand, if any". (para 72(v))
- Binding: **Binding (Art. 141).**
- Later history: widely followed (e.g. Allahabad HC 2025).
- Use in orders: a s.83 order must record the material and reasons. Expect writs to be entertained for breach of natural justice despite an appeal remedy.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment 20.04.2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/62362537/); [SC judgment PDF (api.sci.gov.in)](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2021/1775/1775_2021_36_1502_27668_Judgement_20-Apr-2021.pdf); [ITATOnline digest](https://itatonline.org/digest/verdicts/radha-krishan-industries-vs-state-of-himachal-pradesh-orssupreme-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; the exceptions list (para 27) and the Rule 159(5) hearing and end-of-attachment findings (para 72) added.

### 9. GSTAT: constitution, operationalisation, deadlines

#### Revenue Bar Association v. Union of India - Madras HC (S. Manikumar and Subramonium Prasad JJ.) - 20.09.2019
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 21147, 21148 and 14919 of 2018; AIRONLINE 2019 MAD 794; (2019) 4 Mad LW 689
- Holding: Struck down s.110(1)(b)(iii) (Indian Legal Service officer as Judicial Member) and s.109(3) and (9) (one judicial and two technical members per bench): expert members cannot outnumber judicial members. The plea that excluding lawyers made ss.109-110 ultra vires was **rejected**; the court only recommended that Parliament consider making lawyers eligible (para 106). The October 2019 interim order on technical-member appointments is secondary only.
- Key words: "The number of expert members therefore cannot exceed the number of judicial members on the bench." (para 105)
- Binding: Persuasive (largely historical).
- Later history: no 2025 Madras HC judgment on this was found. Legislative amendments to s.109/110 were not verified in this search (see Gaps). GSTAT has since been constituted. Justice (Retd.) Sanjaya Kumar Mishra was sworn in as President on 06.05.2024, and the Tribunal was formally commissioned on **24.09.2025**. The Chennai Bench was reported operational from 22.01.2026. Bench numbers vary across sources (31 State Benches vs 32 benches/45 locations).
- Use in orders: background only.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 21147/2018 batch (20.09.2019)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/97389181/); [Bar & Bench - composition of GSTAT unconstitutional: Madras HC](https://barandbench.com/composition-of-gstat-is-unconstitutional-madras-high-court/); [Bar & Bench - injunction on technical members](https://www.barandbench.com/news/madras-hc-injunction-technical-members-gstat); [Grant Thornton - GSTAT falls in place](https://www.grantthornton.in/insights/articles/gstat-the-missing-piece-in-indias-gst-framework-finally-falls-in-place/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers and citations added; **corrected:** the court did not hold that advocates cannot be excluded - it rejected that challenge and made only a recommendation (para 106(iii)). GSTAT operational facts in Later history remain secondary.

**GSTAT filing deadlines (notifications, not cases):**
- S.O. 4220(E), 17.09.2025: appeals against orders communicated before 01.04.2026 to be filed by 30.06.2026.
- Notification of 30.06.2026: the deadline was extended to **31.07.2026** for appellate orders communicated before **01.05.2026**, and for departmental applications where the order was passed before 01.02.2026. Sources differ on the 01.04 vs 01.05 cutoff, so check the gazette.
- Later orders: normal s.112 limitation (3 months).
- No further extension beyond 31.07.2026 was found.
- Sources: [NewsOnAir - GSTAT appeal deadline extended to 31 July](https://newsonair.gov.in/government-extends-gstat-appeal-filing-deadline-till-31-july/); [Lexplosion - extension of time limit for GSTAT appeals](https://lexplosion.in/extension-of-time-limit-for-filing-appeals-before-gstat/)

### 10. Provisional attachment (s.83) and recovery (ss.78/79)

#### Kesari Nandan Mobile v. Office of Assistant Commissioner of State Tax (2), Enforcement Division-5 - **Supreme Court** (Dipankar Datta and Augustine George Masih JJ.) - 14.08.2025
- Citation / case no.: 2025 INSC 983; Civil Appeal No. 9543 of 2025, from Gujarat HC (DB) order of 29.01.2025 in SCA No. 16339 of 2024.
- Holding: A provisional attachment under s.83 ceases by operation of law one year after issue (s.83(2)), and the CGST Act gives no power to issue a second attachment order, on substantially the same grounds, after the first lapses. Fresh orders of 13.11.2024 and 18.12.2024 issued after the 2023 orders lapsed were set aside and the bank accounts de-frozen (paras 14, 30-31, 41). The investigation itself could continue (para 43).
- Key words: "fresh issuance of a provisional attachment order premised on substantially the same grounds as the earlier one would be in disregard to the safeguard provided in sub-section (2)." (para 31)
- Binding: **Binding (Art. 141).**
- Later history: none found. Followed by HCs, including a reported decision setting aside attachments dated 11.11.2024 that had outlived one year, and rejecting the argument that pending proceedings keep an attachment alive.
- Use in orders: diarise the one-year expiry. Do not ask banks to continue a freeze after that; a fresh order needs a fresh basis.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment 14.08.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/173193457/); [TaxGuru - SC: provisional attachment lapses after one year](https://taxguru.in/?p=1058307); [TaxHeal - bank attachment ceases after one year under s.83(2)](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=111768)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; full party name, date, bench and case number added; holding sharpened: the actual question was a *second* attachment after the first lapsed (the Gujarat HC had allowed it).

#### M/s Krish Overseas v. Commissioner Central Tax, Delhi West - Delhi HC (DB: Sanjeev Sachdeva and Ravinder Dudeja JJ.) - 16.04.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 5407/2024; 2024:DHC:3159-DB
- Holding: A s.83 attachment of a bank account dated 14.08.2019 ceased to have effect after one year under s.83(2), and HDFC Bank could not keep restraining the account on that order. This was without prejudice to any other attachment order communicated to the bank (paras 6-7).
- Key words: "In view of Section 83 (2) of the Act, the life of an order of provisional attachment is only one year." (para 6)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: consistent with Kesari Nandan (SC 2025).
- Use in orders: same as Kesari Nandan.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 5407/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/174110423/); [Verdictum - Provisional attachment expires after one year: Delhi HC](https://verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/ms-krish-overseas-v-commissioner-central-tax-delhi-west-ors-2024-dhc-3159-db-delhi-high-court-1533497)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and bench added; summary confirmed (the without-prejudice clarification added).

#### Tvl. Cargotec India Pvt. Ltd. v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Manali Assessment Circle - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J., single judge) - 23.04.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 13104 of 2023
- Holding: Assessment orders were dated 28.12.2022, and amounts were debited from the cash and credit ledgers in February 2023, before three months had passed. The s.78 proviso can be used only if the proper officer records written reasons; none were shown. The department was directed to refund or re-credit within one month (paras 5-8).
- Key words: "The proviso to Section 78 ... may be invoked only if the proper officer records in writing the reason as to why he considers it expedient in the interest of the revenue" (para 5)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found. A 2026 HC ruling (court not named) similarly held that debiting the Electronic Cash Ledger on the same day as the demand order violated s.78.
- Use in orders: any early recovery must have a recorded, specific reason (e.g. risk of the taxpayer absconding or alienating assets) on file before the DRC-13 or ledger debit.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 13104/2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198562665/); [Taxo - 22.05.2024 recovery before 3 months not justified: Madras HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/22-05-2024-recovery-proceeding-u-s-78-before-expiry-of-statutory-period-for-filing-appeal-i-e-three-months-not-justified-where-department-failed-to-acknowledged-early-recovery-actions-madras-high/); [AMLegals - recovery only if not paid within 3 months](https://amlegals.com/recovery-proceedings-can-only-be-initiated-if-amount-is-not-paid-within-3-months-from-date-of-service-of-order/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected:** date is 23.04.2024 (not May 2024, the report date); respondent, case number and judge added; summary confirmed. The "2026 HC ruling" in Later history remains secondary.

#### Liakhat Ali Mallick v. State of West Bengal - Calcutta HC (Md. Nizamuddin J., single judge) - 27.04.2023; on appeal, DB (T.S. Sivagnanam CJ and Uday Kumar J.) - 16.06.2023
- Citation / case no.: WPA 7454 of 2023 (single judge); M.A.T. No. 823 of 2023 (Division Bench)
- Holding: The department showed that reasons under the s.78 proviso had been recorded on the same day as the garnishee notice (13.02.2023). "Without going into the merits and legality of the recorded reasons", the single judge stayed the garnishee notice on condition of paying 20% of the interest (Rs 31,58,936), and directed the appeal to be decided within six weeks.
- Key words: "Without going into the merits and legality of the recorded reasons for taking recourse to proviso to Section 78 of the Act during the pendency of the appeal, I am inclined to stay the impugned garnishee order" (single judge, unnumbered)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: **Modified on appeal:** the Division Bench (16.06.2023) set aside the 20% condition, because the whole tax had been paid and s.107(6) requires only 10% of the disputed tax, and directed the appeal to be heard on merits (paras 3-5).
- Use in orders: record written reasons under the s.78 proviso before (not after) any early recovery step. The court did not rule on whether recorded reasons make early recovery valid.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Calcutta HC WPA 7454/2023 (27.04.2023)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/145958297/); [Indian Kanoon - Calcutta HC DB, M.A.T. 823/2023 (16.06.2023)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/115243060/); [Taxmann - Calcutta HC stayed garnishee order before 3 months](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/calcutta-hc-stayed-garnishee-order-passed-for-recovery-of-interest-before-expiry-of-3-months-of-filing-the-appeal)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected:** the court did not uphold the garnishee order - it stayed it without examining the reasons; the 20% condition was later set aside by the Division Bench; dates, case numbers and benches added.

#### Madras HC (party name not found) - overdraft account cannot be attached under s.79 - 03.04.2026
- Citation / case no.: not found
- Holding: Funds in an overdraft account belong to the bank, not the taxpayer, so they cannot be attached under s.79. The petitioner was allowed to file an appeal despite the expired limitation, on depositing 50% of the disputed tax.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: target current or savings balances, not OD/CC limits.
- Source: [Taxo - 03.04.2026 overdraft account cannot be attached: Madras HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/03-04-2026-overdraft-account-cannot-be-attached-as-the-amounts-in-such-account-are-essentially-funds-of-the-bank-and-not-the-petitioner-madras-high-court/); [Taxmann - attachment of OD account impermissible](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/attachment-of-overdraft-account-for-gst-recovery-impermissible-as-no-actual-funds-available-hc/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

*Related (not separate entries):*
- Calcutta and Delhi HCs held that once an appeal is filed with the pre-deposit, recovery of the balance is stayed and attachments must be lifted ([TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=106255); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/27535-of-2025.html)).
- Andhra Pradesh HC upheld a s.79 bank attachment where the demand had become final and unchallenged for over a year, and held that no prior notice to the dealer is required before a third-party notice (reported in the same search; case name not found).
- Telangana HC (2026, name not found) allowed taxpayers to pursue the statutory appeal even after recovery had begun through a garnishee notice, with limited interim protection.

---

### 11. E-way bills and detention (s.129)

#### Assistant Commissioner (ST) v. Satyam Shivam Papers Pvt. Ltd. - **Supreme Court** (Dinesh Maheshwari and Hrishikesh Roy JJ.) - 12.01.2022; arising from Telangana HC (DB: M.S. Ramachandra Rao and T. Vinod Kumar JJ.) W.P. No. 9688 of 2020 decided 02.06.2021 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: SC: SLP (C) No. 21132 of 2021. HC: 2021 SCC OnLine TS 698; (2021) 50 GSTL 459
- Holding:
  - A truck carrying paper (invoice and e-way bill dated 04.01.2020) could not deliver in time because an anti-CAA/NRC agitation blocked traffic at Basheer Bagh on 04.01.2020; 05.01.2020 was a Sunday. The goods were detained on 06.01.2020 after the e-way bill expired.
  - The Telangana HC held that no intent to evade can be presumed merely because the e-way bill lapsed; the officer had to consider the explanation. It set aside the tax and penalty of Rs 69,000 and ordered the officer to pay Rs 10,000 costs.
  - The Supreme Court found no reason to interfere, dismissed the SLP as misconceived, and enhanced the costs.
- Key words: "On account of non-extension of the validity of the e-way bill by petitioner or the auto trolley driver, no presumption can be drawn that there was an intention to evade tax". (HC para 42, quoted with approval by the SC)
- Binding: **The Telangana HC judgment binds Telangana officers.** The Supreme Court order is a dismissal of an SLP, which is not a declaration of law under Art. 141, but it ended the department's challenge.
- Later history:
  - **Confirmed from the SC order:** the Supreme Court imposed a further Rs 59,000 costs on the petitioners (over and above the HC's Rs 10,000), payable within four weeks, and allowed the State to recover the costs from the person(s) responsible for the litigation. It refused to keep the s.129 question of law open, saying the case raised no question of law.
  - Followed by Kerala HC (2022), which holds that the officer must consider the explanation for an expired e-way bill (secondary).
  - The s.129 penalty scheme was amended from 01.01.2022 (Finance Act 2021); the impact was not checked.
- Use in orders: before detaining goods or imposing a penalty under s.129, record the explanation offered and a reasoned finding on intent to evade. Do not penalise a genuine, explained lapse.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 12.01.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17589683/); [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 9688/2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/13104689/); [SupremeCourtCases - Asst. Commr. (ST) v. Satyam Shivam Papers](https://www.supremecourtcases.com/assistant-commissioner-st-and-others-v-satyam-shivam-papers-pvt-limited-and-another/); [Taxmann - HC correctly set aside demand for e-way bill expiry: SC](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-correctly-set-aside-tax-demand-penalty-for-expiry-of-e-way-bill-as-goods-not-delivered-in-time-due-to-genuine-reason-sc); [Taxo - Kerala HC on explanation for e-way bill expiry](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/the-officer-is-duty-bound-to-consider-the-explanation-provided-by-the-assessee-for-expiry-of-e-way-bill-kerala-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (SC order and HC judgment); bench of the HC added; costs enhancement confirmed (further Rs 59,000, total Rs 69,000), which was previously unverified.

#### M/s Auto Industries v. State of U.P. and 2 others - Allahabad HC (Piyush Agrawal J., single judge) - 21.11.2025
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 1985 of 2024; 2025:AHC:208900 (the TaxHeal URL number "1592 of 2025" is not the case number)
- Holding: A s.129(3) penalty for not filling Part-B of the e-way bill (a technical glitch, undisputed; goods matched the invoice) was quashed because no authority recorded any intention to evade tax. Refund within two months. Relied on the DB in Tata Hitachi Construction Machinery (Writ Tax 2148/2025, 09.05.2025), Citykart Retail and Roli Enterprises (paras 8-12). The other 2025 Allahabad rulings mentioned (wrong dispatch place, vehicle mismatch, breakdown) remain secondary.
- Key words: "none of the authorities below has recorded any finding with regard to intention to evade payment of tax" (para 8)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found. Revenue appeals not found.
- Use in orders: separate minor clerical errors from real evasion (no e-way bill, goods mismatch, under-invoicing), and record the finding.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 1985/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109890531/); [TaxHeal - penalty for missing Part-B quashed](https://www.taxheal.com/1592-of-2025.html); [LiveLaw - technical error without financial implication: Allahabad HC](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/allahabad-high-court/allahabad-high-court-ruling-tax-laws-technical-errors-gst-penalties-248007)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; **corrected:** case number is Writ Tax 1985/2024 (not 1592/2025); the authorities relied on are Tata Hitachi (DB), Citykart and Roli Enterprises (Precision Tools is not cited); date, judge and neutral citation added.

## File 04: GST Case Law 04: Arrest, Search, Summons, Prosecution, and Landmark Constitutional and Levy Rulings

**Scope.** This file covers two areas. **Part A** covers coercive powers under the CGST Act: arrest (s.69 read with s.132), summons (s.70), search and seizure (s.67, including seizure of cash), payments taken during searches, anticipatory bail and bail, the CBIC arrest and summons instructions as the courts have read them, and how the CrPC/BNSS applies. **Part B** covers landmark constitutional and levy rulings that a field officer needs when drafting orders. These are: the status of GST Council recommendations (Mohit Minerals), secondment (Northern Operating Systems), lottery and online gaming (Future Gaming, Gameskraft), royalty (Mineral Area Development Authority), clubs and mutuality (Calcutta Club, IMA v. Union of India), corporate guarantee valuation (Torrent Power), the legal weight of CBIC circulars and advance rulings, and judicial discipline (Kamlakshi Finance). Telangana High Court rulings are flagged **BINDING IN TELANGANA**.

**Date compiled:** 13 October 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries; judgment text not yet read. Verify before citing in an order.**
> The web-search budget ran out before every lead could be checked. Items still to verify are listed under "Gaps and doubts" at the end.

---

### 1. Quick answers

**A1. Are ss.69 and 70 valid, and what must an arrest file contain?**
The Supreme Court upheld ss.69 and 70 in *Radhika Agarwal v. Union of India* (27 Feb 2025, 3 judges, 2025 INSC 272, (2025) 6 SCC 545), but it attached safeguards:
- "Reasons to believe" must rest on verifiable material and be recorded in writing.
- Written grounds of arrest must be given to the arrestee.
- CrPC/BNSS safeguards apply to GST arrests.
- Tax liability does not have to be finally assessed before an arrest.

*Rakesh Kumar v. UoI* (SC, 29 Jun 2026) treats the question of validity as closed.
**For orders:** the Commissioner's file should record the material-based reasons, the s.132 threshold, and why arrest is necessary. Give written grounds as an annexure to the arrest memo, with a DIN and an acknowledgement.

**A2. Must the s.69 authorisation be communicated before arrest?**
Yes. *Union of India v. Sunil Biyani* (SC, 2026 INSC 849; decided 12 Aug 2026) held that the s.69 order must be communicated before arrest, by email or mobile number given under Rule 8 or another lawful mode, so that the person can seek anticipatory bail. Without that communication, "question of arrest would not arise."
**For orders:** do not arrest on an authorisation the person has not been served with.

**A3. Can a person who has only been summoned get anticipatory bail?**
Generally no. In *State of Gujarat v. Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer* (SC, 17 Jul 2023) and *Bharat Bhushan v. DGGI* (SC, 5 Jul 2024), the Court held that s.438 CrPC is not available at the summons stage because no FIR exists; the remedy is Article 226, used sparingly. The Telangana High Court followed this in *Vemula Yougander* (14 Nov 2023, **BINDING IN TELANGANA**).
*Radhika Agarwal* and *Sunil Biyani*, however, recognise anticipatory bail once there is a real apprehension of arrest, that is, once a s.69 order exists.
Where the alleged amount is below Rs 5 crore, the offence is non-cognizable and bailable (s.132(4)–(5)), and the SC declined to interfere with refusal of anticipatory bail (*Harish Wadhwani*, 2026).

**A4. Is a summoned person an "accused"?**
No. *Radhika Agarwal* reiterates that a s.70 summons does not make the person an accused, so Article 20(3) is not engaged at that stage.
The Telangana High Court has refused to quash s.70 summons and requires the person to appear (*Devendra Surana*, 8 Apr 2026, **BINDING IN TELANGANA**).
**For orders and summons:** give reasonable time to respond. Follow Instruction 03/2022-23 (do not summon senior management as a matter of routine).

**A5. Can arrest come before adjudication?**
Yes, if the record supports it. Authorities:
- *P.V. Ramana Reddy* (Telangana HC, 18 Apr 2019; SLP dismissed May 2019) — **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- *Vimal Yashwantgiri Goswami* (Gujarat HC final judgment, 20 Oct 2020)
- *Radhika Agarwal* (2025)

The 2016 MakeMyTrip ruling (Delhi HC: no arrest without a show cause notice) was decided under the service tax law and should not be relied on for GST.

**A6. Is tax collected during a search valid?**
Only if it is genuinely voluntary. In *Bhumi Associate* (Gujarat HC, 2021, interim), the court directed:
- no recovery during search, by any mode;
- any voluntary DRC-03 to be filed on the next day, after the officers have left.

*Vallabh Textiles* (Delhi HC, 20 Dec 2022) and *Neeraj Paper Marketing* (Delhi HC) ordered refunds of amounts deposited during or around searches, with interest. *Radhika Agarwal* also condemned coercion to pay during search.
**For orders:** never rely on a DRC-03 paid during a search as an admission. Record the voluntariness separately, and issue a DRC-04 acknowledgement.

**A7. Can cash be seized under s.67(2)?**
The majority High Court view is no; cash is "money," not goods. Supporting rulings:
- *Deepak Khandelwal* (Delhi HC, 17 Aug 2023; Revenue's SLP and review dismissed)
- Rajasthan, Karnataka, Gujarat and Kerala High Courts (reported)

The Madhya Pradesh High Court took the opposite view in *Kanishka Matta* (2020). The Supreme Court issued notice on the point in Oct 2024; no final ruling was found.
**For orders:** do not seize cash merely as "unaccounted"; return seized items not relied on in the SCN (s.67(3)).

**B1. Are GST Council recommendations binding?**
Not on Parliament or the State legislatures; they have persuasive value (*Mohit Minerals*, SC, 19 May 2022). The same case struck down IGST under reverse charge on ocean freight in CIF imports, because it amounted to double levy on a composite supply.

**B2. Is secondment of employees a taxable supply?**
In *Northern Operating Systems* (SC, 2022), secondment was treated as manpower supply under the service tax law, and the reasoning is used in GST cases. CBIC Instruction 05/2023-GST (13 Dec 2023) tells officers not to apply it mechanically and not to invoke the extended period without suppression.
**For orders:** analyse the specific contract.

**B3. Online gaming.**
*DGGI v. Gameskraft* (SC, 27 May 2026, 2026 INSC 595) reversed the Karnataka High Court:
- operators supply actionable claims (betting), and are not intermediaries;
- the whole amount staked is taxable (no deduction for prize pools), valued under Rules 31B/31C, which the Court held retrospective; the SCNs applied 18% to 24.01.2018 and 28% after;
- the 2023 amendments are clarificatory;
- the show cause notices are restored for adjudication.

The rate is 40% from 22 Sep 2025.

**B4. Lottery.**
*Union v. Future Gaming Solutions* (SC, Feb 2025): no service tax on lottery distributors, because lottery is "betting and gambling," which only the States may tax (Entry 62, List II). The ruling concerns the pre-GST Finance Act, 1994 period.

**B5. Royalty.**
*Mineral Area Development Authority* (SC, 9 judges, 25 Jul 2024, 8:1): royalty is not a tax. This removes the argument that "GST cannot be levied on a tax." The separate question of whether royalty is a taxable supply is still being litigated; a Telangana High Court writ was at notice stage in Nov 2025. No final Supreme Court ruling on GST on royalty was found.

**B6. Clubs and associations.**
- *Calcutta Club* (SC, 3 Oct 2019): mutuality applies, so there is no sales tax (and, per summaries, no service tax) on club supplies to members.
- *IMA v. UoI* (Kerala HC Division Bench, 11 Apr 2025): s.7(1)(aa) and s.2(17)(e) CGST struck down as unconstitutional.
- The SC issued notice on the Union's appeal (25 Jul [2025]) with "no recovery steps against IMA."

The Kerala ruling is persuasive in Telangana, not binding, but expect it to be cited.

**B7. Corporate guarantee.**
*Torrent Power v. UoI* (Gujarat HC, 14 Aug 2026):
- Rule 28(2) is valid but read down; where actual consideration exists, it is taxed even if below 1%;
- the 1% value applies where the guarantee is gratuitous or its value cannot be determined;
- no levy for guarantees given before 26 Oct 2023;
- value is based on the subsisting guarantee balance.

The ruling is persuasive in Telangana.

**B8. Circulars, advance rulings and judicial discipline.**
- CBIC circulars bind officers but not courts or taxpayers. A circular contrary to an SC or HC ruling should not be enforced (*Ratan Melting & Wire*, SC Constitution Bench, 2008; CBEC Circular 1006/13/2015-CX).
- Advance rulings bind only the applicant and its jurisdictional officer (s.103).
- Lower authorities must follow orders of the appellate authorities and the jurisdictional High Court (*UoI v. Kamlakshi Finance*, SC).

**For orders:** follow Telangana High Court decisions even if a circular says otherwise. Do not rely on another taxpayer's advance ruling as binding (an HC set aside an SCN that did this).

---

### 2. Part A: Arrest, search, summons and prosecution

#### A-I. Validity of arrest and summons powers, and the safeguards

#### Radhika Agarwal v. Union of India - Supreme Court (3 judges) - 27 Feb 2025
- Citation / case no.: 2025 INSC 272; (2025) 6 SCC 545; [2025] 2 S.C.R. 1331; 2025 SCC OnLine SC 449. Lead case W.P.(Crl.) No. 336 of 2018 with connected criminal appeals and writ petitions. Three judges: Sanjiv Khanna CJI (main judgment, joined by M.M. Sundresh J) and Bela M. Trivedi J (separate concurring opinion).
- Holding:
  - Upheld ss.69 and 70 of the CGST Act and the Customs Act arrest powers.
  - "Reasons to believe" must rest on verifiable material and statutory monetary thresholds, not mere suspicion. Final assessment of liability is not a precondition.
  - Written grounds of arrest must be furnished.
  - CrPC/BNSS provisions "are equally applicable" to arrests under the GST and Customs Acts.
  - Anticipatory bail can be sought even without an FIR.
  - A person summoned under s.70 is not, merely by the summons, an accused.
  - The Court also held that s.74(5) "voluntary" payment "does not postulate payment under force, coercion or threat of arrest", that CBIC's circulars on this are binding, and that taxpayers who paid under threat, force or coercion may move the courts for refund (main judgment paras 64-68).
- Binding: Yes, Supreme Court (Art. 141). Binding on all officers.
- Later history: Followed in *Rakesh Kumar v. UoI* (SC, 29 Jun 2026) and *UoI v. Sunil Biyani* (SC, 2026). No review or reversal found.
- Use in orders: Cite for validity of ss.69/70. Arrest files must show material-based reasons recorded in writing, the s.132 threshold, written grounds, and CrPC/BNSS compliance (D.K. Basu, intimation, production before Magistrate within 24 hours).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Radhika Agarwal v. UoI, 27 Feb 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/144838233/); [TaxGuru – SC upholds arrest powers with strict safeguards](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-69-gst-sc-upholds-arrest-powers-strict-safeguards.html); [Lawfoyer – Radhika Agarwal 2025 INSC 272](https://lawfoyer.in/radhika-agarwal-v-union-of-india-and-others-2025-2-s-c-r-1331-2025-insc-272/); [Taxmann – CrPC applies to GST/Customs arrests](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/provisions-of-code-of-criminal-procedure-would-also-apply-for-search-seizure-and-arrest-under-gst-act-and-customs-act-sc); [SC Observer](https://www.scobserver.in/journal/sc-calls-for-stricter-regulation-of-warrantless-arrests-by-revenue-officers/amp/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and case number confirmed; coercion/refund point now confirmed from the text (paras 64-68 of the main judgment).
- Key words: "the reasons to believe must be explicit and refer to the material and evidence underlying such opinion. There has to be a degree of certainty to establish that the offence is committed". (para 59)

#### Rakesh Kumar v. Union of India - Supreme Court - 29 Jun 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(Crl.) No. 439 of 2024. Manmohan and K. Vinod Chandran, JJ.
- Holding: Challenge to the validity of s.69 disposed of as already settled by *Radhika Agarwal*. Interim order vacated. A short order, not a reasoned judgment.
- Binding: Supreme Court (short order applying *Radhika Agarwal*).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Cite to answer any renewed challenge to s.69's validity. The safeguards on how the power is exercised still apply.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Rakesh Kumar v. UoI, order 29 Jun 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/154767970/); [TaxHeal – validity of s.69 upheld following SC precedent](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=136116); [A2Z Taxcorp – validity conclusively settled](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/constitutional-validity-of-arrest-powers-under-section-69-of-the-cgst-act-stands-conclusively-settled-by-the-supreme-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (noted it is a short order).
- Key words: "The legal validity of the said provision has been upheld by a three Judge Bench of this Court in Radhika Agarwal ... Consequently, the present Writ Petition is disposed of in terms of the aforesaid judgment". (order)

#### Union of India v. Sunil Biyani - Supreme Court - 12 Aug 2026
- Citation / case no.: 2026 INSC 849. Criminal Appeal arising out of SLP (Crl.) No. 12535 of 2026. Dipankar Datta (author) and Sheel Nagu, JJ. Judgment dated 12 Aug 2026 (other reported dates are wrong). Arose from a Bombay High Court order of 13 Feb 2026 in ABA No. 3001/2025.
- Holding:
  - A s.69 arrest authorisation, being a "sine qua non to seek anticipatory bail," must be communicated to the person before arrest. It may be served by the email and mobile number given under Rule 8 CGST Rules or another lawful mode.
  - Without communication, "question of arrest would not arise." Communication does not obstruct investigation.
  - Once an anticipatory bail application is dismissed as not maintainable, a High Court or Sessions Court cannot grant independent interim protection. The Bombay High Court's one-week protection was set aside.
  - Rejection of pre-arrest bail at the bare summons stage was affirmed (summons alone does not make a person an accused).
  - The Court added (para 20) that the order is open to judicial review and that withholding the "reasons to believe" "could have drastic consequences on the right to liberty". The operative direction was to the Commissioner in this case, but the reasoning is general.
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Before any arrest, serve the s.69 order and keep proof of service in the file. An arrest on an uncommunicated authorisation is at serious risk.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – UoI v. Sunil Biyani, 12 Aug 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/180631499/); [Verdictum – UoI v Sunil Biyani 2026 INSC 849](https://www.verdictum.in/supreme-court/union-of-india-v-sunil-biyani-2026-insc-849-order-sec69-cgst-act-anticipatory-bail-accused-arrest-1619773); [TaxGuru – prior communication of arrest authorisation](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/no-secret-arrest-gst-supreme-court-restores-fairness-coercion.html); [Taxo – 14.08.2026 report](https://taxo.online/latest-news/14-08-2026-supreme-court-mandates-communication-of-gst-arrest-authorization-before-arrest-reinforces-right-to-seek-anticipatory-bail/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date fixed as 12 Aug 2026, SLP number added, para 20 remark on reasons added.
- Key words: "we require the Commissioner to communicate the order under Section 69 of the GST Act to the respondent ... We reiterate, without such communication, question of arrest would not arise." (para 22)

#### P.V. Ramana Reddy v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - 18 Apr 2019 — **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 4764, 4769, 4892, 5074, 5130, 5329, 6952 and 7583 of 2019 (batch), decided 18.04.2019 (the 2020 date in one commentary is an error). Division Bench: V. Ramasubramanian and P. Keshava Rao, JJ. SC: SLP (Crl.) No. 4430/2019, reported as 2019 (26) G.S.T.L. J175 (SC).
- Holding:
  - Dismissed the writs seeking protection from arrest. GST officers may pursue arrest in fake ITC cases before assessment.
  - Under s.69(1), "reasons to believe" need only be found in the file; they need not be disclosed in the authorisation (contrast "reasons to be recorded" in s.41A(3) CrPC).
  - Held the writs maintainable (Article 226 can be invoked even where s.438 CrPC does not apply) and that ss.41/41A CrPC protection may be available, but refused relief on the facts (alleged circular trading of about Rs 1,289 crore and ITC of about Rs 225 crore) (paras 47, 58-62).
- Binding: Telangana High Court, binding on Sangareddy officers. Read with the later Supreme Court rulings.
- Later history:
  - SLP (Crl.) No. 4430/2019 dismissed by the SC on 27 May 2019 (confirmed in the SC's *Sapna Jain* order of 29 May 2019 and in *Choodamani*, 2023, which expressly agreed with paras 34-43 of this judgment).
  - In *UoI v. Sapna Jain* (SC, 29 May 2019), the Court reminded High Courts of this dismissal and referred the broader question to a larger bench.
  - **Partly overtaken:** *Radhika Agarwal* (2025) and *Sunil Biyani* (2026) now require written grounds, communication of the s.69 order, and allow anticipatory bail. The "reasons only in the file" point still fits the Allahabad HC's *Jai Kumar Aggarwal* (2026: reasons need not be supplied, but grounds must be). Grounds must still be given in writing.
- Use in orders: Cite for arrest before adjudication in fake-ITC cases. Do not cite it to resist written grounds or communication of the authorisation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – P.V. Ramana Reddy v. UoI, Telangana HC, 18 Apr 2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/87700723/); [Taxo – P.V. Ramana Reddy SLP 4430/2019](https://taxo.online/judgment/p-v-ramana-reddy-vs-union-of-india-2019-26-g-s-t-l-j175-supreme-court/); [LiveLaw – SC upholds Telangana HC](https://livelaw.in/top-stories/gst-evaders-can-be-arrested-145309); [Casemine commentary](https://www.casemine.com/commentary/in/telangana-high-court-upholds-arrest-under-cgst-act,-limiting-use-of-article-226-writ-petitions/view)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 18.04.2019 and bench confirmed; holding refined (writ maintainable, relief refused on facts).
- Key words: "it is enough if the reasons are found in the file, though not disclosed in the order authorizing the arrest." (para 46)

#### Union of India v. Sapna Jain - Supreme Court - 29 May 2019
- Citation / case no.: SLP (Crl.) Nos. 4322-4324/2019 and connected matters (from Bombay HC orders of 11.04.2019). Vacation Bench: the Chief Justice (Ranjan Gogoi) and Aniruddha Bose J. Record of proceedings (order), not a judgment.
- Holding:
  - Issued notice on the Union's SLPs against Bombay HC orders granting pre-arrest bail, noting divergent High Court views; declined to disturb the bail already granted; told High Courts to keep in mind its dismissal (27.5.2019) of the SLP against *P.V. Ramana Reddy*.
  - Directed the matters, with connected cases (including SLP (Crl.) No. 244/2019 and W.P.(Crl.) No. 309/2018), to be listed before a three-judge bench.
- Binding: Supreme Court (interim or referral order).
- Later history: Subsumed by *Radhika Agarwal* (2025): the three-judge judgment's cause list includes SLP (Crl.) No. 244 of 2019, one of the matters tagged here.
- Use in orders: Historical only; cite *Radhika Agarwal* instead.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – UoI v. Sapna Jain, 29 May 2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/120774423/); [P.V. Ramana Reddy BITS commentary](https://dspace.bits-pilani.ac.in/items/1e71f3ad-135d-46a3-9ff3-5c21aeb8aef5); [SS Rana – GST defaulters can be arrested without FIR](https://ssrana.in/articles/supreme-court-gst-defaulters-can-be-arrested-without-fir/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers and bench added; summary confirmed.
- Key words: "the High Courts while entertaining such request in future, will keep in mind that this Court by order dated 27.5.2019 passed in SLP(Crl.) No. 4430/2019 had dismissed the special leave petition". (order)

#### Vimal Yashwantgiri Goswami v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat High Court - Aug 2019 (interim) and 20 Oct 2020 (final)
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 13679 of 2019 with SCA Nos. 3209, 4468, 4456 of 2020 and 13893, 14141 of 2019. Interim order 7 Aug 2019: J.B. Pardiwala and A.C. Rao, JJ. Final CAV judgment 20 Oct 2020: J.B. Pardiwala and Bhargav D. Karia, JJ.
- Holding:
  - **Interim (2019):** Stayed arrest. Arrest powers under s.69 must be used "with lot of care and circumspection," and prosecution should normally follow adjudication.
  - **Final (2020):** The Commissioner may authorise arrest under s.69 read with s.132 before adjudication or determination of liability. Reasons to believe must be recorded but a copy need not be given to the person; grounds of arrest must be informed. D.K. Basu guidelines apply to GST officers; an arrest memo is mandatory. Petitions rejected (para 77, 85).
- Binding: Persuasive (Gujarat HC).
- Later history: Appeal not found. Consistent with *Radhika Agarwal* (2025).
- Use in orders: Cite the 2020 final judgment, not the 2019 interim remarks, which taxpayers often quote.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Vimal Yashwantgiri Goswami, Gujarat HC, 20 Oct 2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/25859143/); [Indian Kanoon – interim order 7 Aug 2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/64439129/); [Vaish Law – Commissioner authorised to arrest prior to assessment](https://www.vaishlaw.com/commissioner-authorized-to-arrest-prior-to-completion-of-assessment-under-the-cgst-act-gujarat-hc/); [TheTaxTalk – power to arrest](https://thetaxtalk.com/2020/10/gst-power-to-arrest-assessment-and-adjudication-of-alleged-evasion-of-gst/); [Taxo – PDF](https://taxo.online/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/Vimal-Yashwantgiri.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (both the 7 Aug 2019 order and the 20 Oct 2020 judgment); final bench corrected to Pardiwala and Bhargav D. Karia JJ; summary confirmed.
- Key words: "the power to arrest as provided under section 69 of the CGST Act can be invoked if the Commissioner has reason to believe ... without there being any adjudication for the assessment". (para 77)

#### Make My Trip (India) Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India - Delhi High Court - 1 Sep 2016
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 525/2016 and W.P.(C) 1283/2016 (Ibibo), decided together. The "(44) STR 481" reference could not be confirmed; neutral reference 2016 SCC OnLine Del 4951 (as cited in *Radhika Agarwal*). Bench: S. Muralidhar and Vibhu Bakhru, JJ.
- Holding (**service tax law, Finance Act 1994**):
  - Two petitions (MakeMyTrip and Ibibo; no Ebiz petition in the judgment). The arrest of MMT's Vice-President without an SCN and without following s.73A(3)-(4) was held contrary to law and violative of Article 21; the searches were held to violate s.82 Finance Act.
  - Payments made during the searches were held not voluntary and ordered refunded within four weeks (6% simple interest if delayed), with costs of Rs 1 lakh in each petition (paras 116-119).
  - *Radhika Agarwal* (2025, para 58-59) agreed that arrest must be used with circumspection but rejected the broad proposition that assessment must precede arrest under GST.
- Binding: Persuasive only, and under a different statute.
- Later history: Reports conflict. One says the SC restrained coercive action; another says the SC upheld the High Court and DGCEI refunded the amounts. For GST, the opposite view prevails (*P.V. Ramana Reddy*, *Vimal Goswami* final, *Radhika Agarwal*).
- Use in orders: If cited by the taxpayer, distinguish it as a service tax case superseded for GST by *Radhika Agarwal* and the Telangana HC.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Makemytrip v. UoI, Delhi HC, 1 Sep 2016](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/183692293/); [Bar & Bench – Delhi HC concern over arrest](https://www.barandbench.com/news/delhi-high-court-concern-over-arrest-makemytrip-official-tax-evasion); [The Quint – refund to travel portals](https://www.thequint.com/amp/story/news/india/delhi-hc-asks-govt-to-refund-service-tax-to-three-travel-portals)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers added; "Ebiz" removed (only MMT and Ibibo were petitioners); SC later history still not confirmed.
- Key words: "The Court is unable to accept that payment by the two Petitioners of alleged service tax arrears was voluntary." (para 116(ix))

#### A-II. Grounds of arrest, DIN and arrest memo

#### Kshitij Ghildiyal v. DGGI - Delhi High Court - 16 Dec 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(CRL) 3770/2024. Single Judge: Anish Dayal J.
- Holding: A DGGI arrest was declared illegal and the remand order set aside because written grounds of arrest were not furnished at arrest (the remand application could not substitute for them). The Court also criticised summons whose DINs were generated after the time fixed for appearance (paras 33-50).
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: CBIC Instruction 01/2025-GST (Inv.) dated 13 Jan 2025 followed it. That instruction requires written grounds as an annexure to the arrest memo, with acknowledgement, and amends para 4.2.1 of Instruction 02/2022-23. One TaxGuru summary says the instruction "followed" *Radhika Agarwal*, but the dates make that impossible (Jan 2025 is before Feb 2025).
- Use in orders: Always serve written grounds and obtain acknowledgement.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Kshitij Ghildiyal v. DGGI, Delhi HC, 16 Dec 2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/171445460/); [Taxscan – GST Investigation Wing guidelines](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=478893); [TaxGuru – arrest illegal for missing grounds / DIN](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-arrest-illegal-due-missing-grounds-cbic-din-arrest-memo-allahabad-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and judge added; DIN-on-summons point added.
- Key words: "the requirement to communicate the grounds of arrest in writing is sacrosanct ... a copy of such written grounds was to be furnished to the arrestee as a matter of course, without exception". (para 33)

#### Ashish Tyagi v. DGGI - Allahabad High Court - 29 May 2026
- Citation / case no.: Habeas Corpus Writ Petition No. 509 of 2026; 2026:AHC:123306-DB. Siddharth and Vinai Kumar Dwivedi, JJ.
- Holding: Arrest and detention held illegal and release ordered. The arrest memo did not state specific grounds or the place of arrest (D.K. Basu), grounds were not shown to be annexed, and the written grounds did not bear a CBIC-DIN as required by Instruction 02/2022-23 (as amended 11.03.2025) (paras 6-9). The quotation previously given here ("Grounds of arrest not bearing a CBIC-DIN cannot satisfy the mandate of law") is not in the judgment text.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Same bench decided *Sushant Goel v. DGGI* (13 May 2026) to the same effect.
- Use in orders: Generate a DIN for the grounds of arrest. State the place and time of arrest in the memo.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Ashish Tyagi v. DGGI, Allahabad HC, 29 May 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/84138962/); [TaxGuru – arrest illegal: missing grounds, CBIC DIN](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-arrest-illegal-due-missing-grounds-cbic-din-arrest-memo-allahabad-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and bench added; unverifiable quotation removed.
- Key words: "the grounds of arrest dated 10.12.2025 do not bear any CBIC DIN." (para 7)

#### Sushant Goel v. DGGI - Allahabad High Court - 13 May 2026
- Citation / case no.: Habeas Corpus Writ Petition No. 427 of 2026; 2026:AHC:110667-DB. Siddharth and Vinai Kumar Dwivedi, JJ.
- Holding: Arrest, detention and the remand order of 01.11.2025 quashed. The arrest memo only said grounds were "explained" and did not show written grounds annexed, omitted the place of arrest, and the grounds lacked a DIN; the remand was mechanical (paras 16-19).
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Same as above; follow a checklist for the arrest memo.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Sushant Goel v. DGGI, Allahabad HC, 13 May 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/65791670/); [TaxHeal – Sushant Goel v DGGI](https://www.taxheal.com/high-court-of-allahabad-sushant-goel-v-directorate-general-of-gst-intelligence-siddharth-and-vinai-kumar-dwivedi-jj-habeas-corpus-writ-petition-no-427-of-2026-may-13-2026.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; neutral citation added; summary confirmed (remand quashing added).
- Key words: "There is no place of arrest mentioned in the arrest memo ... which is in clear violation of the principles laid down by the Apex Court in the case of D.K. Basu". (para 17)

#### Jai Kumar Aggarwal v. (DGGI/Union) - Allahabad High Court - Feb 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found. TaxHeal URL slug "139-of-2026".
- Holding: "Reasons to believe" must be recorded under s.69 but need not be supplied to the accused. Written "grounds of arrest" must be furnished. The remand Magistrate should check that written grounds were served; a remand order was invalidated for non-supply.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Keep two documents: (i) the reasons to believe (internal, in the file) and (ii) the grounds of arrest (served, with DIN).
- Source: [TaxHeal – remand order invalidated for non-supply of grounds](https://www.taxheal.com/139-of-2026.html)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (Allahabad HC judgment not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026). Indian Kanoon does show that the same petitioner's Delhi HC writ, W.P.(CRL) 212/2026 against an arrest of 16.01.2026, was withdrawn on 20.01.2026 with liberty to approach the appropriate forum (https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198905803/). Note *Sunil Biyani* (SC, 12 Aug 2026, para 20) on withholding reasons.

#### A-III. Anticipatory bail and bail

#### State of Gujarat v. Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer - Supreme Court - 17 Jul 2023
- Citation / case no.: Criminal Appeals arising out of SLP (Crl.) Nos. 4212-4213 of 2019 (from Gujarat HC order of 24.12.2018 in SCRA Nos. 11010 and 11076 of 2018). Reportable order. J.B. Pardiwala and Prashant Kumar Mishra, JJ (author: P.K. Mishra J).
- Holding: A person summoned (under s.70 CGST and s.145 Central Excise as applied to service tax) cannot seek anticipatory bail under s.438 CrPC, because no FIR is registered before s.69(1) is invoked. Pre-arrest protection lies only under Article 226, used sparingly. The HC order directing adjudication in 8 weeks was set aside; the Court agreed with *P.V. Ramana Reddy* (paras 16-20).
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history:
  - Followed in *Bharat Bhushan v. DGGI* (SC, 5 Jul 2024) and *Vemula Yougander* (Telangana HC, 14 Nov 2023).
  - Qualified by *Radhika Agarwal* (2025), which allows anticipatory bail without an FIR where arrest is apprehended, and by *Sunil Biyani* (2026), which makes communication of the s.69 order the trigger.
  - The current reconciliation: no s.438 relief at the bare summons stage; available once a s.69 order exists.
- Use in orders and counter-affidavits: Cite at the summons stage to oppose s.438 petitions.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – State of Gujarat v. Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer, 17 Jul 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/155401808/); [Verdictum – person summoned cannot invoke s.438](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/supreme-court/person-summoned-under-section-69-cgst-act-cannot-invoke-section-438-crpc-1486436); [SC order PDF 17-Jul-2023](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2019/13019/13019_2019_4_30_45289_Order_17-Jul-2023.pdf); [MetaLegal](https://www.metalegal.in/post/supreme-court-affirms-anticipatory-bail-not-maintainable-for-summons-under-s-69-of-the-cgst)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date fixed as 17 Jul 2023; bench corrected (Prashant Kumar Mishra J, not Manoj Misra J); SLP numbers added. Note: the order says "summoned under Section 69" though the summons was under s.70.
- Key words: "if any person is summoned under Section 69 of the CGST Act, 2017 for the purpose of recording of his statement, the provisions of Section 438 of Criminal Procedure Code ... cannot be invoked." (para 16)

#### Bharat Bhushan v. DGGI - Supreme Court - 5 Jul 2024
- Citation / case no.: SLP (Crl.) No. 8525/2024 (from Bombay HC, Nagpur Bench, order of 08.04.2024 in Crl. Appeal No. 118/2022). Vacation Bench: J.B. Pardiwala and Ujjal Bhuyan, JJ. Record of proceedings (order).
- Holding: Applying *Choodamani*, the High Court committed no error in cancelling the anticipatory bail the trial court had granted to a summoned person. The SC nevertheless gave liberty to file an Article 226 writ and protected the petitioner from arrest for six weeks.
- Binding: Supreme Court order (short; applies *Choodamani*; no new law).
- Later history: See *Sunil Biyani* (2026).
- Use in orders: As above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Bharat Bhushan v. DGGI, SC order 5 Jul 2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/156114141/); [MetaLegal – SC affirms anticipatory bail not maintainable for summons](https://www.metalegal.in/post/supreme-court-affirms-anticipatory-bail-not-maintainable-for-summons-under-s-69-of-the-cgst)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date corrected from "2025" to 5 Jul 2024; SLP number, bench and the six-week protection added.
- Key words: "We are of the view that the High Court committed no error in cancelling the anticipatory bail granted by the trial court." (para 4)

#### Harish Wadhwani v. DGGI - Supreme Court (SLP from Chhattisgarh HC) - c. June 2026
- Citation / case no.: SC order not found. Reports dated 9–10 Jun 2026. HC order: MCRCA No. 265 of 2026, Chhattisgarh HC (Ramesh Sinha CJ), 25 Mar 2026, 2026:CGHC:14379.
- Holding:
  - The SC declined to interfere with the High Court's refusal of anticipatory bail under s.482 BNSS. Only summons had been issued and no s.69 action taken.
  - The alleged liability was below Rs 5 crore (total about Rs 1.16 crore), so the s.132 offences were non-cognizable and bailable, and the s.482 BNSS application was "devoid of merit" (HC order para 6, confirmed). SLP reported dismissed (not confirmed).
  - A separate Chhattisgarh HC order granted anticipatory bail to the same person in a Rs 17.8 crore ITC matter (single source).
- Binding: An SLP dismissal does not lay down law. The High Court's reasoning remains persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Where the amount is below Rs 5 crore, oppose anticipatory bail on the ground that the offence is bailable.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Harish Wadhwani v. DGGI, Chhattisgarh HC, 25 Mar 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/95135666/); [Taxmann – anticipatory bail not required below Rs 5 crore](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/anticipatory-bail-not-required-for-gst-offences-below-%e2%82%b95-crore-sc); [TaxGuru – SC upholds rejection](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/sc-upholds-rejection-anticipatory-bail-gst-alleged-liability-rs-5-crore-threshold.html); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=133116)
- Verified: Secondary for the SC order (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026). The Chhattisgarh HC order of 25 Mar 2026 was read on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 and matches the summary.
- Key words (HC): "offences involving an amount less than Rs.5 crore are non- cognizable and bailable in nature. Therefore, the anticipatory bail application under Section 482 BNSS ... is devoid of merit." (HC para 6)

#### Vemula Yougander v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - 14 Nov 2023 — **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: Criminal Petition No. 10430 of 2023, K. Surender J (single judge). Not a 2026 decision: the "2026 taxguru.in 15041" reference is only a later report.
- Holding: Anticipatory bail (s.438 CrPC) petition at the DGGI s.70 summons stage disposed of, following *Choodamani*: a summoned person cannot invoke s.438. The department had taken no step under s.69. Liberty to file a writ.
- Binding: Telangana High Court (single judge).
- Later history: The petitioner then filed W.P. No. 34106 of 2023, decided by the Telangana HC on 19.12.2023 (judgment not read); the SC dismissed SLP (Crl.) No. 1154/2024 against it on 29.01.2024 (Sanjiv Khanna and Dipankar Datta JJ) (https://indiankanoon.org/doc/199058343/).
- Use in orders and counters: Cite in Telangana to oppose anticipatory bail where only summons have been issued.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Vemula Yougander v. UoI, Telangana HC, 14 Nov 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26153819/); [TaxGuru – Vemula Yougander](https://taxguru.in/?p=1086968)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date corrected from "2026" to 14 Nov 2023; case number and judge added; later writ and SLP dismissal added.
- Key words: "The Honourable Supreme Court held that at the stage of summons the person summoned cannot invoke Section 438 of the Code of Criminal Procedure." (para 7)

#### A-IV. Section 70 summons

#### Devendra Surana v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - 8 Apr 2026 — **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 10378 of 2026. Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.
- Holding: Challenge to DGGI s.70 summons (second summons, on ITC from M/s AMZ Traders) dismissed. The petitioner argued that the s.74 dues adjudged against the company were paid and that he feared arrest. The court held he was obliged to appear and that a second summons gave no basis for the apprehension.
- Binding: Telangana High Court.
- Later history: Relied on by CBIC in *Deepa Goel / Sandeep Kumar Goel v. UoI* (Telangana HC, 2026). Those writs were then withdrawn, so that decision is not on the merits.
- Use in orders: Cite against writs seeking to quash summons. Keep summons specific (DIN, purpose, documents sought).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Devendra Surana v. UoI, Telangana HC, 8 Apr 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/56040301/); [Telangana HC current-section PDF (23 Apr 2026)](https://tshc.gov.in/documents/currentsection_2026_04_23T12_45_07.pdf); [TaxGuru – Telangana HC summons matters](https://taxguru.in/?p=1068719)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; summary confirmed. "Later history" (Deepa Goel) not checked.
- Key words: "Petitioner is under obligation to appear before respondent No.5 in pursuance of the impugned summons dated 02.04.2026, issued in exercise of statutory powers under Section 70 of the CGST Act". (para 6)

#### Shailesh Pandey v. Union of India - Telangana High Court - 12 Mar 2026 — **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (but a consent disposal)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 7415 of 2026. Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.
- Holding: The summons sought information on 177 entities within about 37 hours. DGGI said fresh summons with sufficient time would issue, so the challenge became academic and the writ was disposed of without going into the merits.
- Binding: No precedent value (disposed of as academic, without deciding the merits).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Give reasonable time in summons; short-notice summons invite writs.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Shailesh Pandey v. UoI, Telangana HC, 12 Mar 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/68960475/); [TaxGuru – Telangana HC disposes summons challenge, fresh notice](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-disposes-gst-summons-challenge-dggi-agrees-issue-fresh-notice-adequate-response-time.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and facts (177 entities, 37 hours) added; "declined to interfere" corrected to "disposed of as academic".
- Key words: "Since the impugned summons could not be acted upon, the challenge thereto in the present writ petition has been rendered academic." (para 6)

#### A-V. Payments during search ("voluntary" DRC-03)

#### Bhumi Associate v. Union of India - Gujarat High Court - 16 Feb 2021 (interim order)
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 3196 of 2021 with SCA Nos. 2426, 2515 and 2618 of 2021; 2021 (2) TMI 925 (Guj); 2021 (46) GSTL 36. J.B. Pardiwala and Ilesh J. Vora, JJ. Orders of 16.02.2021 (proposed directions) and 18.02.2021 (CBIC impleaded and put to notice why the guidelines should not issue as a circular).
- Holding (interim directions to CBIC and the Gujarat Chief Commissioner to issue guidelines):
  - No recovery during search, inspection or investigation by cheque, cash, e-payment or ITC adjustment.
  - If the taxpayer wants to pay voluntarily, advise it to file DRC-03 on the next day, after the officers have left.
  - A grievance mechanism for forced payments.
  - Disciplinary action against officers who defy the directions.
- Binding: Persuasive outside Gujarat, but widely followed (Delhi and Bombay High Courts).
- Later history: CBIC issued Instruction No. 01/2022-23 (GST-Inv.) dated 25.05.2022 on voluntary payments during search (as noted in *Vallabh Textiles*). *Vallabh Textiles* (Delhi HC, 2022) treated direction (2) as binding and told CBIC to align that Instruction with it. Final disposal not found.
- Use in orders: Do not accept tax during a search. If DRC-03 is filed later, record its voluntariness and issue a DRC-04.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Bhumi Associate, Gujarat HC order 16 Feb 2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/98975664/); [order 18 Feb 2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/199163585/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1044115); [SCC Online blog](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2022/12/23/delhi-high-court-failure-follow-prescribed-procedure-result-in-the-deposit-of-tax-interest-and-penalty-being-not-voluntary/); [Taxmann – tax recovered during search refundable](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/tax-recovered-during-search-without-voluntary-consent-refundable/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (16 Feb 2021), SCA number and bench added; the four directions confirmed verbatim.
- Key words: "No recovery in any mode by cheque, cash, e-payment or adjustment of input tax credit should be made at the time of search/inspection proceedings under Section 67 ... under any circumstances." (para 2, direction 1)

#### Vallabh Textiles v. Senior Intelligence Officer - Delhi High Court - 20 Dec 2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 9834/2022; 2022:DHC:005682; 2022 LiveLaw (Del) 1208; 2022 SCC OnLine Del 4508. Rajiv Shakdher and Tara Vitasta Ganju, JJ.
- Holding:
  - Rs 1,80,10,000 (tax at 18%, interest and penalty) deposited by DRC-03 during search proceedings was held not voluntary.
  - The Revenue had no document showing acceptance or acknowledgement of the payment.
  - Breach of the safeguards in the Act, Rules and court directions gives the collection "colour of coercion."
  - Refund of Rs 1,80,10,000 with 6% simple interest from 17.02.2022 was ordered within ten days.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: The judgment directed CBIC to align Instruction No. 01/2022-23 dated 25.05.2022 with the *Bhumi Associate* directions (para 46). Followed by later Delhi HC orders (e.g. *Neeraj Paper Marketing*, 2023).
- Use in orders: A DRC-03 paid during a search will not support an order unless voluntariness is documented. Issue DRC-04.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Vallabh Textiles v. SIO, Delhi HC, 20 Dec 2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/188747488/); [LiveLaw – Rs 1.80 crore to be returned](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/tax-deposited-during-search-not-voluntary-delhi-high-court-directs-gst-dept-return-rs180-crores-217331); [Taxo – Vallabh Textiles](https://taxo.online/judgment/m-s-vallabh-textiles-vs-senior-intelligence-officer-and-ors-in-w-p-c-9834-of-2022-high-court-delhi/); [LiveLaw PDF](https://www.livelaw.in/pdf_upload/vallabh-textiles-450227.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, interest terms and the CBIC-alignment direction confirmed.
- Key words: "Failure to follow the prescribed procedure will, as in this case, have us conclude that the deposit of tax, interest and penalty was not voluntary." (para 40.1)

#### Neeraj Paper Marketing Ltd. v. Special Commissioner, Department of Trade and Taxes, GNCTD - Delhi High Court - 5 Dec 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 158/2023. Vibhu Bakhru and Amit Mahajan, JJ. (State GST search by Delhi DGST, not CGST. The "W.P.(C) 15519/2023" file is a different case.)
- Holding: Rs 28,20,000 paid at 11:49 PM and 12:38 AM during a search on 29.07.2022 was paid under compelling circumstances, not voluntarily; no liability had been ascertained and no Rule 142(1A) notice issued. Refund ordered (Rs 23,70,000 in cash with 6% interest from 13.12.2022, and Rs 4,50,000 by re-crediting the electronic credit ledger).
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: As above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Neeraj Paper Marketing v. Spl. Commr. DT&T, Delhi HC, 5 Dec 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/12649763/); [Taxmann – midnight deposit refund](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/delhi-hc-directed-refund-of-amount-deposited-coercively-during-search-operations-at-midnight)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case name, date (5 Dec 2023, not 2024), case number, bench and amounts corrected; it is a Delhi State GST case.
- Key words: "We are, therefore, inclined to accept the petitioner's contention that the payments made by it were not voluntary payments but under compelling circumstances." (para 18)

*Counter-trend (court not identified in the snippet):* one High Court refused a coercion claim raised two years late. It adopted the Bombay HC's view in *Innovators Facade Systems Ltd.* that coercion allegations without contemporaneous complaint cannot readily be accepted ([TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=136336)). Useful to the Revenue where the taxpayer stayed silent for a long time.

#### A-VI. Search under s.67 and seizure of cash

#### Deepak Khandelwal (Shri Shyam Metal) v. Commissioner of CGST, Delhi West - Delhi High Court (DB) - 17 Aug 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 6739/2021; 2023:DHC:5823-DB. Vibhu Bakhru and Purushaindra Kumar Kaurav, JJ.
- Holding: Section 67(2) does not allow seizure of currency or valuables (here cash and silver bars) merely because they are unaccounted wealth; the Court declined to follow the MP HC's *Kanishka Matta*. Even if seizure were allowed, s.67(3) required return because the later s.74 notice did not rely on them. Release ordered (paras 57-63).
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history:
  - Revenue's SLP dismissed. The date is disputed: 2024 (8) TMI 1041 (SC) vs a 17 Apr 2025 report.
  - Review petition dismissed (SLP No. 18536 of 2024, order 14 Aug 2025).
  - One source lists a pending diary no. 31886/2024, which conflicts.
  - The SC separately issued notice in Oct 2024 on the general issue (below).
- Use in orders: Do not seize cash under s.67(2). If cash is clearly sale proceeds of seized goods, record the link specifically. Expect challenge in any case.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Deepak Khandelwal v. Commr. CGST Delhi West, Delhi HC, 17 Aug 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/172657151/); [MetaLegal – SC on cash and silver bars](https://www.metalegal.in/post/supreme-court-confiscation-of-cash-and-silver-bars-by-gst-authorities-on-the-grounds-of-unaccounte); [A2Z Taxcorp – SC dismisses review](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/gst-dept-to-return-seized-cash-jewellery-etc-during-search-sc-dismisses-review-petition-filed-by-revenue/); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=100876)
- Verified: Primary for the HC judgment - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; s.67(3) ground added. SC later history not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026, so the conflicting SC dates remain unresolved.
- Key words: "it does not permit seizure of currency or valuable assets, simply, on the ground that the same represent unaccounted wealth." (para 57)

#### Commissioner of CGST Delhi West v. Gunjan Bindal - Supreme Court - notice issued 21 Oct 2024; pending
- Citation / case no.: SLP (C) Diary No. 44061/2024, against Delhi HC order of 17.11.2023 in W.P.(C) 8713/2023 (Vibhu Bakhru and Amit Mahajan JJ).
- Holding: No final holding found. P.S. Narasimha and Sandeep Mehta, JJ. issued notice (with delay condonation) on 21.10.2024 on the Revenue's challenge to the Delhi HC direction to refund Rs 1,15,00,000 seized cash, noting the conflict between the Delhi and MP High Courts.
- Binding: Pending; no ruling found as of Oct 2026.
- Later history: Registrar's orders of 17.01.2025 and 20.02.2025 gave time for counter affidavits (last opportunity). No later order found on Indian Kanoon.
- Use in orders: Until decided, the Delhi, Rajasthan, Karnataka, Gujarat and Kerala view (no cash seizure) dominates.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – SC notice order 21 Oct 2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/36789427/); [Indian Kanoon – Delhi HC order 17 Nov 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/178158290/); [LiveLaw – SC to consider whether cash can be seized](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/supreme-court-to-consider-whether-cash-can-be-seized-during-gst-search-section-67-cgst-act-273272); [SAG Infotech](https://blog.saginfotech.com/sc-examine-legality-cash-seizure-gst-section-67-amid-conflicting-high-court-rulings)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case name confirmed; diary number, HC case and amount added.
- Key words: "In view of the submission that there is a conflict between the interpretation adopted by the Delhi High Court and the Madhya Pradesh High Court, issue notice". (order 21.10.2024)

#### Kanishka Matta v. Union of India - Madhya Pradesh High Court - 26 Aug 2020
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8204/2020 (Indore Bench, DB: S.C. Sharma and Shailendra Shukla, JJ); 2020 SCC OnLine MP 4564.
- Holding: Contrary view: cash/money is included in "things" in s.67(2) and may be seized; release of Rs 66,43,130 seized was refused.
- Binding: Persuasive (MP only).
- Later history: Departed from by the Delhi HC and other High Courts. The SC is examining the conflict.
- Use in orders: Revenue may cite it, but it is the minority view; risky.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Kanishka Matta v. UoI, MP HC, 26 Aug 2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/42423515/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/supreme-court-to-consider-whether-cash-can-be-seized-during-gst-search-section-67-cgst-act-273272); [Jurishour – cash can be seized as a "thing"](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/cash-can-be-seized-thing-section-67/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (only the "law laid down" note is on Indian Kanoon, the body text is cut; the amount is from *Deepak Khandelwal* para 57); date, case number and bench added.
- Key words: "It also empowers the proper officer to seize cash / money as the cash / money is included within the meaning of term "things" appearing in Section 67(2)." (law laid down)

#### B. Kusuma Poonacha v. Senior Intelligence Officer, DGGI - Karnataka High Court - 20 Feb 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 25864 of 2023 (T-RES); 2024:KHC:7186. S.R. Krishna Kumar J (single judge).
- Holding: Follows the Delhi, Gujarat and Kerala HCs and holds *Kanishka Matta* wrong: cash, currency or money is not "things" under s.67(2). The seizure order also gave no reasons for seizing cash, and no SCN had issued for 1.5 years. Refund of Rs 1,71,07,500 with accrued interest ordered within three weeks (paras 9.4-11). It records that the SC dismissed the Revenue's SLP (Diary No. 27670/2023) against the Kerala HC's *Shabu George* ruling on 31.07.2023.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Confirms the majority view.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – B. Kusuma Poonacha v. SIO DGGI, Karnataka HC, 20 Feb 2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/106876875/); [TaxHeal – cash seizure illegal, interest](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=141969)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number, judge and amount added; summary confirmed.
- Key words: "the expression "things" contained in Section 67(2) of the CGST Act does not include cash / currency / money found or recovered during the course of search and seizure". (para 9.4)

#### A-VII. CBIC instructions as read by the courts (not cases; for reference)
- **Instruction 02/2022-23 (GST-Investigation), 17 Aug 2022**: guidelines for arrest and bail.
  - Arrest only on "reasons to believe" that are unambiguous and based on credible material, not for technical offences.
  - Conditions precedent, procedure and post-arrest formalities.
  - Report every arrest to the Zonal Member / Member (Compliance Management) within 24 hours.
  - Para 4.2.1 was amended by Instruction 01/2025 (below).
  - Sources: [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/cbic-issues-guidelines-for-arrest-bail-in-relation-to-offences-punishable-under-gst-instruction); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/avoid-arrest-in-gst-cases-of-technical-nature-dont-use-this-power-mechanically-cbic-issues-guidelines-206949).
- **Instruction 03/2022-23, 17 Aug 2022**: guidelines for s.70 summons. Includes not summoning senior management as a matter of routine (detail per general understanding; not confirmed in summaries). Source: [Taxo](https://taxo.online/latest-news/18-08-2022-cbic-issued-guidelines-regarding-arrest-and-bail-for-punishable-offences-and-issuance-of-summons-under-gst/).
- **Instruction 01/2025-GST (Inv.), 13 Jan 2025**: written grounds of arrest as an annexure to the arrest memo, with acknowledgement. Source: [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-arrest-illegal-due-missing-grounds-cbic-din-arrest-memo-allahabad-hc.html).
- **Instruction 01/2022-23 (GST-Investigation), 25 May 2022:** this instruction does exist. It is CBIC's instruction that no tax recovery be made during search, inspection or investigation unless the payment is voluntary (DRC-03). The Delhi HC in *Vallabh Textiles* (20 Dec 2022, paras 38-39, 46) read it and directed CBIC to align it with *Bhumi Associate*. The arrest guidelines are a separate instruction, **02/2022-23**. (Corrected 14 Oct 2026 from the *Vallabh Textiles* text; the instruction itself was not read.)

---

### 3. Part B: Landmark constitutional and levy rulings

#### Union of India v. Mohit Minerals Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court - 19 May 2022
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 1390 of 2022 and connected appeals. D.Y. Chandrachud (author), Surya Kant and Vikram Nath, JJ. Neutral or SCC citation not confirmed.
- Holding:
