  1. GST Council recommendations under Art. 279A are not binding on Parliament or State legislatures (Art. 246A gives them simultaneous power); they have persuasive value. But the Government, when making rules under the CGST and IGST Acts, is bound by the Council's recommendations (para 148(i)(c)).
  2. IGST under reverse charge on ocean freight in CIF imports (Notifications 8/2017 and 10/2017-IT(Rate)) is invalid because a separate levy on the service element of a CIF composite supply violates s.8 CGST. The Court did not strike it down for want of power: it held the notifications within the IGST Act's reverse-charge provisions (para 148(ii)-(v)).
  3. Affirmed the Gujarat HC (2020).
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history: The ocean-freight reverse charge entries were omitted from 1 Oct 2023 on Council recommendation (per general knowledge — not confirmed in this search).
- Use in orders:
  - Do not demand IGST on ocean freight from CIF importers.
  - When a taxpayer argues that a Council recommendation overrides the statute or rules, note that the binding law is the Act, Rules and notifications. The recommendation is persuasive only.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – UoI v. Mohit Minerals, 19 May 2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/98511521/); [BDO – SC strikes down RCM on ocean freight](https://www.bdo.global/en-gb/microsites/tax-newsletters/indirect-tax-news/issue-3-2022/india-supreme-court-strikes-down-gst-reverse-charge-on-ocean-freight-in-cif-imports); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan](https://www.lkslaw.com/insights/news/igst-not-payable-by-indian-importer-on-rcm-basis-on-ocean-freight-in-case-of-cif-imports); [NLIU CBCL](https://cbcl.nliu.ac.in/taxation/mohit-mineral-v-uoi-the-make-or-break-for-the-gst-regime/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; holding refined (rule-making bound by recommendations; levy failed only on the composite-supply ground).
- Key words: "The Government while exercising its rule-making power under the provisions of the CGST Act and IGST Act is bound by the recommendations of the GST Council." (para 148(i)(c))

#### C.C., C.E. & S.T., Bangalore (Adjudication) v. Northern Operating Systems Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court - 19 May 2022
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal Nos. 2289-2293 of 2021; [2022] 18 S.C.R. 901; 2022 INSC 598 (per LawSathi, not checked). Three judges: U.U. Lalit, S. Ravindra Bhat (author) and P.S. Narasimha, JJ.
- Holding (service tax): The overseas group company seconding employees to an Indian entity (the Indian entity reimbursing salaries at cost) was a taxable supply of manpower. The Indian recipient was liable under reverse charge. The CESTAT's "employer-employee" view was reversed. The extended limitation period was not upheld (no wilful suppression); the appeals were partly allowed and demands confined to the normal period (paras 62-68).
- Binding: Supreme Court on service tax. Applied by analogy in GST; persuasive there.
- Later history:
  - CBIC **Instruction 05/2023-GST (13 Dec 2023)**: do not apply the ruling mechanically; examine each contract; invoke the extended period only on proof of fraud or suppression.
  - **Circular 210/4/2024-GST (26 Jun 2024)** on valuation of related-party import of services (value may be declared nil or as invoiced where the recipient is eligible for full ITC) — relevance per Trilegal.
  - Karnataka HC (*Huawei*) took a contrasting fact-specific view where employees were integrated into the Indian entity.
- Use in orders: Examine the secondment agreement (control, who bears the cost, the employment contract). Do not issue s.74 notices on secondment without evidence of suppression.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – CCE&ST Bangalore v. Northern Operating Systems, 19 May 2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/43101309/); [Mondaq – foundation for GST on secondment](https://www.mondaq.com/india/employee-benefits--compensation/1209096/supreme-court-ruling-sets-the-foundation-for-gst-on-secondment-of-employees); [Taxmann – CBIC instructions on NOS](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/cbic-issues-instructions-on-applicability-of-sc-decision-in-case-of-northern-operating-systems-under-gst); [LawSathi record](https://lawsathi.in/judgements/sc/2022/bangalore-adjudication-etc-northern-operating-systems-pvt-ltd/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (19 May 2022), appeal numbers and bench added; extended-period point confirmed.
- Key words: "the assessee was the service recipient for service (of manpower recruitment and supply services) by the overseas entity, in regard to the employees it seconded to the assessee". (para 65)

#### Union of India v. Future Gaming Solutions Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court - 11 Feb 2025
- Citation / case no.: 2025 INSC 181; 2025 LiveLaw (SC) 189; Civil Appeal Nos. 4289–4290 of 2013 and connected appeals. B.V. Nagarathna (author) and N. Kotiswar Singh, JJ.
- Holding:
  - Lottery distributors are not liable to service tax under the Finance Act, 1994.
  - Lottery is "betting and gambling" (Entry 62, List II), which only the States may tax.
  - The distributor buys tickets from the State on a principal-to-principal basis; there is no agency and no service to the State, and the 2012-2016 Finance Act amendments did not change this (para 18.7). *K. Arumugam* (SC, 2024) is discussed.
  - Sikkim HC affirmed.
- Binding: Supreme Court (service tax period).
- Later history: For GST, lottery is taxable as an actionable claim under Schedule III (exclusion carved out). This ruling does not decide GST. Read with *Gameskraft* (2026).
- Use in orders: Not authority against GST on lottery. Distinguish it as a pre-GST case on legislative competence.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – UoI v. Future Gaming Solutions, 11 Feb 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/117744026/); [LiveLaw – lottery distributor not liable to service tax](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/lottery-distributor-not-liable-to-pay-service-tax-supreme-court-rejects-unions-appeals-283545); [Verdictum – 2025 INSC 181](https://www.verdictum.in/app-lite/court-updates/supreme-court/union-of-india-v-future-gaming-solutions-pvt-ltd-2025-insc-181-lottery-service-tax-1567895); [SC judgment PDF 11-Feb-2025](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2013/4727/4727_2013_7_1501_59216_Judgement_11-Feb-2025.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 11 Feb 2025 and appeal numbers confirmed; reasoning point corrected to principal-to-principal.
- Key words: "There being no agency and no service rendered by the respondents-assessees herein as an agent to the Government of Sikkim, service tax is not leviable". (para 18.7)

#### Gameskraft Technologies Pvt. Ltd. v. DGGI - Karnataka High Court - 11 May 2023
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Quashed the s.74(1) show cause notice dated 23.09.2022 (about Rs 21,000 crore) for 2017 to June 2022. Online rummy and skill games were held not to be "betting or gambling."
- Binding: **Reversed** by the SC (27 May 2026).
- Later history: SC stayed it in Sept 2023, stayed all online-gaming notices on 10 Jan 2025, and set it aside on 27 May 2026.
- Use in orders: Do not rely on it; it has been reversed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – SC judgment of 27 May 2026 reversing it (para 86.2)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/69261133/); [Storyboard18 – SC stays Karnataka HC](https://cms.storyboard18.com/?p=13269); [Inc42 – SC stays notices](https://inc42.com/buzz/sc-stay-gameskraft-tax-woes-40-online-gaming-companies)
- Verified: Secondary - the HC judgment was not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; its date (11.05.2023), the SCN date (23.09.2022) and its reversal are confirmed from the SC judgment (para 86.2), read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026.

#### Directorate General of GST Intelligence v. Gameskraft Technologies Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court - 27 May 2026
- Citation / case no.: 2026 INSC 595; Civil Appeal Nos. 8241–8244 of 2026 arising out of SLP(C) Nos. 19366–19369 of 2023 (with C.A. 8240/2026 and Crl. A. 2933/2026, and a connected judgment on the writ petitions). J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan, JJ.
- Holding:
  - Online money-gaming platforms are suppliers of actionable claims in betting and gambling, not mere intermediaries.
  - Skill versus chance is irrelevant where money is staked on an uncertain outcome.
  - The whole amount staked is consideration; no deduction for prize pools or winnings. The 2023 amendments (Entry 6 of Schedule III, Rules 31B and 31C) are clarificatory and retrospective, so pending SCNs are to be valued under Rule 31B (online gaming) or 31C (casinos) (para 84(ix)-(xi), 86). The rate in the SCNs (18% to 24.01.2018, 28% from 25.01.2018) was the Revenue's case; the judgment's operative part does not itself fix a rate.
  - The Karnataka HC judgment of 11.05.2023 was set aside and the SCNs of 23.09.2022 restored. Taxpayers may raise factual and legal defences before the adjudicating authorities. Replies are due within 8 weeks of receiving the judgment, and orders within 12 weeks after that (para 86.1-86.2).
  - A related decision upheld State power to prohibit online money gaming.
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history:
  - Rate became 40% from 22 Sep 2025.
  - Online money gaming was prohibited under the Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Act, 2025 (sources differ on whether the 1 May 2026 date relates to the Act or the 2026 Rules).
  - Whether any portion (e.g. limitation, pre-2023 valuation under Rule 31A) remains open is not clear from summaries.
- Use in orders: For pending gaming SCNs, adjudicate on merits, valuing under Rule 31B/31C as the SC directed, within the SC's time limits. Still deal with each defence (limitation, quantification) on its own terms, since the SC left them open.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – DGGI v. Gameskraft, 27 May 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/69261133/); [Bar & Bench – SC upholds 28% GST](https://www.barandbench.com/news/litigation/supreme-court-upholds-28-gst-levy-on-online-gaming-bets-says-skill-chance-irrelevant-if-money-staked); [Verdictum – 2026 INSC 595](https://www.verdictum.in/supreme-court/directorate-general-of-goods-and-services-tax-intelligence-hqs-v-gameskraft-technologies-private-limited-2026-insc-595-28-gst-1615025); [SC Observer](https://www.scobserver.in/supreme-court-observer-law-reports-scolr/directorate-general-of-goods-and-services-tax-intelligence-hqs-vs-gameskraft-technologies-private-limited-goods-and-service-tax-on-online-gaming/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (conclusion and result paras 84-86); appeal numbers added; "28% on face value" refined to Rule 31B/31C valuation; time limits for adjudication added. The "related decision" on State prohibition was not checked.
- Key words: "Pending show cause notices, adjudication proceedings and consequential demands relating to online gaming, fantasy sports and casino transactions shall accordingly be considered and decided in accordance with the valuation framework embodied in Rules 31B and 31C". (para 86(vi))

#### Mineral Area Development Authority v. Steel Authority of India - Supreme Court (9 judges) - 25 Jul 2024
- Citation / case no.: 2024 INSC 554; Civil Appeal Nos. 4056-4064 of 1999 and connected. Majority 8:1 (D.Y. Chandrachud CJI for the majority); B.V. Nagarathna J. dissenting.
- Holding: Royalty under the MMDR Act is not a tax. It is consideration for the enjoyment of mineral rights under a lease. States have power to tax mineral rights.
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history:
  - A follow-up order (reported) allowed State demands from 1 Apr 2005, payable in instalments over 12 years from 1 Apr 2026, with no interest or penalty for the period before 25 Jul 2024.
  - On GST on royalty: High Courts (e.g. Chhattisgarh) now apply "royalty is not a tax." The **Telangana HC** issued notice (Nov 2025) on a writ challenging GST on royalty, DMF and SMET — pending, **relevant to Telangana**.
  - No final SC ruling on GST on royalty was found. A wiki claim of a "2026 MMDR Amendment" is unverified and appears unreliable.
- Use in orders: The taxpayer argument that royalty is a tax and so cannot attract GST no longer holds. Whether royalty is a "supply" (licence or right) still needs reasoned treatment. Watch the Telangana writ.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Mineral Area Development Authority v. SAIL, 25 Jul 2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/179331686/); [Mondaq – SC on royalty](https://www.mondaq.com/india/court-procedure/1516488/supreme-court); [Jurishour – GST on mining royalty](https://www.jurishour.in/columns/levy-gst-mining-royalty-mmdr-amendment-effect/); [TaxHeal – Telangana HC seeks reply on GST on royalty](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=108466)
- Verified: Primary for the SC holding - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (conclusions, para 342); citation and case number added. The follow-up instalment order and the Telangana writ remain single-source (not checked).
- Key words: "Royalty is not a tax. Royalty is a contractual consideration paid by the mining lessee to the lessor for enjoyment of mineral rights." (majority, para 342(a))

#### Torrent Power Ltd. v. Union of India - Gujarat High Court (DB) - 14 Aug 2026
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 12175 of 2024 and 12 connected SCAs; 2026:GUJHC:51510-DB (neutral citation not seen in the Indian Kanoon text). A.S. Supehia and Vaibhavi D. Nanavati, JJ.
- Holding:
  - GST applies to intra-group corporate guarantees.
  - Section 15(4) and Rule 28(2) are valid, but "whichever is higher" is read down. Actual ascertainable consideration (even if below 1%) is taxed; 1% applies only where the guarantee is gratuitous or its value cannot be determined.
  - Levy on guarantees given before 26 Oct 2023 violates Arts. 14 and 19(1)(g). Levy from 26 Oct 2023 is allowed on continuing guarantees.
  - Value is based on the outstanding debt in the subsidiary's books each year, not the sanctioned amount (para 64).
  - The s.74 SCNs and orders were quashed (no wilful suppression in a disputed interpretation); circulars of 11.07.2023 and 27.10.2023 set aside to the extent contrary; excess GST to be refunded (para 121-122).
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Appeal not found (decision is two months old).
- Use in orders: For Telangana demands, avoid taxing guarantees for periods before 26 Oct 2023 on the deemed 1%. Compute on the outstanding exposure.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Torrent Power v. UoI, Gujarat HC, 14 Aug 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109194998/); [TaxGuru – Gujarat HC upholds GST on corporate guarantees](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gujarat-hc-upholds-gst-corporate-guarantees-reads-1-percent-valuation-rule.html); [Alvarez & Marsal](https://www.alvarezandmarsal.com/node/97846); [AMLegals](https://amlegals.com/corporate-guarantees-and-the-constitutional-limits-of-gst-valuation/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (conclusion, para 122); bench added; s.74 quashing and circular point added.
- Key words: "The levy of GST on the corporate guarantee furnished prior 26th October, 2023 under Rule 28(2) is declared violative of Article 14 and 19(1)(g) ... however, levy gets attracted from this date in case the guarantees continue." (para 122(B))

#### State of West Bengal v. Calcutta Club Ltd. - Supreme Court (3 judges) - 3 Oct 2019
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 4184 of 2009 with C.A. 7497 of 2012 (*Ranchi Club*, service tax) and connected; AIR 2019 SC 5310; (2019) 13 SCALE 474. R.F. Nariman (author), Surya Kant and V. Ramasubramanian, JJ.
- Holding:
  - Mutuality survives the 46th Amendment (Art. 366(29A)). No sales tax on food and drinks supplied by members' clubs (incorporated or not) to members.
  - Service tax: from 2005 the Finance Act, 1994 did not levy service tax on members' clubs in incorporated form (before or after the 2012 negative list); SCNs against incorporated members' clubs declared void (paras 84-85). The service-tax holding is limited to incorporated clubs.
- Binding: Supreme Court (sales tax and service tax).
- Later history: Parliament inserted s.7(1)(aa) and s.2(17)(e) CGST (Finance Act 2021, retrospective from 1 Jul 2017, notified from 1 Jan 2022) to override mutuality for GST. Struck down by the Kerala HC (below).
- Use in orders: Expect clubs and associations to cite it with *IMA*.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – State of WB v. Calcutta Club, 3 Oct 2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/42605759/); [LiveLaw – no sales tax on club supplies](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/no-levy-of-sales-tax-on-supply-of-food-by-incorporated-clubs-to-its-members-sc-read-judgment-148749); [ITATonline](https://itatonline.org/archives/state-of-west-bengal-vs-calcutta-club-limited-supreme-court-doctrine-of-mutuality-a-club-registered-as-a-company-u-s-25-of-companies-act-is-not-like-other-companies-as-it-has-no-shareholders-no/); [BCAJ](https://bcajonline.org/journal/calcutta-club-case-principle-of-mutuality-and-its-relevance-under-gst-regime/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers added; scope of the service-tax holding settled (incorporated clubs).
- Key words: "The doctrine of mutuality continues to be applicable to incorporated and unincorporated members' clubs after the 46th Amendment". (answer (1))

#### Indian Medical Association v. Union of India - Kerala High Court (Division Bench) - 11 Apr 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.A. No. 1659 of 2024 with W.A. Nos. 1487 of 2024 and 468 of 2025; 2025:KER:30517. A.K. Jayasankaran Nambiar and Easwaran S., JJ. From the single judge's judgment of 23.07.2024 in W.P.(C) 21297/2023.
- Holding:
  - Sections 7(1)(aa) and 2(17)(e) (with explanation) of the CGST Act and the KGST Act are unconstitutional and void for lack of legislative competence. Mutuality is a constitutional premise that only a constitutional amendment can alter.
  - The retrospective operation was also unlawful.
  - The single judge (Jul 2024) had upheld the provisions and struck down only the retrospectivity.
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana (Kerala HC).
- Later history: The Union's SLP: SC issued notice on 25 Jul (year not stated; most likely 2025) with "no recovery steps against IMA." Interim protection to IMA only; no stay of the judgment found.
- Use in orders: In Telangana, the provisions remain on the statute book and binding on officers. When proceeding against clubs, record that the SC appeal is pending. Consider keeping adjudication in abeyance or making the order subject to the SC outcome.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – IMA v. UoI, Kerala HC, 11 Apr 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/157623745/); [Onmanorama – Kerala HC strikes down GST on clubs](https://www.onmanorama.com/news/kerala/2025/04/13/kerala-high-court-strikes-down-gst-on-club-supplies.html); [EY alert](https://www.ey.com/content/dam/ey-unified-site/ey-com/en-in/alerts-hub/2025/04/provisions-under-gst-deeming-transactions-between-association-and-its-members.pdf); [Courtbook – SC notice to IMA](https://courtbook.in/posts/supreme-court-notice-to-ima-in-gst-case-on-club-member-services)
- Verified: Primary for the HC judgment - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers and bench added; summary confirmed. The SC notice order was not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 (still single source).
- Key words: "the impugned amendment to the CGST/SGST Acts must necessarily fail the test of constitutionality." (para 22)

#### B-II. Legal value of circulars, advance rulings, and judicial discipline

#### Commissioner of Central Excise, Bolpur v. Ratan Melting & Wire Industries - Supreme Court (Constitution Bench) - 14 Oct 2008
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 4022 of 1999 and connected; (2008) 13 SCC 1 (as cited by the SC in *Samtex Fashions*, 2026). Five judges: K.G. Balakrishnan CJI, Arijit Pasayat (author), Harjit Singh Bedi, P. Sathasivam and J.M. Panchal, JJ.
- Holding:
  - Board circulars bind departmental authorities but not the High Courts or the Supreme Court. They are the executive's understanding of the law.
  - A circular contrary to a judgment of the SC or an HC cannot be enforced.
  - The Revenue is not barred from appealing merely because of its own circular.
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history:
  - CBEC Circular 1006/13/2015-CX (21 Sep 2015): circulars contrary to SC judgments are non est.
  - Applied in *Samtex Fashions v. CIT* (SC, 18 Sep 2026, non-reportable).
- Use in orders: Follow CBIC circulars (they bind you), except where a Telangana HC or SC ruling is contrary; then the ruling prevails. Do not tell taxpayers a circular binds the court.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – CCE Bolpur v. Ratan Melting, 14 Oct 2008](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/189538/); [AMLegals – Circular 1006/13/2015-CX](https://amlegals.com/clarification-regarding-binding-nature-of-circular-and-instructions/); [Verdictum – Samtex Fashions](https://www.verdictum.in/supreme-court/ms-samtex-fashions-ltd-v-commissioner-of-income-tax-new-delhi-cbdt-circulars-1622298); [TaxHeal – circulars contrary to SC](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=1617)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, case number and bench added; summary confirmed.
- Key words: "So far as the clarifications/circulars issued by the Central Government and of the State Government are concerned they represent merely their understanding of the statutory provisions. They are not binding upon the court." (para 6)

#### Samtex Fashions Ltd. v. Commissioner of Income Tax - Supreme Court - 18 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 11063 of 2017 with C.A. No. 12449 of 2017. NON-REPORTABLE. S.V.N. Bhatti (author) and N.V. Anjaria, JJ. Published on Indian Kanoon together with *Orient Crafts Ltd. v. CIT* (2026 INSC 1018, same bench, 18 Sep 2026).
- Holding: Applying *Ratan Melting*, CBDT circulars bind tax authorities but not courts. A CBDT office memorandum cannot override the statute (s.80HHC).
- Binding: Supreme Court, but a non-reportable judgment; it restates *Ratan Melting* rather than laying down new law. Cite *Ratan Melting* as the main authority.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Recent restatement of the circulars principle.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – Orient Crafts v. CIT with Samtex Fashions v. CIT, 18 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/179254833/); [Verdictum – CBDT circulars bind authorities not courts](https://www.verdictum.in/supreme-court/ms-samtex-fashions-ltd-v-commissioner-of-income-tax-new-delhi-cbdt-circulars-1622298)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date fixed (18 Sep 2026), case numbers added; flagged as non-reportable.
- Key words: "The CBDT O.M. is binding on Revenue Officers. The assessee can challenge it before the Court." (para 15)

#### Union of India v. Kamlakshi Finance Corporation Ltd. - Supreme Court - 24 Sep 1991
- Citation / case no.: AIR 1992 SC 711; 1991 (55) ELT 433 (SC); 1992 Supp (1) SCC 443 (the "SCC 648" reference in one commentary is wrong as a Supp citation; Indian Kanoon also lists (1992) 1 SCC 648). Order by S. Ranganathan J.
- Holding: Strongly deprecated Assistant Collectors who refused to follow orders of the appellate authorities (Collector (Appeals) / CEGAT). Such conduct causes "undue harassment to assessees and chaos in administration of tax laws." Principles of judicial discipline require lower authorities to follow higher appellate orders unless those orders are stayed.
- Binding: Supreme Court.
- Later history: Widely followed (e.g. Karnataka HC 2018 criticising non-adherence to CESTAT).
- Use in orders: Follow the Telangana HC, and the orders of the Appellate Authority and Tribunal binding on you. If you disagree, record that the department is appealing; do not refuse to apply them.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon – UoI v. Kamlakshi Finance, 24 Sep 1991](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/13265/); [Casemine – principles of judicial discipline](https://www.casemine.com/in/column/principles-of-judicial-discipline-in-india/view)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (24 Sep 1991) and citations fixed; summary and quotation confirmed.
- Key words: "The principles of judicial discipline require that the orders of the higher appellate authorities should be followed unreservedly by the subordinate authorities." (para 6)

#### Advance rulings: status (statute and High Court treatment)
- **Section 103 CGST:** an advance ruling binds only the applicant and its concerned or jurisdictional officer, and only while the law, facts and circumstances are unchanged. Section 103(1A) (National Appellate AAR) was inserted by Finance (No. 2) Act 2019 but reported as not yet effective; check.
- **High Court rulings (court names not identified in snippets):**
  - (i) The BEML/BMRCL matter: an advance ruling is in personam, so a third party (BMRCL) is not an "aggrieved person" and has no locus. Writ dismissed. Likely Karnataka HC (unconfirmed).
  - (ii) An SCN relying on an advance ruling obtained by an unrelated taxpayer was set aside, because advance rulings are person-specific.
- Commentary treats AAR rulings as persuasive only for others.
- **Use in orders:** Do not base a demand on another taxpayer's advance ruling. Reason independently, and cite it only as persuasive if at all.
- Sources: [GSTGyaan – s.103](https://www.gstgyaan.com/section-103-of-the-cgst-act-applicability-of-advance-ruling); [Jurishour – third parties and advance rulings](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/third-parties-gst-advance-rulings-financial-impact/); [A2Z Taxcorp – AAR of another GSTIN not binding](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/aar-ruling-of-another-distinct-gstin-holder-even-under-same-brand-cant-be-applied-bindingly-to-a-different-gstin-holder-with-an-independent-business/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries. The High Court names were not confirmed, so no separate case entries are made. (Not a single case; not searched on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026.)

### 4. Gaps and doubts

*Update 14 Oct 2026: items 2, 3, 5, 8, 9, 10, 13 and 14 below were resolved by reading the judgments; item 12 is partly resolved (Vemula Yougander is 2023, not 2026). See the Verification log at the end.*

1. **Search budget exhausted.** The shared web-search limit was reached partway through. These items could not be searched:
   - Mohit Minerals neutral/SCC citation
   - Neeraj Paper Marketing citation
   - Kanishka Matta citation
   - GST-on-royalty status in the SC
   - Telangana HC rulings on cash seizure and search-time DRC-03
2. **Radhika Agarwal, coercion during search.** The brief says the SC addressed coercion during search to pay tax and compliance with circulars and instructions. Search summaries confirmed validity, reasons to believe, CrPC/BNSS applicability, anticipatory bail and written grounds. They did **not** confirm the paragraphs on coercion and refund. Read paras on "voluntary payment" before citing.
3. **Instruction 01/2022-23.** No CBIC instruction with this number on arrest, search or DRC-03 was found. The arrest guidelines are Instruction 02/2022-23 (17 Aug 2022), and the summons guidelines are 03/2022-23. The CBIC instruction on voluntary payments during search may be a different instrument (possibly a 2022 instruction issued after Bhumi Associate); not identified.
4. **Instruction 01/2025 timing.** TaxGuru says it "followed" Radhika Agarwal, but it is dated 13 Jan 2025, before the 27 Feb 2025 judgment. It more likely followed Delhi HC rulings (*Kshitij Ghildiyal*) and earlier SC PMLA/UAPA rulings.
5. **Sunil Biyani date.** Reported as 15 Jul, 12 Aug or 13 Aug 2026.
6. **Deepak Khandelwal SLP.** The dismissal date is disputed (2024 vs 17 Apr 2025), a review dismissal is reported for 14 Aug 2025, and one source lists the SLP as pending.
7. **SC on cash seizure.** Notice issued Oct 2024 (Narasimha and Mehta, JJ.); final outcome not found.
8. **P.V. Ramana Reddy date.** 18 Apr 2019 (most sources) vs 2020 (Casemine, apparently wrong). The SLP dismissal date was reported as 27 May 2019.
9. **Choodamani.** Exact date and citation not confirmed. Whether its bar on s.438 extends to s.70-only summons is debated. Reconcile with Radhika Agarwal and Sunil Biyani.
10. **Bharat Bhushan v. DGGI (SC 2025).** Single source; date and citation not found.
11. **MakeMyTrip.** The STR citation is not confirmed, and reports of its SC history conflict.
12. **Telangana High Court gaps.** No 2023–2025 Telangana HC arrest ruling was found. The 2026 Telangana rulings (*Vemula Yougander*, *Devendra Surana*, *Shailesh Pandey*) come from single summaries, and dates and benches are partly missing. The Telangana writ on GST on royalty (Nov 2025) was at notice stage; outcome unknown. A 2019 Telangana HC order in *G. Eswara Rao v. DGGI* (anticipatory bail) and a *VS Ferrous* order (arrest before assessment) were mentioned but not verified.
13. **Northern Operating Systems.** Exact date and Civil Appeal number not confirmed (2022 INSC 598 per LawSathi).
14. **Kamlakshi Finance.** Year and citation (1991 vs 1992 Supp (1) SCC 648; ELT citation) not confirmed.
15. **IMA (Kerala).** The SC notice date (25 Jul) has no year in the source. No stay of the HC judgment was found, and the SLP number was not found.
16. **Gameskraft.** Whether any limitation or valuation issues for the period before 1 Oct 2023 survive is unclear. The statute or rules giving 1 May 2026 as the prohibition date are reported inconsistently.
17. **Section 132 thresholds and amendments.** The Rs 5 crore cognizable/non-bailable split (s.132(4)–(5)) is statutory. Finance Act 2023 raised the prosecution threshold for some offences to Rs 2 crore (not fake invoices) and changed compounding. That is general knowledge, not checked in this search. Any Finance Act 2026 changes to ss.69, 132 or 138 were not checked.
18. **Calcutta Club.** How far the service tax holding extends is described differently by different sources.
19. **Advance-ruling High Court cases.** The courts deciding the BEML/BMRCL and "third-party AAR" matters are not identified.
20. **Not covered (as instructed):** *Vijay Madanlal*, *Ecom Gill*, *Bharti Airtel* (in another file).

Each judgment was read on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026. Entries: 37 in total.

Ramana Reddy; Sapna Jain; Vimal Goswami; MakeMyTrip; Kshitij Ghildiyal; Ashish Tyagi; Sushant Goel; Choodamani; Bharat Bhushan; Vemula Yougander; Devendra Surana; Shailesh Pandey; Bhumi Associate; Vallabh Textiles; Neeraj Paper Marketing; Deepak Khandelwal; Gunjan Bindal (SC); Kanishka Matta; Kusuma Poonacha; Mohit Minerals; Northern Operating Systems; Future Gaming; Gameskraft (SC); Mineral Area Development Authority; Torrent Power; Calcutta Club; IMA (Kerala HC); Ratan Melting; Samtex Fashions; Kamlakshi Finance.

  - Bharat Bhushan: the date is 5 Jul 2024, not 2025. SLP (Crl.) 8525/2024. Protection was for six weeks.

  - Gameskraft (SC): the outcome is valuation under Rules 31B/31C (retrospective). 28% was the Revenue's case. Time limits for replies and orders added.
  - Torrent Power: the s.74 action was quashed and the circulars set aside only to the extent they are contrary.

## File 05: GST Case Law - Telangana High Court (with persuasive Andhra Pradesh HC rulings)

**Scope.** GST judgments of the Telangana High Court (including the pre-2019 common High Court at Hyderabad) and a few Andhra Pradesh High Court rulings that a Telangana officer may meet as persuasive authority. Telangana HC rulings bind CGST and State Tax officers in Sangareddy. Coverage: arrest, e-way bill detention, the s.168A limitation notifications (13/2022, 9/2023, 56/2023), s.73 time limits and composite notices, service through the portal ("Additional Notices" tab), unsigned notices and orders, DRC-01 summaries, provisional attachment, cancellation of registration, ITC (s.16(4), s.16(5), s.16(2)(c)), interest under s.50, refunds, TRAN-1, appeal limitation and pre-deposit, and stay of recovery while GSTAT is not functioning.

**Date compiled:** 13 October 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries; on 14 Oct 2026, 28 of 34 entries were checked against the judgment text (marked "Primary"). Entries still marked "Secondary" must be verified before citing in an order.**
> The web-search budget for this session ran out before every issue could be covered (refund interest, more e-way bill cases, AP HC portal-service rulings and works-contract rate litigation were not searched). See "Gaps and doubts".

| 25 | ITC - s.16(5) | H.R. Constructions | AP HC, 06.08.2026 | s.16(5) retrospectively validates FY 2018-19 ITC in returns filed up to 30.11.2021 | PERSUASIVE |
| 26 | ITC - s.16(2)(c) supplier default | Telangana batch, W.P. Nos. 6880, 6883, 6897, 6899, 6903, 6933 of 2025 | Telangana HC, interim order 18.03.2025 | Interim order; no final decision found | PENDING |
| 27 | Interest s.50 | Megha Engineering & Infrastructures Ltd v. Commissioner | Telangana HC, 18.04.2019 | Interest on gross liability | BINDING when decided; overtaken by retrospective s.50 proviso (verify) |
| 28 | Refund - IDS / concessional rate | Micro Systems and Services | Telangana HC, 05.09.2022 | Circular 173/05/2022 (IDS refund for concessional-rate output) clarificatory, applies from 31.03.2020; remand | BINDING IN TELANGANA |
| 29 | Refund - zero-rated, hearing | Aurobindo Pharma v. State of Telangana | Telangana HC, 05.01.2026 | Compensation-cess refund: by consent, following W.P. 2391/2023 batch, remanded for fresh decision after hearing within 4 months | BINDING IN TELANGANA |
