---

### Quick answers

**Arrest (s.69/s.132).** Telangana HC in *P.V. Ramana Reddy* (2019, SLP dismissed) upheld arrest before assessment in fake-ITC cases and held "reasons to believe" need only be recorded on file, not in the arrest authorisation. A 2023 Telangana ruling (*Vemula Yougander*, following the SC in *Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer*) holds anticipatory bail under s.438 CrPC is not available at the summons stage. **Takeaway:** record reasons to believe in the file before authorising arrest; keep the record ready for the Court. Check the Supreme Court's later ruling on arrest safeguards (see Gaps) before relying on Ramana Reddy alone.

**E-way bill detention.** *Satyam Shivam Papers* (2021; SC dismissed the department's SLP in 2022 and enhanced costs): expiry of an e-way bill caused by a genuine reason is not evidence of intent to evade. **Takeaway:** a MOV-09 order must record why the officer infers evasion; a mere technical lapse with a plausible explanation will not support s.129 tax and penalty, and costs can be awarded personally.

**Limitation-extension notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023.** Telangana HC in *Brunda Infra* (02.01.2025) neither upheld nor struck them down: it treated the challenge as academic in view of the SC's Covid exclusion (15.03.2020-28.02.2022), while holding that later ratification cannot substitute a Council recommendation (relevant to 56/2023). The point is pending before the SC (SLP(C) 4240/2025). **Takeaway:** for FY 2019-20 orders relying on 56/2023, expect challenge; record the SC pendency and avoid conceding invalidity. For SCNs, compute the s.73(2)/(10) dates carefully: Telangana and AP HCs have quashed late notices.

**s.73(2) time limits and composite notices.** Telangana HC quashed a late s.73 SCN (*Balaji Filling Station*); AP HC holds the 3-month s.73(2) gap mandatory, even 2 days' delay fatal. AP HC (from 17.09.2025) quashes multi-year composite orders, but a Karnataka HC Division Bench (23.04.2026) holds composite SCNs permissible; Telangana HC has stayed a composite ss.74/74A/130 SCN (*RR Metal Industries*). **Takeaway:** issue SCNs at least 3 months before the s.73(10) deadline; issue separate SCNs and orders per financial year.

**Notices only on the portal ("Additional Notices" tab).** Telangana HC has not quashed orders outright on this ground in the cases found; it relegates taxpayers to appeal with a delay-condonation application, which the appellate authority decides on its own assessment (*Bollant Industries*, *Sri Sai Ram Traders*). **Takeaway:** also serve by email/RPAD under s.169 and record service; an appellate authority should not dismiss such appeals on delay without a hearing (s.107(8) - the State conceded this in one 2026 case).

**Unsigned SCNs / orders / DRC-07.** **Update 14 Oct 2026: the Full Bench in *Gudla Vanaja* (30.09.2026, file 03) overruled Bigleap. Electronically authenticated SCNs and orders are valid without a visible signature, but a notice or order without a DIN/RFN is invalid. Takeaway now: always quote the DIN/RFN and issue from the officer's own login; digital signature remains good practice.** Earlier position: *Bigleap Technologies* (Division Bench, 28.02.2025) holds unsigned SCNs and orders cannot stand (with liberty to re-issue, limitation no bar); *Water Tech Engineers* (22.04.2025) follows; *Lakshmi Enterprises* (2026) is unverified. In *SBC Chem* (2026) the petitioner itself chose the appeal route, so the point was not decided. **Takeaway:** digitally sign every SCN, order and DRC-07/DRC-01 summary and ensure the signed PDF (not just the summary) is uploaded.

**DRC-01 summary vs SCN.** No Telangana ruling found on summary-instead-of-SCN; Gauhati HC (*Construction Catalysers*) quashes such orders. Telangana HC will not entertain writs against an SCN itself (*S. Shivraj Reddy Constructions*). **Takeaway:** always issue a full reasoned SCN with the DRC-01 summary.

**Provisional attachment (s.83).** Telangana HC consistently holds attachment lapses after one year (*Greenfinch*, *KMC Constructions*, *Aarush Enterprises*). **Takeaway:** diarise the one-year date; a fresh attachment needs fresh material and a fresh order.

**Cancellation of registration.** Orders on a ground not in the SCN, or without hearing, are quashed (*JK Technology*, *Sheeshmahal*); a notice is upheld where documents made the allegation clear and the taxpayer replied (*Trillion Lead Factory*). **Takeaway:** state the specific s.29(2) clause and facts in REG-17, attach documents, give a hearing, and give reasons for any retrospective date.

**ITC.** s.16(4) time-bar was upheld (AP HC, *Thirumalakonda Plywoods*), but s.16(5) (Finance (No.2) Act 2024, retrospective) now protects FY 2017-18 to 2020-21 ITC claimed in returns filed by 30.11.2021 (AP HC *H.R. Constructions*, 2026). s.16(2)(c) challenge pending in Telangana HC. **Takeaway:** drop s.16(4) demands covered by s.16(5); in supplier-default cases, record why the recipient's claim fails, and note the pending Telangana batch.

**Interest (s.50).** *Megha Engineering* (2019) held interest is on gross liability, but s.50 was later amended (proviso, made retrospective from 01.07.2017 - verify) to charge interest on the cash portion for timely-filed returns. **Takeaway:** do not cite Megha Engineering for gross-liability interest.

**Refunds.** Telangana HC remands refund rejections for hearing (*Aurobindo Pharma*, 2026) and allowed IDS refunds for concessional-rate supplies (*Micro Systems*, 2022); some 2026 benches relegate to appeal. **Takeaway:** issue RFD-08 with specific grounds and give a hearing before RFD-06; apply the SC Covid exclusion to s.54 limitation (AP HC view).

**TRAN-1.** *Standard Chartered Bank* (2024): transitional credit allowed despite filing through another State's branch portal due to glitch; SLP reportedly dismissed. **Takeaway:** do not reject TRAN-1 claims solely on portal/technical grounds.

**Appeals, pre-deposit and GSTAT.** Telangana HC routinely gives taxpayers 2 weeks to 45 days to file s.107 appeals with condonation and pre-deposit, rather than deciding merits. In Apr 2026 it directed compliance with Circular 224/18/2024 (20% + undertaking within 15 days) for stay of recovery. **Takeaway:** once 20% is paid and the undertaking filed, stop recovery of the balance; record the s.112(8)/(9) position before any recovery.

---

### 1. Arrest and summons

#### P.V. Ramana Reddy v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench: V. Ramasubramanian and P. Keshava Rao JJ) - 18.04.2019
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 4764, 4769, 4892, 5074, 5130, 5329, 6952 and 7583 of 2019; SC: SLP (Crl.) No. 4430 of 2019; reported as 2019 (26) G.S.T.L. J175 (SC) for the SC order.
- Holding: Writs under Art. 226 are maintainable against threatened s.69 arrest, but protection was refused and the writs dismissed; arrest can precede assessment/adjudication; "reasons to believe" must be on file but need not be stated in the arrest authorisation.
- Key words: "If reasons to believe are recorded in the files, we do not think it is necessary to record those reasons in the authorization for arrest under Section 69(1) of the CGST Act." (para 46)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: SC dismissed the SLP on 27.05.2019 (per secondary sources; SC order not found on Indian Kanoon). Karnataka and Bombay HCs took a different line on anticipatory bail. The SC's 2025 judgment on GST/customs arrests (Radhika Agarwal v. Union of India) lays down safeguards - check it alongside this case.
- Use in orders: Support for arrest authorisation where reasons to believe are recorded on file.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/87700723/); [Casemine commentary](https://www.casemine.com/commentary/in/telangana-high-court-upholds-arrest-under-cgst-act,-limiting-use-of-article-226-writ-petitions/view); [LiveLaw - SC upholds Telangana HC](https://livelaw.in/top-stories/gst-evaders-can-be-arrested-145309); [taxo.online SLP 4430/2019](https://taxo.online/judgment/p-v-ramana-reddy-vs-union-of-india-2019-26-g-s-t-l-j175-supreme-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and full W.P. numbers added; holding refined (writ maintainable but protection refused). SLP dismissal still secondary.

#### Vemula Yougander v. Union of India - Telangana HC (K. Surender J) - 14.11.2023
- Citation / case no.: Criminal Petition No. 10430 of 2023 (s.438 CrPC; DGGI Hyderabad summons). Related: W.P. No. 34106 of 2023 decided 19.12.2023; SC: SLP (Crl.) No. 1154 of 2024 dismissed 29.01.2024.
- Holding: Following the SC in State of Gujarat v. Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer (2023 SCC OnLine SC 1043), a person summoned under the CGST Act cannot seek s.438 CrPC anticipatory bail because no FIR precedes a s.69(1) arrest; petition disposed of with liberty to invoke writ jurisdiction.
- Key words: "The Honourable Supreme Court held that at the stage of summons the person summoned cannot invoke Section 438 of the Code of Criminal Procedure." (para 7)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (the binding rule is really the SC's in Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer - cite that).
- Later history: The petitioner's follow-up writ (W.P. 34106/2023, decided 19.12.2023) went to the SC; SLP (Crl.) 1154/2024 dismissed in limine on 29.01.2024 (Sanjiv Khanna and Dipankar Datta JJ).
- Use in orders: Oppose s.438 applications filed at summons stage; cite Choodamani Parmeshwaran Iyer (SC) as the main authority.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26153819/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 29.01.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/199058343/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1086968)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date was "2026" -> 14.11.2023, single judge, case no. added, SC basis and SLP history added.

### 2. E-way bill detention (s.129)

#### Satyam Shivam Papers Pvt Ltd v. Asst. Commissioner (ST) - Telangana HC (Division Bench: M.S. Ramachandra Rao and T. Vinod Kumar JJ) - 02.06.2021
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 9688 of 2020; SC: SLP(C) No. 21132 of 2021, order 12.01.2022.
- Holding: E-way bill lapsed because an anti-CAA agitation blocked traffic and the next two days were a weekend; no presumption of intent to evade arises merely from expiry; the officer ignored the MOV-07 reply. MOV-09 levy of Rs 69,000 set aside, refund with 6% interest, Rs 10,000 costs on the officer.
- Key words: "On account of non-extension of the validity of the e-way bill by petitioner or the auto trolley driver, no presumption can be drawn that there was an intention to evade tax." (para 42)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA; SC order also binding.
- Later history: SC (Dinesh Maheshwari and Hrishikesh Roy JJ) dismissed the department's SLP on 12.01.2022, found no question of law, and imposed further costs of Rs 59,000 on the petitioners (total costs Rs 69,000).
- Use in orders: A s.129 order must find intent/evasion on facts, not rest only on expiry of validity; deal with the MOV-07 explanation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/13104689/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 12.01.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17589683/); [Moneylife](https://moneylife.in/article/gst-sc-imposes-rs69000-penalty-for-harassing-assessee-for-delay-in-goods-transportation-due-to-road-blockade/66238.html); [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-correctly-set-aside-tax-demand-penalty-for-expiry-of-e-way-bill-as-goods-not-delivered-in-time-due-to-genuine-reason-sc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; costs resolved (HC Rs 10,000; SC added Rs 59,000); reason for blockade corrected (agitation, not a "political rally").

### 3. Limitation: s.168A notifications and s.73 time limits

#### Brunda Infra Pvt Ltd v. Additional Commissioner of Central Tax (and batch) - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Sujoy Paul and Dr G. Radha Rani JJ) - 02.01.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1154 of 2024 and batch (lead petitioner M/s Brunda Infra Pvt Ltd; Indian Kanoon lists the common judgment under several batch titles, e.g. Sri Venkateswara Films); 2025 (1) TMI 299 (per one source).
- Holding: (i) s.168A notifications need a prior GST Council recommendation; "may" does not make it directory, and later ratification cannot substitute the recommendation (said in the context of 56/2023, which rested on a Law Committee decision) (paras 94-96, 102). (ii) But the SC's suo motu order excluding 15.03.2020-28.02.2022 applies to GST limitation, so the challenge to 13/2022, 9/2023 and 56/2023 became academic and was not decided (paras 116, 118). Writs disposed of; liberty to appeal within 45 days, appeal to be decided on merits, not limitation.
- Key words: "we are unable to follow the line of argument of learned Senior Standing Counsel for CBIC and learned Additional Solicitor General that 'ratification' can be a substitute of 'recommendation'." (para 96)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA. Note: it did NOT uphold 56/2023 and did NOT hold that a Council recommendation is unnecessary (that was the Revenue's submission at para 98, misreported by some summaries). The operative ratio is the Covid-exclusion route.
- Later history: Under challenge in SC, SLP(C) No. 4240/2025 (HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV) - see next entry. Other HCs split: Allahabad (Graziano Transmissioni) and Patna (Barhonia Engicon) cited in this judgment; Gauhati quashed 56/2023; Madras reportedly held 56/2023 ultra vires.
- Use in orders: For FY 2018-19/2019-20 adjudications, rely primarily on the SC Covid exclusion (15.03.2020-28.02.2022) to show the order is in time; do not rely on 56/2023 alone, since this bench doubted it. Keep a record of the actual dates of SCN and order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - common judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/90854768/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelawbiz.com/tax-cases/telangana-hc-upholds-2023-notification-extending-lmitation-issuing-gst-notices-after-covid-19-pandemic-ceased-recommendation-of-gst-council-cant-be-substituted-by-subsequent-ratification-280404); [Jurishour](https://jurishour.in/gst/gst-council-recommendation-not-a-precondition-for-issuing-s-168a-notification-telangana-high-court); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-upholds-validity-gst-notification-no-09-2023-56-2023.html); [A2Z Taxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/hc-upholds-gst-notifications-extending-notice-deadlines/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date Jan 2025 -> 02.01.2025, case no. W.P. 1154/2024 added; holding corrected - notifications were neither upheld nor struck down; Court held ratification cannot replace recommendation but decided the batch on the SC Covid exclusion; Jurishour's "recommendation not a precondition" was the Revenue's argument, not the holding.

#### HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV v. Assistant Commissioner of State Tax - Supreme Court (J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ) - notice issued 21.02.2025 (pending)
- Citation / case no.: SLP (C) No. 4240 of 2025, arising from the Telangana HC judgment dated 02.01.2025 in W.P. No. 32883 of 2024 (part of the Brunda Infra batch).
- Holding: No final ruling. The SC framed the issue as whether the s.73 adjudication time limit for FY 2019-20 could be extended by Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 under s.168A, noted a "cleavage of opinion" among HCs, and issued notice on the SLP and interim relief.
- Key words: "The issue that falls for the consideration of this Court is whether the time limit for adjudication of show cause notice and passing order under Section 73 ... for financial year 2019-2020 could have been extended by issuing the Notifications in question" (para 5)
- Binding: Pending. Delhi HC orders (through late 2025) say connected cases will be governed by the SC's decision.
- Later history: Listed again (e.g. 21.03.2025, 09.04.2025, 23.04.2025 - adjourned to 14.05.2025, with connected SLPs). No final SC judgment found on Indian Kanoon as of 14 Oct 2026.
- Use in orders: Note pendency; where the order depends on 56/2023, record that validity is sub judice before the SC.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 21.02.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198807505/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 23.04.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/163692317/); [A2Z Taxcorp - SC to decide](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/supreme-court-to-decide-on-extension-of-time-limit-for-adjudicating-show-cause-notices-by-notification-u-s-168a-of-the-gst-act/); [Delhi HC W.P.(C) 5937/2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/68919052025CW59372025_155954.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, HC W.P. number (32883/2024) and later listings added; summary confirmed; final outcome not found.

#### Balaji Filling Station v. State Tax Officer, Bodhan - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 14.08.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 25574 of 2026.
- Holding: On the State's concession on instructions, an SCN dated 28.12.2023 for FY 2017-18 (order passed two days later, 30.12.2023) was contrary to s.73(2) TGST Act; SCN and order set aside. A concession-based order: no reasoning on how the 3-month gap is computed.
- Key words: "the impugned order dated 30.12.2023 arising out of the show cause notice dated 28.12.2023 ... cannot be sustained in the eye of law being in teeth of the specific mandate of timeline to be adhered under Section 73(2) of the Act." (para 4)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (but weak precedent - rests on the State's concession).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: SCN must issue at least 3 months before the s.73(10) deadline; late SCNs will be quashed (the State will not defend them).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/146036745/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1038612)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and W.P. number added; noted it rests on concession.

#### The Cotton Corporation of India v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) (Audit) (FAC), Vijayawada - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench: R. Raghunandan Rao and Harinath N. JJ) - 05.02.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1463 of 2025.
- Holding: For FY 2020-21 the s.73(10) order deadline was 28.02.2025, so under s.73(2) the SCN had to issue by 28.11.2024 (months counted to the corresponding date); an SCN of 30.11.2024 was late. The s.73(2) period is mandatory, a delay of even two days cannot be condoned, and the SCN was quashed.
- Key words: "the time permit set out under 73(2) of the Act is mandatory and any violation of that time period cannot be condoned, and would render the show cause notice otiose." (para 14)
- Binding: PERSUASIVE.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Same as above; count back exactly 3 months to the corresponding date.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/36822529/); [LiveLaw - timeline mandatory](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/andhra-pradesh-high-court/short-tax-timeline-for-issuing-scn-under-section-732-is-mandatory-not-discretionary-andhra-pradesh-high-court-283510); [LiveLaw - two days delay](https://www.livelawbiz.com/amp/tax-cases/delay-of-two-days-in-issuing-gst-notice-cant-be-condoned-andhra-pradesh-high-court-284268); [EY alert](https://ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2025/02/hc-holds-scn-for-fy-2020-21-cannot-be-issued-after-28-nov-24)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name (Cotton Corporation of India), date, bench and W.P. number identified; summary confirmed.

#### S.J. Constructions v. Assistant Commissioner (lead) and later AP HC orders on composite multi-year SCNs / orders - Andhra Pradesh HC - 17.09.2025 (followed 18-19.09.2026)
- Citation / case no.: Lead: S.J. Constructions, W.P. No. 11028 of 2025 and batch, 17.09.2025. Followed in Tapaswini Constructions v. State of AP, W.P. No. 26668 of 2026, 18.09.2026, and Karthikeya Enterprises, W.P. No. 26791 of 2026, 19.09.2026 (both Ninala Jayasurya and T.C.D. Sekhar JJ).
- Holding: A single SCN or composite assessment order cannot cover more than one tax period (more than one financial year once the annual-return due date has passed); applies to s.74 as well as s.73. Tapaswini: composite order for 2021-24 set aside; Karthikeya: s.74 composite order for 2018-19 to 2022-23 set aside; both with liberty to issue separate year-wise notices.
- Key words: "a single show cause notice or a single composite assessment order cannot be passed in relation to more than one tax period" (S.J. Constructions, para 18, as quoted in Tapaswini, para 7)
- Binding: PERSUASIVE.
- Later history: CONTRARY VIEW: Karnataka HC Division Bench (S.G. Pandit and K.V. Aravind JJ), Principal Commissioner of Central Tax v. Pramur Homes and Shelters, W.A. No. 1751 of 2024 and batch, 23.04.2026, held a consolidated SCN for multiple financial years is permissible (Indian Kanoon doc 712560). No Telangana HC final ruling found.
- Use in orders: Safest course: issue separate SCNs and orders for each financial year (the views of HCs are split).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Tapaswini Constructions](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/179800156/); [Indian Kanoon - Karthikeya Enterprises](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/104329356/); [Indian Kanoon - S.J. Constructions](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/194541087/); [Indian Kanoon - Karnataka HC Pramur Homes (contrary)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/712560/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/single-gst-scn-or-composite-assessment-order-cannot-be-passed-for-more-than-one-tax-period-andhra-pradesh-hc-1448743); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/andhra-pradesh-high-court-quashes-composite-gst-assessment-order-spanning)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (Tapaswini and Karthikeya read in full; S.J. Constructions read as quoted); CORRECTED: party names, case numbers and dates added; the rule originates in a 17.09.2025 ruling, not 2026; the s.74 order covered 2018-19 to 2022-23 (not 2020-21 to 2023-24); contrary Karnataka DB ruling added.

#### RR Metal Industries v. Union of India - Telangana HC - date not found (interim order)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Interim stay of a composite SCN covering 2022-23 to 2025-26 under ss.74, 74A and 130; the Court questioned consolidated proceedings.
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (interim; not a final ruling).
- Later history: final outcome not found.
- Use in orders: Avoid combining multiple years and s.74/74A/130 in one SCN.
- Source: [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1073733)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; tshc.gov.in has no party-name judgment search usable without captcha). Note: Indian Kanoon has an unrelated earlier order in M/s RR Metal Industries v. Union of India, W.P. No. 13362 of 2024 (16.05.2024, withdrawn with liberty to appeal) - do not confuse the two.

### 4. Service of notices and orders only on the portal

#### Bollant Industries Pvt Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 05.01.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 40430 of 2025.
- Holding: Writ (filed 29.12.2025) against a s.73 order of 24.12.2023 (FY 2018-19) said to have been seen only in Sept 2025 because it was uploaded in the "Additional Notices" tab; State relied on Glaxo Smith Kline (2020) 19 SCC 681 on delay. Court did not decide merits; liberty to appeal within two weeks with a delay-condonation application and pre-deposit; the appellate authority to consider delay and, if satisfied, decide on merits.
- Key words: "the appellate authority would consider the question of delay and if he is satisfied with the reasons explained in the delay condonation application, he shall decide the case on merits." (para 5)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Serve by an additional mode (email/RPAD) and keep proof. Note: the Court did NOT direct "sympathetic" consideration (that was only the petitioner's request); the appellate authority must consider the delay application on its own merits.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/84168360/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1050114); [Jurishour](https://www.jurishour.in/?p=1449850)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 05.01.2026 and W.P. 40430/2025 added; "to be considered sympathetically" was the petitioner's prayer, not the Court's direction.

#### Sri Sai Ram Traders v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Hyderabad-II Circle - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 07.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21118 of 2026.
- Holding: s.73 order and DRC-07 dated 29.12.2025 (FY 2021-22) passed ex parte after the SCN was uploaded in "Additional Notices and Orders", which the accountant did not check. No comment on merits; liberty to appeal within two weeks with pre-deposit and a delay-condonation application; if satisfied on delay, the appellate authority to decide on merits.
- Key words: "if the appellate authority is satisfied on the point of delay, proceed to decide the appeal on merits in accordance with law." (para 7)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: As above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/36953900/); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-hc-disposes-gst-writ-petition-grants-liberty-to-file-delayed-appeal)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number and respondent added; summary confirmed.

(Related 2026 practice: in *Win Creatives* and other matters the Court gave two weeks to appeal with condonation and pre-deposit; in one 2026 matter the State conceded an appeal was dismissed without hearing the condonation application "in the teeth of" s.107(8) and the matter was remanded. Sources: [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-gst-taxpayer-file-delayed-appeal-against-drc-07-order.html), [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=130643). Secondary.)

### 5. Unsigned SCNs, orders and DRC-07

#### Bigleap Technologies and Solutions Pvt Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Sujoy Paul ACJ and Renuka Yara J) - 28.02.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21101 of 2024 and batch.
- Holding: SCNs and orders lacking the proper officer's signature cannot withstand judicial scrutiny; the Rules and prescribed forms require signature; a contrary view on unsigned DRC-07 reached without considering the rule is per incuriam. Notices and orders set aside, BUT with liberty to the department to issue fresh SCNs/orders, limitation not being a hurdle.
- Key words: "the impugned show cause notices and the orders which are not pregnant with the signature of the Proper Officer cannot sustain judicial scrutiny." (para 47); "for undertaking this exercise afresh, the limitation will not be a hurdle for the respondents." (para 48)
- Binding: NO LONGER GOOD LAW (overruled 30.09.2026).
- Later history: Overruled by the Telangana HC Full Bench in *Gudla Vanaja v. Assistant Commissioner (ST)*, W.P. 20397/2025 batch, 30.09.2026 (paras 154-155): electronically authenticated SCNs/orders uploaded on the portal are valid without a visible signature; a notice or order without DIN/RFN is invalid. See file 03. (Earlier: one source said the SC directed that any SLP be listed with a related petition; no SC ruling found.)
- Use in orders: Digitally sign every SCN, order and DRC-07; upload the signed document. Where an unsigned order is quashed on this ground, the department may re-issue a signed SCN/order; cite para 48 on limitation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/131900579/); [Verdictum PDF](https://www.verdictum.in/app-lite/pdf_upload/bigleap-technologies-and-solutions-pvt-ltd-v-the-state-of-telanganawatermark-1693437.pdf); [SAG Infotech](https://blog.saginfotech.com/telangana-hc-unsigned-gst-scn-orders-cannot-withstand-judicial-scrutiny)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; ADDED the liberty to re-issue with limitation not a bar (para 48), which the summaries omitted.

#### Water Tech Engineers v. Assistant Commissioner of CGST - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Sujoy Paul ACJ and Renuka Yara J) - 22.04.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21072 of 2024 (CGST case; CBIC respondents).
- Holding: Followed Bigleap (W.P. 21101/2024, 28.02.2025): if DRC-01 and DRC-07 are not signed, for whatever reason, the orders are vitiated; the department's reasons for the unsigned DRC-07 (counter para 21 and "brief note") cannot prevail over the statutory requirement. Orders set aside with the same liberty to re-issue as in Bigleap.
- Key words: "if DRC-01 and DRC-07 are also not signed for whatever reason, the impugned orders are vitiated. The reasons assigned in paragraph No.21 of the counter and the brief note cannot prevail over the statutory requirement." (para 7)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: A later writ by the same firm (W.P. No. 23178 of 2026, against an order-in-original of 15.12.2025 by the Joint Commissioner, Medchal, and a rejection order of 12.05.2026) was withdrawn on 31.08.2026 with liberty to appeal; whether that order is the re-issued one is not stated. (Indian Kanoon also has an unrelated Patna HC case of the same name, CWJC 6457/2025.) Falls with Bigleap after the Full Bench in Gudla Vanaja (30.09.2026).
- Use in orders: As above; a written justification for not signing does not cure the defect.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/195279172/); [Indian Kanoon - W.P. 23178/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/96363520/); [Taxmann - DRC-07 without digital signature invalid](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/drc-07-without-digital-signature-is-invalid-hc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 22.04.2025, bench, W.P. number and respondent added; summary confirmed (it is a short order following Bigleap, not an independent ruling).

#### Lakshmi Enterprises - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 02.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Title of the order copy states unsigned GST orders are void ab initio and r.26(3) CGST Rules requires digital signature. Operative reasoning not seen.
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: As above.
- Source: [order PDF (title only seen)](https://fhwzizvulsqxfzkaqeia.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/judgments/unsigned-gst-orders-void-ab-initio-rule-263-of-cgst-rules-requires-digital-signature-high-court-of-telangana-0ad421.pdf)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (headline only) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; source PDF host not reachable). Do not cite until the order copy is obtained.

#### SBC Chem Corporation v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Division Bench: P. Sam Koshy and Narsing Rao Nandikonda JJ) - 04.05.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 14857 of 2026 (CGST; s.73 order-in-original 28.08.2024 and unsigned DRC-07 30.08.2024, FY 2019-20).
- Holding: Did not decide whether an unsigned DRC-01/DRC-07 is void: after arguing, the petitioner itself sought liberty to appeal, so the Court made no comment on merits and allowed an appeal within two weeks with pre-deposit and a delay-condonation application. *Manna Enterprises* and *Shree Ram Enterprises* reportedly followed a similar pattern (not checked).
- Key words: "since the petitioner seeks liberty to prefer an appeal before the Appellate Authority, this Court is not inclined to make any comment on the merits of the contentions raised by both parties." (para 6)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (but decides nothing on the signature point).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Shows that where the taxpayer opts for appeal the Court relegates. (The merits point is now governed by the Full Bench in Gudla Vanaja, 30.09.2026, which overruled Bigleap.)
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/22044861/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1086623); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1078498)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; respondent and facts added; CORRECTED nuance: relegation was at the petitioner's own request, not a bench preference.

### 6. Writ against SCN / DRC-01 summary vs SCN

#### S. Shivraj Reddy Constructions v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Hyderguda-Ashoknagar Circle - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 23.06.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 19108 of 2026.
- Holding: Writ against a DRC-01 (24.01.2025, FY 2021-22, arising from an AG audit) that included GST on quarry royalty; petitioner tried to confine the challenge to the royalty issue; the State said the SCN could not be stalled as it had other discrepancies. Petitioner directed to file objections and take part; liberty to challenge the adjudication order on all issues, including royalty. Short order: no general statement of principle on writs at SCN stage.
- Key words: "the petitioner is directed to participate in the proceedings by filing objections to the impugned show cause notice. If the petitioner is aggrieved by the adjudication order, it is at liberty to assail both levy of GST on royalty and other discrepancies" (para 5)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (as an example of practice; not a reasoned precedent).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Cite in counter-affidavits against writs filed at SCN/DRC-01 stage, together with the SC's order of 27.02.2026 in Trillion Lead Factory (SLP (C) 7101/2026: "no writ lies against an issuance of show cause notice") - see section 8.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/60577553/); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-on-gst-drc-01-writ-not-maintainable-at-show-cause-stage)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number added; CORRECTED: the order does not state a general rule that HCs "should ordinarily not interfere at SCN stage" - it simply directs participation.

#### Construction Catalysers Pvt Ltd v. State of Assam - Gauhati HC (Devashis Baruah J) - 26.09.2024
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 3912 of 2024 and batch.
- Holding: The DRC-01 summary is not a substitute for the s.73(1) SCN, and the s.73(3) statement attached to it cannot substitute the SCN either; the SCN, statement and order must be issued by the proper officer and authenticated under r.26(3); orders also violated s.75(4) (no hearing). Orders quashed; liberty for de novo s.73 proceedings, with the period from the summary SCN till service of the judgment excluded for s.73(10).
- Key words: "The Summary of the Show Cause Notice in GST DRC-01 is not a substitute to the Show Cause Notice to be issued in terms with Section 73 (1) of the Central Act as well as the State Act." (para 29(A))
- Binding: PERSUASIVE (no Telangana ruling on this point found).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Always issue a full, signed SCN with grounds; the DRC-01 summary is not enough.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/77184328/); [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/summary-of-scn-in-form-gst-drc-01-cannot-substitute-statutory-requirement-of-scn-under-cgst-act-hc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, judge and case number added; summary confirmed; added s.75(4) ground and the limitation-exclusion direction.

### 7. Provisional attachment (s.83)

#### Greenfinch Team Management P Ltd v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and C.V. Bhaskar Reddy J) - 22.12.2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 44235 of 2022.
- Holding: s.83(2) gives a provisional attachment a life of only one year from the s.83(1) order; bank attachments of 02.12.2019 and 08.12.2021 had spent their force and were quashed; the officer was told to inform all bankers. No view expressed on pending ss.67(2)/74 proceedings.
- Key words: "legislature itself has provided for a definite time line in sub-section (2) of Section 83 of the CGST Act mandating that a provisional attachment order would have a life span of only one year from the date of the order" (para 11)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (CGST case).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Track the one-year sunset; do not instruct banks to continue the freeze; inform the bank when it lapses.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/178995273/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-news/09-01-2023-provisional-attachment-under-section-83-can-only-be-temporary-and-not-for-an-indefinite-period-telangana-high-court); [abcaus](https://abcaus.in/tag/attachment-of-bank)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; full party name, bench added; summary confirmed.

#### KMC Constructions Ltd v. Principal Commissioner of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Division Bench: M.S. Ramachandra Rao and T. Amarnath Goud JJ) - 20.10.2020
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 18575 of 2020.
- Holding: Provisional attachment of 07.06.2019 could not continue after 06.06.2020 because of the one-year sunset in s.83(2) (conceded by CGST counsel); continuation would violate Arts. 14, 19(1)(g) and 300A and be without jurisdiction; bank directed to allow operation of the account forthwith. The Revenue's request for an observation that a fresh attachment may be issued was not granted.
- Key words: "the impugned provisional attachment order dt.07.06.2019 ... cannot continue after 06.06.2020 in view of sub-Section(2) of Section 83 of CGST Act, 2017, and any such continuation would be violative of Articles 14, 19(1)(g) and 300A" (para 8)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (CGST case - directly relevant).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: As above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/161737586/); [busy.in](https://busy.in/caselaw/m-s-kmc-constructions-limited-and-another-vs-principal-commissioner-of-central-tax-and-others-telangana-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and W.P. number added; summary confirmed.

#### Aarush Enterprises v. Commissioner - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Sujoy Paul and Namavarapu Rajeshwar Rao JJ) - 02.07.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 14809 of 2024 (CGST; CBIC respondent).
- Holding: s.83(2) is mandatory; the bank-account attachment of 19.05.2023 ceased to have effect on expiry of one year; internal departmental correspondence about "extending" it, with no decision, made no difference. Respondents left free to act otherwise in accordance with law.
- Key words: "it is couched in a mandatory language and therefore, we find no difficulty in holding that impugned provisional attachment order dated 19.05.2023 cease to have effect on expiry of period of one year" (para 7)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: A later order in M/s Aarush Enterprises v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax (06.01.2026) exists on Indian Kanoon; not checked.
- Use in orders: As above; s.83 has no power to extend an attachment beyond one year.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/22805733/); [taxtmi](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=13057)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 02.07.2024, bench and W.P. number added; holding refined (declared to have ceased, not "set aside").

### 8. Cancellation of registration

#### JK Technology v. Deputy State Tax Officer, Charminar - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 09.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21690 of 2026.
- Holding: REG-17 (08.08.2023) alleged only s.29(2)(e) (fraud/suppression), with no supporting documents; REG-19 (22.01.2024) cancelled under s.29(2)(c) (non-filing from 01.01.2023) w.e.f. 01.01.2023. Order quashed solely because it rested on a ground the petitioner was never put to notice; SCN also quashed and registration restored; liberty to issue a fresh SCN with specific grounds and supporting documents.
- Key words: "quashing the order of cancellation of registration for the sole reason that it is based upon a ground upon which the petitioner was never put to notice." (para 4)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: The REG-19 must rest on the ground stated in REG-17; state specific grounds and attach documents in REG-17.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/8041411/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-quashes-gst-registration-cancellation-grounds-scn.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number and dates of REG-17/REG-19 added; summary confirmed (the Court did not separately rule on the retrospective date).

#### Sheeshmahal Enterprises Pvt Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and N. Tukaramji J) - 28.03.2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8344 of 2023 (CGST).
- Holding: Suo motu cancellation (25.03.2021, non-filing for six months) where the petitioner said on oath it never received the SCN, yet the order recorded that its "reply dated 18.03.2021" had been considered: mechanical, non-application of mind. Cancellation order and the appeal order (rejected on delay, 30.01.2023) set aside; remanded for fresh order after reply and hearing within three months; returns may be filed during remand.
- Key words: "This only goes to show the mechanical approach adopted by respondent No.1 and reflects non-application of mind." (para 7)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Ensure the SCN is actually served and the REG-19 reflects the real record; give a hearing before REG-19.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185037428/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1063146)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number added; CORRECTED: it was a remand (not an outright quashing) and the defect was a non-application of mind (order cited a reply never filed). The taxguru item may refer to a later order of the same party; only this order was found.

#### Trillion Lead Factory Pvt Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax, Shamshabad Division - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 02.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 537 of 2026 (CGST; cancellation SCN dated 17.12.2025 under r.21(e) for ITC from non-existent suppliers, based on DGGI material). SC: SLP (C) No. 7101 of 2026, dismissed 27.02.2026.
- Holding: Though the REG-17 only alleged a r.21(e)/s.16 violation, it annexed the DGGI communication as the supporting document, and the petitioner's two replies met the allegations, showing it understood them; no prejudice; proper officer free to decide on the replies within two weeks. Writ disposed of; Delhi HC and A1 Adil Traders rulings held not to help.
- Key words: "No prejudice is likely to be caused, as the petitioner, having understood the contents of the charges contained in the show cause notice, adequately replied thereto." (para 6)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: SC (Aravind Kumar and Prasanna B. Varale JJ) dismissed the SLP in limine on 27.02.2026, holding "no writ lies against an issuance of show cause notice", citing Secretary, Ministry of Defence v. Prabhash Chandra Mirdha (2012) 11 SCC 565 and CCE v. Krishna Wax (P) Ltd. (C.A. 8609/2019).
- Use in orders: Attach relied-upon documents to REG-17; a detailed reply shows no prejudice. The SC order is useful against writs filed at SCN stage.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/29128302/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 27.02.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/72323089/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1043877)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number added; summary confirmed; SC dismissal of SLP added.

### 9. Input tax credit

#### Thirumalakonda Plywoods v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench: U. Durga Prasad Rao and T. Mallikarjuna Rao JJ) - 2023
- Citation / case no.: 2023 SCC OnLine AP 1476 (as cited by Kerala HC in M. Trade Links v. Union of India, 04.06.2024); main case W.P. No. 24235 of 2022 (per the AP HC proceeding sheets on Indian Kanoon).
- Holding: s.16(4) time limit is not violative of Arts. 14, 19(1)(g) or 300A; filing returns with late fee does not extend it; s.16(2) is a restricting provision whose non obstante clause does not override s.16(4) (para 19, as quoted by Kerala HC).
- Binding: PERSUASIVE.
- Later history: Followed by Kerala HC in M. Trade Links (04.06.2024). Overtaken in part by s.16(5) (inserted by Finance (No.2) Act 2024, retrospective from 01.07.2017) for FY 2017-18 to 2020-21. SLP status not found.
- Use in orders: Valid only for periods outside s.16(5)/(6).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Kerala HC, M. Trade Links (quotes para 19)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/168099905/); [Indian Kanoon - AP HC proceeding sheet W.P. 24235/2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/133345688/); [Metalegal](https://www.metalegal.in/post/the-andhra-pradesh-high-court-upholds-the-constitutional-validity-of-s-16-4-of-the-cgst-act); [Taxmann](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/high-court-upholds-time-limit-for-claiming-input-tax-credit-under-cgst-act)
- Verified: Secondary - final AP HC judgment not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 (only 2022 interim proceeding sheets); citation, case number and bench taken from Indian Kanoon records and the Kerala HC's quotation of para 19, which is consistent with the summary.

#### H.R. Constructions v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Andhra Pradesh HC (Division Bench: Ninala Jayasurya and T.C.D. Sekhar JJ) - 06.08.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 12234 of 2021.
- Holding: ITC for FY 2018-19 availed on 20.12.2019 (after the 20.10.2019 s.16(4) deadline) was disallowed by order of 18.02.2021; since s.16(5) allows such ITC in returns filed up to 30.11.2021 (not disputed by Revenue), the order was set aside. The petitioner gave up the challenge to the validity of s.16(4) and agreed to withdraw its pending appeal.
- Key words: "we deem it appropriate to set aside impugned order 18.02.2021 in view of introduction of Section 16(5) of GST Act, inasmuch as a time limit for availing Input Tax Credit was extended to 30.11.2021." (para 11)
- Binding: PERSUASIVE (the statute itself binds).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Drop s.16(4) disallowances for FY 2017-18 to 2020-21 where the ITC was taken in a return filed by 30.11.2021. Madras and other HCs reported to the same effect.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/193746300/); [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=140248)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; respondent, bench and W.P. number added; summary confirmed (Revenue did not dispute the s.16(5) point).

#### Telangana s.16(2)(c) batch - W.P. Nos. 6880, 6883, 6897, 6899, 6903 and 6933 of 2025 - Telangana HC - interim order 18.03.2025
- Citation / case no.: as stated (W.P. No. 6880 of 2025 and analogous petitions; interim order dated 18.03.2025).
- Holding: Challenge to the vires of s.16(2)(c) and to an order-in-original of 27.11.2024 denying ITC for supplier default; interim order passed (rule nisi, records called for, per secondary source). No final decision found.
- Binding: PENDING.
- Later history: Confirmed on Indian Kanoon only through S.S. Infra v. Union of India (Telangana HC, Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J, W.P. No. 31795 of 2025, 23.10.2025), which distinguished the batch: S.S. Infra's own s.16(2)(c)/s.41(2) vires challenge to a 06.10.2023 order was not entertained because of a delay of over two years (Glaxo Smith Kline (2020) 19 SCC 681). Other HCs (Gauhati - Metal Syndicate; Tripura - 06.01.2026; Karnataka - Apr 2026) have read down s.16(2)(c) for bona fide purchasers (not checked here).
- Use in orders: In supplier-default cases, record evidence of payment and why the recipient is not bona fide (or proceed first against the supplier); note this pending batch. Oppose belated writs that piggy-back on the batch (S.S. Infra).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - S.S. Infra (refers to the 18.03.2025 interim order)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/76261317/); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/itc-reversal-for-supplier-default-under-section-162c-telangana-high-court)
- Verified: Secondary - batch orders not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; existence and interim-order date (18.03.2025) confirmed from the S.S. Infra order (read 14 Oct 2026).

### 10. Interest under s.50

#### Megha Engineering & Infrastructures Ltd v. Commissioner of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Division Bench: V. Ramasubramanian and P. Keshava Rao JJ) - 18.04.2019
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 44517 of 2018; AIR 2019 Telangana 23; 2019 (4) TMI 1319.
- Holding: Delay in GSTR-3B (Oct 2017-May 2018); tax is "paid" only when the return is filed and the ledger debited, so interest under s.50(1) was payable on the whole liability including the portion discharged by ITC; the GST Council's 31st-meeting in-principle approval to charge interest on net cash liability could not be used to read s.50 while the Act was unamended. Writ dismissed.
- Key words: "the claim made by the respondents for interest on the ITC portion of the tax cannot be found fault with. Hence, the Writ Petition is dismissed." (para 45)
- Binding: Was binding when decided.
- Later history: A review petition was reportedly admitted with interim stay (one source; not found on Indian Kanoon). s.50(1) was later amended by a proviso charging interest only on the cash portion for timely-filed returns; that it was made retrospective from 01.07.2017 is from general knowledge and was not confirmed in this check - verify against the statute notes.
- Use in orders: Do not rely on this case for gross-liability interest.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/159736439/); [IRIS GST](https://irisgst.com/?p=17754); [VJM Global](https://www.vjmglobal.com/blog/pay-interest-on-gross-tax-liability-for-late-gst-returns); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/confusion-interest-delayed-gst-payment.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: exact date 18.04.2019, bench, W.P. 44517/2018 and AIR citation added; it was a Telangana HC bench (not the "common HC"); summary confirmed.

### 11. Refunds

#### Micro Systems and Services v. Union of India - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and C.V. Bhaskar Reddy J) - 05.09.2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 37465 of 2021 (CGST; CBIC respondents).
- Holding: Circular No. 173/05/2022-GST (06.07.2022), which substituted para 3.2 of Circular 135/05/2020 (31.03.2020) to allow inverted-duty refund where the same goods are supplied at a lower rate under a concessional notification, is clarificatory and operates from 31.03.2020; refund rejection orders set aside and the claim remanded for fresh decision under the clarified circular. (The Court did not itself grant the refund.)
- Key words: "Being clarificatory, Circular dated 06.07.2022 inserting the above clarification would have the effect from the date when Circular dated 31.03.2020 came into effect." (para 14)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Do not reject IDS refund merely because the lower output rate comes from a concessional notification; apply Circular 173/05/2022 to earlier periods (unless the output is nil-rated/exempt or notified for exclusion).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/166094515/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/circular-would-be-applicable-retrospectively-refund-in-case-of-inverted-duty-structure-allowed-telangana-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, respondents and circular numbers added; CORRECTED nuance: the Court remanded rather than "allowed" the refund.

#### Aurobindo Pharma Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 05.01.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 35286 of 2025.
- Holding: Challenge to rejection (07.05.2025) of a refund of Compensation Cess of Rs 15.40 lakh (May 2019, refund claim of 29.04.2021). Both sides agreed the case was covered by the common order of 10.12.2025 in W.P. No. 2391 of 2023 and batch, where, the department being willing to reconsider in the light of Patson Papers and Atul Limited, the orders were set aside and remanded for fresh decision after hearing within four months. Disposed of in those terms.
- Key words: "the impugned Order-In-Original and the appellate order are set aside. The matter is remanded to the original authority to take a fresh decision in accordance with law after opportunity of hearing to the petitioner within a period of four (4) months." (para 4, quoting W.P. 2391/2023 batch, para 11)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA (consent-based; no independent reasoning).
- Later history: none found. Contrast: GBS Impex v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), W.P. Nos. 772 and 774 of 2026, 09.01.2026 (same bench): RFD-06 orders of 14.02.2025 (alleged no hearing under r.92(3)) - no comment on merits; liberty to appeal within two weeks with delay-condonation applications.
- Use in orders: Issue RFD-08, give a hearing, and deal with the reply before RFD-06; for compensation-cess refund claims, note the W.P. 2391/2023 batch.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26011992/); [Telangana HC - GBS Impex order copy](https://tshc.gov.in/documents/currentsection_2026_02_13T11_43_27.pdf); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-refund-rejection-set-matter-remanded-fresh-decision-telangana-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon and tshc.gov.in 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 05.01.2026, W.P. 35286/2025; the refund was of Compensation Cess and the remand followed a consent/department-willing order in W.P. 2391/2023 batch (10.12.2025), not a reasoned "zero-rated supplies" ruling; the tshc.gov.in PDF previously cited is the GBS Impex order, not Aurobindo.

#### AP HC on Covid exclusion for s.54 refunds (party name not found) - Andhra Pradesh HC
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: SC suo motu order excluding 15.03.2020 onwards (later to 28.02.2022) applies to s.54 limitation; refund rejection for Apr 2018-Jan 2019 set aside.
- Binding: PERSUASIVE (Bombay and Allahabad HCs reported to agree).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Exclude 15.03.2020-28.02.2022 when computing the two-year refund limit.
- Source: [supremetoday](https://supremetoday.ai/doc/judgement/00200057139); [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=116968)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; party name still unknown - do not cite without the order copy).

### 12. TRAN-1 / transitional credit

#### Standard Chartered Bank v. Principal Commissioner of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Sujoy Paul and Namavarapu Rajeshwar Rao JJ) - 11.07.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 648 of 2024; SC: SLP (C) Diary No. 2203 of 2025, dismissed 28.02.2025.
- Holding: TRAN-1 could not be filed on the Maharashtra portal due to a glitch (not disputed by Revenue); it was filed through the Telangana branch registration and the credit transferred the same day, which the last proviso to s.140(8) permits for a centralised registrant; the department must keep its portal functional and cannot take advantage of its own wrong. SCN dated 03.09.2021 and OIO dated 31.10.2023 set aside.
- Key words: "it was the duty of the Department to keep their portal functional. If the portal was not functional or having technical glitch ... the petitioner cannot be saddled with demand, interest and penalty." (para 27)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: SC (J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ) condoned delay and dismissed the Revenue's SLP on 28.02.2025 ("we see no reason to interfere").
- Use in orders: Do not deny transitional credit on purely technical/portal grounds where the credit is genuine and s.140(8) permits the transfer.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/184007786/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 28.02.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/114408747/); [AMLegals](https://amlegals.com/standard-chartered-bank/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=494538)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; respondent and bench added; SCN date resolved (03.09.2021); OIO date 31.10.2023 added; SLP dismissal confirmed (28.02.2025).

### 13. Appeals: limitation, pre-deposit and GSTAT

#### Nishka Packaging v. Appellate Joint Commissioner, Hyderabad Rural Division - Telangana HC (Division Bench: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 09.07.2026 (applying the Brunda Infra batch, 02.01.2025)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21553 of 2026 (petitioner's earlier writ was W.P. No. 34094 of 2024, part of the W.P. 1154/2024 Brunda Infra batch decided 02.01.2025).
- Holding: The January 2025 "batch" is the Brunda Infra judgment: the Revenue won on limitation of the adjudication (Covid exclusion, suo motu W.P.(C) 3/2020), but taxpayers got 45 days to appeal, the appeal to be decided on merits, not thrown out on limitation. Nishka appealed on 11.01.2025, within the 45 days, yet the appellate authority rejected it on 13.06.2025 as time-barred under s.107(1)/(4); that order was set aside and the appeal restored.
- Key words: "the rejection of appeal on the ground of limitation was not proper in the eye of law, as the appeal was preferred within the period of 45 days from the date of the judgment dated 02.01.2025 in W.P.No.1154 of 2024 and batch." (para 5)
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Appellate authorities must honour HC-granted appeal windows and decide such appeals on merits; s.107(4) does not override a High Court direction.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/82680540/); [Indian Kanoon - Brunda Infra batch](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/90854768/); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-appeal-filed-hc-granted-45-days-heard-merits-telangana-hc.html); [taxguru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1067131)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: party names found; the "January 2025 batch" is the Brunda Infra judgment (limitation of adjudication under the Covid exclusion, not appeal limitation); the restoration case is Nishka Packaging, 09.07.2026.

#### Telangana HC on stay of recovery pending GSTAT (party name not found) - Telangana HC - April 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Petitioner who lost first appeal sought stay; writ disposed directing compliance with Circular 224/18/2024-GST (11.07.2024) and Notification S.O. 4220(E): undertaking and 20% deposit within 15 days, leading to statutory stay of balance (s.112(9)).
- Binding: BINDING IN TELANGANA.
- Later history: Summaries report GSTAT members joined in early 2026 but hearings were still stabilising.
- Use in orders: On receipt of 20% + undertaking, stop recovery of the balance.
- Source: [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=124593); [taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=124624)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources describe outcome slightly differently) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; party name unknown - do not cite as a precedent without the order copy; Circular 224/18/2024 itself is the authority).

### 14. Works contract for government (rate change from 01.01.2022)

#### KPC Projects Ltd - Telangana AAR (B. Raghu Kiran and S.V. Kasi Visweswara Rao) - TSAAR Order No. 41/2022 (15.07.2022); Shree Constructions - TSAAR Order No. 56/2022 (08.12.2022)
- Citation / case no.: TSAAR 41/2022 (A.R.Com/01/2022, 15.07.2022); TSAAR 56/2022; also Siddhartha Constructions TSAAR 10/2022.
- Holding: KPC: works contracts for Telangana State Tourism Development Corporation (a "Government entity") were taxable at 6% + 6% up to 31.12.2021 under entry 3(vi) of Notification 11/2017-CT(Rate); after Notification 15/2021-CT(Rate) (18.11.2021) deleted "Government entity/Governmental authority" w.e.f. 01.01.2022, the rate is 9% + 9%.
- Key words: "the works executed for 'Governmental Entity or 'Government Authority' from 01.01.2022 are taxable @9% CGST & SGST each." (para 7)
- Binding: AAR - binding only on the applicant; no Telangana HC ruling found.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Rate for works for TSIIC/TSTDC-type bodies after 01.01.2022 is 18% unless they qualify as "government" or a "local authority".
- Source: [gstcouncil.gov.in - TSAAR 41/2022](https://gstcouncil.gov.in/sites/default/files/AAR/tsaar_order_no.41-2022_ms._kpc_projects_limited.pdf); [ClearTax](https://news.cleartax.in/telangana-aar-works-contract-service-govt-entities-attract-18-gst/8748/amp)
- Verified: Primary (TSAAR 41/2022 only) - read on gstcouncil.gov.in 14 Oct 2026; date 15.07.2022 and members added; recipient was TSTDC; summary confirmed. TSAAR 56/2022 and 10/2022 not re-read (Secondary).
