## File 06: 06 - Registration (cancellation, revocation, suspension), rectification under s.161, and vague show cause notices

**Scope.** Case law on (i) cancellation of GST registration under s.29 and rule 22 (SCN in REG-17, order in REG-19, hearing, reasons, retrospective effect), (ii) revocation under s.30 / rule 23 and court-ordered restoration after the time limit, (iii) suspension under rule 21A, (iv) cancellation for non-existence at the premises, (v) cancellation on the taxpayer's own request, liabilities that survive cancellation (s.29(3)), fresh registration after cancellation and refusal of registration (REG-05), (vi) rectification of returns (s.37(3)/s.39(9), Circulars 183/2022 and 193/2023) and rectification of orders under s.161, and (vii) vague show cause notices as an issue on their own. Written for a CGST Assistant Commissioner, Sangareddy Division, Telangana. The effect of a supplier's retrospective cancellation on the recipient's ITC is covered in **02-input-tax-credit.md (Issue 8)** and is only cross-referred here. Telangana HC registration cases already in **05-telangana-high-court.md (section 8)** are summarised briefly, not repeated in full.

**Date compiled:** 14 Oct 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries; on 14 Oct 2026, 43 of the 47 case entries were checked against the judgment text on Indian Kanoon (see each entry's "Verified" line and the Verification log at the end). Entries still marked Secondary have not been checked - verify before citing in an order.**

---

### Quick answers

**1. What the cancellation SCN (REG-17) must contain.**
Courts quash SCNs that only repeat the words of s.29(2) ("fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression", "contravention of the Act") without facts, periods or documents. Leading cases: SC *Brindavan Beverages* (2007, SCN is the foundation; vague SCN = no real opportunity); Gujarat HC *Aggarwal Dyeing* (24.02.2022); Delhi HC 2023-24 line; Madras *PAV Warehouse* (2024); Telangana *A1 Adil Traders* (2025; reasons must be in the SCN itself, not in an annexed internal letter) and *Rayees Metals*. Telangana *Trillion Lead Factory* (2026; SLP dismissed 27.02.2026) is the counterweight: no relief where the documents attached made the charge clear and the taxpayer replied in detail.
*Takeaway:* in REG-17 state the clause of s.29(2), the facts, the tax periods (for non-filing: which returns, which months), the inspection date and findings, and attach the relied-upon documents. Give a date and time for the personal hearing. Missing facts cannot be supplied later in the REG-19 order (*A1 Adil Traders*).

**2. Order on a ground not in the SCN; non-speaking REG-19; no hearing.**
An order that cancels on a different clause than the one proposed (Telangana *JK Technology*: SCN under s.29(2)(e), order under s.29(2)(c)) or that gives no reasons, or that is passed without hearing (Telangana *Sheeshmahal*: remand for non-application of mind; Madras *Suresh Trading*), is set aside. Gauhati HC (2026) requires reasons in REG-19 itself (single paywalled report; order not identified).
*Takeaway:* REG-19 must deal with the reply, record findings on the proposed ground only, and give reasons. If a new ground surfaces, issue a fresh or supplementary REG-17.

**3. Retrospective cancellation.**
Delhi HC (consistent 2023-2025 line: *Riddhi Siddhi Enterprises* W.P.(C) 8061/2024, *Aditya Polymers* W.P.(C) 14493/2022, *Aryan Timber* in file 02; *Subhana Fashion* is not a retrospectivity ruling - see Issue B): the power to cancel from a back date must rest on objective criteria and stated reasons, must have been proposed in the SCN, and cannot be used mechanically (e.g. back to 01.07.2017 for six months' non-filing). Remedies vary: *Riddhi Siddhi* quashed the order with liberty to proceed afresh; *Aditya Polymers* moved the date to the SCN date by consent of both sides; other Delhi orders use the SCN date, the suspension date or the date of the taxpayer's application.
*Contentions across courts:* Delhi HC is the main source; Gujarat and Telangana (*JK Technology*: "give reasons for retrospective date") are consistent. No contrary HC view found. No SC ruling found. No CBIC circular found that defends mechanical back-dating.
*Takeaway:* propose the retrospective date expressly in REG-17 with the reason (e.g. non-existence found on inspection dated X; bogus invoices from date Y) and justify that date in REG-19, noting that recipients' ITC will be affected.

**4. Revocation after the time limit; restoration on payment of dues.**
Statute: application within 90 days of service of the cancellation order, extendable by up to 180 days by the Commissioner/authorised officer (rule 23 as amended by Notf. 38/2023-CT; s.30 proviso omitted by Finance Act 2023 w.e.f. 01.10.2023). One-time amnesty: Notf. 3/2023-CT (cancellations up to 31.12.2022; apply by 30.06.2023, later 31.08.2023 per Notf. 23/2023 - see Gaps). Courts nevertheless restore registrations cancelled for non-filing once the taxpayer files all returns and pays tax, interest and late fee: Madras *Suguna Cutpiece Centre* (31.01.2022) and its many followers; Gauhati *Motaleb Bhuyan* (2025) and 2025-26 followers (restoration plus re-start of s.73(10) limitation); Telangana 2026 orders (*Adarsha Transport*, *KBG Associates*, *Check Security Force*) allowing a physical revocation application beyond the portal's 270-day lock and directing a decision within about 3 weeks.
*Contentions across courts:* Madras, Gauhati, Telangana, Uttarakhand, Kerala and Rajasthan HCs grant restoration or a fresh revocation window; Gujarat (*Radhe Packaging*) pointed the taxpayer to the amnesty notification. No SC ruling found on Suguna (no SLP found). Department's position: the rule 23 limits are statutory; the portal blocks applications after 270 days.
*Takeaway:* where the only ground was non-filing and the taxpayer has filed all returns and paid all dues, consider the revocation application on merits (drop proceedings under the rule 22(4) proviso / pass REG-22) rather than refusing only on time, especially once a Telangana HC order directs it. Record that restoration does not wipe out liabilities (s.29(3)).

**5. Suspension under rule 21A.**
Rule 21A (as amended Dec 2020) allows suspension with the SCN without a prior hearing. Courts still quash suspensions imposed automatically or without any factual basis: Madras *El Tech Power System* (10.03.2026, suspension in REG-17 "unwarranted"); Delhi *Shakti Shiva Magnets* (2022); Telangana *A1 Adil Traders* and *Rayees Metals* (suspension fell with a vague SCN). A Bombay HC challenge to the 2020 amendment (*SAT Industries*, 29.08.2022) is pending; the Court granted an ad-interim stay of that suspension; final outcome not found.
*Takeaway:* suspend only where the SCN itself shows a reason to stop business pending decision, and decide the SCN quickly (suspension is meant to last only till the decision).

**6. Non-existence at premises.**
Cancellation based on an inspection report is set aside where the report is vague (no date, hearsay from unnamed neighbours) or was not shared: Delhi *Umesh* (W.P.(C) 831/2024, 11.09.2024); Kerala *F R Trade Links* (05.08.2021; officer must hold his own rule 25 enquiry, not act only on an intelligence report). Where the procedure was followed and books not produced, Patna HC (W.P. 6794/2025) declined writ relief.
*Takeaway:* record the date, time, officer, persons met, photographs and GPS of the verification, give the report with the SCN, and consider the taxpayer's documents (rent deed, electricity bill, photographs) in REG-19.

**7. Cancellation on request; liabilities after cancellation; fresh registration; REG-05.**
Cancellation does not wipe out past liabilities (s.29(3)): Telangana DB *Manikanta Electronics Services Center* (W.P. 1362/2026) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**. A rejected cancellation request or a refusal of registration needs reasons and the documents relied on (Bombay *Om Impex*, Oct 2024). Earlier cancellation is no automatic bar to fresh registration; the officer must record findings under s.29(2)(b)/(c) per Circular 95/14/2019 (Patna *Maharaj Ji Enterprises*, July 2025). Several HCs dismissing writs left the taxpayer free to apply afresh (Delhi *Kirpal Singh*, Jan 2025).
*Takeaway:* when cancelling on request, cancel from the date sought (or record why another date); keep demand and recovery proceedings alive under s.29(3). When refusing new registration, give a reasoned REG-05 after a REG-03 query.

**8. Rectification of returns (GSTR-1/GSTR-3B) after the s.37(3)/s.39(9) deadline.**
SC *Bharti Airtel* (28.10.2021): GSTR-3B can be corrected only through s.39(9) / rule 61 in the period the error is noticed; no unilateral reopening. But for bona fide, revenue-neutral clerical errors (wrong GSTIN, wrong head), Bombay HC *Star Engineers* (14.12.2023) and *Aberdare Technologies* (2024) allowed GSTR-1 correction after the deadline, and the SC dismissed CBIC's SLP in *Aberdare* (21.03.2025) with observations. That order is not a reasoned judgment under Art. 141; it prima facie disapproved Telangana HC's *Yokohama India*; and CBIC's review petition was still pending (tagged on 11.03.2026). Karnataka *Wipro* (2023) and followers; Madras, Patna, Tripura HCs follow. Circular 183/15/2022 (FY 2017-18, 2018-19) and Circular 193/05/2023 (01.04.2019 to 31.12.2021) give a verification route for GSTR-2A/3B mismatches caused by supplier GSTR-1 errors.
*Contentions across courts:* *Bharti Airtel* (substantive re-working of 3B to claim refund) versus *Aberdare* line (clerical error, no revenue loss). The two are reconciled on facts; The SC order in *Aberdare* does not mention *Bharti Airtel*, and as an SLP dismissal it cannot overrule it. Department's position: the statutory timelines apply; Circulars 183/193 are the administrative relief.
*Takeaway:* in ITC-mismatch adjudication, apply Circular 183/193 where the period fits and the supplier certificate / CA certificate is produced; do not reject a request for a clerical correction only because the portal window closed - record whether there is revenue loss.

**9. Rectification of orders under s.161.**
Scope is limited to errors apparent on the face of the record. Substantive re-writing by way of "rectification" is invalid (Calcutta *Sajal Kumar Das*, 2024); a rectification that adversely affects the taxpayer needs a hearing (Delhi *Future Consumer*, 10.10.2025, including rejection of the taxpayer's own application; Calcutta *JCI Chemicals*, 24.11.2025, rejection with civil consequences); s.161 cannot be used to raise a fresh demand (Madras, Dec 2025 - single report, order not identified). Telangana HC (W.P. 20731/2025 batch, 15.10.2025) treats duplicate SCNs/orders for the same period as rectifiable under s.161, with the six-month bar not applying (second proviso) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**.
*Takeaway:* taxpayer's application within 3 months of the order; officer's own rectification within 6 months, except clerical/arithmetical errors. Pass a speaking rectification order, hear the taxpayer if the result is adverse, and do not use s.161 to re-decide merits (that is the appeal route).

---

### Statutory framework (for reference; not cases)

- **s.29(1)/(2), rule 22:** REG-17 SCN; reply in REG-18 within 7 working days; REG-19 order within 30 days of reply; REG-20 to drop. Proviso to rule 22(4): where cancellation was proposed for non-filing and the person files all returns and pays dues, the officer shall drop proceedings (REG-20). (Text not re-verified by search; Gauhati HC orders summarised below rely on it.)
- **s.29(3):** cancellation does not affect liability for any period before cancellation, whether determined before or after.
- **s.30 / rule 23:** revocation within 90 days of service of cancellation order; extendable by up to 180 days by the Commissioner or an officer authorised by him (not below Joint/Additional Commissioner) for recorded reasons (Notf. 38/2023-CT, 04.08.2023, w.e.f. 01.10.2023). Rule 23 is subject to rule 10B (Aadhaar authentication). Notf. 3/2023-CT (31.03.2023): amnesty for cancellations up to 31.12.2022 for non-filing.
- **rule 21A:** suspension pending cancellation; the December 2020 amendment removed "after affording a reasonable opportunity of being heard".
- **Rule 10A (bank account details) and rule 10B (Aadhaar authentication):** no High Court ruling on either was found in this round (see Gaps).
- **s.161:** rectification on own motion or when brought to notice by an officer or the affected person within 3 months of the order; no rectification after 6 months from the order, except clerical/arithmetical errors; adverse rectification requires natural justice.
- **s.37(3) / s.39(9):** correction of GSTR-1 / GSTR-3B in a later period, up to 30 November following the end of the FY (or the annual return, if earlier) - the deadline at issue in *Star Engineers* and *Aberdare*.

---

### Issue A - Vague show cause notices and the content of REG-17

#### Commissioner of Central Excise, Bangalore v. Brindavan Beverages (P) Ltd - Supreme Court (Pasayat and Kapadia JJ.) - 15 Jun 2007
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal Nos. 3417-3425 of 2002; (2007) 5 SCC 388; AIR 2007 SC (Supp) 1212.
- Holding: The SCN is the foundation of the department's case. Allegations that are not specific, vague, lacking details or unintelligible are enough to hold that the noticee had no proper opportunity to meet them. On facts the SCN did not allege the respondents were party to the arrangement and no material was produced; Revenue's appeals dismissed.
- Key words: "The show cause notice is the foundation on which the department has to build up its case. If the allegations in the show cause notice are not specific and are on the contrary vague, lack details and/or unintelligible that is sufficient to hold that the noticee was not given proper opportunity" (para 10)
- Binding: **SC - binding on all.** (Central Excise case; applied by GST courts to SCNs generally.)
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: cite to explain why the REG-17 / DRC-01 must spell out facts, periods and evidence; the order cannot go beyond the SCN.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/363245/); [LawSathi - judgment page](https://lawsathi.in/judgements/sc/2007/commissioner-central-excise-bangalore-brinda-van-beverages-ltd/); [Taxonation - vague elements in GST SCNs](https://taxonation.com/index.php/show-detail-article/196416/navigating-the-effects-of-vague-elements-in-gst-show-cause-notices)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; citation (2007) 5 SCC 388 confirmed; summary confirmed.

#### Aggarwal Dyeing and Printing Works v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat HC (J.B. Pardiwala and Nisha M. Thakore JJ.) - 24 Feb 2022
- Citation / case no.: Special Civil Application No. 18860 of 2021 and batch; reported [2022] 137 taxmann.com (Guj) (page number not confirmed).
- Holding: Vague cancellation SCNs and orders without particulars (explained by the State as portal "technical glitches") set aside on natural-justice grounds, with liberty to issue fresh notices with reasons and details. Directions: until the portal allows full details, issue the SCN and final order in physical form with all particulars by RPAD; the order must not travel beyond the SCN, and any inspection report or document relied on must first be put to the dealer.
- Key words: "If the authority wants to rely upon any particular piece of evidence then it owes a duty to first bring it to the notice of the dealer so that if the dealer has anything to say in that regard, he may do so." (para 18.2)
- Binding: Gujarat; persuasive elsewhere.
- Later history: Followed by Gujarat DB in a June 2023 case where the SCN was "very vague and cryptic" (party name not confirmed); LiveLaw reports a later DB criticising continued cryptic SCNs "despite 2022 directions" (date not confirmed). No appeal found.
- Use in orders: REG-17 should state facts, not just the statutory clause; share inspection reports before relying on them in REG-19.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/10577071/); [SCC Online blog - Gujarat HC quashes cancellation for cryptic SCN](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2023/06/27/gujarat-high-court-quashes-order-cancelling-firms-gst-registration-legal-news/); [LiveLawBiz - Gujarat HC pulls up GST authority over cryptic SCN](https://www.livelawbiz.com/tax-cases/gujarat-high-court-ruling-gst-authorities-reasoned-show-cause-notices-gst-registration-cancellations-270569)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; summary confirmed with the operative directions (physical SCN/order by RPAD; prior disclosure of evidence).

#### Cuthbert Oceans LLP v. Superintendent of CGST Range 109 - SCN alleging fraud without particulars - Delhi HC (Vibhu Bakhru and Amit Mahajan JJ.) - 08 Aug 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 10421/2023.
- Holding: An SCN that baldly says registration was obtained by fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression, without alluding to any such misstatement or fact, cannot elicit a meaningful response and was set aside; the REG-19 (cancelling retrospectively from 07.02.2020 with no reasons) was "equally cryptic" and also set aside. Writ entertained despite the appeal remedy because of a clear breach of natural justice.
- Key words: "Merely making the bald statement that the registration was obtained by fraud, willful misstatement or suppression of facts without alluding to any such misstatement or the allegedly suppressed facts, provides no clue to the noticee as to the allegation against him." (para 7)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found. Part of a long Delhi line (2023-2025) where SCN and order "bereft of any details" were set aside (e.g. W.P.(C) 4383/2024).
- Use in orders: an allegation of fraud in REG-17 must name the transactions, suppliers or facts.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/161718316/); [Delhi HC judgment PDF W.P.(C) 10421/2023](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/VIB08082023CW104212023_160640.pdf); [LiveLaw - GST registration can't be cancelled on unspecific SCN](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/delhi-high-court/gst-registration-cancelled-unspecific-show-cause-notice-delhi-high-court-266232)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name (Cuthbert Oceans LLP), date 08.08.2023 and bench confirmed; summary confirmed.

#### Shakti Shiva Magnets Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner - Delhi HC (Manmohan and Navin Chawla JJ.) - 31 Jan 2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 1559/2022 (earlier order 27.01.2022).
- Holding: The Delhi GST Commissioner admitted the SCN of 11.11.2021 (proposing cancellation and suspending registration) contained no fact or reason and no supporting document. As the registration had been suspended for over two months on such an SCN (rules 21A/22 contemplate about 30 days), the SCN was quashed and registration restored forthwith; liberty to issue a fresh SCN with all facts and reasons within a week; Commissioner directed to issue a practice direction.
- Key words: "Since in the present case, petitioner's registration has been lying suspended for more than two months on the basis of a show cause notice which is bereft of any reason or fact, this Court quashes the impugned show cause notice" (order of 31.01.2022)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a reasonless SCN also brings down the suspension made with it; do not leave a registration suspended for long.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order 31.01.2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140285831/); [Busy caselaw - Shakti Shiva Magnets](https://busy.in/caselaw/shakti-shiva-magnets-private-limited-vs-assistant-commissioner-ors-delhi-high-court); [taxo.online 21.02.2022](https://taxo.online/latest-news/21-02-2022-show-cause-notice-quashed-being-bereft-of-any-reason-or-fact-issued-for-suspension-of-gst-registration-of-the-assessee/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 31.01.2022, bench and W.P.(C) 1559/2022 added; summary confirmed (department conceded the defect).

#### PAV Warehouse v. Assistant Commissioner (ST), Poonamallee - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J.) - 29 Feb 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 2393 of 2024.
- Holding: SCN of 29.12.2022 alleged only "Non compliance of any specified provisions in the GST Act or the Rules", no document enclosed (State confirmed); the REG-19 of 17.02.2023 both referred to the reply and said no reply was filed. Cancellation quashed; registration to be restored forthwith; department free to start fresh proceedings in accordance with law.
- Key words: "The show cause notice does not indicate as to which provisions of the applicable GST statutes were not complied with by the petitioner." (para 6)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: A later order between the same parties (20.04.2026) is listed on Indian Kanoon; not checked.
- Use in orders: enclose documents, cite the specific provision, and make the REG-19 consistent with the record.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/110584096/); [Taxmann - SCN and order lacked clarity](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/scn-and-order-for-cancellation-of-gst-registration-lacked-clarity-on-the-nature-of-non-compliance-hc-quashed-cancellation-order/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 29.02.2024 and W.P. 2393/2024 added; summary confirmed.

#### Suresh Trading Corporation v. Assistant Commissioner (Circle) of SGST - Madras HC (M. Sundar J.) - 01 Oct 2021
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21109 of 2021.
- Holding: Cancellation order of 30.10.2019 set aside solely because the preceding SCN (11.12.2018) was not in the REG-17 template under rule 22(1) and did not mention the date and time of personal hearing (Revenue conceded its records did not show a REG-17); fresh SCN in the prescribed format allowed; no view on merits.
- Key words: "set aside solely on the ground that SCN which preceded the same has not been issued in the prescribed template i.e., REG-17 under Rule 22(1) of TN-GST Rules, as it does not mention the date and time of personal hearing" (para 13(a))
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: use the REG-17 form and fill in the hearing date, time and venue.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/60738274/); [TaxTMI blog feed](https://www.taxtmi.com/rss_feed_blog?id=730587); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=136968)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 01.10.2021, judge and W.P. 21109/2021 added; summary confirmed.

#### A1 Adil Traders v. Deputy State Tax Officer - Telangana HC (Sujoy Paul ACJ and Renuka Yara J.) - 04 Mar 2025 (SCN 01.11.2024, cancellation 16.01.2025) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 4539 of 2025; 2025 (3) TMI 369 (as cited in Trillion Lead Factory).
- Holding: REG-17 that suspended registration listed only rule 21(e), 21(b), 21(a) and s.29(2)(e) without any factual details; the department recorded "conclusions", not "reasons". Reasons must be in the SCN itself - the assessee is not expected to dig them out of an annexed internal departmental letter. A cryptic SCN cannot gain life from reasons given in the final order. SCN (with suspension) and cancellation order set aside; liberty to proceed afresh. Follows W.P. 20080/2024 and W.P. 17400/2024 (SCN of 29.02.2024 that only reproduced s.29(2)(e)).
- Key words: "if the show cause notice itself is cryptic, it cannot gain life if certain reasons are assigned in the final order." (para 11)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: Distinguished in *Trillion Lead Factory* (02.02.2026), where the petitioner had replied in detail to the annexed DGGI material (no prejudice). The two rulings pull in different directions on annexed documents: safest course is to write the facts into the REG-17 itself.
- Use in orders: all facts must be in REG-17 (not only in an annexure); reasons added only in REG-19 will not save the order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/112338058/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC sets aside cryptic suspension SCN](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-sets-gst-registration-suspension-scn-cryptic.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: name is "A1" (not "AI") Adil Traders; date 04.03.2025, bench and W.P. 4539/2025 added; added the holding that facts in an annexed internal letter are not enough.

#### Rayees Metals v. Deputy State Tax Officer - Telangana HC (Sujoy Paul and Namavarapu Rajeshwar Rao JJ.) - 08 Jul 2024 (SCN 29.02.2024) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 17400 of 2024.
- Holding: REG-17 with suspension whose only "reason" was the text of s.29(2)(e) is cryptic and shows non-application of mind (the State conceded it lacked details); following *T S R Exports v. Superintendent* ([2024] 162 taxmann.com 415 (Telangana)) and SC *Canara Bank v. Debasis Das*, SCN and suspension set aside; the Court deprecated mechanical suspensions ("LIFE" and "FILE" contain the same letters), no costs on the State's assurance.
- Key words: "Apart from this bald statement, there exists nothing in the show cause notice which can throw light as to what is the nature of 'fraud' or 'willful misrepresentation' or 'suppression of fact' by the petitioner. Thus, show cause notice is cryptic" (para 6)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: Followed in W.P. 20080/2024 and in *A1 Adil Traders* (04.03.2025).
- Use in orders: do not issue a REG-17 that only copies the words of s.29(2)(e).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/11024953/); [TaxGuru - cancellation set aside, SCN lacked factual reasons](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-registration-cancellation-set-show-notice-lacked-factual-reasons.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 08.07.2024, bench and W.P. 17400/2024 added; summary confirmed (State conceded).

#### JK Technology v. Deputy State Tax Officer, Charminar - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 09 Jul 2026 (SCN 08.08.2023; order 22.01.2024) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 21690 of 2026. (Full entry in 05-telangana-high-court.md, section 8.)
- Holding: REG-17 proposed only s.29(2)(e) (fraud/suppression), with no supporting documents; REG-19 cancelled under s.29(2)(c) (non-filing) w.e.f. 01.01.2023. Quashed solely because the ground was never put to the petitioner; SCN also quashed, registration restored, liberty for a fresh SCN with specific grounds and supporting documents. (No separate ruling on the retrospective date.)
- Key words: "quashing the order of cancellation of registration for the sole reason that it is based upon a ground upon which the petitioner was never put to notice." (para 4)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: the REG-19 ground must match the REG-17 ground.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/8041411/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-quashes-gst-registration-cancellation-grounds-scn.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and W.P. number added; summary confirmed, except that the Court gave no specific ruling on the retrospective date.

#### Sheeshmahal Enterprises Pvt Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Telangana HC (Ujjal Bhuyan CJ and N. Tukaramji J.) - 28 Mar 2023 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8344 of 2023 (CGST).
- Holding: Suo motu cancellation (25.03.2021, non-filing) where the petitioner swore it never received the SCN, yet the order recorded that its "reply dated 18.03.2021" had been considered: mechanical, non-application of mind. Cancellation and appellate order (rejected on delay) set aside; remanded for a fresh order after reply and hearing within three months; returns may be filed meanwhile.
- Key words: "This only goes to show the mechanical approach adopted by respondent No.1 and reflects non-application of mind." (para 7)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: ensure the SCN is actually served and REG-19 reflects the true record; hear before REG-19.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185037428/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=266399); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1063146)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 28.03.2023 and W.P. 8344/2023 added; it was a remand for non-application of mind (order cited a reply never filed), not an outright quashing for want of hearing.

#### Trillion Lead Factory Pvt Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax, Shamshabad - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 02 Feb 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 537 of 2026; SC: SLP (C) No. 7101 of 2026, dismissed 27.02.2026. (Full entry in file 05.)
- Holding: REG-17 (17.12.2025) alleged r.21(e) / s.16 violation (ITC from non-existent suppliers) and annexed the DGGI communication as the supporting document; the petitioner's replies met the allegations, so it understood the charge; no prejudice; officer to decide on the replies within two weeks.
- Key words: "No prejudice is likely to be caused, as the petitioner, having understood the contents of the charges contained in the show cause notice, adequately replied thereto." (para 6)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.** Useful for the department.
- Later history: SC dismissed the SLP in limine on 27.02.2026: "no writ lies against an issuance of show cause notice" (citing Prabhash Chandra Mirdha (2012) 11 SCC 565 and Krishna Wax).
- Use in orders: attach relied-upon documents; record in REG-19 that the reply addressed each allegation (shows no prejudice).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/29128302/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/72323089/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1043877)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench, W.P. number and SC dismissal added; summary confirmed.

#### King Enterprises v. Commissioner of Commercial Taxes - Karnataka HC, Dharwad Bench (Jyoti M. J.) - 02 Apr 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 101468 of 2026 (T-RES); NC: 2026:KHC-D:4993.
- Holding: SCN made only a bald allegation under s.29(2)(e) (registration obtained by fraud) with no reasons or particulars; the notice is vague and unsustainable and any order on it falls. Cancellation order (28.10.2025) and the confirming order (22.12.2025) quashed; registration to be restored within ten days.
- Key words: "It is a settled principle of law that a show cause notice must contain sufficient details enabling the notice to effectively respond. In the absence of reasons and material particulars, the notice is rendered vague and unsustainable." (para 7)
- Binding: Karnataka; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: as above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/54291616/); [Taxmann - cancellation quashed due to vague fraud SCN](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/gst-cancellation-quashed-due-to-vague-fraud-scn-hc/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 02.04.2026, judge and W.P. number added; summary confirmed.

**Contentions across courts (Issue A):** All HCs found (Delhi, Gujarat, Madras, Telangana, Karnataka, Gauhati, AP) require particulars. The only qualification is prejudice: Telangana *Trillion Lead Factory* refused relief where the taxpayer clearly understood the case. SC *Brindavan Beverages* (2007) supports the general principle. No CBIC circular found on REG-17 drafting.

---

### Issue B - Retrospective cancellation

#### Riddhi Siddhi Enterprises v. Commissioner of GST (CGST), South Delhi - Delhi HC (Division Bench: Yashwant Varma and Ravinder Dudeja JJ.) - 25 Sep 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 8061/2024 (also spelled "Ridhi Sidhi").
- Holding: s.29(2) permits retrospective cancellation, but the mere conferral of power does not justify its use; the order must itself give reasons for the retrospective effect, and the power can be "neither robotic nor routinely applied". Neither SCN nor order gave reasons; the cancellation order (25.07.2023) was quashed, without prejudice to fresh proceedings and to recovery of dues. (It did NOT itself move the date to the SCN date - that remedy appears in other Delhi orders reported by LiveLaw.)
- Key words: "the power to cancel retrospectively can neither be robotic nor routinely applied unless circumstances so warrant." (para 5)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: Followed in many Delhi HC orders (e.g. *Mohit Enterprises*, W.P.(C) 1086/2025, 04.03.2025), with *Subhana Fashion* and *Balaji Industries* (W.P.(C) 11913/2024). Appeal not found.
- Use in orders: record in REG-19 why the chosen back date is justified.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/149073062/); [LiveLaw - retrospective cancellation without specific reasons takes effect from SCN date](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/delhi-hc-absence-of-specific-reasons-retrospective-cancellation-of-gst-registration-cancellation-take-effect-from-date-of-issuance-of-scn-delhi-hc-275697); [Delhi HC 2025 order citing Ridhi Sidhi](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/PMS13082025CW34922025_162014.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 25.09.2024, bench Yashwant Varma and Ravinder Dudeja JJ. (not Dharmesh Sharma J.); remedy was quashing with liberty, not substitution of the SCN date.

#### Aditya Polymers (through its proprietor) v. Commissioner of Delhi GST - Delhi HC (Vibhu Bakhru and Amit Mahajan JJ.) - 28 Mar 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 14493/2022; 2023:DHC:2328-DB.
- Holding: SCN of 11.12.2020 proposed cancellation for six months' non-filing; order of 15.07.2021 cancelled from 01.07.2017. The date chosen cannot be arbitrary - retrospective cancellation must rest on material and some rationale, and the taxpayer must be put on notice of the proposed retrospective date. With both sides agreeing, cancellation made effective from 11.12.2020 (SCN date); recovery action not precluded.
- Key words: "selecting a date from which to cancel the registration cannot be arbitrary. It is essential that the exercise of powers to cancel the registration ab initio, must be based on material on record and some rationale." (para 8)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: retrospective effect must be proposed in REG-17 and justified on material.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/194782147/); [Taxscan - retrospective effect not mentioned in SCN](https://taxscan.in/retrospective-effect-of-cancellation-of-gst-registration-not-mentioned-in-scn-delhi-hc-orders-to-cancel-gst-registration-prospectively/269405)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 28.03.2023 (not 2024) and W.P.(C) 14493/2022 added; the SCN-date relief was by consent of both sides.

#### Subhana Fashion v. Commissioner, Delhi GST - Delhi HC (Vibhu Bakhru and Sachin Datta JJ.) - 19 Sep 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 12255/2024.
- Holding: SCN (03.09.2021) proposed cancellation only for non-payment of dues for three months; order (02.06.2022) cancelled from 01.07.2017. Non-payment of dues for three months is not a ground under s.29(2) or rule 21; the order showed "0.0" as payable; the SCN gave no hearing date or time, so no real hearing. Order set aside and registration restored forthwith; recovery/other proceedings not precluded.
- Key words: "It is apparent to note that non-payment of dues for a period of three months is not a prescribed ground for cancelling the petitioner's GST registration." (para 10)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: Cited in 2025 Delhi orders on retrospective cancellation (e.g. setting aside retrospective cancellation "without prejudice" to the department's right to act if the taxpayer is found non-existent).
- Use in orders: cancel only on grounds listed in s.29(2)/rule 21; give a hearing date and time.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/111838701/); [TaxHeal - retrospective cancellation invalid if not proposed in SCN](https://www.taxheal.com/16684-of-2025.html); [Delhi HC 2025 order citing Subhana Fashion](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/PMS11082025CW52642025_125022.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: date 19.09.2024 and bench added; the order set aside the cancellation outright (ground not in s.29(2); no hearing) - it did NOT discuss retrospective cancellation or move the date to 24.05.2022 (those details, from a later secondary summary, could not be found in the judgment).

#### Delhi HC Division Bench - retrospective cancellation must rest on objective criteria - Delhi HC - 2023-24 (party name not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found (one of several W.P.(C) in the 2023-24 series).
- Holding: Discretion to cancel retrospectively cannot be exercised arbitrarily; it must rest on objective criteria. Where the taxpayer had stopped business, the date was moved to the date of discontinuance.
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: tie the back date to an objective fact (date business ceased, date of non-existence found, date of first bogus invoice).
- Source: [Verdictum - retrospective cancellation must be based on objective criteria](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/gst-registration-cancellation-retrospective-effect-objective-criteria-delhi-hc-1510463)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (specific case not identified on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; the "objective criteria" wording appears in Riddhi Siddhi (25.09.2024) and many later Delhi orders - cite Riddhi Siddhi instead).

#### Aryan Timber Store v. Sales Tax Officer - Delhi HC - 18 Jan 2024
- Cross-reference: full entry in **02-input-tax-credit.md, Issue 8**. W.P.(C) 628 of 2024: retrospective cancellation only where warranted, must be proposed in SCN; officer must weigh the effect on customers' ITC. Cancellation modified to date of application.

**Effect on recipients' ITC:** see **02-input-tax-credit.md, Issue 8** (*LGW Industries*, *Gargo Traders*, *Fathima Traders*): a later retrospective cancellation of the supplier does not by itself justify denial of the recipient's ITC.

---

### Issue C - Cancellation for non-filing; revocation, time limits and restoration

#### Tvl. Suguna Cutpiece Centre v. Appellate Deputy Commissioner (ST) (GST), Salem - Madras HC (C. Saravanan J.) - 31 Jan 2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 25048 of 2021 and batch (large batch of 2021-22 writs); reported [2022] 135 taxmann.com 234 (Mad) and (2022) 99 GSTR 386.
- Holding: Writs against cancellations for non-filing allowed on conditions (para 229): file all pre-cancellation returns with tax, interest and late fee within 45 days; such dues not payable out of unutilised ITC; unutilised ITC to be used only after scrutiny and approval by a competent officer; post-cancellation returns and tax (in cash) to be filed; the department may impose restrictions to prevent bill trading; on payment and upload of returns, the registration stands revived.
- Key words: "If any Input Tax Credit has remained utilized, it shall not be utilised until it is scrutinized and approved by an appropriate or a competent officer of the Department." (para 229(iii))
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: **SLP status: no SLP or SC order was found in targeted searches.** Followed widely: *K. Ramar* (below); *GK Digital Printing* (01.04.2022); a 2026 *Ramalingam Chellappa* order; *El Tech Power* (10.03.2026, Issue D) (followers not checked).
- Use in orders: if an HC directs restoration on Suguna conditions, verify filing and payment before restoring and keep ITC use subject to verification.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/42388732/); [TaxGuru - GST cancellation set aside on compliance conditions](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-cancellation-set-compliance-pending-return-conditions-madras-hc.html); [Taxmann - cancelled registration revived on payment](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/cancelled-registration-would-stand-revived-on-payment-of-tax-penalty-and-uploading-of-returns-hc/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; single judge (C. Saravanan J.) and W.P. 25048/2021 batch added; conditions confirmed (45 days; dues not from ITC; ITC subject to scrutiny).

#### K. Ramar v. Commissioner of CGST & Central Excise, Madurai - Madras HC (Madurai Bench, K. Kumaresh Babu J.) - 21 Jan 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) No. 1396 of 2025.
- Holding: CGST registration cancelled for non-filing; the benefit (conditions) of *Suguna Cutpiece Centre* extended to the petitioner; writ disposed of on those terms.
- Key words: "the benefit extended by this Court vide its earlier order in Suguna Cutpiece Centre's case cited supra, may be extended to the petitioner." (para 6)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: shows the Suguna line is still applied in 2025, including against CGST.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/55784570/); [TaxHeal - W.P.(MD) 1396 of 2025](https://www.taxheal.com/w-p-md-no-1396-of-2025.html); [Taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/gst-registration-not-to-be-cancelled-if-returns-not-filed-madras-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; respondent (CGST Madurai) and judge added; summary confirmed.

#### Motaleb Bhuyan v. State of Assam - Gauhati HC (Devashis Baruah J.) - 11 Mar 2025
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 2244 of 2024 with WP(C) Nos. 3683, 2695 and 4500 of 2024; cited as (2025) SCC OnLine Gau 1429 and 97 GSTL 7 (Gau) (some follow-on orders wrongly say "SC").
- Holding: REG-19 orders passed mechanically (officer confused whether a reply was filed; three orders gave no reason at all) are deemed a non-exercise of jurisdiction and were set aside. As the petitioners came to court years later, equities were balanced: returns from cancellation till date to be filed within 30 days with tax, interest, penalty and late fee, and the s.73(10) period computed from the date of the judgment (except FY 2024-25) (paras 46-50).
- Key words: "this Court declares that in respect to the Petitioners in the present batch of writ petitions, the period for submission of the annual returns would be the date of the instant judgment except for the period 2024-2025" (para 49)
- Binding: Assam and other Gauhati HC States; persuasive.
- Later history: Treated as settling the issue ("no longer res integra") in *Sama Yangfo* (WP(C) 133/2026, 31.03.2026), *Panchatantra Tours and Travels* (WP(C) 1231/2026, 11.03.2026), *Masuk Ahmed Barbhuiya* (WP(C) 6674/2025, 26.11.2025) and *Dhirghat Hardware Stores* (2025) (followers not checked). Gauhati HC also held the 270-day portal limit is not a permanent bar (March 2026). No SC challenge found.
- Use in orders: if restoration is ordered, note the reset of s.73(10) limitation and that restoration is not a "clean slate".
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/97509656/); [TaxGuru - Gauhati follow-on order](https://taxguru.in/?p=1083146); [taxo.online 27.03.2026 - default under s.29(2)(c) not absolute](https://taxo.online/latest-news/27-03-2026-default-under-section-292c-is-not-absolute-registration-can-be-restored-if-returns-are-filed-and-dues-are-paid-gauhati-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: respondent is State of Assam (not Union of India), date 11.03.2025, judge and WP(C) numbers added; the ground for setting aside was mechanical/unreasoned REG-19 orders; the limitation reset works by deeming the annual-return due date to be the judgment date.

#### Gauhati HC - REG-19 must contain reasons - Gauhati HC - 2026 (party name not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: The proper officer must give reasons in REG-19; cancellation order without reasons quashed. A related Gauhati ruling held that an SCN alleging non-filing without specifying the period is inadequate.
- Binding: Gauhati HC States; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: REG-19 must be a speaking order.
- Source: [Taxscan - proper officer must give reasons in REG-19](https://www.taxscan.in/gst/proper-officer-must-give-reasons-in-gst-reg-19-cancellation-order-gauhati-hc-quashes-order-for-lack-of-reasons-read-order-1448263)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (paywalled) (not identified on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 among many 2026 Gauhati registration orders; do not cite without the order copy).

#### Adarsha Transport v. Deputy State Tax Officer, Warangal-Urban-II - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and Vakiti Ramakrishna Reddy J.) - 22 Sep 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 31578 of 2026 (CNR HBHC010647422026); reported as 2026 taxguru.in 15030.
- Holding: Registration cancelled by REG-19 dated 12.08.2025 for non-filing (accountant left); appeal time-barred and portal blocked a late revocation application. Liberty to apply for revocation online within two weeks, physically if not accepted for technical reasons; competent authority to entertain it and decide in accordance with law within three weeks. Merits left to the authority.
- Key words: "The competent authority would entertain it and take a decision thereon in accordance with law within a period of three weeks thereafter." (para 5)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (as a direction; it does not decide the statutory limit).
- Later history: none found. One of a 2025-26 Telangana series (*Ramesh*, *Chaitanya Traders*, *SVP Enterprises* 23.04.2026, *Sri Surya Enterprises*, *Holi Restaurant and Bar*, *Upper Vantage LLP*, W.P. No. 35268 of 2025 - not checked).
- Use in orders: when such an application is received under an HC direction, decide it on merits (returns filed? dues paid?) by a speaking order within the time fixed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109427636/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC permits revocation despite time-barred appeal](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-permits-fresh-gst-registration-revocation-application-time-barred-appeal.html); [TaxGuru - Adarsha Transport](https://taxguru.in/?p=1087218)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 22.09.2026, bench, W.P. number added; summary confirmed.

#### KBG Associates v. Superintendent of Central Tax (Secunderabad CGST Range) - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 23 Jul 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 23897 of 2026.
- Holding: CGST registration cancelled by REG-19 dated 14.08.2025 for six months' non-filing (consultant did not tell the firm of the SCN); appeal time-barred. Liberty to apply for revocation online within two weeks, physically if not accepted for technical reasons; authority to entertain and decide in accordance with law within three weeks.
- Key words: "we are inclined to allow liberty to the petitioner to approach the competent authority within a period of two weeks from today for submission of application for revocation of cancellation of GST registration online and if it is not being accepted for any technical reasons then to submit it in physical form." (para 5)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.** Note: a **CGST** (central) range in Hyderabad - directly relevant to the reader's department.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: as for *Adarsha Transport*.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26255496/); [Taxscan - consultant's negligence, Telangana HC allows revocation application](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/tax-consultants-negligence-telangana-hc-allows-revocation-application-filing-for-cancelled-gst-registration-read-order-1449472); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC manual revocation application](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-manual-gst-revocation-application-registration-cancellation.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 23.07.2026, bench and W.P. 23897/2026 added; summary confirmed.

#### Check Security Force v. Deputy State Tax Officer, Mehdipatnam-II - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 16 Jul 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 22632 of 2026.
- Holding: Registration cancelled by REG-19 dated 04.07.2025 for non-filing; a time-barred appeal had already been dismissed on delay. If the petitioner files a physical revocation application within one week, the authority must entertain it and decide in accordance with law within three weeks.
- Key words: "if the petitioner approaches the competent authority within a period of one week from today for submission of application for revocation of cancellation of GST registration, in physical form, the competent authority would entertain it and take a decision thereupon in accordance with law" (para 5)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: as above; note the HC gives this route even after the appeal was dismissed as time-barred.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/86108973/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1068713); [TaxGuru - revocation permitted after non-filing](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/gst-cancellation-revocation-application-permitted-non-filing-telangana-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 16.07.2026, bench, W.P. 22632/2026 added; summary confirmed (also: appeal had been dismissed as time-barred).

#### Radhe Packaging v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (N.V. Anjaria and Devan M. Desai JJ.) - 05 Apr 2023
- Citation / case no.: Special Civil Application No. 5673 of 2023.
- Holding: Petitioner whose registration was cancelled was left to apply for revocation under the amnesty Notification 3/2023-CT (31.03.2023); if it did so, the authority was to decide without delay. No view on merits.
- Key words: "If the petitioner approaches the competent authority in light of the Notification dated 31.03.2023, the authority will take appropriate decision without booking any delay." (para 6.1)
- Binding: Gujarat; persuasive.
- Later history: amnesty window has closed (see statutory notes).
- Use in orders: historical; shows courts first point to statutory/amnesty routes.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/99669339/); [GSTGyaan - Notf. 3/2023 amnesty](https://gstgyaan.com/notification-no-03-2023-central-tax-dated-31-03-2023-amnesty-scheme-for-revocation-of-cancellation-of-registration); [GST Council - Notf. 3/2023 text](https://gstcouncil.gov.in/sites/default/files/2024-05/03_eng.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 05.04.2023, bench, SCA 5673/2023 and respondent added; summary confirmed.

#### Snowline Travel Ventures LLP v. Commissioner, State GST - Uttarakhand HC (Manoj Kumar Gupta CJ and Subhash Upadhyay J.) - 23 Jul 2026
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition (M/B) No. 593 of 2026; 2026:UHC:6250-DB.
- Holding: SCN (06.11.2024) and cancellation (26.12.2024) on the ground of "filing NIL return from last several months" quashed: filing NIL returns is not a ground under s.29(2); agreed with AP HC in *Kali Shanker Enterprises* (2024 (1) TMI 430). (The words "not a statutory offence" come from the secondary report, not the judgment.)
- Key words: "We agree with the view taken by the Andhra Pradesh High Court that filing of NIL return is not a ground contemplated under Sub Section (2) of Section 29 of the Act." (para 4)
- Binding: Uttarakhand; persuasive (AP HC Kali Shanker Enterprises to the same effect).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: NIL returns are returns; do not treat them as non-filing. If NIL returns hide real business, propose a ground that s.29(2) actually provides, with facts.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/139769506/); [taxo.online 30.07.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/30-07-2026-gst-registration-cancellation-quashed-filing-nil-returns-is-not-a-statutory-offence-uttarakhand-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, case number and the AP HC authority added; summary confirmed.

#### Anil Art and Craft v. State of U.P. - Allahabad HC (Saumitra Dayal Singh and Indrajeet Shukla JJ.) - 20 Nov 2025 [CORRECTED - previous summary was wrong]
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 5924 of 2025; 2025:AHC:207978-DB. Any SC order: not found.
- Holding: REG-19 of 15.10.2025 (SCN 08.10.2025, after a DGGI survey) rejected a detailed reply only as "REPLY IS NOT SATISFACTORY", reason "Others": a non-speaking order, set aside. Cancellation "announces the economic death of the business entity". The Commissioner was asked to transfer the proceeding to an officer "well informed in law" and to issue instructions, with penal consequences, against unreasoned cancellation orders. Writ ALLOWED.
- Key words: "By stating that the reply is "not satisfactory", the said respondent-authority has only indicated the conclusion but has made no disclosure of the reason, to reach that conclusion." (para 6)
- Binding: Uttar Pradesh; persuasive.
- Later history: The TaxGuru report of an SC order "upholding" Allahabad HC could not be confirmed on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026. If the State's SLP was dismissed, it confirms this pro-taxpayer result.
- Use in orders: REG-19 must deal with the reply and give reasons; "reply not satisfactory" is not a reason.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/180892373/); [TaxGuru - SC upholds Allahabad HC on cancellation](https://taxguru.in/?p=1061892)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED materially: the HC did NOT decline relief on disputed facts - it allowed the writ and set aside an unreasoned REG-19; date 20.11.2025 (not 2026; order under challenge was 15.10.2025, not 03.02.2026); SC order not found.

**Contentions across courts (Issue C):** Madras (*Suguna*), Gauhati (*Motaleb Bhuyan*), Telangana (2026 series), Uttarakhand, Kerala and Rajasthan give restoration or a fresh revocation window once returns and dues are cleared, invoking the right to carry on business (Art. 19(1)(g)) and livelihood (Art. 21) and the revenue interest in keeping the taxpayer in the system. Patna (*JD Enterprises*, below) declined writ relief where a documented inspection showed non-existence and the order was reasoned. (*Anil Art and Craft*, Allahabad HC 20.11.2025, earlier listed here, in fact allowed the writ against an unreasoned REG-19; no SC order on it was found.) No SC judgment on the rule 23 time limit was found. CBIC position: rule 23 limits; Notf. 3/2023 was a one-time relief.

### Issue D - Suspension under rule 21A

#### Tvl. El Tech Power System Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) - Madras HC (C. Saravanan J.) - 10 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 9579 of 2026.
- Holding: REG-17 of 27.02.2026 (after an inspection on 13.11.2025 and a s.67 statement on 14.11.2025) proposed cancellation from 01.07.2017 and suspended registration from 27.02.2026. The State called the writ premature; the Court held the suspension "unwarranted" and quashed the notice to that extent only (no detailed reasons given), leaving the cancellation SCN to be decided in line with *Suguna Cutpiece*. Disposed of at admission with consent.
- Key words: "To the extent the suspension of the registration was unwarranted and therefore to that extent the Show Cause Notice stands quashed." (para 7)
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive (a short single-judge order).
- Later history: Further orders between El Tech Power System and the State Tax Officer (10.06.2026, 13.07.2026) are on Indian Kanoon; not checked.
- Use in orders: do not suspend as a matter of course with every REG-17; record why suspension is needed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/135319392/); [TaxGuru - Madras HC quashes suspension in SCN](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/madras-hc-quashes-gst-registration-suspension-scn-unwarrantedly-suspend-registration.html); [TheTaxCorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/madras-high-court-suspension-of-gst-registration-via-show-cause-notice-held)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added inspection/statement dates and that the order gives no reasoning for "unwarranted".

#### SAT Industries Ltd v. Union of India - Bombay HC (K.R. Shriram and A.S. Doctor JJ.) - notice issued 29 Aug 2022; outcome not found
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 3643 of 2022.
- Holding: Challenge to the 22.12.2020 amendment of rule 21A(2) (CGST and MGST Rules) that removed "after affording the said person a reasonable opportunity of being heard", where suspension was made in the SCN itself (08.08.2022). Notice issued to the Attorney General and Advocate General; ad-interim stay of the suspension and direction to restore registration pending hearing.
- Key words: "In the meanwhile, there shall be ad-interim stay in terms of prayer clause (c)" (para 9 - staying the SCN's suspension and directing restoration)
- Binding: none yet (interim order only).
- Later history: outcome not found.
- Use in orders: the rule as amended stands; suspension without prior hearing is lawful on the text, but courts still look for a reason and may stay suspensions.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185195794/); [taxo.online 06.09.2022 - HC issues notices](https://taxo.online/?p=130329)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; W.P. 3643/2022, bench and date 29.08.2022 added; ADDED that ad-interim stay of the suspension was granted; final outcome not found.

(See also *AI Adil Traders* and *Rayees Metals* - Telangana, Issue A - and *Shakti Shiva Magnets* - Delhi, Issue A: suspension falls with a vague SCN.)

### Issue E - Cancellation for non-existence at the place of business

#### Umesh v. Union of India - Delhi HC (Vibhu Bakhru and Sachin Datta JJ.) - 11 Sep 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 831/2024.
- Holding: SCN (22.12.2023) rested only on an Anti-Evasion letter that the principal place of business was non-existent. On a court-ordered re-inspection (23.01.2024) the premises, stock and a signboard with the GSTIN were found; the "non-existence" view rested only on unnamed nearby shop owners saying the shop had opened days earlier. Petitioner to reply with documents; officer to decide without relying on that report as conclusive of non-existence.
- Key words: "We find it difficult to countenance that a taxpayer's registration can be cancelled solely on the basis of some general queries/enquiries from random persons, of which there is no record." (para 15)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: verification reports must record direct observation, not hearsay; name persons spoken to.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/66183243/); [taxo.online - Umesh v. UoI](https://taxo.online/judgment/umesh-versus-union-of-india-anr-vide-w-p-c-831-2024-dated-11-09-2024-delhi-high-court/); [taxo.online - unnamed shop owners](https://taxo.online/blogs/field-officer-placing-reliance-on-inquiries-from-unnamed-nearby-shop-owners-could-not-justify-the-non-existent-of-place-of-business-delhi-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; summary confirmed.

#### Delhi HC (DB) - physical verification date and material not given - Delhi HC - Sept 2024 (party name not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Cancellation order gave no reason beyond the SCN; the department did not state when physical verification was done or what material showed non-existence. Order quashed; petitioner allowed to respond to the SCN.
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: give the verification date and findings in REG-17.
- Source: [LiveLaw - Delhi HC quashes retrospective cancellation](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/delhi-high-court-quashes-order-cancelling-gst-registration-with-retrospective-effect-270432)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not identified on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; party name unknown).

#### F R Trade Links v. State Tax Officer - Kerala HC (A.M. Badar J.) - 05 Aug 2021
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 28917 of 2020.
- Holding: Cancellation because the business was in a partly completed building without a building number is not a ground under s.29(2); further, the registering officer acted only on an intelligence squad report, did no rule 25 verification himself and ignored the building-tax receipt produced. Cancellation and the rejection of revocation quashed; registration restored.
- Key words: "The proper officer ought to have independently assessed the situation, particularly, when the petitioner had produced the receipt of the building tax from the local authority" (para 12)
- Binding: Kerala; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: the proper officer must apply his own mind to the verification, not just forward another wing's report.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/147528276/); [TaxGuru - Kerala HC](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/kerala-hc-cancellation-gst-registration-absence-proper-enquiry-gst-authority.html); [StudyCafe](https://studycafe.in/kerala-hc-no-cancellation-of-gst-registration-in-absence-of-proper-enquiry-by-gst-authority-104889.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; judge added; summary confirmed; registration restored.

#### JD Enterprises v. State of Bihar - coal trader found non-existent; writ declined - Patna HC (Rajeev Ranjan Prasad and Sourendra Pandey JJ.) - 02 Sep 2025
- Citation / case no.: CWJC No. 6794 of 2025.
- Holding: Inspections found the principal place (a small rented room with a board, confirmed by the landlord as used only for bill trading) and the additional coal depot non-existent/non-functional; the proprietor evaded the visit and gave inconsistent addresses; the findings were not challenged. The cancellation order was a reasoned order; no case for writ interference; writ dismissed.
- Key words: "In the facts of the present case, we are of the considered opinion that no case for interference by this Court in exercise of its extraordinary writ jurisdiction has been made out." (para 15)
- Binding: Bihar; persuasive. Useful for the department.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a well-documented inspection (landlord statement, photos, both premises) and a reasoned REG-19 survive challenge.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/17187734/); [TaxHeal - 6794 of 2025](https://www.taxheal.com/6794-of-2025.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party (JD Enterprises), date 02.09.2025 and bench added; CORRECTED: the writ was dismissed outright as the order was reasoned and findings unchallenged (no express relegation to appeal).
