### Issue F - Cancellation on request, surviving liabilities, fresh registration, refusal (REG-05)

#### Manikanta Electronics Services Center v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 19 Jan 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1362 of 2026; [2026] 183 taxmann.com 314 (Telangana).
- Holding: Registration cancelled on 16.07.2021 on voluntary application (nil liability); s.73 SCN (23.01.2024, served on the portal with DRC-01) and order (22.04.2024) for 2018-19 followed, with reminders and hearing notices ignored; a s.161 application was rejected as seeking re-examination of merits. Cancellation does not absolve the taxpayer of existing liability (s.29(3)); the plea that a cancelled registrant need not watch the portal was rejected; no infirmity found; liberty to appeal within two weeks with pre-deposit.
- Key words: "We are of the considered view that cancellation of registration does not absolve the taxpayer of any existing liability which the petitioner may have to face." (para 6)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (Division Bench).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: proceed against cancelled registrants for earlier periods; serve on the portal and send reminders/hearing notices (as done here) so service can be proved.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/150095927/); [TaxHeal - W.P. 1362 of 2026](https://www.taxheal.com/1362-of-2026.html); [Taxmann - cancellation doesn't wipe out tax liability](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/gst-registration-cancellation-doesnt-wipe-out-tax-liability-hc/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 19.01.2026 added; summary confirmed; added that portal service was upheld and the s.161 rejection stood.

#### Euro Pratik Sales Corporation v. Union of India - Bombay HC (K.R. Shriram and A.S. Doctor JJ.) - 17 Nov 2022
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 3380 of 2022.
- Holding: Registration cancelled on the taxpayer's own application (18.07.2019) after closing business; it could not transition Rs 39.13 lakh deemed excise credit (TRAN-1/TRAN-2) because the registration was cancelled, and s.30 / the portal provide no route to revoke a voluntary cancellation. Since liability survives cancellation under s.29(3), the taxpayer cannot be made to forgo the credit: physical application for restoration to be accepted and registration restored by 22.11.2022 so that TRAN-1/TRAN-2 could be filed within the SC-ordered window (till 30.11.2022).
- Key words: "petitioner cannot be permitted to forgo a sum of Rs.39,13,025/- which according to petitioner is entitled to deemed excised credit particularly when under sub-section 3 of Section 29 his liability will continue even after cancellation of registration." (para 9)
- Binding: Maharashtra; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a voluntary cancellation may be reopened by court order for a limited purpose (here the TRAN window); liability and ledger balances both survive cancellation.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/103169888/); [taxo.online 30.11.2022](https://taxo.online/latest-news/30-11-2022-assessee-cannot-be-deprived-of-accumulated-credit-due-to-cancellation-of-registration-on-closure-of-business-bombay-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: name is "Euro Pratik" (not "Partik"); date 17.11.2022 and W.P. 3380/2022 added; the relief was restoration of the cancelled registration for TRAN-1/TRAN-2 transition credit (not a fresh registration to carry forward ITC).

#### Maharaj Ji Enterprises v. Union of India - Patna HC (Rajeev Ranjan Prasad and Ashok Kumar Pandey JJ.) - 16 Jul 2025
- Citation / case no.: CWJC No. 15175 of 2024.
- Holding: Rejection (REG-05) of a fresh registration application after an earlier cancellation was a completely unreasoned order: no finding as required by Circular 95/14/2019-GST, and the reason later given in the counter-affidavit (s.29(2)(b)) was never put in the query notice. Rejection set aside; remanded to the Deputy Commissioner for fresh consideration, with opportunity to comply with any statutory requirement.
- Key words: "No finding in terms of the requirement of the circular has been recorded by the Proper Officer. In fact, the reason for rejection of the fresh application of the petitioner as disclosed in the counter affidavit was not the issue mentioned in the notice" (para 13)
- Binding: Bihar; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a REG-05 refusing new registration must record findings in the order, on a point first raised in the REG-03 query.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/111102415/); [taxo.online 25.07.2025 - Patna HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/25-07-2025-cancellation-of-earlier-gst-registration-does-not-automatically-bar-fresh-registration-order-must-reflect-compliance-with-section-292-and-cbic-guidelines-patna-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 16.07.2025, bench and respondent added; summary confirmed.

#### Om Impex v. State of Maharashtra - Bombay HC - 08.10.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 11541 of 2024; 2024:BHC-AS:40347-DB (M.S. Sonak and Jitendra Jain JJ.).
- Holding: Voluntary cancellation (REG-16, accepted from 08.05.2023) was revoked about 10 months later without an SCN or hearing. The REG-05 rejection left the "following reasons" space blank, and the State GST material was supplied only later. The Court quashed the revocation and the consequential retrospective cancellation (from 27.06.2020, a date before registration was even granted), restored the 08.05.2023 position and gave liberty to issue an SCN and pass a speaking order by 31.01.2025. ITC use was barred till then.
- Key words: "the said Form does not contain any reasons ... the omission is indicative of non-application of mind." (para 14)
- Binding: Maharashtra; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: An accepted voluntary cancellation is revoked only after an SCN and hearing. REG-05 must state reasons and the material relied on. A retrospective date cannot predate the registration.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Om Impex v. State of Maharashtra, Bombay HC, 08.10.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/22421312/); [SAG Infotech - Bombay HC on rejection of voluntary cancellation](https://blog.saginfotech.com/bombay-hc-rejection-order-voluntary-gst-cancellation-lack-stated-reasons); [LiveLawBiz - Bombay HC quashes order, reasons not recorded](https://www.livelawbiz.com/high-court/bombay-high-court/bombay-high-court-quashes-gst-registration-cancellation-order-reasons-not-recorded-272992)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; the application rejected was for voluntary cancellation, which had been accepted and then revoked; added case no., bench, the retrospective-date point and the 31.01.2025 timeline.

#### Kirpal Singh v. Superintendent Range 118, CGST - Delhi HC - 13.01.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 17799/2024 (Yashwant Varma and Harish Vaidyanathan Shankar JJ.).
- Holding: Challenge to the cancellation order of 26.04.2021 dismissed, noting the evident laches. No provision of the CGST Act makes the petitioner ineligible to apply for registration afresh (Circular 95/14/2019-GST), so the Court gave liberty to apply for fresh registration.
- Key words: "no provision of the Central Goods and Services Tax Act, 2017 ... renders the writ petitioner ineligible to apply for registration afresh." (para 2)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found. Similar: Delhi W.P.(C) 10892/2025 (July 2025); Rajasthan *Mateshwari Construction* (not re-checked).
- Use in orders: fresh registration is a recognised route; treat it under Circular 95/14/2019.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Kirpal Singh v. Superintendent Range 118 CGST, Delhi HC, 13.01.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/173295234/); [TaxHeal - W.P.(C) 17799 of 2024](https://www.taxheal.com/w-p-c-no-17799-of-2024.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added date, bench, laches ground.

**Cancellation on the taxpayer's request - effective date:** Delhi HC orders (see Issue B, *Aryan Timber*) move the cancellation date to the date of the taxpayer's application or discontinuance where the department back-dated without reasons. Where the officer kept an application pending and later issued its own REG-17, Delhi HC has criticised the delay (single Taxscan report, not confirmed).

---

### Issue G - Rectification of returns (s.37(3), s.39(9)) and Circulars 183/2022 and 193/2023

#### Union of India v. Bharti Airtel Ltd - Supreme Court (A.M. Khanwilkar, Dinesh Maheshwari and C.T. Ravikumar JJ.) - 28 Oct 2021
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal arising from SLP(C) 8654 of 2020; 2021 INSC 684; [2021] 131 taxmann.com 319 (SC). Full entry in **02-input-tax-credit.md, Issue 3**.
- Holding: Circular 26/26/2017 para 4 valid; GSTR-3B for Jul-Sep 2017 can be rectified only under s.39(9) / rule 61 in the period the error is noticed; an assessee cannot unilaterally rectify past GSTR-3B returns because of the cascading effect on other parties. Delhi HC judgment set aside; Bharti's writ dismissed.
- Key words: "assessee cannot be permitted to unilaterally carry out rectification of his returns submitted electronically in Form GSTR-3B, which inevitably would affect the obligations and liabilities of other stakeholders" (para 48)
- Binding: **SC - binding on all.**
- Later history: distinguished (not overruled) by the *Aberdare* line for clerical, revenue-neutral errors.
- Use in orders: substantive re-working of past returns is outside the statute.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - judgment](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/60500851/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/gst-rectification-of-errors-permissible-only-at-initial-stages-supreme-court-dismisses-bharti-airtels-plea-for-refund-of-rs923-crore-184507); [Casemine commentary](https://www.casemine.com/commentary/in/supreme-court-upholds-gst-circular-on-form-gstr-3b-rectification%3A-union-of-india-v.-bharti-airtel-ltd./view)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CORRECTED: bench was three judges (C.T. Ravikumar J. also); summary confirmed.

#### Star Engineers (I) Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Bombay HC (G.S. Kulkarni and Jitendra Jain JJ.) - 14 Dec 2023
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 15368 of 2023.
- Holding: In bill-to-ship-to supplies the supplier entered the GSTIN of the third-party consignee instead of the buyer (BAL) in GSTR-1 for July 2021, November 2021 and January 2022. Revenue accepted there was no loss of revenue. The Court read s.37(3) and s.39(9) purposively, so their time-limit provisos do not bar correcting a bona fide, inadvertent error, and directed the department to permit amendment of GSTR-1, online or manually, within four weeks.
- Key words: "a bonafide, inadvertent error in furnishing details in a GST return needs to be recognized, and permitted to be corrected by the department, when ... there is no loss of revenue" (para 22)
- Binding: Maharashtra; persuasive.
- Later history: Relied on in *Aberdare* (Bombay HC 2024; SC dismissed CBIC's SLP 21.03.2025) and a Karnataka HC ruling of Nov 2025.
- Use in orders: a wrong-GSTIN error with no revenue loss is likely to be corrected by the courts; consider manual correction requests on merits.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Star Engineers (I) Pvt Ltd v. Union of India, Bombay HC, 14.12.2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/74682902/); [LiveLaw - Bombay HC directs amendment of GSTR-1](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/bombay-high-court/bombay-high-court-dept-permit-gstr-1-online-manual-244721); [Verdictum - correction of bona fide error](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/rectification-modification-gst-returns-cascading-effect-bombay-hc-1509804)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added periods and purposive reading of s.37(3)/s.39(9).

#### Aberdare Technologies Pvt Ltd v. CBIC - Bombay HC (29 Jul 2024); CBIC v. Aberdare Technologies - Supreme Court (Chief Justice Sanjiv Khanna and Sanjay Kumar J.) - 21 Mar 2025
- Citation / case no.: Bombay HC Writ Petition No. 7912 of 2024, decided 29.07.2024; SC: SLP (C) Diary No. 6332 of 2025 (later SLP(C) No. 7903/2025), order 21.03.2025; reported 2025 SCC OnLine SC 1323.
- Holding: Bombay HC allowed rectification of GSTR-1 errors after the deadline where there was no revenue loss, following *Star Engineers*. The SC condoned delay and dismissed CBIC's SLP as the HC judgment was "just and fair, as there is no loss of revenue"; it observed that the right to correct clerical/arithmetical errors flows from the right to do business, software limitation is no justification, and CBIC must re-examine the timelines. It added that *Bar Code India* (P&H HC) and *Yokohama India Pvt Ltd v. State of Telangana* ((2023) 108 GSTR 115) "prima facie, do not lay down good law".
- Key words: "Right to correct mistakes in the nature of clerical or arithmetical error is a right that flows from right to do business and should not be denied unless there is a good justification and reason to deny benefit of correction." (SC order, 21.03.2025)
- Binding: A dismissal of an SLP with observations (a record-of-proceedings order, not a reported reasoned judgment): it does not lay down law under Art. 141, but the observations are strong guidance. Note: it casts doubt on the Telangana HC's *Yokohama India* ruling.
- Later history: CBIC's review petition (Diary No. 35961/2025 in SLP(C) 7903/2025) was ordered on 11.03.2026 to be listed after the decision in SLP(C) No. 8559/2025 - i.e. still pending as of that date. Followed by Madras HC (*Deepa Traders*, 01.04.2025), Patna, Tripura and Karnataka HCs (not checked). No CBIC circular implementing it was found.
- Use in orders: where a taxpayer seeks to correct a clerical GSTR-1/3B error with no revenue loss, do not refuse only on the deadline; record whether revenue is affected. Do not rely on *Yokohama India* (Telangana HC) against correction.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 21.03.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/5609622/); [Indian Kanoon - SC review order 11.03.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/48246775/); [Indian Kanoon - Bombay HC 29.07.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/195460917/); [Acuity Law - CBIC v. Aberdare](https://acuitylaw.co.in/rectification-of-errors-in-returns-cbic-v-aberdare-technologies/); [StudyCafe - SC allows rectification after due date](https://studycafe.in/supreme-court-allows-rectification-of-gstr-1-and-gstr-3b-after-due-date-380220.html)
- Verified: Primary - SC orders read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (Bombay HC judgment identified, not re-read); CORRECTED: SC order is an SLP dismissal with observations (not a reasoned judgment); it disapproved Yokohama India (Telangana) prima facie; CBIC review petition pending (tagged 11.03.2026).

#### Wipro Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Taxes - Karnataka HC (S.R. Krishna Kumar J.) - 06.01.2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 16175 of 2022 (T-RES).
- Holding: The invoices and returns carried the GSTIN of ABB India Ltd instead of the actual recipient, ABB Global Industries and Services Pvt Ltd (a different entity). The Court held this a bona fide error and directed the revenue to apply Circular 183/15/2022-GST for 2017-18, 2018-19 and also 2019-20 ("justice oriented approach"), and to consider the petitioner's rectification request of 06.09.2021. This is one order: the two "Wipro" reports describe the same judgment, and it does not direct revision of GSTR-3B.
- Key words: "though the Circular refers only to the years 2017-18 and 2018-19, since there are identical errors ... by adopting a justice oriented approach, the petitioner would be entitled to the benefit of the Circular for the year 2019-20 also." (para 8)
- Binding: Karnataka; persuasive.
- Later history: FY 2019-20 extension later matched by Circular 193/05/2023 (01.04.2019 to 31.12.2021). Karnataka HC (2024) held the adjudicating authority should apply Circular 183 even if the assessee did not cite it (not re-checked).
- Use in orders: apply Circulars 183/193 in mismatch cases suo motu where the period fits.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Wipro Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Taxes, Karnataka HC, 06.01.2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/109488484/); [EY alert - HC directs revenue to deal with bona fide errors](https://www.ey.com/en_in/alerts-hub/2023/01/hc-directs-revenue-to-deal-with-bona-fide-errors-in); [LiveLaw - circular applicable for 2019-20](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/circular-mismatch-itc-applicable-2019-20-identical-errors-karnataka-high-court-218972)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 06.01.2023, single judge, W.P. 16175/2022 confirmed; the two reports are one order; "revise GSTR-3B" was wrong (the direction is to apply Circular 183).

**Circulars:** Circular 183/15/2022-GST (27.12.2022) - procedure for GSTR-3B vs GSTR-2A ITC differences in FY 2017-18 and 2018-19 caused by supplier GSTR-1 errors (CA/supplier certificate). Circular 193/05/2023-GST - extends it to 01.04.2019 to 31.12.2021 (for FY 2019-20 onwards subject to rule 36(4) limits). File 02 lists Kerala, AP and Madras rulings applying Circular 183.

---

### Issue H - Rectification of orders under s.161

#### Sajal Kumar Das v. State of West Bengal - Calcutta HC (T.S. Sivagnanam CJ and Supratim Bhattacharya J.) - 09.01.2024
- Citation / case no.: M.A.T. 2475 of 2023 (with W.P.A. 25754 of 2023); 2024 (1) TMI 1011.
- Holding: The appellate authority had allowed the taxpayer's appeal against cancellation of registration (09.11.2022). On the original authority's s.161 application, it later recalled that order and upheld the cancellation (23.08.2023). The DB held that s.161 is akin to Order 47 Rule 1 CPC, not an appeal in disguise, and that no error apparent was pointed out. It quashed the rectification, restored the appellate order and directed restoration of registration within three weeks.
- Key words: "what the authorities sought to do in an application under Section 161 of the Act is seeking for rewriting and reviewing the entire decision ... which is clearly impermissible under Section 161 of the Act." (para 10)
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: if the change needs argument or new evidence, it is not s.161; use review/appeal routes.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Sajal Kumar Das v. State of West Bengal, Calcutta HC, 09.01.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/9864160/); [TaxTMI article](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=12319); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1052883)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; added date, DB bench, case no., and that the s.161 recall reversed an appellate order restoring registration.

#### Future Consumer Ltd v. Union of India - Delhi HC (Prathiba M. Singh and Shail Jain JJ.) - 10.10.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 15611/2025.
- Holding: (i) An order-in-original that is not signed is not invalid where the accompanying DRC-07 carries the officer's name, designation and ward. (ii) The Court found, on its own, that the s.161 rectification application was rejected (26.12.2024) without a personal hearing. It held that a hearing is mandatory under the third proviso to s.161, following *HVR Solar* (W.P.(C) 4506/2025). As relief it did not remand the rectification. Instead it allowed an appeal against the OIO by 30.11.2025 with pre-deposit, not to be dismissed as time-barred.
- Key words: "a personal hearing would be mandatory in terms of the third proviso of Section 161 of the Central Goods and Service Tax Act, 2017." (para 9)
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: issue a notice and give a hearing before rejecting a rectification application or passing any adverse s.161 order. The DRC-07 summary with officer details answers an "unsigned order" objection.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Future Consumer Ltd v. Union of India, Delhi HC, 10.10.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/120118137/); [taxo.online 16.10.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/16-10-2025-no-rectification-order-u-s-161-having-an-adverse-impact-on-the-taxpayer-can-be-passed-without-providing-an-opportunity-of-personal-hearing-delhi-high-court/); [Delhi HC PDF W.P.(C) 15611/2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/PMS10102025CW156112025_165443.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party, case no. and date confirmed. Added the unsigned-OIO/DRC-07 holding, and that the relief was leave to appeal (not remand of the rectification).

#### JCI Chemicals India Pvt Ltd v. State of West Bengal - natural justice in s.161 rejections - Calcutta HC (single judge) - 24.11.2025
- Citation / case no.: WPA 21260 of 2025.
- Holding: The CGST Deputy Commissioner had rejected an application to rectify an OIO without reasons or a hearing, although a hearing was requested. The application alleged the DRC-07 and the OIO showed different tax amounts, which affects pre-deposit. Revenue said the third proviso to s.161 applies only where rectification adversely affects someone. The Court held that where a rejection has civil consequences, natural justice is "almost mandatory". It set the rejection aside and remanded the matter for a hearing within three weeks, without going into the merits.
- Key words: "If the action or order would have civil consequence, observance of natural justice would be almost mandatory." (para 9)
- Binding: West Bengal; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a rejection of a s.161 application should be reasoned and preceded by a hearing where it affects the taxpayer, especially when a hearing was requested.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - JCI Chemicals India Pvt Ltd v. State of West Bengal, Calcutta HC, 24.11.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/172824445/); [TaxHeal - HC quashes cryptic rejection of rectification application](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=115598); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/?p=543128)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. Party identified as JCI Chemicals; the order is dated 24.11.2025, not 27.11.2025 (27 Nov is likely the report date). The match to the secondary reports is my inference from the identical holding.

#### Madras HC - s.161 cannot create a fresh demand - Madras HC - Dec 2025 (reported 17.12.2025; party name not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: A substantial demand cannot be confirmed by a rectification order without a proper notice explaining the basis; s.161 is not a tool for a fresh demand.
- Binding: Tamil Nadu; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: new demand = SCN under s.73/74, not s.161.
- Source: [taxo.online 17.12.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/17-12-2025-section-161-cannot-be-used-as-a-tool-to-create-a-fresh-tax-demand-without-due-process-madras-high-court/)
- Verified: Secondary - single source. (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; 4 searches. *Nivedhana Constructions*, Madras HC 01.12.2025, a s.161 matter, was checked and is not this case.)

#### Hi Reach Broadband Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) and batch - duplicate SCNs/orders for the same period rectifiable under s.161 - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - common order 15.10.2025 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 20731, 24077, 27416, 28893 and 30267 of 2025 (lead: Hi Reach Broadband, W.P. 20731/2025).
- Holding: Multiple SCNs/orders for the same tax period. The Advocate General produced the Commercial Taxes SOP (CCT's Ref. No. LIV(2)/33/2025, 14.10.2025) and stated that such errors can be rectified under s.161 TGST Act on own motion or on application, the six-month bar not applying under the second proviso. Petitioners agreed; the Court found the stand "in consonance" with s.161 and disposed of the writs for rectification within a reasonable time with intimation to the assessees. (The six-month point is the State's stated position, accepted by the Court, not a contested ruling.)
- Key words: "in view of the stand of the Department, which appears to be in consonance with the power of rectification conferred upon the proper officer under Section 161 of the TGST Act, we deem it proper to dispose of the writ petitions" (para 9)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.** Arose under the State Act; CGST officers should apply the same reasoning to duplicate central orders.
- Later history: Followed in later orders (e.g. *Charminar Traders* 30.10.2025, *Ennem Excel Engineering* 08.01.2026, *Sudhakar Impex* 03.02.2026, *Hitech Enterprises* 17.03.2026 - listed on Indian Kanoon, not read). The SOP also says that where the later order carries more tax, the case goes to the revisional authority (s.108) / s.107(2) review.
- Use in orders: when duplicate State/Central orders exist for the same period, rectify under s.161 by a speaking order rather than leaving the taxpayer to appeal.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - common order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/164406433/); [TaxHeal - W.P. 20731 batch](https://www.taxheal.com/20731-24077-27416-28893-and-30267-of-2025.html); [Telangana HC order PDF (Feb 2026) reproducing it](https://tshc.gov.in/documents/currentsection_2026_02_17T12_22_18.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; lead party (Hi Reach Broadband) and date 15.10.2025 confirmed; SOP date 14.10.2025 confirmed; CORRECTED nuance: the second-proviso point was the State's concession accepted by the Court.

#### Shri Sharada v. Deputy Commissioner STU-1, Saroornagar - liberty to file s.161 application against orders of 30.12.2025 and 31.12.2025 - Telangana HC (Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 03 Feb 2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 1963 of 2026.
- Holding: Two orders (30.12.2025 and 31.12.2025, by two different authorities) for the same period (FY 2021-22) on an issue already dropped on 15.03.2024, ignoring the 14.10.2025 SOP. On the State's instructions, liberty to file a s.161 application within two weeks before the Deputy Commissioner STU-1; decision within three weeks after hearing.
- Key words: "Let such application be filed within a period of two (2) weeks. On such application being filed, respondent No.1 would take a decision in accordance with law within three (3) weeks thereafter, after affording an opportunity of hearing." (para 4)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (direction only).
- Later history: Applied by consent in *Rondeyvoo Eurasia* (W.P. 4743/2026, 17.02.2026, with no coercive steps on a garnishee notice for two weeks) and *Tallapally Ravinder Goud* (W.P. 5743/2026, 25.02.2026). A separate Telangana petition (royalty/RCM, DRC-07 of 06.10.2025) was steered to rectification; outcome not confirmed.
- Use in orders: duplicate orders and overlooked earlier dropping orders are classic errors apparent on record; decide such applications promptly with a hearing.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P. 1963/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/73887056/); [Indian Kanoon - Rondeyvoo Eurasia](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/148806505/); [TaxGuru - Telangana HC directs rectification route](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-high-court-directs-rectification-route-gst-royalty-rcm-dispute.html); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1045957)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party (Shri Sharada), date 03.02.2026 and W.P. 1963/2026 identified; facts (duplicate orders, earlier dropping order) added; summary confirmed.

**s.161 versus appeal:** s.161 is an alternative remedy, not a bar to appeal. Commentary warns that pursuing rectification does not by itself extend the appeal period; officers can rely on that when a taxpayer later seeks condonation. No HC ruling squarely on this point was found in this round (see file 03 on s.107 limitation).

**Rectification route for s.16(5)/(6) cases:** Circular 237/31/2024-GST (15.10.2024) provides a rectification route for orders under ss.73/74/107/108 that denied ITC now saved by s.16(5)/(6) - see file 02.

---

### Tribunal / GSTAT

- **No GSTAT decision on cancellation or revocation of registration was found** (one search, Oct 2026). Reported items: a May 2026 writ dismissed because the GSTAT appeal route is now functional; GSTAT Principal Bench order of 14.05.2026 relaxing registry scrutiny until 31.12.2026 (not a limitation extension); GSTAT appeal deadline for older orders extended to 31.07.2026. Source: [JurisHour - appeal before GSTAT](https://www.jurishour.in/tag/appeal-before-gstat/). Verified: Secondary - single source.
- Practical effect: HCs may increasingly refuse writs against appellate orders on cancellation, pointing to GSTAT.

---

### Gaps and doubts

1. **Suguna Cutpiece - SLP status.** Three searches found no SLP or SC order. The W.P. number and bench composition were also not found. Do not state that the SC has approved or disapproved it.
2. **Ridhi Sidhi Enterprises** - exact date and bench not confirmed; a search on the case number returned nothing. It is known only through later Delhi HC orders and one LiveLaw report. Whether the LiveLaw DB report (Yashwant Varma and Dharmesh Sharma JJ.) is Ridhi Sidhi itself is unclear. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** *Riddhi Siddhi Enterprises*, 25.09.2024, Yashwant Varma and Ravinder Dudeja JJ.; it quashed the order and did not substitute the SCN date.**]**
3. **Aditya Polymers** - judgment date and case number not found; one source only. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** 28.03.2023, W.P.(C) 14493/2022; the SCN-date relief was by consent.**]**
4. **Aberdare Technologies (SC, 21.03.2025)** - whether this is a reasoned judgment (law under Art. 141) or a dismissal with observations is not settled from summaries; the Bombay HC date (29.07.2024) comes from one source. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** the order is an SLP dismissal with observations, not law under Art. 141; Bombay HC date 29.07.2024 confirmed; CBIC review pending (11.03.2026).**]**
5. **Motaleb Bhuyan** - one set of follow-on orders cites "2025 SCC OnLine SC 1429"; others cite Gauhati. Treated here as a Gauhati HC judgment. Paragraph 50 not seen. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** Gauhati HC (Devashis Baruah J.), 11.03.2025, WP(C) 2244/2024 batch; "SC" in follow-on orders is an error.**]**
6. **Notification 23/2023-CT** - one source says it changed the amnesty deadline; the substituted date (believed 31.08.2023) is my recollection, not confirmed by search.
7. **Rule 22(4) proviso, rule 10A, rule 10B (Aadhaar)** - no case law found; statutory descriptions are from secondary sources or my own knowledge and should be checked against the current Rules.
8. **Telangana HC orders (AI Adil Traders, Rayees Metals, Adarsha Transport, KBG Associates, Check Security Force, Sheeshmahal)** - W.P. numbers and judgment dates not found; reports are TaxGuru/Taxscan summaries, often paywalled. Check the Telangana HC website before citing. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** all six read on Indian Kanoon; W.P. numbers and dates are now in the entries. "AI Adil" is *A1 Adil Traders*, W.P. 4539/2025.**]**
9. **Anil Art and Craft** - the nature of the SC order (SLP dismissal vs. reasoned order) is unknown. **[14 Oct 2026:** the Allahabad HC judgment (20.11.2025) ALLOWED the writ - the earlier summary was wrong; no SC order found.**]**
10. **Gujarat DB "despite 2022 directions" case (c. Sept 2024)** - party name and date not confirmed.
11. **Future Consumer (Delhi HC)** - linking it to W.P.(C) 15611/2025 is my inference. **[Resolved 14 Oct 2026:** confirmed - W.P.(C) 15611/2025, 10.10.2025.**]**
12. **No Telangana HC ruling** found specifically on (a) retrospective cancellation dates, (b) rule 21A suspension as such, (c) refusal of new registration (REG-05), (d) s.37(3)/39(9) rectification of returns.
13. **Gauhati *Sujoy Paul v. UoI* (WP(C) 3260/2026, June 2026)** on an unreasoned REG-05 rejecting revocation - outcome not found, so not included as an entry.
14. **Search budget** (45 searches) used up; Allahabad, Rajasthan and AP HC lines on cancellation were not separately researched.

### Verification log (14 Oct 2026)

- **Entries checked:** 47 case entries. The *Aryan Timber* line in Issue B is a cross-reference to file 02 and was not counted.
