## File 07: GST case law: levy and scope of supply, valuation, classification, rates, place and time of supply, reverse charge, exemptions

**Scope.** This file covers case law on what is a "supply" under s.7 CGST Act and Schedules I-III: assignment of leasehold rights, transfer of development rights (TDR/JDA), sale of land and developed plots, liquidated damages, notice pay, employee canteen recoveries, director's remuneration and cross-charge between distinct persons. It also covers valuation (reimbursements and pure agent, the one-third land deduction), classification of goods and services by the Supreme Court and High Courts, works-contract rates for government work, change in rate (s.14), place of supply and tax paid under the wrong head, reverse charge on renting residential property, and how exemption notifications are read (Dilip Kumar, hostels, education). Some topics are only cross-referenced because other files cover them: corporate guarantees and rule 28 (Torrent Power), Mohit Minerals and ocean freight, online gaming and actionable claims, and clubs and mutuality (all in file 04); intermediary and s.77 refunds (file 01); GSTAT procedure (file 03). Prepared for the Assistant Commissioner, CGST, Sangareddy Division (Telangana).

**Compiled:** 14 Oct 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries. On 14 Oct 2026, 27 of the 37 entries were checked against the judgment text on Indian Kanoon (marked "Primary"); the rest remain "Secondary". Verify Secondary entries before citing in an order.**

**Binding status key.** Supreme Court = binding on everyone (Art. 141). Telangana HC = **BINDING IN TELANGANA**. Other High Courts = persuasive. AAR/AAAR = binds only the applicant and its jurisdictional officer (s.103). CESTAT (service tax / excise) = persuasive. CBIC circulars bind officers.

---

### Quick answers

**1. Assignment of leasehold rights (GIDC/MIDC/TSIIC-type industrial plots).** *Gujarat Chamber of Commerce & Industry* (Guj HC, 03.01.2025) held that assigning long leasehold rights in land and building is a transfer of an interest in immovable property, akin to sale of land (Sch. III para 5). It is not a supply of services. *Aerocom Cushions* (Bom HC, 09.01.2026) followed it. **The SC dismissed the Revenue's SLPs**: in *Aerocom* on 22.05.2026 (SC order read; no reasons given) and against the Gujarat judgment on 21.07.2026 (Narasimha and Aradhe JJ). *Takeaway:* don't raise demands on outright assignment of leasehold rights by a lessee to a third party. The **original allotment** or lease by the development corporation (premium or lease rent) is a different transaction and stays a taxable supply, subject to any exemption entry.

**2. Transfer of development rights under a JDA (Telangana).** *Prahitha Constructions* (**Telangana HC, 09.02.2024**, BINDING IN TELANGANA): a JDA does not transfer title, so it is not "sale of land". TDR is a taxable supply, and Notf. 4/2018-CT(Rate) only fixes the time of supply. The SC issued notice in SLP(C) 11079/2024 (13.05.2024) **without a stay**; no final decision was found. *Takeaway:* TDR/JDA demands can continue in Telangana. Record that the matter is pending in the SC and frame the order so it does not depend on the outcome of other issues.

**3. Liquidated damages, notice pay, compensation.** Circular 178/10/2022 (03.08.2022): an amount is taxable only if it is consideration for a separate agreement to tolerate or refrain. Pure compensation for breach is not. *Krazybee* (Kar HC, WP 16471/2024) quashed a DGGI SCN on liquidated damages. *Aavanti Solar* (Kar HC) set aside an order that **did not discuss Circular 178**. For notice pay, *GE T&D India* (Mad HC, service tax) and a line of CESTAT rulings hold it is not consideration. *Takeaway:* an SCN under Sch. II para 5(e) must identify a separate, independent promise to tolerate an act, with evidence, and must deal with Circular 178 expressly. A demand on liquidated damages alone will be quashed.

**4. Employee recoveries and director's remuneration.** Circular 172/04/2022: perquisites under the employment contract, and canteen recoveries where a contractor supplies the food, are not supplies. Gujarat AARs consistently hold that recoveries from **permanent** employees are not taxable but recoveries from **contract** workers are. Circular 140/10/2020 (10.06.2020): director's pay taxed as salary under s.192 IT Act is outside GST (Sch. III para 1). Fees to non-employee directors, or pay under s.194J, attract **RCM** (Notf. 13/2017 entry 6). *Takeaway:* test the TDS section and the accounts entry before raising RCM on directors. (The brief's "Circular 140/2019" is in fact Circular 140/10/2020-GST.)

**5. Cross-charge between distinct persons (HO and branch).** Sch. I para 2 taxes supplies between distinct persons even without consideration. Circular 199/11/2023 says that where the recipient branch is eligible for full ITC, the invoice value (including nil) is deemed open-market value under rule 28. A Delhi HC ruling (June 2025, party name not found) set aside an IGST demand on HO services that were not cross-charged where full ITC was available, and remanded in light of the circular. *Takeaway:* don't raise cross-charge demands against branches that are fully ITC-eligible. Focus on recipients with exempt or blocked turnover.

**6. Valuation: reimbursements and pure agent.** *Intercontinental Consultants* (SC, 07.03.2018, service tax) struck down rule 5(1) of the 2006 Valuation Rules because tax applies only to the value of the service itself. Under GST, s.15(2)(c) expressly includes incidental expenses, so the pure-agent exclusion in **rule 33** is the only route and it is applied strictly (WB AAR, toll charges). The one-third deemed land deduction (Notf. 11/2017 para 2) was **read down** (not struck down) by Gujarat HC in *Munjaal Manishbhai Bhatt* (06.05.2022): it is optional, and available only where the actual land value can't be ascertained. The Union's SLP is pending. *Takeaway:* to deny pure-agent status, record which of the rule 33 conditions fails. Outside Gujarat, accept the actual land value where the sale deed gives it. Persuasive only; no SC ruling was found.

**7. Classification.** The SC dismissed the Revenue's SLPs in *Sri Vijaya Visakha Milk Producers* (flavoured milk is heading 0402, 5%; AP HC 10.12.2024; SC May 2025) and in the *fusible interlining cloth* case (Guj HC; not 5903; SC around May 2025). *Madhan Agro* (SC, 18.12.2024, excise): small packs of pure coconut oil are edible oil under heading 1513 unless Chapter 33 Note 3 is met. *Cadila Health Care* (SC, 05.10.2026, MP VAT): classify by the form of the goods at sale, not end use. *X'SS Beverage* (Gauhati HC, 04.03.2025): carbonated fruit drinks are 12%, not 28%. *Takeaway:* the burden of proving the higher heading is on the Revenue. Apply the GRIs in order and the section/chapter notes, and deal with the HSN Explanatory Notes. Dismissing a contrary HC ruling in a single paragraph will not survive.

**8. Classification disputes and writs in Telangana.** *Sahasra Enterprises* (**Telangana HC**, BINDING IN TELANGANA): rate and classification disputes involving facts go to a s.107 appeal, not Art. 226. *Takeaway:* the order-in-original is likely to be tested in appeal, so make the factual findings complete. Also justify any s.74 invocation separately: a rate dispute alone is not suppression (see *G.R. Infra*, SC, in file 03).

**9. Works contracts for government (12%/18%).** "Government Entity/Authority" was removed from entry 3(vi) of Notf. 11/2017 from 01.01.2022 (Notf. 15/2021), so 18% applies thereafter (Telangana AAR rulings, file 05). *STS-KEC JV* (Mad HC, 28.01.2025): an RVNL railway works contract (railways being in entry 3(v)) stayed at 12% because the **nature of the work**, not the recipient's identity, decides the entry. *Takeaway:* check entry 3(v) (railways, roads, bridges, etc.) before 3(vi). The recipient's status matters only under 3(vi). No Telangana HC merits ruling was found.

**10. Rate change and time of supply (s.14).** When the rate changes, s.14 overrides ss.12/13: for a supply completed before the change, if both invoice and payment fall after the change the new rate applies; if either the invoice or the payment came before the change, the date of that earlier event generally fixes the time of supply and the old rate. (Paraphrase of s.14; read the section.) Madras HC (*SPK and Co*, 10.12.2025) refused writ relief to a contractor who finished work before 18.07.2022 but invoiced afterwards. *Takeaway:* record all three dates (completion, invoice, payment) in the order.

**11. Place of supply and wrong head (IGST vs CGST/SGST).** Karnataka HC (*GR Tech Services*, June 2026) and Madras HC (*SYA Homes*, 07.07.2026) held that IGST wrongly paid on an intra-State supply must be adjusted, or recovery must await the s.77 refund. Tax already paid in full under the wrong head is not "unpaid tax". *Takeaway:* before demanding CGST/SGST on a supply treated as intra-State, give credit or adjustment for IGST paid and don't levy interest or penalty as if nothing was paid. Ocean freight (*Mohit Minerals*) is in file 04; s.77 refunds are in file 01.

**12. RCM on renting residential dwellings.** From 18.07.2022 (Notf. 5/2022), renting a residential dwelling to a registered person attracts RCM. *Seema Gupta* (Del HC, 27.09.2022), relying on the government's affidavit and clarification: renting by a proprietor in a **personal capacity** for their own residence stays exempt (the government's affidavit says the same for partners). *Takeaway:* check who uses the dwelling and for what before demanding RCM.

**13. Exemptions: strict interpretation, then liberal application.** *Dilip Kumar* (SC Constitution Bench, 30.07.2018): an exemption notification is read strictly and any ambiguity goes to the Revenue (once eligibility is established, the notification may be construed liberally: paras 46-47). **Telangana HC applied it** in *Care College of Nursing v. KNRUHS* (17.10.2023, BINDING IN TELANGANA): affiliation and inspection fees paid to a university are not exempt under Notf. 12/2017 entry 66. Madras HC DB (*Bharathidasan University*, 10.02.2026) held the same. *Takeaway:* cite Dilip Kumar for the **eligibility** question and set out exactly which condition of the notification the assessee fails.

**14. Hostels and residential dwelling.** *State of Karnataka v. Taghar Vasudeva Ambrish* (SC, 04.12.2025, 2025 INSC 1380) affirmed Karnataka HC (2022): leasing residential premises to an operator who runs a hostel for students or working professionals is "renting of residential dwelling for use as residence" (entry 13, Notf. 9/2017-IT(Rate)). The lessee need not live there itself. The period at issue was before 18.07.2022. *Takeaway:* drop demands on landlords leasing residential buildings to hostel or PG operators for periods up to 17.07.2022. For later periods, check the amended entry and the RCM entry (Notf. 5/2022) before taxing.

---

### 1. Assignment of leasehold rights, TDR/JDA, sale of land

**Contentions across courts.** *Leasehold assignment:* Gujarat HC (*Gujarat Chamber*, *Dhiraj Can*) and Bombay HC (*Aerocom Cushions*) hold it is not a supply. The Revenue's SLPs were dismissed in 2026, and no contrary HC was found. CBIC's position before these rulings was that it is taxable (SCNs under Sch. II para 2(b)/5(b)). *TDR/JDA:* Telangana HC (*Prahitha*) holds it taxable, and the SC appeal is pending without a stay. *Developed plots:* MP HC (*Shraddha Tiwari*) and Circular 177/09/2022 (as summarised) treat sale of developed land as sale of land. Gujarat AAR took the opposite view; Kerala AAR (*Dharmic Living*, 2021) held it not a supply.

#### Gujarat Chamber of Commerce and Industry v. Union of India - Gujarat HC - 03.01.2025
- Citation / case no.: Special Civil Application No. 11345 of 2023 and connected matters (lead number as cited in *Dhiraj Can*).
- Holding: Assignment by sale of leasehold rights in a GIDC plot (with building) by the lessee to a third party for consideration is a transfer of benefits arising out of immovable property. S.7(1)(a) read with Sch. II para 5(b) and Sch. III para 5 does not apply to it, so no GST is leviable under s.9. SCNs and orders were quashed (paras 83-85, as quoted in *Dhiraj Can* and *Aerocom*). The point that the original GIDC allotment is a taxable but exempt supply comes from summaries only.
- Key words: "assignment/sale/ transfer of benefits arising out of \"immovable property\" by the lessee-assignor in favour of third party-assignee" (para 83, quoted in *Dhiraj Can*, para 5).
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana. The SC's SLP dismissal leaves it standing; see below.
- Later history: LiveLaw (24.10.2025) reported that the SC (Mithal and Varale JJ) was to examine the issue in SLP(C) Diary No. 33270/2025. Later reports say the SC (P.S. Narasimha and Alok Aradhe JJ) **dismissed the Revenue's SLP on 21.07.2026**. Commentators differ on whether a dismissal at the SLP stage settles the law.
- Use in orders: Don't demand tax on assignment of leasehold rights by an existing lessee. Distinguish the original lease or allotment premium.
- Source: [Dhiraj Can (Guj HC) quoting paras 83-85 - Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/118604422/); [TaxGuru - SC dismissed Revenue SLP](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/sc-dismissed-revenue-slp-gst-levy-gidc-leasehold-rights-transfer.html); [taxo.online 22.07.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/22-07-2026-no-gst-on-assignment-of-industrial-leasehold-rights-supreme-court-affirms-gujarat-high-courts-landmark-verdict/); [LiveLaw - SC to examine](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/supreme-court-hearing-whether-leasehold-rights-transfer-attract-gst-307737)
- Verified: Secondary - judgment itself not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 (3 searches); operative paras 83-85 read as quoted verbatim in *Dhiraj Can* (Guj HC) and *Aerocom* (Bom HC) on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. Holding reworded to the court's words (it does not say "akin to sale of land"). SC dismissal of 21.07.2026 not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026.

#### Dhiraj Can Co. Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India - Gujarat HC - 04.07.2025
- Citation / case no.: R/Special Civil Application No. 3596 of 2025 (DB: Bhargav D. Karia, Pranav Trivedi JJ).
- Holding: Following *Gujarat Chamber* (SCA 11345/2023), which the State could not distinguish, assignment of leasehold rights in a GIDC plot is a transfer of benefits arising out of immovable property and is not liable to GST. The s.73 order and DRC-07 of 29.08.2024 and the DRC-01 SCN of 30.05.2024 were quashed.
- Key words: "the same would not be subject to levy of GST as provided under Sec.9 of the Act." (para 6)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Shows the Gujarat line extends to s.73 orders already passed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/118604422/); [taxo.online 11.07.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/11-07-2025-transfer-of-leasehold-rights-is-not-a-taxable-supply-under-gst-as-it-is-akin-to-sale-of-land-which-is-excluded-under-schedule-iii-gujarat-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case no. and bench added; holding reworded (court did not say "akin to sale of land").

#### Aerocom Cushions Pvt. Ltd. v. Assistant Commissioner (Anti-Evasion) - Bombay HC (Nagpur Bench, DB) - 09.01.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 2145 of 2025; 2026:BHC-NAG:348-DB (DB: Anil L. Pansare, Nivedita P. Mehta JJ).
- Holding: Assignment of a 95-year MIDC lease for Rs 1.5 crore, with MIDC's consent, is a transfer of benefits arising from immovable property and not a supply of services. The s.74 SCN dated 20.12.2024 (Rs 27,00,000) was quashed. Classifying the assignment as "other miscellaneous services" (entry 35, Notf. 11/2017) was also held wrong. Once the rights are assigned, the assignor's rights end, so there is no lease or letting under Sch. II. The court followed Gujarat HC as a ruling on a central Act (citing *Godavari Devi Saraf*, Bom HC 1978).
- Key words: "the transaction would not be subject to levy of GST in terms of the GST Act." (para 14)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: SC (Dipankar Datta, Satish Chandra Sharma JJ) condoned delay and dismissed the Revenue's SLP(C) Diary No. 26041/2026 on 22.05.2026 ("not inclined to interfere"; no reasons given).
- Use in orders: Same as Gujarat Chamber. Note the *Godavari Devi Saraf* reasoning: absent a contrary HC, a HC ruling on a central Act is followed across States.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/23506487/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 22.05.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/173317666/); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/bombay-high-court/aerocom-cushions-private-limited-v-assistant-commissioner-2026bhc-nag348-db-assignment-leasehold-rights-not-taxable-gst-1605125); [Mondaq (SAM)](https://www.mondaq.com/no-gst-on-assignment-of-industrial-leasehold-rights-bombay-high-court-grants-relief-to-industrial-lessees-holding-midc-plots/1839628)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; HC judgment and SC order both read; SLP number corrected (was "SLP(C) 18772/2026", order shows Diary No. 26041/2026); bench and WP no. added.

#### Prahitha Constructions Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India - Telangana HC - 09.02.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 5493 of 2020 (DB: P. Sam Koshy, Laxmi Narayana Alishetty JJ).
- Holding: A JDA does not transfer title to the developer, so transfer of development rights is not "sale of land" under Sch. III para 5. It is a taxable supply linked to construction service. The challenge to Notf. 4/2018-CT(Rate) (as amended by Notf. 23/2019) failed, because the notification only fixes the time of supply and levies nothing.
- Key words: "the transfer of development rights as it stands is amenable to GST and cannot be brought within the purview of Entry 5 of Schedule-III of the GST Act." (para 39)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: SLP(C) No. 11079/2024. The SC (Khanna and Datta JJ) issued notice on 13.05.2024. Reports say **no stay** was granted, so tax stays payable. No final decision was found.
- Use in orders: Rely on it for TDR/JDA demands in Telangana. Note the pending SLP and confirm the time of supply under Notf. 4/2018 (as amended) and, from 01.04.2019, the RCM entry for TDR.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/63683670/); [EY alert Feb 2024](https://www.ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2024/02/hc-holds-transfer-of-development-rights-by-landowner-to-developer-under-jda-is-exigible-to-gst); [CAclubindia - SC notice](https://www.caclubindia.com/judiciary/sc-issues-notice-against-hc-judgment-imposing-gst-on-transfer-of-development-rights-to-real-estate-developers-by-joint-development-agreement-5678.asp); [taxo.online 14.05.2024](https://taxo.online/latest-news/14-05-2024-supreme-court-seeks-gst-clarity-on-joint-development-agreements-by-real-estate-developers/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; bench added. SC notice/no-stay in SLP(C) 11079/2024 not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026 (secondary only).

#### Munjaal Manishbhai Bhatt v. Union of India - Gujarat HC - 06.05.2022
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 1350 of 2021 with SCA Nos. 6840 of 2021 and 5052 of 2022 (DB: J.B. Pardiwala, Nisha M. Thakore JJ).
- Holding: The mandatory one-third land deduction in para 2 of Notf. 11/2017-CT(Rate) (and the State notification) is ultra vires the GST Acts and violates Art. 14. It was **read down, not struck down**: the one-third is available only at the taxpayer's option where the actual value of land or undivided share is not ascertainable. Refund with 6% interest was ordered to the petitioner-buyer.
- Key words: "read down to the effect that the deeming fiction of 1/3rd will not be mandatory in nature. It will only be available at the option of the taxable person" (para 124)
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: The Union's SLP(C) No. 21703/2022 (with connected SLPs and W.P.(C) 1382/2023) is pending in the SC without any stay shown in the orders read; on 18.08.2026 it was listed for 07.12.2026.
- Use in orders: Where a builder shows the actual land value (sale deed or guideline value) and asks for it, don't insist on the one-third. Where the notification is applied, record why the actual value can't be ascertained.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/48062053/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 18.08.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/95800048/); [Taxmann case analysis](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/case-analysis-munjaal-manishbhai-bhatt-v-union-of-india); [AMLegals](https://amlegals.com/mandatory-deduction-of-1-3rd-of-land-value-is-ultra-vires-the-cgst-act/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; "struck down" corrected to "read down" (para 124); bench and case nos. added; pending SLP found (was "no appeal found").

#### Shraddha Tiwari and others v. Bhopal Development Authority - Madhya Pradesh HC (DB: Sheel Nagu, Prakash Chandra Gupta JJ) - 27.10.2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 257 of 2022 (Jabalpur).
- Holding: Relying on the CBIC circular of 03.08.2022 (para 14) (sale of land after levelling or laying drainage is still sale of land under Sch. III para 5), the court disposed of the writ by directing the BDA to reconsider levying 12% GST on developed plots sold to the petitioners. It did not itself decide the point.
- Key words: "directing the Bhopal Development Authority to reconsider the question of levy of GST on the sale of developed plots ... in the light of aforesaid circular" (para 4)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Sale of developed plots alone (no construction) is generally outside GST. Development services supplied to the developer remain taxable.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/184414473/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=221459); [busy.in](https://busy.in/caselaw/shraddha-tiwari-and-others-vs-bhopal-development-authority-and-others-madhya-pradesh-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and WP no. added; clarified that the court only directed reconsideration.

---

### 2. Liquidated damages, notice pay, compensation (Sch. II para 5(e); Circular 178/10/2022)

**Contentions across courts.** Karnataka HC (*Krazybee*, *Aavanti Solar*) follows Circular 178 in favour of taxpayers. Under service tax, Madras HC (*GE T&D*) and several CESTAT benches held notice pay and damages not taxable. Maharashtra AAR (*MSPGCL*, before the circular) held liquidated damages taxable at 18% as "tolerating" non-performance. Gujarat AAR (*JBM Ecolife*, Nov 2025) follows the circular. No SC ruling. **Department position:** Circular 178/10/2022 says compensation for breach is not consideration unless it pays for a separate promise to tolerate or refrain. Some payments (e.g. late payment charges, cancellation charges where the contract provides for them) are treated as part of the main supply.

#### Krazybee Services Pvt. Ltd. v. Additional Director, DGGI - Karnataka HC (S.R. Krishna Kumar J) - 08.12.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 16471 of 2024 (T-RES); NC: 2025:KHC:51726.
- Holding: Liquidated damages an NBFC recovered from a lending service provider for breach or delay under a master service agreement are compensation, not consideration for "tolerating an act" under Sch. II para 5(e). The DGGI SCN dated 25.04.2024 was quashed, relying on paras 7.1 to 7.1.6 of Circular 178/10/2022, and refund of Rs 5 crore paid under protest, with interest, was ordered.
- Key words: "the amount received by the petitioner from its LSPs by way of liquidated damages fall outside the purview of GST in terms of the said Circular" (para 10)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: A para 5(e) demand needs evidence of a separate agreement to tolerate. A penalty clause in the main contract is not enough.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/125199237/); [LiveLaw Biz](https://www.livelawbiz.com/high-court/karnataka-high-court/no-gst-on-liquidated-damages-for-breach-of-contract-karnataka-high-court-quashes-scn-516620); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/liquidated-damages-gst-misconceptions-notices-legal-position.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 08.12.2025 confirmed; SCN date and refund direction added.

#### Aavanti Solar Energy Pvt. Ltd. v. Joint Commissioner of Central Tax - Karnataka HC (S.R. Krishna Kumar J) - 09.09.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 19477 of 2024 (T-RES); NC: 2024:KHC:36649.
- Holding: The order-in-original dated 28.03.2024 demanding GST on liquidated damages had not considered Circular 178/10/2022 (03.08.2022). That vitiated the order, which was set aside and remitted for fresh decision bearing the circular in mind. No ruling on merits.
- Key words: "had not been adverted/considered in the impugned order, which is vitiated on account of non-consideration of the said circular." (para 5)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: **Always discuss Circular 178 in a damages or compensation case.** Leaving it out is enough to get the order quashed.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140676627/); [TaxGuru - Karnataka HC remits](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/karnataka-hc-remits-gst-case-liquidated-damages.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and WP no. added; summary confirmed.

#### GE T&D India Ltd. v. Deputy Commissioner of Central Excise - Madras HC (Anita Sumanth J) - 07.11.2019 (service tax)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 35728 to 35734 of 2016 (07.11.2019); followed in W.P. No. 26292 of 2018 (13.12.2019). Reporter citations differ: [2020] 119 taxmann.com 55 (Mad.); 2019 (12) TMI 1566; 2020 (1) TMI 1096.
- Holding: An employer who lets an employee leave early on payment of the notice-pay amount is not "tolerating an act" for consideration. It is only facilitating exit on compensation, so notice pay is not taxable. The judgment deals only with notice pay; it notes that breach of a non-compete stipulation could be a different case.
- Key words: "the employer has not 'tolerated' any act of the employee but has permitted a sudden exit upon being compensated by the employee in this regard." (para 11)
- Binding: Persuasive (service tax; s.66E(e) Finance Act 1994 has the same wording as GST Sch. II para 5(e)).
- Later history: Followed by CESTAT in *Rajasthan Rajya Vidyut Prasaran Nigam* (Delhi), *Intas Pharmaceuticals*, *Cosmo First*, *Ami Lifesciences* (Ahmedabad), *Balaji Medical* (Delhi). No reversal found.
- Use in orders: Don't demand GST on notice-pay recoveries without a reasoned distinction. In GST, Sch. III para 1 (employer-employee) also applies.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - 07.11.2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/36001213/); [Indian Kanoon - 13.12.2019](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/110741215/); [Taxmann - CESTAT on notice pay](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/no-service-tax-on-notice-pay-recovery-by-employer-from-employee-cestat); [TaxTMI](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=10565)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, judge and WP nos. added; removed "also cited for liquidated damages" (nothing in the judgment supports it). Later CESTAT rulings not checked.

#### Maharashtra State Power Generation Co. Ltd. - Maharashtra AAR - before 2022 (date not found); and JBM Ecolife Mobility (Surat) - Gujarat AAR - Nov 2025
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: MSPGCL: liquidated damages were consideration for tolerating non-performance, taxable at 18%. JBM Ecolife: liquidated damages are compensation, not consideration (follows Circular 178).
- Binding: AAR, binding only on each applicant.
- Later history: MSPGCL came before Circular 178, which takes the contrary view. Its weight is now doubtful.
- Use in orders: Don't cite MSPGCL against the circular.
- Source: [Mondaq - GST on liquidated damages](https://www.mondaq.co.uk/india/sales-taxes-vat-gst/1282986/gst-implications-on-liquidated-damages); [taxo.online 13.11.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/13-11-2025-liquidated-damages-being-compensatory-and-not-contractual-consideration-are-not-exigible-to-gst-gujarat-aar/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; AAR rulings are generally not on it).

---

### 3. Employee recoveries, director's remuneration, cross-charge

**Contentions.** *Canteen:* Gujarat AARs (Suzuki Motor Gujarat; Torrent Pharmaceuticals; a Dec 2025 ruling) say recoveries from permanent employees are not taxable but recoveries from contract or deputed workers are. ITC on canteen services is available only to the extent of cost the employer actually bears (Torrent). No HC ruling found. *Directors:* Rajasthan AAR (*Clay Craft*) taxed whole-time directors' salary under RCM. Circular 140/10/2020 overtook that ruling. *Cross-charge:* Karnataka AAAR (*Columbia Asia*) says HO employee costs must be cross-charged. Circular 199/11/2023 and a Delhi HC ruling (June 2025) take a softer line where ITC is fully available.

#### Gujarat AAR rulings on canteen recoveries (Suzuki Motor Gujarat; Torrent Pharmaceuticals; Dec 2025 ruling) - Gujarat AAR - various
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: The portion recovered from **permanent** employees for a statutory canteen (Factories Act) is not a supply by the employer (Circular 172/04/2022). Recoveries from contract or deputed workers are taxable because they aren't employees of the applicant. ITC on canteen service is restricted to the cost the employer bears.
- Binding: AAR, binding only on each applicant.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Check whether recoveries include contract workers (taxable) and whether ITC was proportionately reversed.
- Source: [taxo.online 03.12.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/03-12-2025-canteen-recoveries-from-regular-employees-remain-non-taxable-however-recoveries-from-contractual-workers-continue-to-be-taxable-gujarat-aar/); [Conventus Law](https://conventuslaw.com/report/india-gst-on-canteen-facilities-and-its-applicability-on-non-permanent-employees/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Clay Craft India Pvt. Ltd. - Rajasthan AAR - 2020 (date not found); and CBIC Circular 140/10/2020-GST - 10.06.2020
- Citation / case no.: not found (AAR).
- Holding: AAR: salary to whole-time directors is liable to RCM under Notf. 13/2017 because a director is not an employee. Circular 140/10/2020 resolved the issue: (a) pay declared as salary and subject to TDS under s.192 IT Act is a service by an employee, outside GST under Sch. III para 1; (b) pay to non-employee or independent directors, or pay booked as professional fees with TDS under s.194J, is taxable under RCM (Notf. 13/2017 entry 6).
- Binding: AAR binds only the applicant. The circular binds officers.
- Later history: AAAR outcome not found. The circular has overtaken the AAR.
- Use in orders: Base an RCM demand on the TDS section and the accounts treatment. Don't rely on Clay Craft.
- Source: [Indiacorplaw on Circular 140](https://indiacorplaw.in/2020/07/04/gst-implication-on-directors-remuneration-cbics-circular/); [Vinod Kothari](https://vinodkothari.com/2020/08/gst-on-consideration-paid-to-a-director/); [Circular text (GST Council)](https://www.gstcouncil.gov.in/sites/default/files/2024-06/circular_refund_140_10_2020.pdf)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources agree). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Delhi HC on HO services not cross-charged (party name not found) - Delhi HC - around June 2025
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Per summaries, IGST is not payable on HO services not cross-charged to branches where the branches are entitled to full ITC. The demand was set aside and remanded for reconsideration under the CBIC circular (199/11/2023) and *Metal One Corporation*. Internal costs (HO salaries) need not be included in value where the recipient is fully ITC-eligible.
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Before raising a cross-charge demand, establish that the recipient branch is **not** fully ITC-eligible. Otherwise the deemed value under rule 28 / Circular 199 is the invoice value, including nil.
- Source: [Taxscan tag page (headline)](https://www.taxscan.in/tags/justice-prathiba-m.-singh/page-7); [CBCL NLIU commentary](https://cbcl.nliu.ac.in/taxation/delving-into-unsettled-dispute-of-cross-charging-within-organisation-its-time-for-decision/)
- Verified: Secondary - single source for the holding; case name not found. (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Columbia Asia Hospitals Pvt. Ltd. - Karnataka AAAR - 2018 (date not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Services of HO employees (accounts, HR, management) that benefit other registered units are supplies between distinct persons and must be cross-charged. ISD can only distribute input-service ITC, while cross-charge covers all costs including salaries.
- Binding: AAAR, binding only on the applicant.
- Later history: Diluted for fully ITC-eligible recipients by Circular 199/11/2023 and the Delhi HC ruling above.
- Use in orders: Persuasive for recipients with exempt or blocked supplies only.
- Source: [Khaitan & Co](https://www.khaitanco.com/thought-leadership/appellate-authority-confirms-that-activities-carried-out-by-an-employee-for-an-office-located); [Taxmann opinion](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/opinion-services-given-by-branch-employees-to-its-head-office-is-under-gst-purview/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Karnataka HC stay - import of services by Indian HO from overseas branches (party name not found) - Karnataka HC - around Aug 2024
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Interim stay of GST proceedings alleging import of services by an Indian HO from its overseas branches. Commentary notes tribunals have held no supply arises between an Indian HO and its own overseas branch.
- Binding: Interim order only.
- Later history: Final outcome not found.
- Use in orders: Treat HO/overseas-branch "import" demands as contested. Check the final outcome before proceeding.
- Source: [EY alert Aug 2024](https://ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2024/08/karnataka-hc-stays-gst-proceedings-alleging-import-of-services-by-indian-head-office)
- Verified: Secondary - single source. (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

**Cross-reference:** corporate guarantees and rule 28(2) - *Torrent Power* (file 04). Secondment - *Northern Operating Systems* (SC, file 04).

---

### 4. Valuation: reimbursements and pure agent

**Contentions.** Under service tax, the SC (*Intercontinental*) excluded reimbursements before 14.05.2015. Under GST, s.15(2)(c) includes incidental expenses, so only rule 33 (pure agent) excludes them. AARs apply rule 33 strictly (WB AAR, toll charges: taxable; Feel Good Hospitality: taxable) but sometimes allow it (WB AAR, electricity recovered at cost from a sub-lessee: excluded). No HC ruling under GST was found.

#### Union of India v. Intercontinental Consultants and Technocrats Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court (A.K. Sikri, Ashok Bhushan JJ) - 07.03.2018
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 2013 of 2014 with connected appeals and transferred cases (affirms Delhi HC, 2012).
- Holding: Rule 5(1) of the Service Tax (Determination of Value) Rules, 2006 (including reimbursed expenses in value) was ultra vires ss.66/67 Finance Act 1994. Tax is only on the value of the service actually rendered. Reimbursements became includible only after s.67 was amended (14.05.2015), a substantive and not declaratory change. The Revenue's appeals were dismissed.
- Key words: "it is the value of the services which are actually rendered, the value whereof is to be ascertained for the purpose of calculating the service tax payable thereupon." (para 23)
- Binding: SC, binding, but on service tax.
- Later history: Overtaken for GST by s.15(2)(c) CGST Act and rule 33. Still cited for the principle that rules cannot widen the charging section.
- Use in orders: Don't cite it to exclude reimbursements under GST. Test them against rule 33 instead.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/64314027/); [TaxGuru - judgment PDF link](https://taxguru.in/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/Union-of-India-ANR.-Vs-Ms-Intercontinental-Consultants-and-Technocrats-Ltd.-Supreme-Court-of-India.pdf); [StudyCafe](https://studycafe.in/no-service-tax-on-reimbursements-prior-to-may-14-2015-held-by-sc-22056.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; bench and case no. added.

#### West Bengal AAR on pure agent (toll charges) - 20/WBAAR/2018-19 - 02.11.2018
- Citation / case no.: 20/WBAAR/2018-19.
- Holding: Toll charges recovered from banks by a cash-logistics provider are part of the value because the banks never authorised the applicant as pure agent or accepted the toll as their own liability.
- Binding: AAR, binding only on the applicant.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: When denying pure-agent status, cite the specific rule 33 condition not met (contractual authorisation, separate invoice line, recipient's liability, no title).
- Source: [TaxHeal](https://taxheal.com/pay-gst-on-reimbursement-of-toll-taxes-aar.html); [Taxmann - electricity charges (contrary facts)](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/electricity-charges-reimbursed-by-sub-lessee-not-taxable-as-lessee-acts-as-pure-agent-aar)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

---

### 5. Classification of goods (GST and excise/VAT rulings still applied)

**Contentions.** *Flavoured milk:* AP HC (5%, heading 0402) was upheld after the SC dismissed the Revenue's challenge; Karnataka HC followed it in Jan 2026. Earlier AARs and the department applied 12% (heading 2202). *Fruit-based carbonated drinks:* Gauhati HC says 12%; the department applied 28% (2202 10). *Parotta:* Kerala HC single judge says 5% (1905); the State's writ appeal is pending with an interim stay. Earlier AAR/AAAR said 18% (2106). *Odomos:* AAR, AAAR and Allahabad HC say 18% (3808), not a medicament; SC status unclear. *Fryums:* AARs disagree (TN: papad; Gujarat: 18%). No SC GST ruling.

#### Sri Vijaya Visakha Milk Producers Company Ltd v. State of Andhra Pradesh - AP HC (DB: R. Raghunandan Rao, Maheswara Rao Kuncheam JJ) - 10.12.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 254 of 2024 (APHC010000852024).
- Holding: Flavoured milk stays classified under heading 0402 99 90 (2.5% CGST + 2.5% SGST). Adding about 0.5% badam flavour does not take it out of 0402 into beverages (2202). Heading 0402 itself covers milk with added sugar and milk products.
- Key words: "flavoured milk cannot be taken out of tariff heading 0402 merely because of addition of 0.5% of Badam flavour." (para 12)
- Binding: Persuasive in Telangana (AP HC). The SC dismissal adds weight.
- Later history: The SC (Sanjiv Khanna CJI, Sanjay Kumar J) condoned delay and dismissed the Revenue's SLP(C) Diary No. 17602/2025 on 09.05.2025 ("not inclined to interfere"; no reasons). A separate Revenue SLP (Diary No. 18877/2025, against AP HC W.P. 28222/2024 of 08.01.2025 in the same company's case) was dismissed on 05.05.2025. Heritage Foods' writs were disposed of on the same lines with liberty to seek refund. Karnataka HC followed it in Jan 2026.
- Use in orders: Don't apply 12% to flavoured milk that remains milk in essential character.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/59808889/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 09.05.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/41399892/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 05.05.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/79731816/); [TaxHeal - SLP dismissed](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=104300); [taxo.online 10.05.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/10-05-2025-supreme-court-upholds-5-gst-on-badam-flavoured-milk/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/supreme-court-upholds-gst-rate-5-percent-rate-flavoured-milk.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; HC holding confirmed; SC order read: it was an SLP dismissal on 09.05.2025 (not a civil appeal). Heritage Foods and Karnataka HC points not checked.

#### Girish Pravinbhai Rathod (Jay Ambey) v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (Bhargav D. Karia, Niral R. Mehta JJ) - 14.06.2024
- Citation / case no.: Special Civil Application No. 17980 of 2021; 2024 Supreme (Online) (GUJ) 9112.
- Holding: Cotton fusible interlining fabric that is only partially coated or covered with plastics, with a plastic-coated pattern visible on one side (ATIRA test report), is excluded from heading 5903 by Chapter 59 Note 2(a)(4) and falls under Chapters 50-55, 58 or 60. The Gujarat AAAR order of 08.04.2021 was quashed.
- Key words: "the product manufactured by the petitioner being partially covered with plastic coated pattern i.e. visible on one side of the fabric and the same will fall under Chapters 50 to 55 58 or 60" (para 13)
- Binding: Persuasive. The SC dismissal adds weight.
- Later history: **The SC (J.B. Pardiwala, R. Mahadevan JJ) dismissed the Union's SLP(C) Diary No. 22241/2025 on 16.05.2025** on delay (225 days) "as well as on merits".
- Use in orders: The chapter and section notes decide classification. An AAR ruling for another party carries no weight.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HC](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/136703578/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 16.05.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/16179432/); [Taxscan - SC upholds](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=519676); [TaxGuru - Gujarat HC](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/fusible-interlining-fabric-cotton-falls-chapters-50-55-58-60-gujarat-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date added; basis corrected (was "coating can't be seen with the naked eye"; court relied on partial coating with pattern visible on one side, Note 2(a)(4)); SC dismissal date and grounds added.

#### X'SS Beverage Co. v. State of Assam - Gauhati HC (Soumitra Saikia J) - 04.03.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) Nos. 5340, 5341, 5342, 5346 and 5347 of 2022 (periods July 2017 to August 2021).
- Holding: Before 01.10.2021, carbonated drinks based on fruit pulp or juice fell under tariff item 2202 99 20 ("fruit pulp or fruit juice based drinks") at 12%, not 2202 10 (28% plus 12% cess). The specific 28% + 12% cess entries for carbonated fruit beverages (Notf. 8/2021-CT(Rate) and 1/2021-Comp. Cess (Rate)) apply only from 01.10.2021. The court applied the essential character test, relied on *Parle Agro (Appy Fizz)*, and also set aside the s.74 invocation, penalty and interest. The SCN of 17.02.2022 and order of 14.07.2024 were quashed.
- Key words: "earlier the items in question were covered by Tariff Item 2022 90 20 and only after issue of the aforesaid Notification, the same is made taxable at a higher rate." (para 205)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Appeal not found. The department applied 28% plus 12% compensation cess (the judgment does not mention 40%).
- Use in orders: For periods from 01.10.2021 the specific carbonated-fruit-beverage entry applies, so this ruling matters mainly for earlier periods. When rejecting a fruit-drink claim, deal with fruit content and essential character on evidence (test reports).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/59998946/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=498313); [taxo.online 12.03.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/12-03-2025-fruit-pulp-carbonated-drinks-to-attract-12-gst-rules-gauhati-high-court/); [Desikaanoon](https://desikaanoon.in/gauhati-hc-fizzy-fruit-juice-to-be-taxed-at-12-gst-as-a-fruit-based-drink-not-28)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date confirmed; WP nos. and respondent added; holding narrowed to pre-01.10.2021 periods (rate notifications of 30.09.2021); 28%/40% point corrected.

#### Modern Food Enterprises Pvt. Ltd. v. Union of India - Kerala HC (Dinesh Kumar Singh J) - April 2024
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 13935 of 2021; 2024 LiveLaw (Ker) 269.
- Holding: "Classic" and "Whole Wheat" Malabar parotta are akin to bread under HSN 1905, at 5%, not 18%. The AAR and AAAR were overturned.
- Binding: Persuasive, and **stayed** (see below).
- Later history: The State filed a writ appeal on 20.05.2024. A Division Bench (Muhamed Mustaque and S. Manu JJ) stayed the judgment for two months. One report says it was referred to mediation. Final outcome not found.
- Use in orders: Don't treat it as settled law while the appeal is pending.
- Source: [LiveLaw](https://livelaw.in/high-court/kerala-high-court/malabar-parota-bread-gst-kerala-high-court-255264); [Taxscan - stay](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=410730)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources agree). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

#### Dabur India Ltd. v. Union of India (Odomos) - Allahabad HC (DB: Biswanath Somadder, Ajay Bhanot JJ) - 17.01.2020
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 1308 of 2019; AIRONLINE 2020 ALL 72.
- Holding: Odomos is a mosquito repellent (Chapter 38, 18%), not a medicament (3004). The court applied the twin test from *Puma Ayurvedic* (SC) and common parlance or market identity. Reviewing the UP AAAR order of 19.08.2019 within the limits of judicial review, it found no infirmity and dismissed the writ.
- Key words: "The product is a mosquito repellent by virtue of its mosquito repelling characteristics and is so understood in common parlance." (para 32)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: A TaxGuru listing suggests an SC matter titled *Dabur India Ltd v. Union of India*. Outcome not found.
- Use in orders: For medicament vs cosmetic or repellent disputes, apply the common-parlance test plus the therapeutic test, with evidence.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/25294488/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1040995); [Taxscan - AAAR](https://www.taxscan.in/18-gst-applicable-on-odomos-aaar/106173)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and case no. added; summary confirmed. SC outcome not checked.

#### Commissioner of Central Excise, Salem v. Madhan Agro Industries (India) Pvt. Ltd. - Supreme Court (3 judges: Sanjiv Khanna CJI, Sanjay Kumar, R. Mahadevan JJ) - 18.12.2024
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 1766 of 2009 with C.A. Nos. 6703-6710 of 2009; 2024 INSC 1002 (judgment by Sanjay Kumar J).
- Holding: Pure coconut oil in small packs (5 ml to 2 litres) is edible oil under heading 1513 unless the packaging meets Chapter 33 Note 3 (labels or other indications that it is for use as hair oil). The possibility of hair-oil use alone does not move it to 3305. This resolved the 2018 split (Gogoi J vs Banumathi J). The Revenue's appeals were dismissed.
- Key words: "pure coconut oil sold in small quantities as 'edible oil' would be classifiable under Heading 1513 ... unless the packaging thereof satisfies all the requirements set out in Chapter Note 3" (para 48)
- Binding: SC, binding (Central Excise Tariff). GST rate schedules follow the same HSN structure, but applying it to GST is an inference; no GST-specific ruling was found.
- Later history: none.
- Use in orders: For coconut oil packs, look at labelling and packaging under Ch. 33 Note 3, not end use.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/61010102/); [SC judgment PDF (sci.gov.in)](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2008/36232/36232_2008_1_1501_57992_Judgement_18-Dec-2024.pdf); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/app-lite/court-updates/supreme-court/commissioner-of-central-excise-v-madhan-agro-industries-india-private-ltd-2024-insc-1002-pure-coconut-oil-edible-oil-1561655?hasShare=1); [LiveLaw round-up](https://livelaw.in/tax-cases/tax-weekly-round-up-16th-december-22nd-december-2024-279654)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; connected appeals added.

#### Addl. Commissioner, Commercial Tax v. Cadila Health Care Ltd. - Supreme Court (Manmohan, Arun Palli JJ) - 05.10.2026
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal Nos. 9788-9789 of 2013; 2026 INSC 1078 (judgment by Manmohan J; appeal from MP HC, W.P. Nos. 3020 and 4633 of 2003, 03.08.2011).
- Holding: Classification depends on the form of the goods at the time of sale (the taxable event), not on how the consumer later uses them. "GRD Powder" and "GRD Mix" (powder and biscuit form) fell under the residuary entry of the MP Commercial Tax Act 1994, not "non-alcoholic drinks and beverages". Goods not answering a specific entry go to the residuary entry and can't be forced into one for a higher rate. The MP HC was upheld and the appeals dismissed.
- Key words: "the tax authorities are bound to levy tax based on the form of the good at the time of sale. The tax authorities are bound to look at what is supplied and not at what is the 'end use' of the good." (para 17)
- Binding: SC, binding as a principle of classification (decided under MP Commercial Tax Act 1994).
- Later history: none (very recent).
- Use in orders: Classify goods as supplied. Don't rely on end use unless the tariff entry itself is end-use based.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/9098471/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/supreme-court/tax-classification-must-be-based-on-form-of-good-at-stage-of-sale-not-its-end-use-supreme-court-553219); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/supreme-court/addl-commr-commercial-tax-ors-v-cadila-health-care-ltd-2026-insc-1078-tax-goods-consumer-1623458); [SCObserver](https://www.scobserver.in/supreme-court-observer-law-reports-scolr/addl-commr-commercial-tax-v-cadila-health-care/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed (the statute is the MP Commercial Tax Act 1994, not "MP VAT").

#### Commissioner of Customs (Import) v. Welkin Foods (aluminium shelves) - Supreme Court (J.B. Pardiwala, R. Mahadevan JJ) - 06.01.2026
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 5531 of 2025; 2026 INSC 19.
- Holding: The General Rules of Interpretation must be applied in sequence. GRI 1 (heading terms and section/chapter notes) comes first. Imported aluminium shelves for a mushroom-growing system were classified under CTI 7610 90 10 as "aluminium structures", not as parts of agricultural machinery (8436). The Revenue's appeal was allowed and the CESTAT order of 19.04.2024 set aside.
- Key words: "GRIs 1-4 must be applied sequentially. ... GRI 1, which gives primacy to the headings and notes, is the non-negotiable starting point." (para 36)
- Binding: SC, binding (Customs Tariff; same GRIs apply to GST through the explanation to the rate notifications).
- Later history: none.
- Use in orders: In the classification paragraph, go through GRI 1 and the notes before invoking GRI 3.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/96464777/); [Taxscan weekly round-up](https://www.taxscan.in/other-taxations/supreme-court-high-courts-weekly-round-up-1441792); [LiveLaw Biz digest Jan 2026](https://www.livelawbiz.com/digests/monthly-digests/indirect-tax-monthly-digest-january-2026-521986)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; full title, date, case no. and INSC citation added; summary confirmed.

#### Sahasra Enterprises v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax - Telangana HC (DB: Aparesh Kumar Singh CJ, G.M. Mohiuddin J) - 20.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 5324 of 2026.
- Holding: A sub-sub-contractor challenged a s.74 order (OIO 17.09.2025, Rs 36,81,736) applying 18% instead of 12%. The court declined to decide rate or classification in writ jurisdiction because it needs examination of facts and transactions, and relegated the petitioner to a s.107 appeal (within two weeks, with delay to be considered in light of the writ). No comment on merits.
- Key words: "the issue involved herein is not of jurisdictional error or violation of principles of natural justice, which requires interference in the writ jurisdiction under Article 226" (para 5)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Expect the merits to be tested in appeal, so set out full findings of fact on the nature of work, the contract and the notification entry. Justify s.74 separately (see *G.R. Infra Projects*, SC, file 03).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/106789372/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-directs-gst-taxpayer-pursue-statutory-appeal-classification-dispute.html); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-on-gst-classification-disputes-assessee-directed-to-use)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, bench and WP no. added; summary confirmed.

---

### 6. Works contracts and rates; change in rate (s.14)

**Contentions.** Madras HC (*STS-KEC JV*) says the nature of the work (entry 3(v): railways) decides the rate, regardless of who the recipient is. Telangana AARs (KPC Projects, Shree Constructions, Siddhartha Constructions; see file 05) say that after 01.01.2022, works for "Government Entity" under entry 3(vi) attract 18%, and that before then TSIIC warehouses used for business were not covered. No Telangana HC merits ruling was found. A Telangana HC petition (2026) on 2BHK housing contracts (12% to 17.07.2022 and 18% from 18.07.2022) is pending; outcome not found.

#### STS-KEC JV v. State Tax Officer - Madras HC (Mohammed Shaffiq J) - 28.01.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) Nos. 3938 to 3942 of 2024 (Madurai Bench).
- Holding: A works contract from RVNL for railway infrastructure is covered by entry 3(v) of Notf. 11/2017-CT(Rate) (as amended by Notf. 20/2017), Notf. 8/2017-IT(Rate) and the State G.O., and is taxable at 12%, not 18%. "Railway" in entry 3(v) is not confined to Indian Railways or to any particular entity, so RVNL's corporate status (PSU, not under direct Railway control) did not matter: a track-doubling contract is "original work pertaining to railway". The impugned orders were set aside.
- Key words: "the definition of Railway under IRA ... does not appear to limit its operation to any particular entity or in particular Indian Railways instead covers the entire industry / utility viz., Railway." (para 17.1)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: Appeal not found.
- Use in orders: For railway, road, bridge or similar contracts before the Sept 2025 rate changes, check entry 3(v) before applying 3(vi) or 18%.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/134831908/); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/madras-high-court/sts-kec-jv-v-the-state-tax-officer-wp-md-nos-3938-to-3942-of-2024-1573396); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=494287); [taxo.online 27.02.2025](https://taxo.online/?p=526591)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; holding stated in the court's terms (scope of "railway").

#### M/s SPK and Co. v. Additional Commissioner (s.14: contractor invoicing after the 18.07.2022 rate increase) - Madras HC, Madurai Bench (Krishnan Ramasamy J) - 10.12.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) No. 35386 of 2025.
- Holding: Work was completed on 08.06.2022 (12%). The invoice was issued after the rate rose to 18% on 18.07.2022. The court refused under Art. 226 to direct the recipient (Divisional Engineer, National Highways) to reimburse the 6% difference, because the delay in invoicing was prima facie the petitioner's and the facts were disputed. The writ was dismissed with liberty to approach a civil court or arbitral forum.
- Key words: "the delay in raising the invoice prior to 18.07.2022 and in effecting payment subsequently is attributable to the petitioner" (para 9)
- Binding: Persuasive (a contractual reimbursement dispute; no ruling on tax liability).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Apply s.14 mechanically. Where completion is before the change and both invoice and payment are after it, the new rate applies (s.14(a)(i) / (b)).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/121006711/); [TaxHeal - GST rate change and s.14](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=115227)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; court, parties, date and WP no. identified; holding confirmed with the recipient and the liberty added.

**Cross-reference:** KPC Projects / Shree Constructions / Siddhartha Constructions (Telangana AAR, works for government entity) - file 05, section 14.

---

### 7. Place of supply and tax paid under the wrong head

**Contentions.** Karnataka and Madras HCs (2026): where IGST was paid on what is held to be an intra-State supply, the IGST must be adjusted or credited before recovering CGST/SGST with interest and penalty. Madras HC: a clerical wrong-head payment is outside s.77 and should be corrected by appropriation. The department's position is a demand for CGST/SGST plus a separate s.77 refund (rule 89(1A)). Ocean freight under s.13(9) IGST (omitted from 01.10.2023) and *Mohit Minerals* are in file 04. Intermediary is in file 01. No HC ruling on s.12(8) or OIDAR was found in this round.

#### GR Tech Services v. Assistant Commissioner - Karnataka HC (B.M. Shyam Prasad J) - 03.06.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 15670 of 2026 (T-RES); NC: 2026:KHC:26145.
- Holding: Where IGST was paid on a supply later treated as intra-State, the wrongly paid IGST must be adjusted against CGST/SGST before any coercive recovery of tax, interest or penalty (s.77(2) read with rule 92). Following Kerala HC (*Saji S.*, 2018), the adjudication order, order-in-original and DRC-13 demand were quashed and the matter remanded (writ allowed in part).
- Key words: "the original authority has not read the provisions of Section 77(2) of the CGST/KGST Act in conjunction with Rule 92 of the GST Rules in raising a demand on the petitioner along with interest and penalty." (para 6)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: In a place-of-supply demand, quantify the IGST already paid and provide for adjustment. Don't charge interest on the full CGST/SGST as if nothing had been paid.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/165730450/); [thetaxcorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/igst-mistakenly-paid-on-inter-state-supply-can-offset-cgstsgst-dues-without); [A2Z Taxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/?p=36712)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, judge and WP no. added; summary confirmed.

#### SYA Homes v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) (wrong tax head is not unpaid tax) - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J) - 07.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 23463 of 2026.
- Holding: Where the entire liability was reported under IGST by clerical error instead of CGST/SGST, the taxpayer can't be forced to pay CGST/SGST again before seeking a refund. Ss.19 IGST / 77 CGST and rule 89(1A) cover misclassification of a supply as inter-State or intra-State, not clerical head errors. Rs 5,84,832.70 shown under IGST in a Sept 2021 GSTR-3B was ordered to be appropriated to CGST/SGST within 30 days of the petitioner's application; the rectification order of 17.02.2026 was set aside to that extent.
- Key words: "None of these provisions apply to a case where the tax was paid inadvertently under a wrong head." (para 6)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Separate a genuine place-of-supply dispute (s.77 route) from a clerical head error (appropriation route).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/139797004/); [taxo.online 16.07.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/16-07-2026-wrong-tax-head-does-not-mean-unpaid-tax-when-the-total-tax-liability-has-already-been-discharged-madras-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name, judge and WP no. identified; date corrected from 16.07.2026 (report date) to 07.07.2026.

---

### 8. Reverse charge: renting residential dwellings

#### Seema Gupta v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Manmohan, Manmeet Pritam Singh Arora JJ) - 27.09.2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 10986/2022; [2022] 142 taxmann.com 564 (Delhi).
- Holding: The petitioner challenged clause (A)(b) of Notf. 04/2022-CT(Rate) (13.07.2022), which withdrew the exemption for renting residential dwellings to registered persons. The court accepted the government's clarification (second supplementary affidavit, 23.09.2022) that renting to a **proprietor** of a registered proprietorship who rents in a personal capacity, for own residence and on own account, stays exempt, held all respondents bound by it, and disposed of the writ. The same treatment for partners appears only in the government's earlier counter affidavit quoted in the order, not in the court's operative direction.
- Key words: "the aforesaid clarification ... shall be exempt from GST, is accepted by this Court and all the respondents are held bound by the same." (order, last para)
- Binding: Persuasive (it records the government's own position).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Before an RCM demand, establish that the tenant rented in a business capacity or for business use. Odisha AAR held RCM applies whatever the use. Rajasthan AAR held property leased for commercial use is not a "residential dwelling" (AAR, binding only on the applicant).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/92452318/); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/renting-of-residential-dwelling-by-registered-proprietor-in-his-personal-capacity-is-exempt-under-gst-delhi-hc/); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/renting-of-residential-dwelling-not-for-use-in-course-or-furtherance-of-business-would-be-exempt-under-gst-delhi-high-court/); [busy.in](https://busy.in/caselaw/seema-gupta-vs-union-of-india-and-others-delhi-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; corrected: only Notf. 04/2022 clause (A)(b) was challenged (not Notf. 5/2022); the court accepted a clarification for proprietors only; the partners point comes from the government's affidavit.

---

### 9. Exemptions: interpretation, hostels, education

**Contentions.** *Affiliation fees:* Telangana HC (*Care College of Nursing*, 2023), Kerala HC DB and Madras HC DB (*Bharathidasan University*, 2026) hold them taxable. The Madras single judges had split before the reference, and some taxpayers rely on a Karnataka HC view. The SC (Pardiwala and K. Vinod Chandran JJ) closed Bharathidasan's SLPs without deciding the merits and observed that dismissing an SLP against a Karnataka HC judgment was not an affirmation on merits. *Hostels:* the SC (*Taghar*, 2025) settled it in favour of the exemption up to 17.07.2022. *Coaching:* Gujarat AAR (May 2026) says supplementary coaching is not an "educational institution", so 18%.

#### Commissioner of Customs (Import), Mumbai v. Dilip Kumar and Company - Supreme Court (Constitution Bench) - 30.07.2018
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 3327 of 2007; AIR 2018 SC 3606 (Ranjan Gogoi, N.V. Ramana, R. Banumathi, Mohan M. Shantanagoudar, S. Abdul Nazeer JJ).
- Holding: An exemption notification is construed strictly. The burden is on the assessee to show it comes within it, and any ambiguity about eligibility is resolved in favour of the Revenue. *Sun Export* (1997), which had given the benefit of ambiguity to the assessee, was overruled. The Court also concurred (paras 46-47) with the line that once eligibility is strictly established, the notification may be construed liberally.
- Key words: "When there is ambiguity in exemption notification which is subject to strict interpretation, the benefit of such ambiguity cannot be claimed by the subject/assessee and it must be interpreted in favour of the revenue." (para 52)
- Binding: SC, binding.
- Later history: Applied by Telangana HC under GST in *Care College of Nursing* (2023). The "liberal once eligible" point is in paras 46-47 of the judgment itself (approving *Parle Exports*: "Do not extend or widen the ambit at stage of applicability. But once that hurdle is crossed, construe it liberally").
- Use in orders: Cite it when holding that the assessee has not shown it is eligible. Identify the exact condition or wording that is not met.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/29030278/); [supremecourtcases.com](https://www.supremecourtcases.com/commissioner-of-customs-import-mumbai-v-m-s-dilip-kumar-and-company-ors/); [Manupatra (MANU/SC/0789/2018)](https://www.manupatracademy.com/LegalPost/MANU_SC_0789_2018)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and citation added; Sun Export confirmed overruled; liberal-construction corollary confirmed at paras 46-47.

#### Care College of Nursing and others v. Kaloji Narayana Rao University of Health Sciences - Telangana HC (DB: P. Sam Koshy, Laxmi Narayana Alishetty JJ) - 17.10.2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 34617 of 2022 and batch (34 W.P.s of 2023); [2023] 156 taxmann.com 450 (Telangana). Judgment pronounced 17.10.2023 (Indian Kanoon lists it as "Care Institute of Medical Sciences ... v. KNRUHS").
- Holding: Affiliation and inspection fees that colleges pay to a university are not exempt under entry 66 of Notf. 12/2017-CT(Rate). Entry 66 covers services **by** educational institutions to students, faculty and staff, and services **to** them relating to admission or examinations. Applying *Dilip Kumar*, the exemption can't be stretched. The court disagreed with the Karnataka HC rulings (*Rajiv Gandhi University of Health Sciences*, *Bangalore University*) as based on s.66D of the Finance Act 1994. The 35 writ petitions were dismissed.
- Key words: "inspection and affiliation fees charged by the 1st respondent-University from the educational institutions has not been specifically exempted ... the said benefit cannot be extended to the petitioners." (para 34)
- Binding: **BINDING IN TELANGANA.**
- Later history: Appeal not found. Consistent with Kerala HC DB and Madras HC DB (2026).
- Use in orders: For universities and boards in Telangana, affiliation and inspection fees are taxable. Cite this case together with *Dilip Kumar*.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/181678350/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/telangana-high-court/telangana-high-court-gst-exemption-doesnt-cover-affiliation-fee-and-inspection-fee-paid-by-colleges-to-university-242470); [Taxmann](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/inspection-fee-and-affiliation-fee-charged-by-universities-from-colleges-is-not-exempt-under-gst-hc/); [taxo.online 30.10.2023](https://taxo.online/latest-news/30-10-2023-telangana-high-court-cancels-plea-against-gst-on-fees/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 17.10.2023 confirmed (30.10.2023 was a report date); WP nos. added; Karnataka HC disagreement added.

#### Bharathidasan University v. Joint Commissioner of GST (ST) (Intelligence) - Madras HC (DB, Madurai Bench: G. Jayachandran, K.K. Ramakrishnan JJ) - 10.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) Nos. 27453 and 27456 to 27458 of 2025.
- Holding: On a reference arising from conflicting single-judge views (*Madurai Kamaraj University* vs *Pondicherry University*): affiliation is independent of admission or conduct of examination and falls outside entry 66 of Notf. 12/2017, so affiliation fees are taxable; *Pondicherry University* states the correct law. Sent back to the single judge to decide other grounds (DRC-01/s.74(5) notices for 2019-20 onwards).
- Key words: "the fee collected by the University from the Colleges as affiliation fees is amenable for levy of GST." (para 9)
- Binding: Persuasive.
- Later history: The SC (Pardiwala and K. Vinod Chandran JJ) closed the University's SLPs with liberty to come back after the single judge decides. It observed that an earlier dismissal of an SLP against a Karnataka HC judgment was not an affirmation on merits. The sequence of the SC order and the DB ruling is unclear in sources; the DB judgment does not mention the SC order (SC order not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).
- Use in orders: Supports *Care College* (Telangana).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/117138760/); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/gst/affiliation-charges-collected-by-universities-taxable-under-gst-madras-hc-1442962); [taxo.online 17.02.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/17-02-2026-affiliation-fees-collected-by-universities-is-not-covered-under-entry-66-of-nn-12-2017-ctr-hence-taxable-under-gst-madras-high-court/); [JurisHour](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/gst-university-affiliation-fees-open-residual/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and WP nos. added; summary confirmed. SC SLP point remains secondary.

#### State of Karnataka v. Taghar Vasudeva Ambrish - Supreme Court (J.B. Pardiwala, K.V. Viswanathan JJ) - 04.12.2025
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 7846 of 2023 with C.A. No. 7847 of 2023; 2025 INSC 1380 (judgment by J.B. Pardiwala J).
- Holding: Leasing a residential building to a lessee who runs it as a hostel for students and working professionals is "renting of residential dwelling for use as residence" under entry 13 of Notf. 9/2017-IT(Rate) (12/2017-CT(Rate) equivalent). The exemption does not require the lessee to live there itself. "Residential dwelling" takes its common-parlance meaning, and long-stay hostels differ from hotels and guest houses. Entry 13 is an activity-specific, not person-specific, exemption. The Revenue's appeals were dismissed.
- Key words: "the exemption is extended to cases wherein residential dwelling is rented out and ultimately used as residence, irrespective of the person using it." (para 61)
- Binding: SC, binding.
- Later history: The decision covers the period before the 18.07.2022 amendment. The Court said that from 18.07.2022 no exemption is available where the dwelling is rented to a registered person, and that applying the amendment to earlier periods would be impermissibly retrospective (para 66). It also noted the Explanation added from 01.01.2023 for proprietors renting for own residence (paras 67-68). The judgment says nothing about refunds under s.54; that point is commentary only.
- Use in orders: Drop demands on landlords leasing residential buildings to hostel or PG operators for periods up to 17.07.2022. For later periods, analyse the amended entry 12 and the RCM entry.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/171394234/); [SCObserver](https://www.scobserver.in/supreme-court-observer-law-reports-scolr/gst-treatment-of-residential-buildings-used-as-hostels/); [Verdictum](https://www.verdictum.in/app-lite/court-updates/supreme-court/2025-insc-1380-state-of-karnataka-v-taghar-vasudeva-ambrish-1600097?hasShare=1); [taxo.online 05.12.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/05-12-2025-leasing-out-of-residential-premises-to-an-entity-which-is-using-it-as-hostel-for-students-and-working-professionals-is-exempted-from-gst-supreme-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; summary confirmed; case nos. added; the judgment applies entry 13 of Notf. 9/2017-IT(Rate); s.54 refund point removed (not in judgment); para 66 (no exemption from 18.07.2022 for registered tenants) added.

#### Taghar Vasudeva Ambrish v. Appellate Authority for Advance Ruling - Karnataka HC (DB: Alok Aradhe, M.I. Arun JJ) - 07.02.2022
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 14891 of 2020 (T-RES).
- Holding: Reversed the Karnataka AAAR (which had called the hostel "sociable accommodation"). Held that leasing residential premises as a hostel is covered by entry 13 of Notf. 9/2017-IT(Rate) and the lessee need not itself use the premises as a residence. The AAAR order of 31.08.2020 was quashed.
- Key words: "the notification does not require the lessee itself use the premises as residence." (para 15)
- Binding: Superseded by the SC affirmance (above).
- Later history: Affirmed by the SC on 04.12.2025.
- Use in orders: Cite the SC judgment instead.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/35368170/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/news-updates/karnataka-high-court-tax-exemption-owner-of-building-gst-act-residential-premises-leased-out-as-hostel-191732); [taxo.online 16.02.2022](https://taxo.online/latest-news/16-02-2022-lease-of-residential-premises-as-hostel-not-to-attract-gst-karnataka-hc)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date 07.02.2022 and WP no. added; summary confirmed.

#### Gujarat AAR - supplementary coaching not "educational institution" (applicant not found) - Gujarat AAR - May 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Entry 66 is limited to institutions that meet the definition of "educational institution" (para 2(y) of Notf. 12/2017). Coaching for school students is commercial training and coaching (SAC 999293) at 18%.
- Binding: AAR, binding only on the applicant.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: Persuasive support for taxing coaching centres.
- Source: [taxo.online 02.05.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/02-05-2026-gst-exemption-under-entry-66-is-restricted-to-institutions-meeting-the-precise-definition-of-educational-institution-supplementary-coaching-institutes-fall-outside-its-a/); [TaxHeal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=131225)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026)

### 10. GSTAT and other tribunals (classification and valuation)

- No GSTAT decision on classification or valuation was found. The first reported GSTAT judgment (*Sterling & Wilson*, Principal Bench, 14.02.2026) concerned jurisdiction and is in file 03. A GSTAT office order effective 01.08.2026 puts classification and time/value-of-supply appeals in "Category-I" before Division Benches ([taxo.online 31.07.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/31-07-2026-gstat-reconstitutes-benches-and-reclassifies-appeals-major-procedural-overhaul-effective-from-1-august-2026/)).
- CESTAT service-tax rulings still relied on in GST: notice pay (*Rajasthan Rajya Vidyut Prasaran Nigam*, *Intas*, *Cosmo First*, *Ami Lifesciences*, *Balaji Medical*); see section 2.

---

### Gaps and doubts

1. **Not covered in this round** (the search budget of about 45 ran out): actionable claims beyond online gaming (file 04); ESOP cross-charge (no ruling found); slump sale (AAR only, not searched); OIDAR and s.12(8)/13 place-of-supply disputes (no HC ruling surfaced); RCM on security services (Notf. 29/2018), with no ruling found; discounts and free supplies under s.15(3); TCS inclusion in value (nothing found); health insurance exemption (not searched; any Sept 2025 rate-change exemption is **not verified** here); education services beyond affiliation and coaching.
2. **Leasehold assignment, SC status:** Two dismissal reports (Aerocom SLP 22.05.2026, single source; Gujarat Chamber SLP 21.07.2026, two sources). LiveLaw (Oct 2025) had reported that the SC would "examine" the Gujarat matter. [14 Oct 2026: Aerocom order read - SLP(C) Diary No. 26041/2026 dismissed 22.05.2026 without reasons; Gujarat Chamber SC order not found on Indian Kanoon.] A dismissal at the SLP stage without reasons may not be a binding precedent under Art. 141 (*Kunhayammed*).
3. **Prahitha Constructions:** Final SC outcome of SLP(C) 11079/2024 not found. Check before each TDR order.
4. **Munjaal Bhatt:** [Resolved 14 Oct 2026] Para 2 was read down (para 124). Union's SLP(C) 21703/2022 is pending (listed 07.12.2026).
5. **Krazybee / Aavanti Solar:** [Resolved 14 Oct 2026] Dates 08.12.2025 and 09.09.2024. A third Karnataka HC liquidated-damages ruling (SupremeToday, dated around July 2026) was seen but its details were not confirmed, so it is not included.
6. **Delhi HC cross-charge (June 2025):** Party name not found. Circular 210/04/2024 (valuation of related-party services with full ITC) was **not confirmed by search**; Circular 199/11/2023 was.
7. **Flavoured milk SC order:** [Resolved 14 Oct 2026] SLP dismissal, 09.05.2025 (Diary No. 17602/2025).
8. **Fusible interlining:** [Resolved 14 Oct 2026] HC 14.06.2024; SC dismissed SLP 16.05.2025.
9. **Dabur (Odomos):** HC date 17.01.2020 (resolved 14 Oct 2026); SC outcome not found.
10. **Kerala parotta:** Outcome of the State's writ appeal not found. Don't cite as final.
11. **Section 14 rate-change ruling:** [Resolved 14 Oct 2026] *SPK and Co*, Madras HC, 10.12.2025.
12. **Telangana HC works-contract rates:** Only *Sahasra* (procedural) and AAR rulings were found. A reported Telangana HC 12% RVNL ruling turned out on search to be **Madras HC** (*STS-KEC JV*). The 2026 Telangana 2BHK housing petition outcome was not found.
13. **Circular numbering:** The brief's "Circular 140/2019" is in fact **Circular 140/10/2020-GST dated 10.06.2020**. Circular 177/09/2022 is named for developed land from general knowledge; the search source only says "CBIC circular dated 03.08.2022". Verify.
14. **Dilip Kumar:** The "liberal once eligible" corollary is confirmed at paras 46-47 (14 Oct 2026). No GST HC ruling other than *Care College* was found expressly applying it.
15. **Verification status (14 Oct 2026):** 27 entries are "Primary" (read on Indian Kanoon); 10 remain "Secondary". Read the judgment before citing a Secondary entry.
