## File 08: 08 - Interest, Penalty, Late Fee, Amnesty, Anti-profiteering, Jurisdiction and Other Procedures

**Scope.** This file covers interest under s.50 CGST Act: net versus gross cash liability, the retrospective proviso, interest on ITC "availed and utilised", and whether interest can be recovered without an SCN or DRC-01D. It also covers:
- penalties under ss.73(9)/(11), 74, 122 (mens rea; s.122(1A) on non-taxable persons) and 125;
- the s.129 / s.130 interplay (only points not already in file 03), and e-invoice lapses;
- late-fee amnesty notifications;
- the s.128A waiver scheme (only additions to file 03) and Circulars 238/2024 and 248/2025;
- anti-profiteering under s.171 (Reckitt Benckiser, Excel Rasayan, the GSTAT Principal Bench and the 1.4.2025 sunset);
- cross-empowerment under s.6(2)(b) (Armour Security, SC 2025);
- DGGI's power to issue SCNs (Circular 239/2024, Canon India analogy);
- audit (ss.65/66);
- compensation cess refunds.

Search (s.67) and DRC-03 payments during search are in file 04. Megha Engineering (Telangana HC 2019, gross-liability interest) is in file 05.

**Date compiled:** 14 Oct 2026.

> **Compiled from web-search summaries, then checked against judgment text on Indian Kanoon on 14-15 Oct 2026. See each entry's "Verified" line and the Verification log at the end. Entries still marked "Secondary" have not been checked against the text.**

---

### Quick answers

**1. Interest on delayed returns: net cash or gross?**
- The s.50(1) proviso was substituted by the Finance Act 2021 (s.112) with retrospective effect from 01.07.2017 and brought into force by Notf. 16/2021-CT (01.06.2021). Interest is charged only on tax paid by debiting the electronic cash ledger.
- The exception: where the return is filed after proceedings under s.73/74 have commenced, interest is on the gross amount.
- *Megha Engineering* (Telangana HC 2019, gross-liability view) is overtaken by this amendment; see file 05.
- *Takeaway:* compute s.50(1) interest on the cash portion only, unless the return was filed after the s.73/74 proceedings began. If so, record the date those proceedings began.

**2. Interest on ITC wrongly availed but not utilised (s.50(3), rule 88B).**
- Calcutta HC (*Utpal Das*, 2024, following *L&T*, 2022) and Orissa HC (*Manoja Kumar Nayak*, Apr 2026) hold that there is no interest unless the ITC is both availed **and** utilised.
- Orissa tests utilisation against the **total** credit-ledger balance, not head-wise.
- Madras HC (June 2026, name not found) gave "wrongful availment and utilisation" a wide reading, extending it to GSTR-3B/2A mismatches. It is not confined to fraud.
- *Takeaway:* before demanding s.50(3) interest, show in the order, with ledger figures, that the balance fell below the disputed ITC and when. Charge interest only from the date of utilisation to the date of reversal (rule 88B(3)).

**3. Can interest be recovered straight through DRC-07 or s.79 without adjudication?**
- **Jharkhand HC** (*Mahadeo Construction*; *R.K. Transport*; *Bluestar Malleable*; *East India Udyog*, 2024): where interest is disputed, it must be adjudicated under s.73/74. A DRC-07 without an SCN was quashed.
- **Gujarat HC** (*Reliance Formulation*, June 2025; *Bombay Art*, Oct 2025): interest on self-assessed tax can be recovered under s.79 only after an intimation in **DRC-01D** under rule 142B (in force from 04.08.2023).
- The older Gujarat view (*Rajkamal Builder*, 2021) treated interest as recoverable under s.79 without an SCN.
- *Takeaway:* for undisputed interest on self-assessed tax, issue DRC-01D first. For disputed interest, issue an SCN under s.73/74. Never go straight to DRC-07 or DRC-13.

**4. Penalty under s.122: mens rea; civil or criminal; independent of s.74?**
- *Patanjali Ayurved* (Allahabad HC, 29.05.2025) held that s.122 penalty is a civil liability adjudicated by the proper officer. Mens rea is essential for prosecution; for a penalty it depends on the statute's scheme and is not needed for breach of a civil obligation.
- Section 122 proceedings are independent of s.73/74 and survive the dropping of the s.74 demand.
- For **s.122(1A)** on persons who are not taxable persons, the High Courts are split:
  - Bombay HC: the person must be a taxable person. The Revenue's SLP was reportedly dismissed with the question of law kept open.
  - Delhi HC (2025) and Gauhati HC (2026): "any person", including directors and partners.
- *Takeaway (Telangana):* no binding Telangana or SC ruling was found on s.122(1A). For acts before 01.01.2021 (when s.122(1A) came in), do not invoke it, because Article 20(1)/retrospectivity challenges are live. Record the specific benefit the person retained and the person's role.

**5. Penalty where tax is paid before or within 30 days of the SCN (s.73(5)/(8)).**
- Under s.73(8), proceedings conclude without penalty if tax and interest are paid within 30 days of the SCN.
- Kerala HC (*Global Plastowares*, 2023): this does not cover tax **collected** from customers but not deposited. Section 73(11) penalty still applies to that.
- *Takeaway:* if the taxpayer pays within 30 days, drop the s.73 penalty unless the s.73(11) facts (tax collected and not paid) are recorded.

**6. General penalty under s.125.**
- Madras HC (*Kandan Hardware Mart*, 2026, followed in *NKR Traders*, 2026) holds that s.125 is available only where no specific penalty applies.
- Section 125 cannot be imposed for late returns when a late fee under s.47 applies.
- *Takeaway:* do not add a s.125 penalty on top of a s.47 late fee or any specific penalty.

**7. s.129 versus s.130, and e-invoices.**
- *Panchhi Traders* (Gujarat HC DB, 11.12.2025) held ss.129 and 130 independent. Section 130 can be invoked directly where facts show a clear intent to evade, ordinarily formed within the rule 138B/138C timelines.
- Earlier *Moon Traders* (Gujarat 2020) took the sequential view, as did the AP HC view reported contra; see file 03 for Satyam Shivam Papers (Telangana).
- *Nancy Trading* (Allahabad HC 2024): no s.129 penalty for a missing e-invoice where the other documents were present and there was no intent to evade.
- *Takeaway:* any s.130 order must contain a reasoned, fact-based finding of intent to evade.

**8. Late-fee waivers.**
- The HP HC (*R.T. Pharma*), Madras HC (*Kandan Hardware Mart*) and Kerala HC (*Anishia Chandrakantha*, 2024) extended the GSTR-9/9C amnesty (Notf. 7/2023 and 25/2023-CT) to returns filed **before** 01.04.2023. No late fee was allowed on GSTR-9C for FY 2017-18 to 2019-20.
- No refund is due of late fee already paid above Rs 10,000 (Kerala).
- *Takeaway:* do not demand late fee above the amnesty cap for those years.

**9. s.128A waiver (file 03 has the Madras and Karnataka cases).**
- Circular 238/32/2024 (15.10.2024) excludes self-assessed tax and interest on delayed returns.
- Madras HC (*Incompressible Fluid Control*, 2026, per secondary reports) held that circulars cannot cut down the statute.
- A Bombay HC writ challenging the circular is pending (notice issued).
- Circular 248/05/2025 says GSTR-3B payment before 01.11.2024 does not bar eligibility.
- Madras HC (*WFB Baird*, 07.07.2026): where an order was wrongly passed under s.74, denying s.128A on that ground is unsustainable. The case was remanded for a fresh s.73 order and a fresh s.128A application.
- No Telangana ruling was found.
- *Takeaway:* decide SPL applications on the s.128A and rule 164 conditions only, with reasons. Expect the Circular 238 exclusions to be challenged.

**10. Anti-profiteering (s.171).**
- *Reckitt Benckiser* (Delhi HC DB, 29.01.2024) upheld s.171 and rules 122-134 (selected). The SC issued notice in Excel Rasayan's SLP in Feb 2024; the outcome was not found.
- The GSTAT Principal Bench has heard these cases since 01.10.2024 (Notf. 18/2024-CT).
- Notf. 19/2024-CT: no new applications are accepted from 01.04.2025. Pending cases continue.
- The first GSTAT order was *DGAP v. Urban Essence* (05.08.2025).
- *Takeaway:* there are no new complaints after 01.04.2025. Officers are unlikely to originate s.171 cases.

**11. Parallel Central/State proceedings (s.6(2)(b)).**
- *Armour Security* (SC, 14.08.2025) held that a **summons, search or seizure is not "initiation of proceedings"**. The bar is triggered by an SCN on the "same subject matter".
- Its guidelines direct that action from audit or scrutiny be taken by the administration to which the taxpayer is assigned.
- The Telangana HC (2025-26) applies "first SCN prevails" and tests whether the issues and periods truly overlap.
- *Takeaway:* before an SCN, check the GST portal and the State or Centre for an earlier SCN on the same issue and period, and record that check in the SCN. Audit and scrutiny of State-assigned taxpayers should not be taken up by Central officers.

**12. DGGI as proper officer to issue SCNs.**
- Most High Courts (Madras, Rajasthan, Delhi 2026) uphold DGGI's pan-India power to issue SCNs (Notf. 14/2017-CT).
- *NCS Pearson* (Karnataka HC, 16.07.2025) quashed a DGGI s.74 SCN where the officer was not authorised.
- Adjudication follows Circulars 31/05/2018, 169/01/2022 and 239/33/2024 (All-India Additional/Joint Commissioners, Notf. 27/2024-CT from 01.12.2024).
- *Takeaway:* adjudicating officers should confirm they are the notified adjudicating authority for a DGGI SCN and say so in the order.

**13. Audit (ss.65/66).**
- *Koenig Solutions* (Delhi HC, 09.07.2026) quashed a special-audit ADT-03 and DRC-01 because the s.73 time limit had expired.
- *Varian Medical* (Delhi HC 2025): an SCN issued before the reply time on a pre-SCN expired violates natural justice.
- *Takeaway:* special audit does not extend the s.73/74/74A limitation. Allow the full reply period on pre-SCN or audit communications.

---

### 1. Interest under s.50

**Contentions across courts.**
- **Net or gross:** the retrospective proviso settled it (cash portion only) except for returns filed after s.73/74 proceedings commenced.
- **Utilisation:** Calcutta and Orissa require actual utilisation measured on the total ledger balance. Madras (2026) reads s.50(3) broadly to include GSTR-3B/2A mismatches but still ties interest to utilisation via rule 88B(3).
- **Recovery route:** Jharkhand requires adjudication once interest is disputed. Gujarat requires DRC-01D before s.79.
- No SC ruling was found on these questions. The CBIC position (per secondary reports) is that the s.50 proviso is retrospective and interest is on net cash liability.

#### Utpal Das v. State of West Bengal - Calcutta HC (Raja Basu Chowdhury J.) - 18.07.2024
- Citation / case no.: WPA 18241 of 2022; 2024 (10) TMI 1016 (as reported).
- Holding: ITC wrongly availed through a clerical error in GSTR-9 and reversed by debiting the credit ledger (DRC-03) after a DRC-01A, but **not utilised** (the credit balance never fell below the Rs 5 lakh involved), attracts no interest under s.50(3), amended retrospectively from 01.07.2017 by the Finance Act 2022. A DRC-01A does not commence s.73 proceedings; they begin only with the SCN. The s.73 order and the appellate order were set aside and the interest and penalty quashed (paras 14-20). It followed *Larsen & Toubro v. State of West Bengal* (Calcutta HC, WPA 2654 of 2020, 13.12.2022), *Ranjan Sarkar*, *Grundfos Pumps* (Madras) and *Deepak Sales* (P&H).
- Key words: "unless the ITC is both availed and utilized, interest cannot be levied on the registered tax payer." (para 17)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: do not demand s.50(3) interest without a finding that the wrong ITC was actually utilised.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Calcutta HC WPA 18241/2022, 18.07.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/51857497/); [TaxTMI blog](https://www.taxtmi.com/tmi_blog_details?id=775211); [Taxo - 04.11.2024 HC quashes interest order](https://taxo.online/latest-news/04-11-2024-hc-quashes-order-imposing-interest-clarifies-itc-interest-cant-be-levied-unless-both-availed-and-utilized/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date is 18.07.2024 (not Oct 2024); case no. WPA 18241 of 2022 and single-judge bench added; DRC-01A point added.

#### Manoja Kumar Nayak v. Commissioner, GST & Central Excise, Rourkela (with Babamani Roadways & Borewells) - Orissa HC (DB: Harish Tandon CJ and Murahari Sri Raman J.) - 08.04.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) Nos. 12682 and 12686 of 2025.
- Holding: Read together, s.50(3), rule 88B and the CBIC circular of 17.07.2023 mean interest arises only where ITC is both wrongly availed and utilised. Utilisation is tested on the **total** ITC balance, not head-wise. The disputed ITC of Rs 4,39,970 was reversed before the SCN and the ledger balance always exceeded it, so there was no interest. Demanding the same amount again without credit for the reversal was double taxation, and the penalty fell too. The s.74 OIO (03.02.2025) was quashed (paras 9.5-12).
- Key words: "since there was availability of surplus/excess balance left in the Electronic Credit Ledger after adjustment of input tax credit so voluntarily reversed, no interest is exigible" (para 11)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: give credit for pre-SCN reversals. Compute interest only for days when the total credit balance fell below the wrongly availed amount.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Orissa HC W.P.(C) 12682/2025, 08.04.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/65248458/); [Taxo - 13.04.2026 Orissa HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/13-04-2026-wrong-itc-does-not-attract-interest-unless-it-is-actually-utilised-and-utilisation-must-be-tested-on-total-itc-balance-not-head-wise-orissa-high-court/); [TheTaxCorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/orissa-high-court-no-gst-demand-or-interest-after-voluntary-itc-reversal-with)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** judgment date 08.04.2026 (13.04.2026 was the report date); case nos. and bench added; summary otherwise confirmed (the total-balance test is taken from the CBIC circular of 17.07.2023, which the court applied).

#### Madras HC (party name not found) - Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J. - reported 26.06.2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: A GSTR-3B vs GSTR-2A mismatch can amount to "wrongful availment and utilisation". Section 50(3) is the special provision for ITC cases and is not limited to fraud or fake invoices. Interest runs from utilisation to reversal (rule 88B(3)). The cash-ledger argument under the s.50(1) proviso was rejected. The writ was dismissed.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: support for s.50(3) interest on mismatch-based ITC, but still record the utilisation dates.
- Source: [Taxo - 26.06.2026 Madras HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/26-06-2026-wrongful-availment-and-utilisation-of-itc-includes-gstr-3b-gstr-2a-mismatch-interest-payable-under-section-503-madras-high-court/); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1057785)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2-3 sources agree; party name not found) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Mahadeo Construction Co. v. Union of India - Jharkhand HC (DB: H.C. Mishra and Deepak Roshan JJ.) - 21.04.2020
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(T) No. 3517 of 2019; cited in the later Jharkhand HC judgment in *East India Udyog* as 2020 (36) GSTL 343 (Jhar.) (another source gave vol. 33).
- Holding: If the assessee disputes liability to interest under s.50 (its calculation or leviability), the revenue must adjudicate under s.73 or s.74; until then the interest is not an "amount payable" and cannot be recovered under s.79. The Superintendent's order (08.03.2019) and the s.79 garnishee notice to the bank for Rs 19,59,721 were quashed, with liberty to adjudicate (paras 21-24).
- Key words: "without initiation of any adjudication proceedings, no recovery proceeding under Section 79 of the Act can be initiated for recovery of the interest amount." (para 22)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: an SLP was reportedly filed in the SC; the outcome was not found. The case was followed in *R.K. Transport* (16.02.2022, W.P.(T) 1404/2020) and *Bluestar Malleable* (18.08.2022, W.P.(T) 2043 and 2051/2020).
- Use in orders: if the taxpayer disputes interest, issue an SCN rather than a recovery letter.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Jharkhand HC W.P.(T) 3517/2019, 21.04.2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/31840117/); [Taxo - Jharkhand HC interest needs adjudication](https://taxo.online/latest-articles/interest-cannot-be-demanded-without-initiating-any-adjudication-proceedings-under-section-73-or-74-jharkhand-high-court/); [Taxo - Bluestar Malleable](https://taxo.online/judgment/bluestar-malleable-pvt-ltd-vs-the-state-of-jharkhand-ors-in-w-p-t-no-2043-of-2020-with-w-p-t-no-2051-of-2020-high-court-jharkhand)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (21.04.2020) and bench added; summary confirmed. SLP status still secondary.

#### M/s East India Udyog Ltd v. State of Jharkhand - Jharkhand HC (DB: Shree Chandrashekhar ACJ and Navneet Kumar J.) - 13.03.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(T) Nos. 2853, 2854 and 2855 of 2023.
- Holding: A demand of interest (Rs 92.96 lakh in the lead case) on delayed GSTR-3B returns, raised through a DRC-07 dated 04.01.2021 "under s.73" without any adjudication, was set aside together with the appellate orders and APL-04s, following *Mahadeo Construction*, *R.K. Transport* and *Narsingh Ispat*. The department was left at liberty to initiate adjudication proceedings in accordance with law (paras 15-17).
- Key words: "there was no adjudication before a demand for the payment of interest was raised because of the delayed filing of the Returns." (para 15)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a DRC-07 must follow an adjudication order. It is not a substitute for one.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Jharkhand HC W.P.(T) 2853/2023, 13.03.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/115091900/); [Taxo - 13.05.2024 Jharkhand HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/13-05-2024-interest-cannot-be-demanded-on-delayed-filing-of-returns-without-proper-adjudication-where-liability-has-been-disputed-jharkhand-high-court/); [LiveLaw - Jharkhand HC](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/jharkhand-high-court-justice-aparesh-kumar-singh-justice-deepak-roshan-cgst-act-194809)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party names, bench and case numbers confirmed; summary confirmed (liberty was to "initiate the adjudication proceeding", not specifically an SCN under s.73(1)).

#### Reliance Formulation Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner of State Tax, Ghatak 21 - Gujarat HC (DB: Bhargav D. Karia and Pranav Trivedi JJ.) - 27.06.2025
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 5453 of 2025 (oral order).
- Holding: The challenge to an advisory (18.12.2024) on outstanding s.50(1) interest was disposed of without interference. The court held that since rule 142B (from 04.08.2023) the department cannot go straight to s.79 recovery: it must first issue an intimation in **Form DRC-01D**, and then give an opportunity to reply and a hearing if requested (s.75(4)). *Mahadeo Construction* was distinguished as rendered before rule 142B and DRC-01D (paras 11-13).
- Key words: "after issuance of the notice in Form GST DRC-01D, as per the provisions of Section 75(4) of the GST Act, the respondent-Authority is bound to provide an opportunity to file reply as well as an opportunity of hearing" (para 12)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: followed in *Bombay Art v. Union of India* (Gujarat HC, 10.10.2025, R/SCA 16855 of 2024; read on Indian Kanoon), which quashed a DRC-13 bank attachment (20.05.2024) for interest and allowed fresh proceedings by DRC-01D with reply and hearing, and in a further Gujarat ruling reported on 06.11.2025 (name not found).
- Use in orders: issue DRC-01D and consider the reply before any recovery of s.50(1) interest.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 5453/2025, 27.06.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/134461778/); [Indian Kanoon - Bombay Art, 10.10.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/23074237/); [Taxmann - Recovery of GST interest needs DRC-01D](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-recovery-of-gst-interest-needs-drc-01d-notice-hearing/); [A2Z Taxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/issuance-of-notice-in-drc-01d-for-recovery-of-interest-mandatory-prior-to-recovery-under-section-79-of-the-cgst-act/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (with *Bombay Art*); bench added; holding refined: the writ was disposed of without quashing anything, and the court treated DRC-01D plus reply and hearing (not a s.73/74 SCN) as the route, distinguishing *Mahadeo*.

#### Rajkamal Builder Infrastructure Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (DB: J.B. Pardiwala and Ilesh J. Vora JJ.) - 23.03.2021
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 21534 of 2019.
- Holding: In view of the retrospective substitution of the s.50(1) proviso (then clause 103 of the Finance Bill 2021), interest is on the net (cash) tax liability, not gross. A DRC-01 cannot be issued for interest under s.50 (rule 142(1)(a) does not cover s.50); under s.75(12), unpaid interest on self-assessed tax is recovered under s.79 "notwithstanding" ss.73/74, by notice in DRC-07. The DRC-01 was quashed with liberty to start fresh proceedings (paras 6-19).
- Key words: "the interest under Section 50 of the CGST Act, 2017 can only be levied on the net tax liability and not on the gross tax liability." (para 6)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: on the procedure point, overtaken in practice by rule 142B / DRC-01D (2023) and *Reliance Formulation* (2025).
- Use in orders: do not rely on it for direct recovery. Use the DRC-01D route.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 21534/2019, 23.03.2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/127260360/); [StudyCafe - net liability, Gujarat HC](https://studycafe.in/interest-under-gst-can-only-be-levied-on-the-net-tax-liability-and-not-on-the-gross-tax-liability-gujarat-hc-109350.html); [StudyCafe - DRC-07 instead of DRC-01](https://studycafe.in/interest-to-be-demanded-only-on-net-tax-liability-in-form-drc-07-instead-of-drc-01-97879.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (23.03.2021), case number and bench added; summary confirmed.

#### Retrospective s.50(1) proviso (statutory) and Telangana/Madras/Gujarat follow-ups
- Citation / case no.: s.112, Finance Act 2021; Notf. 16/2021-CT dated 01.06.2021. One source gives a different commencement date; check the Gazette.
- Holding: The proviso was substituted from 01.07.2017. Interest on delayed returns is on the cash-ledger portion only, unless the return is filed after s.73/74 proceedings commence. Other reported rulings:
  - Madras HC (*Srinivasa Stampings*, per one source): the proviso is retrospective.
  - **Telangana HC (*Orange Auto Pvt Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax*, W.P. Nos. 18236 of 2019 & batch, DB: Ujjal Bhuyan and P. Madhavi Devi JJ., 24.12.2021 - read on Indian Kanoon):** noting the retrospective substitution of the proviso by the Finance Act 2021, the HC allowed the writs and directed the authorities to issue notice, hear the petitioners and re-quantify interest under the amended s.50 (paras 6-8).
  - Gujarat HC (*Symphony Ltd*, per one source): no interest for the period between cash deposit and filing of GSTR-3B.
- Key words: "respondents shall issue notice and hear the petitioners before quantifying the interest to be paid by the petitioners as per the amended provision of Section 50 of the CGST Act." (Orange Auto, para 8)
- Binding: statute. *Orange Auto* (Telangana HC DB, 24.12.2021) is **BINDING IN TELANGANA** for the direction to re-quantify on the amended basis after a hearing.
- Later history: a later Telangana order in *Megha Engineering* reportedly directed re-quantification under the amended s.50 after a hearing (see file 05).
- Use in orders: state the cash-portion computation and the date of commencement of any s.73/74 proceedings.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Orange Auto, Telangana HC, 24.12.2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/183094458/); [LKS - proviso retrospective](https://lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/interest-on-delayed-payment-proviso-to-s-50-of-cgst-act-is-applicable-retrospectively); [TaxGuru - Budget 2021 interest on net liability](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/budget-2021-interest-net-tax-liability-u-s-50-cgst-act.html); [busy.in - Orange Auto (Telangana HC)](https://busy.in/caselaw/orange-auto-private-limited-and-others-vs-superintendent-of-central-tax-and-others-telangana-high-court)
- Verified: Statutory point and Symphony: secondary. *Orange Auto*: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (date, bench and case numbers added; it directs re-quantification after hearing rather than itself computing interest).

---

### 2. Penalties: ss.73/74, 122, 122(1A), 125

**Contentions across courts.**
- **s.122(1A) on non-taxable persons:** Bombay HC says only taxable persons (Revenue's SLP reportedly dismissed, question kept open). Delhi HC (2025) and Gauhati HC (2026) say "any person" who retains the benefit.
- **Retrospectivity of s.122(1A) for pre-01.01.2021 acts:** one HC barred it under Art. 20(1). Others say the SCN date controls, and a Delhi bench reportedly disagreed with the SCN-date test.
- **Mens rea for s.122:** Allahabad HC says not required for a civil penalty.
- No SC ruling on the merits was found. No CBIC circular on s.122(1A) was found in this search.

#### Patanjali Ayurved Ltd v. Union of India - Allahabad HC (DB: Shekhar B. Saraf and Vipin Chandra Dixit JJ.) - 29.05.2025
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 1603 of 2024; 2025:AHC:92242-DB.
- Holding:
  - A DGGI SCN (19.04.2024) under ss.74 and 122 proposed a penalty (reported as Rs 273.51 crore) under s.122(1)(ii) and (vii). The s.74 tax demand was dropped by the adjudication order of 10.01.2025.
  - The HC held that s.122 is a penalty provision adjudicated by the proper officer (SCN in DRC-01, order in DRC-07), not a prosecution under s.134; prosecution is under ss.132-138. On mens rea, it held that for prosecution it is essential, while for a penalty it depends on the scheme of the statute; a penalty for breach of a civil obligation does not need it (paras 38-39).
  - Being taxed first under s.73/74 is not a precondition for a s.122 penalty, and dropping the s.74 proceedings does not abate s.122 proceedings for distinct contraventions such as fake invoices (paras 41, 51-54). The writ was dismissed and the s.122 proceedings directed to continue (para 55).
- Key words: "abatement of proceedings under Section 74 of the CGST Act does not ipso facto abate the proceedings under Section 122 which are for completely different offences." (para 53)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: appeal or SLP status not found.
- Use in orders: a s.122 penalty for fake-invoice or ITC-pass-through contraventions can be adjudicated even where no tax demand stands. Frame the SCN on the specific clause of s.122(1).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 1603/2024, 29.05.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/195105563/); [Allahabad HC judgment PDF](https://www2.allahabadhighcourt.in/files_ilr/english/splitted/4716032024_29-05-2025_english.pdf); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/allahabad-high-court/allahabad-high-order-gst-penalty-proceedings-against-patanjali-294033); [EY alert](https://www.ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2025/06/hc-holds-penalty-imposed-under-section-122-is-a-civil-liability)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** the mens rea point was overstated: the court did not say a penalty never needs mens rea; it said this depends on the statute's scheme and is not needed for breach of a civil obligation. Neutral citation added. The Rs 273.51 crore figure was not located in the text and remains secondary.

#### Amit Manilal Haria v. Joint Commissioner, CGST & Central Excise - Bombay HC (DB: G.S. Kulkarni and Aarti Sathe JJ.) - 25.02.2026
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 5001 of 2025; 2026:BHC-AS:9556-DB.
- Holding: Penalties of Rs 133.6 crore each under s.122(1A) on three employees of Shemaroo Entertainment (OIO 01.02.2025) were quashed as without jurisdiction. Section 122(1A) reaches only a "taxable person" (s.2(107)) who retains the benefit of a transaction under s.122(1)(i), (ii), (vii) or (ix) and at whose instance it is conducted; the order recorded no finding that the employees retained any benefit. It followed the earlier Bombay HC decision in *Shantanu Sanjay Hundekari v. Union of India*, 2024 (89) GSTL 62 (Bom.), which is the source of the "ill-conceivable" (no vicarious liability under ss.122/137) words. It also held s.122(1A), in force from 01.01.2021, cannot apply to July 2017 to 01.01.2021 (Art. 20(1)) (paras 26-30).
- Key words: "There could not have been any retrospective application of Section 122(1A) of the CGST Act in issuing the impugned show cause notice for the period July 2017 to 1 January 2021" (para 30)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: the SC dismissed the Revenue's SLPs against *Hundekari* (a separate Maersk-employee case, reported (2025) 27 Centax 14 (SC)), saying it saw no reason to interfere but "the question of law as regards the two provisions ... is kept open" (order quoted in Haria, para 26). Appeal against Haria itself not found.
- Use in orders: before a s.122(1A) penalty on an employee or director, record why the person is a taxable person or retained the benefit. Expect this decision to be cited.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Bombay HC WP 5001/2025, 25.02.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/133627148/); [Lakshmisri - penalty only on taxable person](https://lakshmisri.com/newsroom/news-briefings/gst-penalty-under-section-122-1a-can-only-be-imposed-on-a-taxable-person); [LiveLaw - Delhi HC differs from Bombay HC](https://www.livelawbiz.com/high-court/delhi-high-court/penalty-us-1221a-of-cgst-act-can-be-imposed-on-both-taxable-and-non-taxable-person-delhi-high-court-291406)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** date is 25.02.2026 (not 2025) and the case number is confirmed; the "ill-conceivable" passage and the SC SLP dismissal belong to *Hundekari*, a separate earlier Bombay HC case that Haria followed; Haria also decides the pre-01.01.2021 retrospectivity point. The SC order text is as quoted in Haria (not read separately).

#### Gurudas Mallik Thakur v. Commissioner of CGST (with Dinesh Kumar Raghav) - Delhi HC (DB: Prathiba M. Singh and Rajneesh Kumar Gupta JJ.) - 23.04.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 5083/2025 and 5133/2025.
- Holding: s.122(1) speaks of a "taxable person" but s.122(1A) of "any person" who retains the benefit, so it can reach non-taxable persons such as directors who manage the company; who retained the benefit is a factual question (paras 24-29). Following *Bharat Parihar* (Bombay HC 2023), it **distinguished** (did not expressly differ from) the Bombay HC interim order in *Amit Manilal Haria* and the SC order in *Hundekari* as cases of mere employees, with the question of law left open (paras 34-36). The writs were not decided on merits: the directors were relegated to appeal, with directions on how non-registered persons can file (paras 37-44).
- Key words: "Section 122 (1A) of the CGST Act clearly relates to 'any person' who retains the benefit of a transaction" (para 26)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: support for penalising beneficiaries such as directors or controllers; record the benefit retained.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 5083/2025, 23.04.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/153321521/); [LiveLaw](https://www.livelawbiz.com/high-court/delhi-high-court/penalty-us-1221a-of-cgst-act-can-be-imposed-on-both-taxable-and-non-taxable-person-delhi-high-court-291406); [EY alert - penalty on directors](https://www.ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2025/06/hc-holds-penalty-122-1a-of-cgst-act-can-be-levied-on-directors-of-the-company) (the EY alert may be a different HC)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** party names, date (23.04.2025) and case numbers found; the court distinguished *Haria* (then only an interim order) rather than differing from it, and relegated the petitioners to appeal; the "any person" view is its reasoning, not a final adjudication of the penalty. Haria's final judgment (25.02.2026) came later.

#### Mayank Bansal (partner, Quantum Infratech) v. Union of India - Gauhati HC (Devashis Baruah J.) - 08.06.2026
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 24 of 2026 (with a connected petition by the other partner); 2026:GAU-AS:8028.
- Holding: s.122(1A) reaches the natural persons behind a juridical taxable person (here partners of a firm) who retained the benefit and at whose instance the s.122(1)(i)/(vii) transactions were done; it expressly disagreed with Bombay HC in *Hundekari* and *Haria* (paras 36-41). Because s.122(1A) creates no new violation and is a civil penalty, it can apply to transactions before 01.01.2021 if it was in force when the SCN issued; Art. 20(1) is not attracted (paras 50-54, 58(ii)). Only jurisdiction was decided; the partners were relegated to the GSTAT on facts and quantum (paras 57-58).
- Key words: "the proposition of law laid down by the learned Division Bench of the Bombay High Court in Shantanu Sanjay Hundekari (supra) and Amit Manilal Haria (supra) if accepted, it would render the provision of Sub-Section (1A) of Section 122 of the Act of 2017 otiose and nugatory." (para 37)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found. The judgment records (para 47) that the SC granted leave on 04.08.2025 in *Mukesh Kumar Garg v. Union of India* (SLP (C) No. 18178/2025, from Delhi HC) on both questions (non-taxable person; pre-01.01.2021 application), with recovery stayed on deposit of 25%; that appeal is pending.
- Use in orders: support for partner or beneficiary penalties.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gauhati HC WP(C) 24/2026, 08.06.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/133565210/); [Taxo - 11.06.2026 "any person" wider than taxable person](https://taxo.online/latest-news/11-06-2026-expression-any-person-in-section-1221a-is-wider-than-taxable-person-reflecting-a-conscious-legislative-intent-to-impose-liability-on-beneficiaries-g/); [Taxmann - HC upholds penalty on partners](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-upholds-gst-penalty-on-partners-under-section-1221a-for-tax-evasion/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party, date (08.06.2026), case number and single-judge bench added; holding confirmed, with the retrospectivity ruling and the pending SC appeal in *Mukesh Kumar Garg* added (SC order seen only as quoted).

#### s.122(1A) and acts before 01.01.2021 (Bombay HC in Amit Manilal Haria; contra Gauhati HC in Mayank Bansal)
- Citation / case no.: see the *Amit Manilal Haria* entry (Bombay HC DB, WP 5001/2025, 25.02.2026, paras 29-30).
- Holding: the Bombay HC held that a s.122(1A) penalty cannot apply to July 2017 to 01.01.2021, because of the Art. 20(1) bar on ex post facto penalties. Whether the Taxmann item below reports the same case is not confirmed.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: the Gauhati HC (*Mayank Bansal*, 08.06.2026) disagreed: s.122(1A) creates no new violation, so it applies to earlier transactions if in force at the SCN date. The SC granted leave on both questions in *Mukesh Kumar Garg* (04.08.2025); pending.
- Use in orders: avoid s.122(1A) for pre-2021 transactions, or record why it applies.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Bombay HC WP 5001/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/133627148/); [Taxmann - Retrospective penalty u/s 122(1A) barred](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/retrospective-penalty-u-s-1221a-cgst-barred-hc/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (Haria and Mayank Bansal). **Corrected:** the court is now identified (Bombay HC, *Haria*); the contrary Gauhati view and the pending SC appeal added.

#### Global Plastowares v. (State) - Kerala HC - 2023
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 33787 of 2023.
- Holding: Section 73(8) (proceedings conclude, with no penalty, on payment of tax and interest within 30 days of the SCN) does not help a person who **collected** tax and failed to deposit it within 30 days of the due date. Section 73(11) penalty applies. The writ was dismissed.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: when tax collected was not deposited, cite s.73(11) and record that the tax was collected. Otherwise, honour s.73(8).
- Source: [LiveLaw PDF - Global Plastowares](https://www.livelaw.in/pdf_upload/lobal-plasto-wares-501260.pdf); [Taxo - 06.11.2023 Kerala HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/06-11-2023-failure-to-deposit-collected-tax-within-a-period-of-30-days-from-the-due-date-penalty-has-to-be-paid-kerala-high-court/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources). (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Kandan Hardware Mart v. Assistant Commissioner (ST) (FAC) & batch - Madras HC (C. Saravanan J.) - 02.01.2026; followed in NKR Traders v. State Tax Officer - Madras HC (G.R. Swaminathan J.) - 21.05.2026
- Citation / case no.: Kandan: W.P. No. 27029 of 2023 and batch (about 27 writs). NKR Traders: W.P. No. 20291 of 2026.
- Holding:
  - A general penalty under s.125 can be imposed only where no specific penalty is provided. Where a late fee under s.47 applies for late returns, s.125 cannot be added. A late fee, though not called a penalty, is penal in character.
  - *Kandan Hardware* also extended the GSTR-9/9C late-fee amnesty (Notf. 7/2023 as amended by 25/2023-CT) to annual returns filed before 01.04.2023, following *R.T. Pharma* (HP HC): those petitioners pay a late fee of Rs 10,000 under each Act and no s.125 penalty (paras 164, 205-212). Denying them the benefit was held discriminatory under Art. 14.
  - *NKR Traders* set aside the s.125 penalty for 2020-21 following *Kandan*, but held the s.47 late fee payable.
- Key words: ""General Penalty" under Section 125 of the respective GST Enactments can be imposed only in the absence of 'any other penalty' under the respective GST Enactments." (Kandan, para 207)
  - *Lasar Samuvel* (Madras HC 2026) followed this and remitted the matter.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: do not combine s.125 with s.47 late fee or with any specific penalty.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Kandan Hardware Mart, 02.01.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/185914693/); [Indian Kanoon - NKR Traders, 21.05.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/72276853/); [Taxmann - general penalty invalid where late fee applies](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/general-penalty-invalid-where-gst-late-fee-already-applies-hc/); [Taxheal - no simultaneous s.125 penalty](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=126219)
- Verified: Primary - *Kandan* and *NKR Traders* read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; dates, judges and case numbers added; summary confirmed (the amnesty caps the late fee at Rs 10,000 per Act rather than removing it). *Lasar Samuvel* not checked (secondary).

#### [DOUBTFUL] Raghuvansh Agro Farms v. (State) - Allahabad HC - 2025 (the only Raghuvansh judgment found is on s.130 for excess stock, not s.74 ingredients)
- Citation / case no.: not found for the s.74 holding. The only Raghuvansh Agro Farms GST judgment on Indian Kanoon is Writ Tax No. 3827 of 2025, 12.08.2025 (Piyush Agrawal J.), 2025:AHC:136431.
- Holding: A s.74 demand (with s.74 penalty) was quashed because the order did not record the foundational ingredients of fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression. A Madras HC ruling (name not found) similarly remanded a s.74 order for re-decision under s.73.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found. For periods from FY 2024-25, s.74A applies (see file 03).
- Use in orders: do not cite *Raghuvansh* for the s.74 point. The general principle (a s.74 penalty needs an express finding of fraud or suppression with intent, in both the SCN and the order) should be supported by a verified authority. *Raghuvansh* (12.08.2025) can be cited for this: s.130 confiscation cannot be used for excess stock found in a survey; the route is ss.73/74 (s.35(6)), following *Vijay Trading Co.* (SLP Diary No. 5881/2025 dismissed) (paras 8-12).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 3827/2025, 12.08.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/151992092/); [Taxguru (s.74 ingredients)](https://taxguru.in/?p=1035058); [Taxscan Annual Digest 2025 Part XII](https://www.taxscan.in/other-taxations/annual-tax-corporate-law-digest-2025-complete-high-court-cases-part-xii-1440473)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (summary) for the s.74 holding (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026). **Doubtful:** the Raghuvansh judgment that was found (read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026) quashed s.130/s.122 orders for excess stock found in a survey and says nothing about s.74 ingredients. The secondary source probably mixed up the cases.

### 3. s.129 / s.130 and e-invoicing (new points only; see file 03 for Satyam Shivam Papers)

**Contentions across courts.**
- Whether s.130 can be invoked without first going through s.129:
  - Gujarat (*Moon Traders*, 2020) required the sequence.
  - The AP HC (reported) allowed direct invocation.
  - Gujarat DB (*Panchhi Traders*, 2025) now holds the two sections independent, with intent to evade as the bridge.
- Uttarakhand HC (June 2025, name not found) quashed a confiscation completed from notice to final order in a single day.
- No SC ruling was found. No Telangana ruling on s.130 was found in this search.

#### Panchhi Traders v. State of Gujarat - Gujarat HC (DB: A.S. Supehia and Pranav Trivedi JJ.) - 11.12.2025
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 9250 of 2020 and allied matters (SCAs of 2021, 2022 and 2025); reported [2026] 113 GST 318 / 104 GSTL 289 (Guj.) (one source).
- Holding:
  - Sections 129 and 130 operate independently, even after the 2022 amendments. Section 130 can be invoked directly where facts disclose a clear, demonstrable intent to evade.
  - The proper officer must form that opinion within the rule 138B/138C timelines (ordinarily about six days from interception). Otherwise the goods are released under s.129.
  - Confiscation may follow later if incriminating material emerges (following *Synergy Fertichem*).
  - The petitions were not decided on merits: all MOV-10/MOV-11 notices and orders were remanded for re-examination in light of these observations within 12 weeks, with two weeks' protection before any confiscation (paras 65-67).
- Key words: "the opinion of "intention to evade the payment of tax', has to be confined within the aforesaid period for confiscation, and if no opinion is formed, the goods and conveyance are required to be released by resorting to the provisions of Section 129" (para 65)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: followed by later Gujarat orders disposing of writs in its terms.
- Use in orders: record the material showing intent to evade and the date the opinion was formed, within the rule timelines.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 9250/2020 batch, 11.12.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/170350193/); [LiveLaw - Gujarat HC upholds confiscation power](https://www.livelawbiz.com/amp/high-court/gujarat-high-court/cgst-gujarat-high-court-upholds-power-to-confiscate-goods-during-transit-for-tax-evasion-clarifies-scope-of-sections-129-130-513256); [Taxo - 15.12.2025](https://taxo.online/latest-news/15-12-2025-section-130-can-be-independently-invoked-for-confiscation-where-the-facts-disclose-a-clear-and-demonstrable-intent-to-evade-payment-of-tax-gujarat-high-court/); [Taxheal (case nos.)](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=113078)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** lead case is SCA 9250 of **2020** (not 2025); the outcome (remand of all notices for re-examination) added; holding confirmed.

#### Nancy Trading Company v. State of U.P. - Allahabad HC (Piyush Agrawal J.) - 15.07.2024
- Citation / case no.: Writ Tax No. 892 of 2023; 2024:AHC:112666.
- Holding:
  - The dealer did not generate an e-invoice (rule 48), apparently a bona fide mistake after the threshold dropped from Rs 20 crore to Rs 10 crore.
  - All other rule 138A documents were present and there was no intent to evade, so the s.129 penalty was quashed and a refund ordered.
- Key words: "in absence of any finding with regard to mens rea the proceeding under section 129(3) of the Act cannot be initiated." (para 7)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found. Similar 2025-26 Allahabad rulings: penalty unsustainable where the e-way bill was generated before interception; *Rambabu* (Aug 2026) remanded an Rs 18 lakh penalty for ignoring the e-way bill, e-invoice and bilty produced.
- Use in orders: a missing e-invoice alone, with the other documents in order, will not sustain a s.129 penalty without an evasion finding.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC Writ Tax 892/2023, 15.07.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/31298928/); [Taxo - Nancy Trading](https://taxo.online/?p=510947); [Taxo blog - bona fide mistake](https://taxo.online/blogs/penalty-orders-to-be-quashed-imposed-for-not-generating-an-e-tax-invoice-due-to-a-bona-fide-mistake/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; single-judge bench and neutral citation added; summary confirmed. The later 2025-26 Allahabad rulings (incl. *Rambabu*) remain secondary.

---

### 4. Late-fee waivers (GSTR-9/9C)

**Contentions across courts.** The HP, Madras and Kerala HCs have extended the amnesty to returns filed before the amnesty notification. No contrary HC ruling and no SC ruling were found, and no challenge to the validity of the notifications was found. No GSTR-3B-specific late-fee waiver ruling was found.

#### M/s R.T. Pharma v. Union of India - Himachal Pradesh HC (DB: Tarlok Singh Chauhan ACJ and Satyen Vaidya J.) - 21.12.2024
- Citation / case no.: CWP No. 4899 of 2024; 2024:HHC:15432.
- Holding: The petitioner filed its annual return late (due 07.02.2020) but on 13.03.2023, before the amnesty notification of 31.03.2023. Denying it the late-fee waiver under Notf. 7/2023-CT just because it filed before the notification is unjust. The s.74 SCN (22.08.2023) and order (30.11.2023) were set aside and the matter remanded for a fresh order extending the notification's benefit (paras 6-9), relying on the Kerala HC in *Anishia Chandrakanth*.
- Key words: "It would be unjust to deny the benefit to the petitioner merely because the petitioner filed the return prior to the issuance of the amnesty notification dated 31.03.2023" (para 7)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: followed by Madras HC (*Kandan Hardware Mart*, *Lasar Samuvel*).
- Use in orders: cap the late fee at the amnesty level for early filers of the relevant years.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - HP HC CWP 4899/2024, 21.12.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/20662864/); [TaxGuru - waiver admissible for returns filed before 01.04.2023](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/waiver-late-fee-amnesty-scheme-admissible-taxpayer-filed-gst-annual-return-01-04-2023.html); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1046266)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 15 Oct 2026; date, bench, case number and the remand outcome added; summary confirmed.

#### Anishia Chandrakanth v. Superintendent, Central Tax and Central Excise - Kerala HC (single judge) - 2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) Nos. 30644, 32069, 38545, 38547, 38891, 39203 and 40762 of 2023; (2024) 04 KL CK 0099. The date is given as 09.04.2024 in one HP HC order and 10.07.2024 in another; not confirmed.
- Holding: No late fee can be levied for late GSTR-9C for FY 2017-18, 2018-19 and 2019-20, given the amnesty's purpose. However, late fee already paid above Rs 10,000 is not refundable.
- Key words: "the petitioners will not be entitled to claim refund of the late fee which has already paid by them over and above Rs. 10,000/-." (para 25, as quoted in *R.T. Pharma*, HP HC, para 4)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: drop pending GSTR-9C late-fee notices for those years, but do not grant refunds of late fee already paid.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - R.T. Pharma (HP HC) quoting para 25](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/20662864/); [Taxo - No late fee for GSTR-9C 17-18 to 19-20](https://taxo.online/?p=494982); [Taxo blog - waiver retrospective](https://taxo.online/blogs/late-fee-waiver-on-gstr9c-has-retrospective-applicability/)
- Verified: Secondary - the judgment itself was not found on Indian Kanoon on 15 Oct 2026 (only a 17.01.2024 order against the State Tax Officer appears). Party name, case numbers and para 25 were confirmed from the extract quoted in *R.T. Pharma* (HP HC, read 15 Oct 2026). **Corrected:** party name spelling (Chandrakanth) and respondent.

### 5. s.128A waiver (additions to file 03)

File 03 already covers *Incompressible Fluid Control System* (Madras), *Sri Laxmi Borewell* (Karnataka: deadline directory) and Madras HC rulings that s.128A must be tested only on statutory conditions.

**Contentions across courts.**
- Circular 238/32/2024 (15.10.2024) excludes self-assessed tax (declared in GSTR-1, not paid in GSTR-3B) and interest on delayed returns. The Madras HC (per Taxscan, decided around 28.07.2026) held that circulars cannot curtail the statute.
- A Bombay HC writ challenging the circular has been admitted with notice; the outcome was not found.
- A separate Madras remand is reported as pending a larger-bench ruling, so the Madras position may not be final.
- Circular 248/05/2025: a taxpayer who paid through GSTR-3B before 01.11.2024 remains eligible.
- Telangana HC: no ruling found. SC: none found.

#### WFB Baird and Company India Pvt Ltd v. State Tax Officer - Madras HC (Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J.) - 07.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 24237 and 24239 of 2026.
- Holding: The SCN only compared GSTR-3B ITC with GSTR-2A, and the order (08.12.2023, summary superscribed "under Section 73") confirmed the demand for failure to prove the ITC claim. Neither contained any s.74 ingredient (fraud, wilful misstatement or suppression). Under s.75(2) the proceedings should have been under s.73. The order was set aside and remanded for a fresh s.73 order, the s.128A rejection was set aside as a corollary, and the taxpayer may file a fresh s.128A application in time (paras 4-6).
- Key words: "in terms of subsection (2) of Section 75 of applicable GST enactments, I conclude that the proceedings should appropriately have been initiated and concluded under Section 73 and not under Section 74." (para 6)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: when rejecting an SPL application because the demand is under s.74, check that the s.74 invocation itself is supportable on the record.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 24237/2026, 07.07.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/171588318/); [Taxo - 13.07.2026 Madras HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/13-07-2026-where-an-order-is-wrongly-passed-under-section-74-the-consequential-denial-of-benefits-available-under-section-128a-is-unsustainable-madras-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 15 Oct 2026; party name, case numbers, judge and date (07.07.2026; 13.07.2026 was the report date) found; summary confirmed. The relief was a remand for a fresh s.73 order and a fresh s.128A application, not a direct grant of waiver.

#### Circular 238/32/2024-GST challenge - Madras HC (Incompressible Fluid Control System v. Assistant Commissioner, Senthilkumar Ramamoorthy J., 28.07.2026) / Bombay HC writ - 2026
- Citation / case no.: Madras: W.P. No. 21378 of 2026 and batch (28 writs); 2026:MHC:3296. Bombay writ: not found.
- Holding: The Madras HC held (para 38): (i) a s.73 proceeding is a precondition for a s.128A application, so where the department recovers self-assessed tax directly under ss.75(12)/79 without s.73, no s.128A application lies; (ii) once the department adjudicates under s.73, self-assessed tax is not impliedly excluded from s.128A; (iii) Circular 238 cannot curtail s.128A; (iv) an SPL-05 approval cannot later be declared void except under rule 164(16)/(17), and non-decision within rule 164(13) is deemed approval; (v) rule 164 time limits are mandatory. Twenty writs were allowed. The lead petitioner's own writ (21378/2026) and seven others were **dismissed** on other grounds (late payment, appeal not withdrawn, or no or belated application) (para 43). Bombay HC has issued notice on a writ challenging the exclusion of GSTR-3B short-payment and delayed-payment interest (secondary).
- Key words: "a circular issued by the CBIC cannot curtail the scope and ambit of the statute. Therefore, the reliance on Circular No.238 for the rejection of waiver applications is misconceived." (para 29)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Bombay HC outcome not found.
- Use in orders: if rejecting relying on Circular 238, note that the circular is under challenge and record the statutory ground as well.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Madras HC W.P. 21378/2026 batch, 28.07.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/45893232/); [Taxscan - Madras HC allows waiver for self-assessed tax](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/madras-hc-allows-gst-amnesty-waiver-us-128a-for-self-assessed-tax-cases-says-cbic-circulars-cannot-curtail-scope-of-statute-1450363); [JurisHour - challenge to circular](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/challenge-to-gst-circular-s-128a-relief/); [Circular 248/05/2025](https://gstcouncil.gov.in/sites/default/files/2025-04/circular-no-248-05-2025.pdf)
- Verified: Madras judgment: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 15 Oct 2026; case number, date and judge added; holding expanded. Note that the named lead petitioner lost; the self-assessed-tax point applies only where s.73 proceedings were started. Bombay writ: secondary.

### 6. Anti-profiteering (s.171)

**Contentions across courts.**
- The Delhi HC upheld s.171's validity. Challenges to individual NAA orders on methodology remain to be decided on merits.
- Excel Rasayan's SLP (notice issued Feb 2024) is pending; the outcome was not found.
- The GSTAT has refused to stay proceedings because of the pending SLP (Sai Kripa / Helene Curtis matter, date not found).
- Department position: GSTAT Principal Bench from 01.10.2024 (Notf. 18/2024-CT); no new applications from 01.04.2025 (Notf. 19/2024-CT, under the s.171(2) proviso). Section 171 remains on the statute.
- The "transfer to CCI" mentioned in the brief was **not confirmed** in any search result; the functions went to the GSTAT instead.

#### Reckitt Benckiser India Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Manmohan ACJ and Dinesh Kumar Sharma J.) - 29.01.2024
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 7743/2019 and connected matters (over 100 cases, including Excel Rasayan, Hindustan Unilever, IFB, Pyramid Infratech); [2024] 158 taxmann.com 675 (Delhi) as reported.
- Holding:
  - Section 171 and rules 122, 124, 126, 127, 129, 133 and 134 were upheld. They are beneficial consumer-welfare legislation, within Art. 246A, and are not price fixing. They do not violate Art. 19(1)(g) or 300A.
  - The court acknowledged that the mechanism can be applied arbitrarily, for example by enlarging the scope or ignoring genuine cost escalation. The remedy is to challenge individual orders on merits.
- Key words: "What will be struck down in such cases will not be the provision itself which invests such power on the concerned authority but the erroneous application of the power." (para 163)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana (Delhi HC); widely followed by the GSTAT.
- Later history: SLPs filed. The SC issued notice in *Excel Rasayan* (Feb 2024, CJI Chandrachud, Pardiwala and Misra JJ.). Final outcome not found.
- Use in orders: validity can be assumed. Any order must rest on a sound computation methodology.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 7743/2019 batch, 29.01.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/160016725/); [LiveLaw - Delhi HC upholds anti-profiteering](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/high-court/delhi-high-court/delhi-high-court-upholds-anti-profiteering-provisions-cgst-act-247928); [SCC Online blog](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2024/01/31/del-hc-upholds-constitutional-validity-gst-anti-profiteering-mechanism-cautions-potential-arbitrary-use-legal-news/); [LiveLaw - SC issues notice](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/supreme-court-cgst-act-anti-profiteering-provisions-validity-notice-issued-249630)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (indexed as "Excel Rasayan Pvt Ltd v. Union of India", 29.01.2024); bench and holding confirmed (para 163). The SC notice details remain secondary.

#### Excel Rasayan Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Supreme Court - notice issued Feb 2024
- Citation / case no.: SLP number not found.
- Holding: no final ruling found. The petitioner argues that s.171 and rule 133 are arbitrary and vague for want of a methodology. Underlying NAA Order No. 94/2020 (11.12.2020) found Rs 4,64,894 not passed on after the 28% to 18% rate cut.
- Binding: pending.
- Later history: status not found as of Oct 2026.
- Use in orders: none yet. Check SC status before citing Reckitt as final.
- Source: [Verdictum - SC issues notices](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/supreme-court/supreme-court-issues-notices-on-challenge-to-cgst-act-provision-upheld-by-delhi-high-court-1520284); [Business Today](https://businesstoday.in/latest/economy/story/gst-anti-profiteering-supreme-court-issues-notice-to-centre-417211-2024-02-12)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3 sources agree) (SC order not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; the Delhi HC judgment under appeal was read - see Reckitt Benckiser).

#### DGAP v. Urban Essence (Subway franchisee, Pune) - GSTAT Principal Bench (President Justice (Retd.) Sanjaya Kumar Mishra) - 05.08.2025
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: This was the first GSTAT anti-profiteering order. It upheld the DGAP finding that the restaurant-service rate cut (18% to 5%, from 15.11.2017) was not passed on. It directed deposit of about Rs 5.45 lakh (sources differ: Rs 5,45,005 / Rs 5,47,005) with 18% interest into the Maharashtra Consumer Welfare Fund. Commentary says it held that a probe can cover all products under the registration and rejected cost escalation as a defence.
- Binding: persuasive (tribunal); binding on the parties.
- Later history: *DGAP v. APV Realty Ltd* (NAPA/66/PB/2025, Jan 2026) was remanded to the DGAP for re-investigation on correct data, following a Delhi HC judgment.
- Use in orders: for pending legacy cases only.
- Source: [Nexdigm - GSTAT's first anti-profiteering ruling](https://www.nexdigm.com/media/gstats-first-anti-profiteering-ruling-expands-probe-scope-rejects-cost-based-defences-hospitality-retail-on-notice/); [A2Z Taxcorp](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/gstat-orders-subway-franchisee-to-deposit-profiteered-sum-for-pocketing-gst-rate-cut-benefit/); [Taxheal - APV Realty remand](https://www.taxheal.com/66-pb-2025.html)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3 sources agree; amount differs) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 15 Oct 2026).

#### Anti-profiteering sunset - Notf. 18/2024-CT and 19/2024-CT (30.09.2024) - statutory
- Citation / case no.: Notf. 18/2024-CT (GSTAT Principal Bench from 01.10.2024); Notf. 19/2024-CT (no requests accepted from 01.04.2025).
- Holding: Complaints filed before 01.04.2025 continue before the Principal Bench. Commentary says no fresh complaints lie for the September 2025 rate cuts.
- Binding: statutory.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: do not entertain new s.171 complaints filed after 31.03.2025.
- Source: [Taxo - 01.10.2024 no new anti-profiteering cases from FY26](https://taxo.online/latest-news/01-10-2024-no-new-anti-profiteering-cases-from-fy26-as-gst-tribunal-takes-over/); [ICSI journal July 2026](https://www.icsi.edu/media/webmodules/CSJ/July2026/25.pdf)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (several sources agree; the Gazette text was not read).

### 7. Cross-empowerment and parallel proceedings (s.6(2)(b))

**Contentions across courts.**
- Before *Armour Security*, High Courts were split on whether a summons is "initiation of proceedings". The SC settled this: it is not; an SCN is.
- On "same subject matter", the Delhi HC (Sept 2026) and Telangana HC (United Bakers, 2026, court to be confirmed) look at whether the issue, period and liability are truly identical. The same assessee or an overlapping period is not enough.
- A Feb 2025 Delhi ruling (following *DLF Home Developers*) held simultaneous proceedings impermissible.
- Department position: Circulars 31/05/2018, 169/01/2022 and 239/33/2024 on adjudication; no 2025 CBIC instruction following Armour was found.

#### Armour Security (India) Ltd v. Commissioner, CGST, Delhi East Commissionerate - Supreme Court (J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ.) - 14.08.2025
- Citation / case no.: 2025 INSC 982; SLP (C) No. 6092 of 2025 (disposed of with conclusions and guidelines; leave not granted). [2025] 177 taxmann.com 478 (SC) and AIR 2025 SC 3854 as reported (not seen on Indian Kanoon).
- Holding:
  - "Initiation of any proceedings" in s.6(2)(b) means formal adjudicatory proceedings by an SCN; summons, search, seizure and other evidence-gathering are not proceedings (para 96(v)-(vi)).
  - "Subject matter" is the tax liability or obligation from a particular contravention; an identical **or partial overlap** attracts the bar, but distinct infractions do not, even if the amount is similar. Two-fold test: same liability/offence on the same facts, and identical demand (para 96(vii)-(x)).
  - Audit and detailed-scrutiny action must be initiated by the administration to which the taxpayer is assigned; intelligence-based enforcement can be taken by either, but the other should not start parallel proceedings (para 96(ii)-(iv)).
  - Guidelines (para 97): taxpayer must still comply with summons; must tell the later authority in writing of the overlap; authorities must verify and reply with reasons; an SCN on a liability already covered by an existing SCN "shall be quashed"; if they cannot agree, the authority that started first continues.
- Key words: "The expression 'initiation of any proceedings' occurring in Section 6(2)(b) refers to the formal commencement of adjudicatory proceedings by way of issuance of a show cause notice, and does not encompass the issuance of summons". (para 96(vi))
- Binding: **BINDING ON ALL (Art. 141)**.
- Later history: applied by the Delhi HC in *Sanskriti Exim* and the Sept 2026 DGGI batch.
- Use in orders: investigation can proceed in parallel. Before issuing an SCN, verify that no SCN on the same subject matter has been issued by the other administration, and record that check. Audit and scrutiny of State-assigned taxpayers stay with the State.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC judgment 14.08.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/47476951/); [TaxGuru - Armour Security clarifies s.6(2)(b)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/armour-security-case-supreme-court-clarifies-section-6-2-b-gst-investigation-guidelines.html); [SC Observer](https://www.scobserver.in/supreme-court-observer-law-reports-scolr/subject-matter-and-statutory-bar-in-cgst-proceedings-summons-m-s-armour-security-india-pvt-ltd-v-commissioner-cgst/); [AMLegals](https://amlegals.com/parallel-gst-proceedings-triggered-only-upon-issuance-of-scn-and-not-by-summons/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** bench confirmed as J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ. (the Datta/Masih attribution is wrong; that bench decided Kesari Nandan the same day); neutral citation 2025 INSC 982 added; conclusions are para 96 and guidelines para 97 (not para 96).

#### [DOUBTFUL] Telangana HC on s.6(2)(b) - Central acted first, State order quashed (party name not found; "3204 of 2025" not a matching Telangana writ) - 2025
- Citation / case no.: reported as "3204 of 2025" (writ number, per Taxheal).
- Holding: State proceedings were without jurisdiction because the Central authority had acted first. The State order was quashed and the State was directed to transfer its documents and data to the Central authority. In another reported Telangana matter, where the State had already adjudicated, the Central SCN was kept in abeyance until the State appellate proceedings concluded (interim order).
- Binding: Unconfirmed. Do not cite. For the same proposition use Dott Services (Telangana HC DB, 21.03.2024, below), which is verified.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: if a TGST SCN or order exists on the same issue and period, do not issue a CGST SCN. Seek a transfer of the material instead.
- Source: [Taxheal - 3204 of 2025](https://www.taxheal.com/3204-of-2025.html); [Taxheal - parallel proceedings](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=114459)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources, same publisher) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026). Telangana HC W.P. No. 3204 of 2025 on Indian Kanoon is an unrelated fisheries payment case (V.V. Hatcheries, 24.02.2025), so the reported number does not identify a Telangana GST writ; the case may be from another HC.

#### M/s United Bakers Cooperative Society Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner of Central Tax (Court), Malkajgiri Division - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and G.M. Mohiuddin J.) - 23.04.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 7427 of 2026.
- Holding: Two Orders-in-Original (22.08.2025 and 13.11.2025) for the same period (2021-22). The first was on GSTR-1 vs GSTR-3B short payment; the second arose from ASMT-10 scrutiny, and after the reply the SCN of 15.09.2025 was confined to excess ITC (GSTR-3B/GSTR-9 vs GSTR-2A), dropping the GSTR-1/3B point. Applying Armour Security, the HC held there was no overlap of subject matter despite the same period; the petitioner was relegated to appeal within two weeks with a condonation application and pre-deposit (paras 2-10). **Both proceedings were by the Central tax authority**, so the case is about overlapping SCNs generally, not a Central-State conflict.
- Key words: "the subject matter of the second show cause notice does not overlap the first proceedings, though relating to the same tax period." (para 6)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: where a second SCN for the same period follows scrutiny, drop any point already covered by the first proceedings and record in the SCN how the issue differs. That is what saved the second order here.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 7427/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/22251570/); [JurisHour](https://www.jurishour.in/?p=1453108); [JurisHour - overlap SGST/CGST proceedings](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/overlap-scgst-cgst-proceedings-appeal/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** court confirmed as Telangana HC (DB); case number added; both proceedings were Central, not "Central and State".

#### M/s Dott Services Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (DB: P. Sam Koshy and N. Tukaramji JJ.) - 21.03.2024 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 5886 of 2024.
- Holding: The Central authority had already adjudicated the same facts and issue for July 2017 to March 2018 (OIO dated 31.10.2023, endorsed to the State; under challenge in W.P. 1357/2024). The State's later DRC-07 dated 30.12.2023 for the same period was quashed under s.6 CGST Act. The State's pleas that the OIO had not been uploaded on the portal and that the taxpayer had not informed it were rejected; the taxpayer had in fact intimated the authorities (paras 7-9).
- Key words: "mere not uploading of the order passed by the Central Authorities does not by itself empowers the State agencies to again initiate the proceedings in which the Central Authority ... has already initiated and passed an Order-in-Original." (para 8)
- Binding: Binding in Telangana. Pre-dates Armour Security (SC, 14.08.2025) but is consistent with it.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: before passing an order, check whether the other administration has already adjudicated the same issue and period (ask the taxpayer, check endorsements and the portal); non-upload of the other side's order is no answer.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Telangana HC W.P. 5886/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/2937799/); [Taxo - Dott Services](https://taxo.online/?p=497961)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; holding found (previously "not found"), with date and bench added.

#### Delhi HC DGGI batch (real-estate company with KA/TN/Telangana registrations) and Sanskriti Exim Pvt Ltd v. DGGI - Delhi HC (DB: Anil Kshetarpal and Shail Jain JJ.) - reported Sept 2026
- Citation / case no.: *Sanskriti Exim Pvt Ltd v. DGGI, DZU*: W.P.(C) 929/2025, decided 18.09.2026. The batch: not found; petitioner names in the batch were not found.
- Holding:
  - Assignment of a taxpayer to the State administration does not oust DGGI's pan-India jurisdiction (Notf. 14/2017-CT).
  - The same assessee, overlapping periods or overlapping ITC do not by themselves attract s.6(2)(b). The taxpayer must show that the identical liability and facts were already adjudicated.
  - A common SCN (29.04.2022) to 95 noticees, made answerable to the Additional/Joint Commissioner, CGST Delhi North, was upheld.
  - *Sanskriti Exim* applied the Armour two-fold test. The State's s.73 proceedings (ineligible ITC) and DGGI's s.74 proceedings (ITC on fictitious suppliers' invoices used for exports and IGST refunds) were not on the same subject matter; being later in time does not by itself attract the bar; a consolidated multi-year s.74 SCN is not without jurisdiction. The writ was dismissed, leaving the petitioner to appeal the OIO of 31.01.2025 under s.107 (paras 35-49).
- Key words: "The statutory prohibition is against initiation of proceedings on the same subject matter and not against every subsequent proceeding concerning the same assessee or a related transaction." (Sanskriti Exim, para 37)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: supports DGGI or Central SCNs against State-assigned taxpayers on distinct issues.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Sanskriti Exim, Delhi HC W.P.(C) 929/2025, 18.09.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/30876479/); [LiveLaw - assignment to State doesn't bar DGGI's pan-India jurisdiction](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst/high-court/taxpayers-assignment-to-state-tax-administration-doesnt-bar-dggis-pan-india-jurisdiction-delhi-high-court-550733); [SupremeToday](https://supremetoday.ai/delhi-high-court-upholds-dggis-pan-india-jurisdiction-and-rejects-bar-on-parallel-proceedings-20260919061); [Taxheal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=141068)
- Verified: *Sanskriti Exim*: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon (text fetched 14 Oct 2026, reviewed 15 Oct 2026); case number and date added; summary confirmed. DGGI batch (95 noticees): Secondary (not found on Indian Kanoon on 15 Oct 2026).
