### 8. DGGI and the "proper officer"

**Contentions across courts.**
- **Upheld DGGI power to issue SCNs:**
  - Madras HC 2024 (name not found), relying on CBIC Circular 31/05/2018.
  - Rajasthan HC (name not found): petition premature.
  - An HC reported by Taxmann (court not identified), relying on Notf. 14/2017-CT.
  - Delhi HC (Sept 2026).
- **Contra:** Karnataka HC, *NCS Pearson* (16.07.2025), holding the officer was not authorised.
- **Canon India analogy:** the SC (2021) held DRI officers were not "proper officers" under Customs s.28. The SC later **allowed the Revenue's review** (Canon II; date not found in these results), so the Canon I reasoning no longer helps taxpayers even by analogy. The Finance Act 2022 also amended the Customs Act.
- No GST ruling applying or distinguishing Canon for DGGI was found.
- Department position: Circular 239/33/2024 substituted para 7.1 of Circular 31/05/2018. With Notf. 27/2024-CT (from 01.12.2024), it empowers specified Additional/Joint Commissioners with All-India jurisdiction to adjudicate DGGI SCNs.
- "Indo International" (Delhi HC) and a Kerala HC ruling named in the brief were **not found**.

#### NCS Pearson Inc. v. Joint Director, DGGI (Chennai Zonal Unit) - Karnataka HC - 16.07.2025
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Only officers specifically authorised (assigned functions) by the Commissioner are "proper officers" for s.74. The DGGI officer here was not so authorised, so the SCN (12.02.2024) was without jurisdiction and was quashed.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: appeal not found. The Karnataka HC is reportedly not uniform on the point (a Taxmann-reported decision went the other way).
- Use in orders: a DGGI SCN should cite the notification or assignment order conferring proper-officer functions. The adjudicating officer should record the circular or notification giving adjudication jurisdiction.
- Source: [LawText](https://lawtext.in/judgement.php?bid=52027); [LiveLaw - Karnataka HC on improper officer](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/investigation-substantially-completed-improper-officer-scn-issued-by-proper-officer-us-74-of-cgst-act-liable-to-be-set-aside-karnataka-high-court-279927)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (LawText); the LiveLaw item may be a separate Karnataka case (not found on Indian Kanoon on 15 Oct 2026).

#### Rajasthan HC - Central Tax and DGGI officers have nationwide authority to issue SCNs (party name not found) - date not found
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: Central tax officers and DGGI have nationwide authority to issue SCNs. The writ was dismissed as premature because the petitioner should first reply to the SCN.
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: jurisdiction objections should be answered in the adjudication order with reference to Notf. 14/2017-CT.
- Source: [Taxscan - Rajasthan HC](https://www.taxscan.in/central-tax-officers-and-dggi-hold-nationwide-authority-to-issue-scns-rajasthan-hc/464948)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 15 Oct 2026).

#### Canon India Pvt Ltd v. Commissioner of Customs - Supreme Court (S.A. Bobde CJ, A.S. Bopanna and V. Ramasubramanian JJ.) - 09.03.2021; reviewed in Commissioner of Customs v. Canon India Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court (D.Y. Chandrachud CJI, J.B. Pardiwala and Manoj Misra JJ.) - 07.11.2024
- Citation / case no.: Canon I: Civil Appeal No. 1827 of 2018; AIR 2021 SC 1699. Canon II: Review Petition (C) No. 400 of 2021; 2024 INSC 854.
- Holding: Canon I held DRI officers were not "the proper officer" to issue s.28 Customs SCNs. Canon II **allowed the review**: DRI officers were appointed officers of customs (Notf. 19/90-Cus (N.T.), later 17/2002), and Circular 4/99-Cus and Notf. 44/2011 assigning proper-officer functions had not been placed before the Court in Canon I. Canon II also **upheld s.97 of the Finance Act 2022** (validation) (paras 167-168).
- Key words: "the judgment in Canon India (supra) was rendered without looking into the circular and the notification referred to above thereby seriously affecting the correctness of the same." (Canon II, para 168(ii)(a))
- Binding: binding (Customs). Its GST relevance is only by analogy.
- Later history: The validity of the Finance Act 2022 validation was decided in Canon II itself (upheld). The separate Daikin challenge was not checked.
- Use in orders: if a taxpayer cites Canon I against DGGI, respond that it was reviewed and that Notf. 14/2017-CT expressly appoints DGGI officers as central tax officers.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Canon I, 09.03.2021](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/42020425/); [Indian Kanoon - Canon II (review), 07.11.2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/136686091/); [Grant Thornton - Canon India](https://www.grantthornton.in/insights/articles/the-supreme-courts-landmark-verdict-in-canon-india-redefining-the-role-of-dri-under-customs-law/); [Taxscan - Delhi HC on Canon II](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=521216)
- Verified: Primary - both judgments read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers, dates, benches and citations added; Canon II's date (07.11.2024) and its upholding of s.97 FA 2022 added.

---

### 9. Audit (ss.65 / 66)

**Contentions across courts.** No ruling was found that squarely decides when a special audit under s.66 may be ordered (before or after an SCN). The Delhi HC decisions turn on limitation and natural justice. No Telangana ruling was found.

#### MS Koenig Solutions Pvt Ltd v. Assistant Commissioner, Audit Branch, State GST, GNCTD - Delhi HC (DB: Anil Kshetrapal and Shail Jain JJ.) - 09.07.2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 11410/2024.
- Holding: The ADT-03 (24.08.2023) directing a special audit for FY 2018-19 and the resulting DRC-01 (01.08.2024) were set aside. On the department's admission, the time limit to pass the s.73 order (30.04.2024, as extended by Notf. 56/2023) had expired. The s.66 / rule 102 grounds were **not** decided: this was a short order on the admitted limitation point (paras 2-6).
- Key words: "Keeping in view the aforesaid admitted position, this Court is left with no choice but to allow the writ petition and quash the communication dated 24.08.2023 on form GST ADT-03" (para 5)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a special audit does not extend limitation. Plan the s.66 audit so that the SCN and order fit within the s.73(10)/74(10)/74A deadlines.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 11410/2024 order, 09.07.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/77596316/); [SAG Infotech](https://blog.saginfotech.com/no-gst-demand-order-deadline-delhi-hc-adt-03-audit-notice-drc-01); [TheTaxCorp](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/delhi-hc-quashes-gst-adt-03-and-drc-01-after-section-73-order-deadline-expires)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 15 Oct 2026; summary confirmed. It is a short order resting on the department's concession, so it has limited value as precedent; no s.66 ruling.

#### Varian Medical Systems International India Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - Delhi HC (DB: Prathiba M. Singh and Shail Jain JJ.) - 28.10.2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 1064/2025 and 13605/2025; reported (2025) 10 DEL CK 2072.
- Holding: The pre-SCN (DRC-01A dated 25.11.2024) allowed submissions until 28.11.2024, but the SCN was issued on 27.11.2024, one day early. That violated natural justice, so the SCN was set aside and the matter relegated to the pre-SCN stage, with the time spent in the writ excluded from limitation (paras 19-22). The audit-limitation challenge **failed**: under the Explanation to s.65, audit "commences" when the registered person makes the records available (here its final submission on 11.10.2024), and the audit report of 11.02.2025 was held not time-barred (paras 14-17).
- Key words: "surprisingly, the authority has decided to issue the SCN itself one day before the said day expires ... Thus, this would be completely in violation of the principles of natural justice" (para 19)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: wait for the full reply period on DRC-01A or audit observations before issuing the SCN.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Delhi HC W.P.(C) 1064/2025, 28.10.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198406201/); [Delhi HC judgment text](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/PMS28102025CW10642025_174455.txt); [CourtKutchehry](https://courtkutchehry.com/judgments/varian-medical-systems-international-india-pvt-ltd-vs-union-of-india-and-ors)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 15 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** case no. is W.P.(C) 1064/2025 (not 10642/2025); date and bench added; on commencement of audit the court used the date records are made available (not "the later of" two dates) and rejected the limitation challenge.

---

### 10. Compensation cess refunds

**Contentions across courts.** The Gujarat HC (*Patson Papers*, 28.03.2025, followed in *Atul Ltd*) allows refund of unutilised compensation-cess ITC where goods are exported on payment of IGST. Bombay HC (*Crystal Overseas*, 2025) involved denial on an LUT export; its outcome was not clearly stated. The department's position relied on Circulars 45/19/2018 and 125/44/2019, which the Gujarat HC said were misread. The SC dismissed the Revenue's SLPs in both (27.10.2025 and 13.02.2026) but left the question of law open. Neither is a Supreme Court ruling on the point.

#### Patson Papers Pvt Ltd v. Union of India - **Gujarat HC** (DB: Bhargav D. Karia and D.N. Ray JJ.) - 28.03.2025; SLP dismissed by the Supreme Court (Pankaj Mithal and Prasanna B. Varale JJ.) on 27.10.2025, question of law left open
- Citation / case no.: HC: R/SCA No. 26250 of 2022 with SCA Nos. 20341 of 2022 and 19279 of 2023. SC: SLP (C) Diary No. 49578/2025 (later numbered SLP (C) Nos. 30826-30828/2025, per the SC order in Atul).
- Holding: Where the exporter pays IGST on a zero-rated supply under s.16(3) IGST Act and claims refund of that IGST, it cannot use the compensation-cess ITC (cess is not payable on the export), so the proviso to s.11(2) of the Cess Act does not apply and the unutilised cess ITC on coal used to make the exported goods is refundable; Circulars 45/19/2018 and 125/44/2019 do not bar it (paras 21-22). The SC, "in view of the finding recorded in paragraph 21", declined to interfere and dismissed the SLP "leaving the question of law, if any, open".
- Key words: "the petitioner would be entitled to refund of the unutilized input tax credit on cess paid on purchase of coal utilized for the purpose of manufacture of goods which are exported." (HC para 21)
- Binding: **Not a Supreme Court ruling.** It is a Gujarat HC judgment (persuasive in Telangana); the SLP dismissal is not a declaration of law under Art. 141, and the question of law was expressly left open.
- Later history: Followed by the Gujarat HC in Atul Ltd (24.07.2025); the Revenue's SLP in Atul was dismissed on 13.02.2026 because the Patson SLP had been dismissed.
- Use in orders: do not reject cess-ITC refund claims on the IGST-paid export route on this ground alone where the Gujarat view is followed. Do not cite it as Supreme Court law.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 26250/2022, 28.03.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26206233/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 27.10.2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/60669455/); [TaxGuru - cess refund, exports on payment of IGST (SC)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/cess-refund-denied-exports-payment-igst-sc.html); [Taxheal - SC upholds refund of unutilised cess ITC](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=112043)
- Verified: Primary - HC judgment and SC orders (13.10.2025, 27.10.2025) read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026. **Corrected:** it is a Gujarat HC judgment, not a Supreme Court judgment; the SC only dismissed the Revenue's SLP with the question of law left open.

#### Atul Ltd v. Union of India - Gujarat HC (DB: Bhargav D. Karia and Pranav Trivedi JJ.) - 24.07.2025
- Citation / case no.: R/SCA No. 20038 of 2022 with SCA No. 19949 of 2022. (SCA No. 26250 of 2022, earlier thought to be this case, is Patson Papers.)
- Holding: Facts "absolutely identical" to Patson Papers: unutilised compensation-cess ITC on coal used for exported goods (exports on payment of IGST) is refundable; refund rejections relying on Circulars 45/19/2018 and 125/44/2019 quashed (paras 7.2-8).
- Key words: "In view of the decision and law laid down by this Court in the case of Patson Papers (supra), the present petitions stands allowed." (para 8)
- Binding: persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: Revenue's SLP (C) No. 6586/2026 dismissed on 13.02.2026 (Manoj Misra and N.V. Anjaria JJ.) because the SLP in Patson Papers had been dismissed on 27.10.2025.
- Use in orders: as above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - Gujarat HC SCA 20038/2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/134390581/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 13.02.2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/122628176/); [Taxmann - refund of compensation cess ITC on coal](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/hc-allows-refund-of-unutilised-compensation-cess-itc-on-coal-used-for-export-goods); [TaxTMI blog](https://www.taxtmi.com/tmi_blog_details?id=842239)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026 (HC judgment and SC order of 13.02.2026); date, bench and case number added; it follows Patson Papers rather than deciding the point afresh.

---

### Gaps and doubts

1. **Armour Security bench.** Resolved: the judgment (read 14 Oct 2026) is by J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ.; conclusions at para 96, guidelines at para 97.
2. **"Indo International" (Delhi HC) and the Kerala HC ruling on DGGI SCNs** named in the brief were **not found**. "Transfer of anti-profiteering to CCI" was not confirmed. The searches show the function moved to the GSTAT Principal Bench, with a 01.04.2025 sunset for new applications.
3. **No Telangana HC ruling found** on: s.50(3) utilisation; DRC-01D/DRC-07 interest recovery; s.122(1A); s.125; s.128A; s.130; special audit. *Orange Auto* (Telangana HC DB, 24.12.2021), *Dott Services* (Telangana HC DB, W.P. 5886/2024, 21.03.2024) and *United Bakers* (Telangana HC DB, W.P. 7427/2026, 23.04.2026) have now been read and verified. "3204 of 2025" is marked [DOUBTFUL] (Telangana W.P. 3204/2025 is an unrelated case).
4. **s.122(1A).** Resolved in part: *Hundekari* is a separate, earlier Bombay HC case (Maersk employee). The SC dismissed the SLP keeping the question of law open, as quoted in *Haria* (Bombay HC, 25.02.2026). The Delhi HC case is *Gurudas Mallik Thakur* (23.04.2025). The SC has granted leave on both s.122(1A) questions in *Mukesh Kumar Garg* (04.08.2025, per the Gauhati HC); it is pending.
5. **Madras HC June 2026 (s.50(3))**: party name still not found. The July 2026 s.74 / s.128A case is *WFB Baird* (07.07.2026).
6. **Excel Rasayan SLP / Reckitt appeals**: current SC status was not found as of Oct 2026.
7. **Patson Papers**: resolved. It is a Gujarat HC judgment (R/SCA 26250/2022, 28.03.2025), not a Supreme Court ruling. The SC dismissed the SLP on 27.10.2025, "leaving the question of law, if any, open".
8. **Not searched (budget of 45 exhausted):**
   - TDS/TCS disputes under ss.51/52 (the only search returned income-tax cases);
   - GSTR-9/9C as the basis for demands;
   - GST on compensation cess beyond refunds;
   - Circular 3/3/2017 proper-officer challenges;
   - GSTAT decisions on penalty or interest (other than anti-profiteering);
   - CBIC instructions issued after Armour Security;
   - the Telangana review petition in *Megha Engineering*.
9. **s.50(1) proviso commencement.** Notf. 16/2021-CT notified 01.06.2021 as the commencement date of s.112 FA 2021, but one source said 01.07.2021. Check the Gazette.
10. Entries were compiled from search-result summaries; 28 of 38 have since been checked against the judgment text (see the Verification log). For the rest (marked "Secondary"), confirm the citation, date and holding before citing them in an order.

## File 09: GST case law: gaps from round one, tribunal-level decisions and 2026 updates

**Scope.** This file follows up the "Gaps and doubts" lists at the end of files 01 to 05. It covers:
- what happened to the pending matters those files flagged (the Supreme Court s.168A case, *Sahil Enterprises*, the *Rollmet* reference, cash seizure, GST on royalty);
- refund points those files could not search (refund of tax on cancelled supplies, withholding under s.54(10)/(11), cash-ledger refunds, *Bansal International*);
- Telangana gaps (cash seizure, tax paid during search, works-contract rate);
- the first reported decisions of the GST Appellate Tribunal (GSTAT) and its procedure rulings;
- a short set of conflicting Advance Rulings that officers meet often;
- how Finance Act 2026 and the 57th GST Council meeting (8 Oct 2026) affect the case law.

Entries are grouped under the earlier file they belong to (for example, "Additions to refunds"). Read this file with those files, not on its own.

**Date compiled:** 14 Oct 2026. **Updated:** 17 Oct 2026 (5 entries added; 57th Council status re-checked).

> Compiled from web-search summaries. On 14 Oct 2026, 21 entries were checked against the judgment text on Indian Kanoon (marked "Primary"); the rest remain secondary. See the verification log at the end.

**Binding status key.** Supreme Court: binding on all (Art. 141). Telangana HC: **BINDING IN TELANGANA** (Sangareddy). Other High Courts: persuasive in Telangana. GSTAT: binding on the parties and in practice followed by lower authorities in that bench's area, but not precedent in the way a High Court ruling is; treat as strongly persuasive. AAR/AAAR: binds only the applicant and its jurisdictional officer (s.103); persuasive at most elsewhere.

---

### 1. Quick answers

**1. SC s.168A case (Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023), SLP(C) 4240/2025, *HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV*.** No final judgment was found as of 14 Oct 2026. Notice was issued on 21 Feb 2025. High Courts are split: Allahabad upheld 9/2023, Patna upheld 56/2023, Gauhati quashed 56/2023. Delhi HC (Jan 2026) declined to rule on vires "keeping in view judicial discipline". *Officer:* in any FY 2018-19 / 2019-20 order that relies on the extended dates, record that the validity of the notification is sub judice before the SC and that the order is subject to its outcome.

**2. Finance Act 2026 and the 57th Council.** Finance Act 2026 (Act 4 of 2026, assent 30.03.2026) did **not** amend s.54(3) or s.69. Its GST clauses (ss.153-157) deal with discounts and credit notes (s.15, s.34), provisional refund for inverted duty (s.54(6)), the s.54(14) minimum, NAA appeals and the intermediary place-of-supply rule. These are not in force except s.156 and s.157 (per the project's statute notes in `01-acts.md`). The 57th Council (8 Oct 2026) **recommended** omitting s.69 and refunding input-services ITC under inverted duty for ITC availed from 1 Nov 2026. As of 14 Oct 2026 these are recommendations only; no Bill, ordinance or notification was found. *Officer:* until the law is amended, *VKC Footsteps* (SC 2021) still bars refund of input-services ITC under inverted duty, and s.69 still applies, subject to *Radhika Agarwal* and *Sunil Biyani*.

**3. Refund of tax on a supply that was cancelled.** Karnataka HC (DB) in *Nam Estates* (W.A. 1195/2024) held that where the contract was cancelled and no supply took place, GST paid cannot be kept by the State just because the supplier did not issue a credit note. *Officer:* do not reject a recipient's refund only because no credit note was issued. Examine whether a supply took place and whether the same tax has already been refunded or adjusted at the supplier's end (to avoid a double refund).

**4. Withholding a refund under s.54(10)/(11).** Orissa (*Rashmi Agency*, 25 Jun 2026), Patna (*KPIL-JWIL JV*, 13 Aug 2026) and Telangana (*ADV Industrial Services*, Sep 2026, in file 01) agree on three points. Withholding needs an appeal or proceeding actually pending on the date of the order. It needs the Commissioner's own recorded opinion that paying the refund will hurt revenue. It needs a hearing and a speaking order. The fact that time to appeal is still running is not enough, and neither is an intention to appeal. But Delhi HC (*Devi Electronics*, W.P.(C) 15557/2024, 1 Oct 2026) upheld a withholding where an anti-evasion investigation was already pending and the Commissioner had recorded an opinion of fraud: "any other proceedings" covers a pending investigation. *Officer:* if you want to hold back a refund, get a reasoned Commissioner-level order under s.54(11), after a hearing, once the appeal is filed. Do not simply reject the RFD.

**5. Cash-ledger refunds.** No unjust-enrichment certificate is needed (rule 89(2)(l)/(m) does not apply). Delhi HC (*Harbhajan Singh Thukral*, W.P.(C) 3967/2025, Aug 2025) ordered payment with interest of an accepted cash-ledger refund that had been adjusted against a demand already cancelled. The 57th Council recommends full auto-sanction. *Officer:* any adjustment must follow s.54(10) or s.79 with notice and an order.

**6. Cash seizure under s.67.** Cash seizure is now opposed by Delhi (*Deepak Khandelwal*), Bombay DB (*Smurti Waghdhare*, 10 Mar 2026), Gauhati (*Ankit Choudhary*) and others. Only MP (*Kanishka Matta*, 26 Aug 2020) supports it. The SC has issued notice (2024) but has not decided. **No Telangana HC GST cash-seizure ruling was found.** *Officer:* do not seize cash unless it is itself the subject of the offence, with recorded reasons to believe. Never hand cash over to Income Tax. Issue the s.67(7) notice within six months.

**7. GSTAT.** Benches are working: Principal Bench since late 2025, and Hyderabad from 20 Apr 2026. The first substantive decision was *Sterling & Wilson* (PB, 11 Feb 2026, APL/1/PB/2026). It held that a return mismatch alone is not a ground for s.74, and that only the proper officer can re-determine tax under s.73 after s.74 fails, so the case was remanded. *Augustan Textile Colours* (Ernakulam, Sep 2026) held that a refund cannot be rejected as "ineligible ITC" without a s.73/74 order. *Officer:* these are the first signals of how GSTAT will review our orders. Invoke s.74 only with specific findings of fraud or suppression, and do not decide ITC eligibility inside a refund proceeding.

**8. Pre-deposit for GSTAT.** The SC (*PVCON Engineering*, SLP(C) 28059/2026, 7 Sep 2026) issued notice on whether the post-1.10.2025 pre-deposit for penalty-only appeals applies to older matters. As interim relief, it allowed a GSTAT appeal to be heard without pre-deposit. GSTAT Cuttack (*Manoranjan Dash*, 2026) gave credit for the first-appeal deposit. Orissa HC (15 May 2026) called s.112(8) pre-deposit mandatory. *Officer:* the point is unsettled. Follow the statute but do not take coercive steps where the SC has granted interim protection to the specific appellant.

**9. Rule 86A.** A block lapses after one year (r.86A(3)). Delhi HC (*Shri Sai Ram Enterprises*, 28 Apr 2025) and Calcutta HC (12 Jan 2026) lifted blocks older than one year. Negative blocking is illegal (Gujarat, Telangana, Delhi and Bombay, per *King Enterprises*, 18 Nov 2025). *Officer:* diarise the one-year lapse and block only the balance actually available.

**10. Section 16(2)(c), supplier default.** The SC **stayed** the Tripura HC's *Sahil Enterprises* read-down (Diary No. 33460/2026; Pardiwala and Vinod Chandran JJ.). Read with *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (SC, 24 Jul 2026, in file 02), the read-down is not good law for now. But *Safecon Lifescience* (SC dismissed the Revenue's SLP, 17 Jul 2026) leaves intact the rule that s.74 cannot rest on another wing's report that was never verified or disclosed to the buyer. *Officer:* recover ITC under s.16(2)(c) on evidence specific to the buyer. Put the investigation report to the noticee. Use s.74 only where the buyer's own fraud or collusion is shown.

---

### 2. Statutory developments that overtake or qualify case law

#### Finance Act, 2026 (Act 4 of 2026) - Parliament - assent 30 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: Act 4 of 2026. GST clauses are ss.153-157; commencement is in s.1(2). This summary is from the project's statute notes (`/mnt/project-files/gst-law/01-acts.md`), which were checked against the CBIC copy of the Act.
- Holding (content):
  - s.153/s.154: post-sale discounts reduce value if a credit note is issued and the recipient reverses the ITC.
  - s.155: s.54(6) provisional refund extended to inverted-duty refunds, and the s.54(14) minimum-amount bar lifted for exports with payment of tax.
  - s.156: NAA appeals go to the GSTAT Principal Bench, in force 01.04.2026.
  - s.157: IGST s.13(8)(b) omitted, so intermediary services now take the recipient's location, from 30.03.2026.
- Binding: statute.
- Later history: ss.153-155 start only on a date to be notified; **none had been notified as of 12.10.2026**. **The Act does not amend s.54(3) or s.69.** A TaxGuru article about the Finance Bill 2026 said s.54(3) was "rewritten" to overturn *VKC Footsteps*. The project's reading of the enacted Act does not bear that out, so treat the article as wrong or as describing a proposal.
- Use in orders: *VKC Footsteps* (SC 2021) still governs inverted-duty refunds of input-services ITC. For intermediary disputes, *Dharmendra M. Jani* (file 01) is overtaken for supplies from 30.03.2026 but stays relevant for earlier periods.
- Source: [CBIC - Finance Acts page](https://taxinformation.cbic.gov.in/explore-others); [TaxGuru - s.54(3) "rewritten" in Finance Bill 2026 (contrary claim)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/section-54-3-rewritten-finance-bill-2026-overturns-vkc-footsteps-extending-inverted-duty.html)
- Verified: Primary for the Act (via the project's statute notes). The TaxGuru claim is a single source and is contradicted.

#### 57th GST Council meeting - 8 Oct 2026 (recommendations, not law)
- Citation / case no.: PIB press release, 8 Oct 2026 (PRID 2320934), and Council FAQs. Details are in the project's `05-gst-council-meetings.md`.
- Holding (content relevant to case law):
  - **Omit s.69 (arrest).**
  - Raise the prosecution threshold from ₹1 crore to ₹5 crore, omit s.132(1)(i), and narrow s.132(1)(c).
  - **Inverted-duty refund to cover input services for ITC availed on or after 1 Nov 2026.**
  - Capital-goods ITC refund over 60 months for ITC availed from 1 Apr 2027.
  - Auto-sanction of cash-ledger refunds, and 90% provisional refunds.
  - Remove the 1.5x cap in rule 89(4)(C).
  - No SCN where tax is below ₹10,000.
  - Pre-deposit cap of ₹40 crore for penalty-only appeals.
  - Rule 86A: a right to object and to a hearing.
  - E-way bill interception limits, and no s.130 confiscation of goods in transit.
- Binding: no. The release itself says the recommendations take effect only through notifications, circulars or amendments to the Act.
- Later history: **no amending Bill, ordinance or notification was found as of 14 Oct 2026, nor on a re-check on 17 Oct 2026.** From 15-16 Oct 2026 the CBIC Chairman and Member (GST) held outreach meetings with trade on the 57th Council reforms (Hyderabad, Chennai, Bengaluru), and sought feedback on a proposed centralised GST administration for taxpayers under Central jurisdiction (a proposal, not a decision). One news source says the changes are expected from 1 Apr 2027. Sources differ slightly on the date (8 vs 9 Oct); the official release says 8 Oct.
- Use in orders: do not apply these as law yet. Arrest law under s.69 still runs (*Radhika Agarwal*, *Sunil Biyani*, file 04). Expect noticees to cite the Council's "intent" (for example, on 86A hearings or e-way bill transit checks). Answer that the provisions are unamended, while giving a hearing anyway where natural justice already requires one.
- Source: [Moneylife - Council recommends scrapping arrest powers](https://www.moneylife.in/article/gst-council-recommends-scrapping-arrest-powers-raises-prosecution-threshold-to-5-crore/81904.html); [Taxscan - 57th Council proposes refund of capital goods and input services ITC](https://www.taxscan.in/gst/refund-of-accumulated-itc-57th-gst-council-proposes-inclusion-of-capital-goods-and-input-services); [StudyCafe - proposed removal of s.69](https://studycafe.in/57th-gst-council-meeting-proposed-removal-of-arrest-provision-under-section-69-and-changes-to-prosecution-rules-427363.html)
- Verified: Secondary (news, several agree), plus the official release as recorded in the project notes.

---

### 3. Additions to refunds (file 01)

#### Joint Commissioner of Commercial Taxes (Appeals-1), Bengaluru v. Nam Estates Pvt Ltd - Karnataka HC (Division Bench: Krishna S. Dixit and G. Basavaraja JJ.) - 9 Jan 2025
- Citation / case no.: Writ Appeal No. 1195 of 2024 (T-RES); NC: 2025:KHC:680-DB (oral judgment). Appeal from the single judge's order of 18.04.2024 in W.P. 9075/2024.
- Holding: The recipient paid GST on an advance of about ₹14.09 crore (tax about ₹2.54 crore). The supplier defaulted, the contract was cancelled and the advance was recovered by encashing the bank guarantee. With no supply, there was no GST liability, so the tax could not be kept by the State only because the supplier issued no credit note. The single judge relied on unjust enrichment and restitution. The DB upheld him: the purchaser can claim refund (s.54(1) "any person", following *Oswal Chemicals*), and the Revenue could not refuse it because the vendor issued no credit note, since goods were never delivered. Refund within eight weeks, with interest at the statutory rate if delayed.
- Binding: Karnataka; persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: SLP not found.
- Use in orders: before rejecting a recipient's refund for want of a credit note, record whether supply took place and whether the supplier has already reduced its liability or claimed a refund for the same tax. A finding of double benefit is the only safe ground to reject.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WA 1195/2024, 9 Jan 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/137439029/); [Taxmann - GST on advance not to be retained where contract cancelled](https://taxmann.com/post/blog/gst-paid-on-advance-amount-is-not-to-be-retained-by-state-if-contract-is-cancelled-due-to-breach-of-contract-hc); [TaxHeal - WA 1195 of 2024](https://www.taxheal.com/1195-of-2024-t-res.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date (9 Jan 2025), bench and NC added; holding confirmed.
- Key words: "Caesar cannot retain monies of citizens without statutory justification." (para 5.4)

#### Emerald Haven Realty Developers (Paraniputhur) Pvt Ltd v. S.V. Ramesh - Madras HC (Division Bench: J. Nisha Banu and R. Sakthivel JJ.) - 24 Jan 2025
- Citation / case no.: C.M.S.A. No. 55 of 2024 (second appeal under s.58 of the RERA Act, from the TN Real Estate Appellate Tribunal).
- Holding: **Corrected.** The court upheld the RERA orders making the promoter refund the GST component (₹11,40,376) to an allottee who cancelled. The promoter's "GST loss" deduction (its s.34(2) credit-note window had closed) was not provided for in the agreement, and it did not prove it had paid GST twice. Noting the allottee's own refund application and undertaking to pass any GST refund to the promoter, it directed the GST department to process that application within two months. It did **not** hold that the buyer must apply under s.54 instead of the builder (the blog overstated it). Circular 188/20/2022-GST (refund to unregistered buyers on cancellation) is not discussed in the operative part.
- Binding: persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: for a flat buyer's refund on cancellation, process the buyer's s.54 / rule 89 application on its merits (the court directed exactly that), and check that the builder has not also reduced its liability for the same tax.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - CMSA 55/2024, 24 Jan 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/116456944/); [CA Sahuja - GST implications on property](https://www.casahuja.com/2025/02/gst-implications-on-property.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party names, court, date and holding corrected (blog summary overstated). Circular reference still from general knowledge.
- Key words: "it is for the appellant/ Promoter to prove that he has paid GST two times for the same apartment before the competent Authority, which has not been done in the present case." (para 8)

#### Bansal International v. Commissioner of DGST - Delhi HC (Vibhu Bakhru and Amit Mahajan JJ.) - 21 Nov 2023
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 11629/2023.
- Holding: **Corrected.** The refund had already been granted after appellate proceedings; the dispute was the interest claim (₹13,12,761 at 9%), rejected on 11.07.2023. Held: s.56 interest at 6% runs from day 61 after the **original** s.54(1) application; where the claim succeeds in appeal and a fresh application is filed, 9% runs from day 61 after **that** later application, with 6% continuing for the period in between. The rejection was set aside and the interest application (16.05.2023) directed to be processed accordingly.
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive in Telangana. Later Delhi HC orders treat it as setting out the "entire scheme" of refunds, e.g. W.P.(C) 7485/2024 (20 May 2025) and W.P.(C) 16315/2025 (28 Oct 2025).
- Later history: followed by the Telangana HC in *Qualcom India* / *Microsoft Global Services* (W.P. 8869 and 8871/2023, 20.03.2024; entry below). The blog's two-stage description is the correct one.
- Use in orders: compute s.56 interest at 6% from day 61 after the original application; where the refund follows an appellate order, 9% from day 61 after the post-appeal application, and 6% for the gap before it. Telangana's own authority on interest is *Qualcom* and *Synchrony* (file 01).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(C) 11629/2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/3180268/); [SAG Infotech - Delhi HC on interest after 60 days](https://blog.saginfotech.com/delhi-hc-interest-gst-refunds-60-days-filing-refund-application-finalization-refund-claim); [Delhi HC W.P.(C) 16315/2025 citing Bansal](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/PMS28102025CW163152025_174620.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; facts and interest computation corrected; bench added.
- Key words: "The applicant claiming a refund is entitled to interest at the rate of 6% per annum from a date immediately after the expiry of sixty days from making an application under Section 54(1) of the CGST Act." (para 33)

#### M/s Rashmi Agency v. Deputy Commissioner CT & GST, Cuttack-I City - Orissa HC (Harish Tandon CJ and M.S. Raman J.) - 25 Jun 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) No. 17470 of 2026 (reported 1 Jul 2026).
- Holding: ₹33 lakh paid in DRC-03 during a DGGI search became refundable under a first-appeal order of 27.02.2026. The officer refused to consider the refund (RFD-06, 02.05.2026) as "premature" because the State's six-month window under s.112(3) was open. Held: s.54(11) needs an appeal or other proceeding pending when the order is passed; none was (the State appealed to GSTAT only on 24.06.2026). Order set aside; remitted for fresh decision within two weeks after a hearing.
- Binding: Orissa; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: process the consequential refund. If the Department will appeal, file the appeal first and then pass a reasoned s.54(11) order after a hearing.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(C) 17470/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/173916935/); [taxo.online - 01.07.2026 Orissa HC](https://taxo.online/latest-news/01-07-2026-refund-cannot-be-withheld-merely-because-time-to-file-departmental-appeal-has-not-expired-orissa-high-court/); [TaxHeal - refund cannot be withheld without express order and hearing](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=140174)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name and decision date (25.06.2026, not 1.07.2026) added; relief is remand, not a direct refund order.
- Key words: "Unless there has already been proceeding pending, invocation of said provision smacks arbitrariness and tainted with irrational application of mind." (para 7.1)

#### Devi Electronics Pvt Ltd v. Commissioner CGST Delhi South - Delhi HC (Anil Kshetarpal and Shail Jain JJ.) - 1 Oct 2026
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 15557/2024.
- Holding: **Corrected: the petition was dismissed and the withholding upheld.** IGST export refunds of ₹3,15,30,605 allowed by an order-in-appeal (19.06.2024) were withheld by the Commissioner under s.54(11) on 24.09.2024. Held: "any other proceedings under this Act" covers an anti-evasion investigation already pending (from 26.03.2024), and s.54(11) works without any court stay where the Commissioner records an opinion of fraud on contemporaneous material. Cases where the Revenue relied only on a proposed appeal (*Alex Tour & Travel*, *Mandy Enterprises*) were distinguished; a mere decision to appeal is still not enough. Merits left to GSTAT.
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: an earlier bench (Yashwant Varma J., 16.01.2025) had prima facie doubted that an investigation counts as "other proceedings"; the final bench held that it does.
- Use in orders: a s.54(11) withholding can rest on a pending investigation, but only through a Commissioner's reasoned order, after a hearing, recording the material showing malfeasance or fraud. A bare intention to appeal is not a ground.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(C) 15557/2024, 1 Oct 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/152005308/); [TaxGuru - mere decision to file appeal no stay (headline reflects the cases distinguished, not the result)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/mere-decision-file-appeal-automatically-stay-gst-refund-delhi-hc.html); [Delhi HC order](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/75001102026CW155572024_153733.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name and date added; holding was wrong (petition dismissed, withholding upheld) and is rewritten.
- Key words: "Section 54(11) is a self-contained, statutorily engrafted safeguard for protection of revenue, operating independently of, and not contingent upon, any separate stay granted by a court or tribunal." (para 15)

#### KPIL-JWIL Joint Venture v. State of Bihar - Patna HC (Anil Kumar Sinha and Vikash Kumar JJ.) - 13 Aug 2026
- Citation / case no.: CWJC No. 5398 of 2026.
- Holding: A ₹1.75 crore cash-ledger refund, allowed in first appeal (31.12.2024), was stalled by a deficiency memo (RFD-03, 01.03.2025) citing past dues; the State had filed a GSTAT appeal (30.06.2026). Since no s.54(11) exercise had been undertaken, the court directed the Commissioner to issue a notice within a week, hear the petitioner and pass a specific reasoned s.54(11) order on payment or withholding within a month of the hearing.
- Binding: Patna; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: a checklist for any s.54(11) order: (i) pending appeal number and date; (ii) Commissioner's recorded reasons; (iii) hearing notice and speaking order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - CWJC 5398/2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/63152793/); [TaxHeal - cannot reject consequential refund without stay or withholding safeguards](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=140784); [TaxGuru](https://taxguru.in/?p=1078532)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case number and bench added; holding narrowed to what the court directed.
- Key words: "we direct the Commissioner to pass a specific order in terms of Section 54(11) ... after giving Show Cause Notice and proper opportunity of hearing to the petitioner." (para 10)
- **s.54(10) note:** no 2025-26 ruling specific to s.54(10) (withholding against unpaid returns or dues) was found. The cases above are all s.54(11).

#### Royale Edible Company v. State Tax Officer, SGST Dept, Thrissur - Kerala HC (A.K. Jayasankaran Nambiar J.) - 18 Dec 2020
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 22593 of 2020.
- Holding: (The balance arose from TDS credits; the officer wrongly treated the claim as one under s.51(8).) A balance left in the electronic cash ledger after all liabilities are paid can be claimed under the proviso to s.54(1). The officer should refund the excess after providing for known liabilities.
- Binding: Kerala; persuasive.
- Later history: none found. The 57th Council (Oct 2026) recommends full auto-sanction of cash-ledger refunds.
- Use in orders: do not reject cash-ledger refunds on the ground that the money "may be needed later". Adjust only known, crystallised dues under the proper procedure.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WP(C) 22593/2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/120924035/); [taxo.online - Royale Edible](https://taxo.online/?p=52627); [Busy - case summary](https://busy.in/caselaw/royale-edible-company-vs-the-state-tax-officer-and-others-kerala-high-court)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date and judge added; summary confirmed.
- Key words: "if there was such a balance, the refund had necessarily to be granted to the petitioner." (para 5)

#### Harbhajan Singh Thukral v. GNCTD, Department of Trade and Taxes - Delhi HC (Prathiba M. Singh and Shail Jain JJ.) - 20 Aug 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 3967/2025.
- Holding: **Narrowed.** A cash-ledger refund accepted on 12.04.2023 was appropriated (order 18.09.2023) against a demand that had already been cancelled by a DRC-08A of 24.07.2023, which was never uploaded to the portal. On the Department's own admission, the court directed the refund (₹9,09,727) to be paid with statutory interest within two months. It is a fact-specific order and does not hold that an accepted refund can never be adjusted against live dues.
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive.
- Later history: none found. Contrast the Delhi HC ruling of Nov 2024 (party name not found) that the Commissioner may withhold cash- or credit-ledger refunds in a proper case (reported under s.108 / s.54).
- Use in orders: before appropriating a refund against dues (s.54(10) / s.79, with notice and an order), confirm that the demand is still live on the portal and in the records (check for DRC-08A rectifications). Never adjust silently.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(C) 3967/2025, 20 Aug 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/115866020/); [Dev International v. UoI (Delhi HC) summary](https://busy.in/caselaw/dev-international-vs-union-of-india-and-others-delhi-high-court); [LiveLaw - Commissioner empowered to withhold refunds](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/tax-cases/cgst-act-commissioner-empowered-withhold-refunds-electronic-cash-ledger-electronic-credit-ledger-delhi-hc-275329)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party name and bench added; holding narrowed (the adjusted demand had been cancelled). The "Dev International" link is a different case.
- Key words: "let the refund application be processed and the refund be paid to the Petitioner along with statutory interest in accordance with law within a period of two months from today." (para 7)

#### Gujarat HC: mistaken self-assessed payment not barred by s.54(1) - Gujarat HC - Jan 2025 (party name not found)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: ₹40 lakh paid by mistake from the cash ledger, when no notification required it, was ordered refunded and not treated as time-barred under s.54(1). No interest was allowed because the payment was voluntary.
- Binding: Gujarat; persuasive.
- Later history: none found. A related Jan 2025 ruling found on Indian Kanoon, *Aculife Health Care v. UoI* (Gujarat HC DB, SCA 17800/2023, 9 Jan 2025; https://indiankanoon.org/doc/38235202/), ordered ₹15,90,288 paid under a mistake of law refunded with 9% interest and held s.54(1) limitation inapplicable. Its facts (amount, interest) do not match this entry, so it is not the same case.
- Use in orders: for money paid without any liability, limitation under s.54(1) is a weak ground of rejection. Courts treat it as a deposit, not tax.
- Source: [TaxTMI blog feed](https://www.taxtmi.com/rss_feed_blog?id=785437)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Gupta Battery House Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC - 2026 (interim order; date not found) - **BINDING IN TELANGANA (interim only)**
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding (interim): A refund of ₹3,92,50,762 was sanctioned on 05.09.2023, and the Revenue did not appeal. The Department later passed an order on 05.05.2025, upheld in appeal on 09.04.2026, recovering it with a s.73 penalty. The court questioned whether a sanctioned refund can be recovered without first setting aside the sanction order through appeal or review. It stayed the appellate order and called for a counter.
- Binding: interim; not a final ruling.
- Later history: final judgment not found.
- Use in orders: before recovering an erroneous refund under s.73/74, consider whether the sanction order should first be reviewed or appealed (s.107(2)), and record why s.73/74 is available. This issue is live in Telangana.
- Source: [TheTaxCorp - Telangana HC halts refund recovery without challenging sanction order](https://thetaxcorp.in/article/telangana-high-court-halts-gst-refund-recovery-initiated-without-challenging); [Jurishour - invoking s.73 against refund sanction order](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/invoke-s-73-refund-sanction-order/)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2-3 sources agree) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026; a 28.02.2025 Telangana HC order titled *Gupta Battery House* is the W.P. 21101/2024 batch on unsigned notices, a different matter).

#### Qualcom India Pvt Ltd and Microsoft Global Services Center (India) Pvt Ltd v. State of Telangana - Telangana HC (P. Sam Koshy and N. Tukaramji JJ.) - 20 Mar 2024 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. Nos. 8869 and 8871 of 2023 (common order; W.P. 8871/2023 is Microsoft's petition).
- Holding: **Previously "not found"; now located.** Both petitioners were entitled to s.56 interest on delayed refunds of ITC. The court adopted *Bansal International* (Delhi HC) on how 6% and 9% interest run, and read the Explanation to s.56 as treating an appellate order as one under s.54(5). Interest directed to be paid forthwith.
- Binding: Telangana HC; binding in Telangana. This is the same decision as *Qualcom* in file 01.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: cite as *Qualcom India / Microsoft Global Services*, W.P. 8869 and 8871/2023, 20.03.2024, for s.56 interest on delayed ITC refunds.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P. 8869 and 8871/2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/170536753/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; located; the earlier "not found" note was wrong.
- Key words: "The respondents are directed to forthwith take steps for payment of interest on the delayed refund of ITC released to the petitioners in terms of sub-section (1) of Section 56 and the proviso thereto." (para 20)

---

#### Union of India v. ABB Global Industries and Services Pvt Ltd - Karnataka HC (Division Bench: Vibhu Bakhru CJ and K.S. Hemalekha J.) - 15 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: Writ Appeal No. 1524 of 2026, arising from W.P. No. 23839 of 2023 (148-day delay in filing the appeal condoned).
- Holding: The Revenue's appeal against a single-judge order directing refund of IGST paid on exports of services (Dec 2019) was dismissed. The single judge had followed the Karnataka HC's earlier decision in *Hikal Ltd v. Union of India* (rule 96(10) omitted from 08.10.2024 without a saving clause, so pending rule 96(10) proceedings fall). The Revenue's reliance on *Goodluck India* (SC, 06.08.2026) did not help it: that judgment itself holds that proceedings under the omitted rule cannot continue.
- Binding: Karnataka; persuasive in Telangana. Follows the binding SC ruling in *Goodluck India* (file 01).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: confirms the *Goodluck India* position; do not resist or withhold an export refund on rule 96(10) grounds in any matter pending on 08.10.2024. Revenue appeals on this point now risk costs and interest.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WA 1524/2026, 15 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/129893674/); [TaxGuru - Hikal Ltd, Karnataka HC (7 Oct 2026)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/pending-rule-96-10-gst-proceedings-cannot-survive-omission-karnataka-hc.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 17 Oct 2026 (bench, case number, date, result). The single-judge *Hikal Ltd* judgment itself was not found on Indian Kanoon; its holding is as reported by TaxGuru and as recorded in this DB judgment.

### 4. Additions to input tax credit (file 02)

#### Union of India v. Sahil Enterprises - Supreme Court (J.B. Pardiwala and K. Vinod Chandran JJ.) - stay order 1 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: SLP(C) Diary No. 33460/2026, now SLP(C) No. 31573/2026 (with SLP(C) No. 31578/2026), against the Tripura HC judgment of 06.01.2026 in WP(C) 688/2022 (M.S. Ramachandra Rao CJ and S. Datta Purkayastha J.).
- Holding: On 1 Sep 2026 the SC condoned delay, issued notice, permitted dasti service and **stayed the Tripura HC judgment**, listing the matter for final disposal on 17.09.2026. The Tripura HC had upheld s.16(2)(c) as valid but read it down to apply only to transactions that are not bona fide, collusive or fraudulent, and directed ITC of ₹1,11,60,830 to be allowed.
- Binding: interim. While the stay lasts, the Tripura read-down cannot be relied on.
- Later history: on 17.09.2026 the matter was simply ordered "List on 14th October, 2026." The outcome of the 14.10.2026 listing was not found.
- Use in orders: if *Sahil Enterprises* is cited, record the SC stay and rely on *Bhandari Scrap Traders* (SC, 24 Jul 2026, file 02).
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 1 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/21662778/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 17 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/77102710/); [Indian Kanoon - Tripura HC 6 Jan 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/128910236/); [LiveLaw Biz - SC stays Tripura HC ruling](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gst/supreme-court/supreme-court-stays-tripura-hc-ruling-allowing-itc-to-buyer-despite-suppliers-failure-to-deposit-gst-548059); [LiveLaw Biz - indirect tax digest Sep 2026](https://www.livelawbiz.com/digests/monthly-digests/livelawbiz-indirect-tax-monthly-digest-september-2026-552847)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; stay date (1 Sep 2026), SLP number and next listing (14 Oct 2026) added; Tripura HC operative directions confirmed.
- Key words: "In the meantime, the judgment and order(s) passed by the High Court shall remain stayed." (SC order 1.9.2026, para 4)

#### Additional Commissioner Grade 2 v. Safecon Lifescience Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court (Aravind Kumar and Vipul M. Pancholi JJ.) - 17 Jul 2026 (SLP dismissed)
- Citation / case no.: SLP (C) No. 23993 of 2026, against the Allahabad HC judgment of 09.09.2025 in Writ Tax No. 389 of 2023 (Piyush Agrawal J.; 2025:AHC:158800).
- Holding: The SC dismissed the Revenue's SLP and left the Allahabad HC ruling in place. That ruling quashed s.74 proceedings (UP GST, April 2021) for wrong ITC because the buyer's evidence of movement of goods, payment and return disclosure was not rebutted, and information from another departmental wing was used without verification or disclosure to the taxpayer.
- Binding: the dismissal does not by itself lay down law under Art. 141 (it is a non-speaking dismissal: delay condoned, "We do not find any good ground to entertain this petition"). The Allahabad ruling is persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: commentary reads it with *Bhandari Scrap Traders*. *Bhandari* settles that s.16(2)(c) applies; *Safecon* settles that s.74 needs buyer-specific proof of fraud and disclosure of the material relied on.
- Use in orders: annex or summarise the investigation report or DGGI intimation in the SCN. Deal with the buyer's evidence of movement and payment. Invoke s.74 only on specific findings.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 17 Jul 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/187217076/); [Indian Kanoon - Allahabad HC 9 Sep 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/5952556/); [taxo.online - 23.07.2026 SC affirmed no s.74 without proof of fraud](https://taxo.online/latest-news/23-07-2026-supreme-court-affirmed-high-courts-taxpayer-friendly-ruling-no-section-74-action-without-proof-of-fraud-or-fake-itc/); [KNA LLP - Safecon after Bhandari Scrap Traders](https://knallp.com/upstream-gst-default-itc-denial-and-section-74-safecon-after-bhandari-scrap-traders/); [TaxGuru - SC dismissed SLP](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/sc-dismissed-slp-quashing-section-74-gst-proceedings-itc-denial.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, HC case number and date added; summary confirmed (the outside information came from the CIU, Vadodara).
- Key words: "The information sent by the Central Intelligence Unit must be verified by the authority before using the same against the registered dealer." (Allahabad HC, para 13)

#### Shri Sai Ram Enterprises v. ADG, DGGI, Gurugram - Delhi HC (Prathiba M. Singh and Rajneesh Kumar Gupta JJ.) - 28 Apr 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(C) 5438/2025.
- Holding: Under r.86A(3), a credit-ledger block lapses after one year. A block of ₹3,91,23,722 (made 15.01.2024) was lifted because more than a year had passed, "independent of any other action that the adjudicating authority may have taken, in accordance with law".
- Binding: Delhi; persuasive. The rule itself binds everywhere.
- Later history: Calcutta HC (12 Jan 2026, party name not found) took the same view; the State conceded the one-year outer limit. The 57th Council recommends a right to object and to a hearing under r.86A (not yet law).
- Use in orders: diarise the one-year lapse. After a year, protect revenue by adjudication (s.73/74) or s.83 attachment, not by keeping the block alive.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(C) 5438/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/197517062/); [LiveLaw Biz - credit ledger can't be blocked beyond one year](https://www.livelawbiz.com/high-court/delhi-high-court/rule-86a-cgst-rules-credit-ledger-cant-be-blocked-for-more-than-one-year-delhi-high-court-291042); [taxo.online - credit ledger cannot be blocked beyond one year](https://taxo.online/blogs/credit-ledger-cannot-be-blocked-beyond-period-of-one-year/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; amount added; the "liberty" wording corrected to the order's own words.
- Key words: "Considering the statutory position, the blocking of the ITC shall be lifted in view of the fact that it has been more than one year." (para 12)
- **Note on the "25 Mar 2026 Delhi HC" order mentioned in file 02:** not found. The confirmed Delhi authority is the April 2025 ruling above.

#### King Enterprises v. Union of India - Bombay HC (M.S. Sonak and Advait M. Sethna JJ.) - 18 Nov 2025 (negative blocking)
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 5094 of 2025; 2025:BHC-AS:49747-DB.
- Holding: Blocking under r.86A cannot exceed the ITC actually available in the ledger, so a negative balance is illegal; the block was confined to ₹7,06,770, the amount available. It followed *Rawman Metal & Alloyes* (Bombay) and noted the same view in Gujarat, Telangana (*Laxmi Fine Chem v. Assistant Commissioner*, (2024) SCC OnLine TS 2328) and Delhi, and the contrary view of the Calcutta HC and Allahabad HC (*R.M. Dairy Products*).
- Binding: Bombay; persuasive. The Telangana authority is *Laxmi Fine Chem* (binding in Telangana; not itself read in this round).
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: block only the available balance, and record the amount available on the date of the block.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WP 5094/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/22636225/); [TaxHeal - negative blocking held illegal](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=112131); [Taxonation - r.86A cannot block beyond available ITC](https://taxonation.com/show-detail-blog/281/rule-86a-cannot-block-electronic-credit-ledger-debits-beyond-the-input-tax-credit-actually-available-at-the-time-of-the-order-and-creating-a-negative-balance-is-illegal)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench, case number and the Telangana case name added; contrary Calcutta/Allahabad view noted.
- Key words: "As noted above, the High Courts of Gujarat, Telangana, and Delhi have held that such negative blocking was ultra vires Rule 86A." (para 9)

### 5. Additions to demands, adjudication and appeals (file 03)

#### HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV v. Assistant Commissioner of State Tax - Supreme Court - notice 21 Feb 2025; **still pending (no judgment found by 14 Oct 2026)**
- Citation / case no.: SLP (C) No. 4240 of 2025, against the Telangana HC judgment of 02.01.2025 in W.P. 32883/2024. Bench: J.B. Pardiwala and R. Mahadevan JJ. Notice issued 21.02.2025 (returnable 07.03.2025); later listings 21.03.2025, 09.04.2025 and 23.04.2025 (adjourned to 14.05.2025).
- Holding: none yet. The question framed is whether the s.73 adjudication time limit for FY 2019-20 could be extended by Notifications 9/2023 and 56/2023 under s.168A. The challenge to 56/2023 is that it issued before the GST Council recommended it and was ratified only afterwards.
- Binding: pending. Delhi HC disposes connected writs "subject to the outcome" of this SLP, and in Jan 2026 declined to rule on vires (TaxSutra lists a Delhi HC disposal of 48 such petitions).
- Later history: High Court split: Allahabad upheld 9/2023; Patna upheld 56/2023; Gauhati quashed 56/2023. A LiveLaw alert of 20 Aug 2026 appeared in results for "Supreme Court to pronounce its judgment on..."; it could not be linked to this case. **Outcome unconfirmed.**
- Use in orders: record the pendency in every order that depends on the extended dates. Where possible, pass orders within the unextended period.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC notice order 21 Feb 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/198807505/); [Indian Kanoon - SC order 23 Apr 2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/163692317/); [Delhi HC W.P.(C) 5937/2025 quoting the SC order](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/68919052025CW59372025_155954.pdf); [Rawlaw - Delhi HC declines to rule on vires](https://rawlaw.in/tag/section-168a-cgst)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; notice order confirmed (issue and bench as stated); no judgment found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026, so the outcome is still unconfirmed.
- Key words: "whether the time limit for adjudication of show cause notice and passing order under Section 73 ... for financial year 2019-2020 could have been extended by issuing the Notifications in question under Section 168-A" (para 5)

#### Bombay HC reference to Larger Bench on consolidated multi-year SCNs (the *Rollmet LLP* batch) - Bombay HC (G.S. Kulkarni and Aarti Sathe JJ.) - 17 Apr 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP No. 16848 of 2025 and connected matters; 2026:BHC-AS:18153-DB.
- Holding: The DB doubted *Milroc Good Earth Developers* (Bombay), which held that years cannot be clubbed in one SCN, observing that s.73/74(10) limits the passing of orders, not the issue of a notice. It referred five questions to a Larger Bench, including the effect of s.160 and the Art. 141 effect of the SC order in *Mathur Polymers*. The Delhi HC (*Ambika Traders*, *Mathur Polymers*) and Allahabad HC (*S.A. Aromatics*) allow consolidated SCNs; the SC dismissed the SLP in *Ambika Traders* as not pressed (1.9.2025) and in *Mathur Polymers* by an order of 7.11.2025. Interim orders continue.
- Binding: Milroc stands in Bombay until the Larger Bench decides. Persuasive only in Telangana.
- Later history: on 9 Jul 2026 a Division Bench recorded that the Larger Bench was yet to be constituted and adjourned the batch, continuing ad-interim relief. **Larger Bench decision not found as of 14 Oct 2026.**
- Use in orders: issue year-wise SCNs (or at least compute year-wise and record year-wise limitation) to avoid the question altogether.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - reference order 17 Apr 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/151468047/); [Indian Kanoon - order 9 Jul 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/196672720/); [TaxGuru - consolidated notices referred to larger bench](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/consolidated-gst-notices-referred-larger-bench-due-conflicting-judicial-views.html); [Lakshmikumaran & Sridharan - Bombay HC refers issue](https://www.lkslaw.com/insights/news/consolidated-show-cause-notice-for-multiple-financial-years-tax-periods-bombay-high-court-refers-issue-to-its-larger-bench)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench and neutral citation added; the 7 Nov 2025 SC order is in *Mathur Polymers*, not *Ambika Traders*.
- Key words: "A limitation to pass any order cannot be a limitation on issuance of a show cause notice." (para 28)

#### PVCON Engineering Co. v. CGST and Central Excise, Bhopal - Supreme Court (K.V. Viswanathan and Arun Palli JJ.) - 7 Sep 2026 (notice and interim order)
- Citation / case no.: SLP (Civil) No. 28059 of 2026, against MP HC (Jabalpur) W.P. 24931/2026, 06.07.2026; tagged with SLP(C) Diary No. 28833/2026.
- Holding (interim): The order was a penalty under s.122 (SCN 26.07.2024; order-in-original 03.10.2024). Counsel argued that the pre-deposit amendment, in force only from 1 Oct 2025, could not apply, and that the SCN was not issued by a proper officer. The SC issued notice and allowed the appeal to be filed before GSTAT and entertained without pre-deposit, subject to the SLP's outcome. A similar interim order was reported on 27 Jun 2026 (party name not found).
- Binding: interim, for the parties only.
- Later history: pending.
- Use in orders: do not resist GSTAT admission or start recovery against an appellant who holds such an SC order.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - SC order 7 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/149677234/); [Indian Kanoon - MP HC 6 Jul 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/135080975/); [TaxHeal - SC permits GSTAT appeal without pre-deposit](https://www.taxheal.com/?p=141274); [Jurishour - SC on GSTAT pre-deposit](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/sc-gstat-appeal-pre-deposit-company/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; party names, bench and HC case added; the issue is as argued by counsel, not framed by the court.
- Key words: "the petitioner is permitted to file an appeal before the GSTAT and the appeal shall be entertained without a pre-deposit. However, this will be subject to the outcome of the present petition." (para 5)
- Contrary: Orissa HC, 15 May 2026 (party name not found), held s.112(8) pre-deposit mandatory.

#### T G S Trading Co. v. Superintendent - Kerala HC - 2026 (date and bench not found; reported 2026)
- Citation / case no.: not found (TaxGuru gives it only to paid members).
- Holding: A single composite SCN, and the order on it, covering several financial years were quashed as not legally sustainable, with liberty to issue separate notices year by year. The period from the impugned order to receipt of the certified copy of the judgment is to be excluded when computing limitation for the fresh notices. It followed two Kerala HC Division Bench rulings: *Joint Commissioner (Intelligence & Enforcement) v. Lakshmi Mobiles Accessories* (2025 KHC OnLine 149) and *Tharayil Medicals v. Deputy Commissioner, Thrissur* (2025 KHC OnLine 467).
- Binding: Kerala; persuasive only in Telangana, where *Goldstone Infra* (Telangana DB, 27.01.2026) binds and allows one SCN for several years.
- Later history: none found. Adds Kerala (DB) to the courts against composite SCNs (with Madras *Titan*, Bombay *Milroc* pending the *Rollmet* Larger Bench, and Karnataka single-judge *Bangalore Golf Club*, since overruled), against Delhi, Allahabad and Telangana which allow them.
- Use in orders: in Telangana a composite SCN is lawful under *Goldstone Infra*, but year-wise SCNs (or at least year-wise computation and year-wise limitation recorded in the SCN) remain the safer course, as already advised.
- Source: [TaxGuru - Kerala HC quashes composite GST notice covering multiple years](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/kerala-hc-quashes-composite-gst-notice-covering-multiple-years.html)
- Verified: Secondary - TaxGuru summary only; the judgment and the two Kerala DB rulings were not found on Indian Kanoon on 17 Oct 2026. Read the judgment before quoting.

#### Manpar Icon Technologies v. Assistant Commissioner, CGST Division - Supreme Court - order date not found (reported 6 Oct 2026)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: The SC declined to interfere with a High Court order dismissing the writ on the ground of an alternative remedy, but extended the time to file an appeal under s.107 to 16 Oct 2026. It did not decide the validity of the corrigendum, limitation or the disputed ITC.
- Binding: order for the parties only.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: when a writ is dismissed for alternative remedy and the SC (or HC) extends time, the appellate authority must admit the appeal within the extended time without a separate condonation inquiry.
- Source: [TaxGuru - SC extends limitation for GST appeal after HC dismisses writ (6 Oct 2026)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/supreme-court-extends-limitation-gst-appeal-hc-dismisses-writ.html)
- Verified: Secondary - TaxGuru summary only; not found on Indian Kanoon on 17 Oct 2026.

### 6. Additions to arrest, search and landmark (file 04)

#### Union of India v. Mohit Minerals Pvt Ltd - Supreme Court (D.Y. Chandrachud, Surya Kant, Vikram Nath JJ.) - 19 May 2022 (citation update)
- Citation / case no.: Civil Appeal No. 1390 of 2022; [2022] 138 taxmann.com 331 (SC). The SCC citation "(2022) 10 SCC 700" is **not confirmed**.
- Holding: (as in file 04) IGST on ocean freight under reverse charge in CIF imports is invalid, as a separate levy on the service leg of a composite CIF supply (s.8 CGST Act). Council recommendations have persuasive value and do not bind legislatures; but the Government, when exercising rule-making power under the CGST and IGST Acts, is bound by them.
- Binding: Supreme Court; binding on all (Art. 141).
- Later history: Calcutta HC (23 Jan 2026, party name not found) applied it to periods before 1 Oct 2023.
- Use in orders: cite as CA 1390/2022, decided 19.05.2022.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - CA 1390/2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/98511521/); [Taxmann - analysis of Mohit Minerals](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/analysis-sc-judgment-in-mohit-minerals-striking-down-the-levy-on-ocean-freight); [taxo.online - Calcutta HC 23.01.2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/23-01-2026-igst-on-ocean-freight-in-cif-imports-is-not-leviable-even-for-periods-prior-to-1-october-2023-calcutta-high-court/)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; CA number, bench and date confirmed; the "recommendations not binding" summary was overstated and is qualified. SCC citation still not confirmed.
- Key words: "The Government while exercising its rule-making power under the provisions of the CGST Act and IGST Act is bound by the recommendations of the GST Council." (para 148)

#### Kanishka (Kaniska) Matta v. Union of India - Madhya Pradesh HC (Indore Bench; S.C. Sharma and Shailendra Shukla JJ.) - 26 Aug 2020 (citation update)
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 8204/2020; 2020 (9) TMI 42; also cited as 2020 SCC OnLine MP 4564.
- Holding: ₹66,43,130 cash seized from a residence (panchnama 30.05.2020) was held validly seized. "Things" in s.67(2) includes cash, on a purposive reading.
- Binding: MP; persuasive. This is the minority view.
- Later history: contrary Delhi (*Deepak Khandelwal*), Bombay (*Smurti Waghdhare*, below) and Gauhati (*Ankit Choudhary*, below). SC notice in SLP (C) Diary No. 44061/2024 (order of 21.10.2024, as recorded by the Gauhati HC in *Ankit Choudhary*); no stay; pending.
- Use in orders: do not rely on it alone.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P. 8204/2020](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/42423515/); [Jurishour - cash can be seized as a "thing"](https://www.jurishour.in/gst/cash-can-be-seized-thing-section-67/); [Prime Legal - s.67 empowers seizure of cash](https://blog.primelegal.in/section-67-of-cgst-act-empowers-officer-to-seize-cash-during-a-search)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; bench added; holding confirmed from the court's "Law laid down" note (the body of the judgment and the ₹66,43,130 figure are not in the Indian Kanoon text).
- Key words: "It also empowers the proper officer to seize cash / money as the cash / money is included within the meaning of term "things" appearing in Section 67(2)." (Law laid down, point 1)

#### Smurti (Smruti) Waghdhare v. Joint Director, DGGI - Bombay HC (G.S. Kulkarni and Aarti Sathe JJ.) - 10 Mar 2026
- Citation / case no.: Writ Petition No. 839 of 2025; 2026:BHC-OS:6209-DB; 2026 (3) TMI 582.
- Holding: The ₹1 crore cash seizure (INS-02 orders of 27 and 28 Jun 2023: ₹40 lakh and ₹60 lakh) was quashed. No "reason to believe" was recorded, and s.67(2) does not mandate the seizure of cash. No s.67(7) notice was issued within six months. GST officers have no power to hand cash to the Income Tax Department. Release with interest within two weeks was ordered. (The Revenue cited *Kanishka Matta*; the court did not discuss it expressly.)
- Binding: Bombay; persuasive. One source (JSA) reads it narrowly: cash is not seizable unless shown to be relevant to GST proceedings.
- Later history: SLP not found.
- Use in orders: three things to avoid: (1) seizing cash without recorded relevance; (2) missing the six-month notice; (3) transferring cash to another agency.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WP 839/2025](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/140198130/); [Taxscan - Bombay HC quashes cash seizure](https://taxscan.in/top-stories/bombay-hc-quashes-gst-seizure-of-crore-cash-rules-gst-act-does-not-authorise-cash-seizure-1443932); [Verdictum - Smurti Waghdhare](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/high-courts/bombay-high-court/smurti-waghdhare-v-joint-director-directorate-general-of-gst-intelligence-ors-2026bhc-os6209-db-1609764); [JSA newsletter Apr 2026](https://www.jsalaw.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/JSA-Newsletter-Indirect-Tax-April-2026.Final_.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case number added; the claim that the court "rejected" *Kanishka Matta* removed (not in the judgment).
- Key words: "In fact, the provisions of Section 67(2) do not mandate the seizure of any cash, and therefore there was no power under which the Respondents could seize the cash from the Petitioner's premises." (para 10)

#### Ankit Choudhary v. Union of India - Gauhati HC (Rajesh Mazumdar J.) - 11 Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: WP(C) No. 2513 of 2026; 2026:GAU-AS:13255.
- Holding: ₹11 lakh cash seized by DGGI on 5.6.2025 did not fall within the articles that could be seized, so the seizure was illegal. The cash had been returned on 19.08.2026, so the court awarded interest for the period of retention, and ordered seized phones and pen drives returned since the SCN had issued. It followed Delhi (*Deepak Khandelwal*) and Karnataka (*B. Kusuma Konachar*), noting that the SC's notice in SLP Diary No. 44061/2024 imposed no impediment.
- Binding: Gauhati; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders: as above.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - WP(C) 2513/2026, 11 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/71401969/); [TaxGuru - Ankit Choudhary v UoI](https://taxguru.in/?p=1088169); [Jurishour](https://www.jurishour.in/?p=1453316)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; date, judge, case number added; the reasoning is "not seizable articles" (it did not decide whether cash is "goods").
- Key words: "the cash which was seized by the respondent authorities during the seizure procedure did not fall within the category of articles which could have been seized and therefore, the seizure is held to be illegal." (para 16)

**Contentions across courts (cash seizure).** Against: Delhi (*Deepak Khandelwal*, DB 2023), Bombay (*Smurti Waghdhare*, 2026), Gauhati (*Ankit Choudhary*), and Rajasthan, Karnataka, Gujarat and Kerala (per file 04; one Kerala ruling is reported the other way). For: MP (*Kanishka Matta*, 2020). SC: notice issued (Diary No. 44061/2024; *Gunjan Bindal*), no stay, not decided. One TaxTMI summary claims an SC holding that cash is not "goods". It is unconfirmed and should not be cited. Department: no CBIC circular authorising cash seizure was found. **Telangana HC: no GST cash-seizure ruling found.** The only Telangana hit was an Income Tax s.132 case ordering return of ₹5 crore with 12% interest, which is not GST.

#### GST on mining royalty - Supreme Court - status as of Oct 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: no SC ruling on whether royalty attracts GST was found. *Mineral Area Development Authority* (9 judges, 25 Jul 2024, file 04) holds that royalty is not a tax. The SC has issued notice on an SLP against a Rajasthan HC verdict that upheld a GST notice on royalty (date not found). In 2021 it issued notice on writs challenging 18% GST on mining-rights services (no outcome found).
- Binding: n/a.
- Later history: the Telangana HC writ (Nov 2025, file 04) is still pending as far as found.
- Use in orders: treat royalty as consideration for a licence or right to use natural resources (Notification 11/2017-CTR, heading 9973). Record that the SC challenge is pending.
- Source: [Verdictum - SC issues notice on SLP against Rajasthan HC](https://www.verdictum.in/court-updates/supreme-court/gst-on-mining-royalty-rajasthan-high-court-1451374); [LiveLaw - SC notice on 18% GST on mining rights](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/supreme-court-gst-mining-rights-mineral-exploration-royalty-circular-notifications-187149)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources) (no SC ruling or notice order found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Puneet Batra v. Union of India - Supreme Court (Dipankar Datta and Sheel Nagu JJ.) - 28 Sep 2026 (notice and stay)
- Citation / case no.: SLP number not found; against Delhi HC (DB) judgment of 18 Sep 2026, 2026:DHC:7976-DB.
- Holding (interim): The Delhi HC had held that GST officers may search a lawyer's office where there is prima facie evidence that the lawyer committed an offence, and upheld the search of the petitioner-advocate's office and seizure of his computer. The SC issued notice and stayed that judgment.
- Binding: interim, for the parties only. The Delhi HC ruling is stayed and should not be relied on meanwhile.
- Later history: pending.
- Use in orders: avoid searching an advocate's (or other professional's) premises on the strength of the Delhi HC ruling. If it cannot be avoided, record the prima facie material against the professional personally, keep privileged client material out of the seizure, and get the authorisation reasoned on file.
- Source: [Verdictum - SC stays Delhi HC order on GST search of advocate's office (28 Sep 2026)](https://www.verdictum.in/amp/supreme-court/delhi-high-court-order-on-gsts-search-of-advocates-office-stayed-1622905)
- Verified: Secondary - Verdictum report; the SC order and the Delhi HC judgment were not read on 17 Oct 2026.

### 7. Additions to Telangana (file 05)

**Telangana gaps searched this round, with result:**
- **Cash seizure during search (Telangana HC):** none found. Follow the Delhi and Bombay view (Part 6).
- **Tax paid during search (DRC-03) (Telangana HC):** none found for 2025-26. Use *Vallabh Textiles* (Delhi HC 2022, files 01 and 04) and *Radhika Agarwal* (SC 2025). Delhi HC in *Nector Foods* (2026) refused relief where coercion raised disputed facts. That is a useful counterpoint: where the taxpayer's own DRC-03 is backed by a contemporaneous voluntary statement, a writ court may not intervene.
- **Interest on delayed refunds (Telangana HC):** *Qualcom* (20 Mar 2024) and *Synchrony International Services* (30 Jul or 12 Aug 2026; date still unresolved) are already in file 01. *Synchrony*: interest on an already-paid refund cannot be held up for a rule 89(2)(m) certificate.
- **Works-contract rate (12% vs 18%) in Telangana HC:** no decided Telangana HC judgment found. A 2026 writ is pending, in which a contractor for the Telangana State Education and Welfare Infrastructure Development Corporation (2BHK housing) seeks reimbursement where the order-in-original applied 12% up to 17.07.2022 and 18% after. A 2021 writ (W.P. 13288/2020, order 16.02.2021) sought 12% GST reimbursement; outcome not seen. Telangana AAR rulings (*Siddhartha Constructions*, TSAAR 10/2022; *KPC Projects*, 41/2022; *Shree Constructions*, 57/2022) apply 18% from 01.01.2022 for "government entity" works after Notification 15/2021-CTR. For railways, see *STS-KEC JV* below.

#### STS-KEC (JV) v. State Tax Officer, Nanguneri - Madras HC (Madurai Bench; Mohammed Shaffiq J.) - 28 Jan 2025
- Citation / case no.: W.P.(MD) Nos. 3938 to 3942 of 2024 (DRC-07 orders of 12.12.2023).
- Holding: A works contract awarded by RVNL for railway infrastructure falls under entry 3(v)(a) of Notification 11/2017-CTR (original works pertaining to railways) and attracts 12%, not 18%. The Department's reasons (RVNL is a company, not "railways") were rejected, and the court stressed consistency with AAR/AAAR rulings that applied 12% to railway works. Demands for 2018-19 to 2022-23 were set aside.
- Binding: Madras; persuasive in Telangana.
- Later history: appeal not found. No SC ruling found (the "SC RVNL" reference in file 05 searches appears to be this Madras HC case).
- Use in orders: for railway works (including through RVNL or other PSU intermediaries), do not deny 12% merely because the contractee is not "Indian Railways". Examine whether the work is "original work pertaining to railways".
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P.(MD) 3938-3942/2024](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/134831908/); [Taxscan - works contract with RVNL attracts 12%: Madras HC](https://www.taxscan.in/?p=504894); [taxo.online - 27.02.2025 Madras HC 12% for railway contracts](https://taxo.online/latest-news/27-02-2025-madras-high-court-rules-in-favour-of-12-gst-for-railway-contracts/); [Verdictum - judgment PDF link](https://www.verdictum.in/pdf_upload/sts-kecjv-v-the-state-tax-officer-1701998.pdf)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 14 Oct 2026; case numbers and judge added; "nature of work, not identity of recipient" phrasing replaced with the court's actual reasons.
- Key words: "the contract ... between the petitioner and RVNL would be covered by Notification 11 of 2017 CGST (RATE) dated 28.06.2017 ... and liable to tax at 12%." (para 18.4)
