### 8. GST Appellate Tribunal (GSTAT): first decisions and procedure

**Status.** The Principal Bench (New Delhi) is functional; its first second-appeal decision came in Feb 2026. The **Hyderabad bench** (Telangana: CGST, IGST and TGST appeals) was constituted Jan 2026 and began hearing on **20 Apr 2026** from a temporary office at GST Bhavan, Basheerbagh. Its members are Justice Sushil Kumar Sharma and Justice A.P. Ravi (judicial) and Dr D.K. Srinivas (technical). A public notice revised its two-court sitting arrangement from 5 Oct 2026 (single source: a2ztaxcorp).

**Procedure orders:**
- GSTAT President's order of 14 May 2026: all pending and fresh matters go first to Division Benches. Single-Member listing only for amounts up to ₹50 lakh with no question of law, with the President's approval. The same order extended relaxed portal-scrutiny guidelines to 31 Dec 2026, a procedural relaxation that does not extend limitation.
- Office Order No. 4/GSTAT/PB/2026 reconstituted benches and reclassified appeals from 1 Aug 2026.
- **Filing deadline for backlog appeals:** originally 30.06.2026, extended to **31.07.2026**. It covers taxpayer appeals against orders communicated before 1 May 2026 and Departmental applications against orders passed before 1 Feb 2026 (one source says 1 Apr 2026). The notification number is reported as S.O. 3502(E) dated 30.06.2026 or F. No. A-50/7/2025-GSTAT-DoR; not confirmed from the Gazette. No extension after 31.07.2026 was found. Later orders follow normal s.112 limitation: three months, plus a further three months condonable for sufficient cause under s.112(6).
- Sources: [StudyCafe - Hyderabad bench functional from 20 Apr 2026](https://studycafe.in/big-update-most-awaited-gstat-hyderabad-bench-is-now-functional-effective-april-20-2026-417302.html); [LiveLaw Biz - matters to go first to Division Benches](https://www.livelawbiz.com/amp/regulatory/cgst/gstat-says-all-pending-fresh-matters-must-first-go-before-division-benches-constitutes-benches-across-india-534474); [taxo.online - deadline extended to 31 July 2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/30-06-2026-gstat-filing-window-extended-till-31-july-2026-government-issues-fresh-notification-under-section-112-of-the-cgst-act/); [taxo.online - benches reconstituted from 1 Aug 2026](https://taxo.online/latest-news/31-07-2026-gstat-reconstitutes-benches-and-reclassifies-appeals-major-procedural-overhaul-effective-from-1-august-2026/)

#### Sterling & Wilson Pvt Ltd v. Commissioner, Odisha - GSTAT Principal Bench - 11 Feb 2026 (first substantive second-appeal decision)
- Citation / case no.: APL/1/PB/2026; 2026 LLBiz GSTAT (DEL) 7.
- Holding:
  - A GSTR-1 vs GSTR-3B mismatch (FY 2018-19, ₹27,06,634) alone does not justify s.74 without fraud or suppression.
  - Once s.74 fails, s.75(2) requires the **proper officer** to re-determine tax under s.73. Neither the first appellate authority nor GSTAT can itself confirm a s.73 demand.
  - The case was remanded to the proper officer, with liberty to amend within one month and a hearing.
  - GSTAT has full jurisdiction on facts and law (s.112, rule 112).
- Binding: GSTAT decision; followed by lower authorities, persuasive generally. **This updates file 03's entry, which had the date as "not found".**
- Later history: commentators flag tension with s.107(11), which bars remand by the first appellate authority, and note Circular 254/11/2025-GST (27.10.2025) on the proper officer for s.75(2).
- Use in orders: (1) Do not invoke s.74 for return mismatches without evidence of intent. (2) Where s.74 is not sustained, the adjudicating officer must re-determine under s.73 within its limitation. Plan for that.
- Source: [LiveLaw Biz - GSTAT's first second-appeal judgment](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gstat/gstat-principal-bench-overturns-demand-for-non-reconciliation-asks-proper-officer-to-re-examine-523016); [EY alert - re-adjudication under s.73 only by proper officer](https://www.ey.com/en_in/technical/alerts-hub/2026/02/gstat-holds-re-adjudication-under-section-73-can-be-done-only-by); [Mondaq - appellate authority cannot convert s.74 into s.73](https://www.mondaq.com/india/tax-authorities/1783520/appellate-authority-cannot-convert-section-74-proceedings-into-section-73-gstat)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (4+ sources agree) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Augustan Textile Colours v. Commissioner of State Tax - GSTAT Ernakulam Bench - 8 or 10 Sep 2026 (sources differ)
- Citation / case no.: 2026 LLBiz GSTAT (EKM) 35.
- Holding: A s.54(3) refund cannot be rejected on the ground that the ITC is ineligible unless a s.73/74 order has first disallowed that ITC. Until then, the credit is presumed valid (relying on Circular 125/44/2019-GST paras 20-21). A vague SCN and an appellate finding on a ground not in the SCN also vitiated the order. The rejection of ₹7,07,173 was set aside.
- Binding: GSTAT; persuasive.
- Later history: none found.
- Use in orders (refunds): if ITC eligibility is doubted, issue a s.73/74 SCN (it can run alongside the refund process) instead of deciding eligibility in RFD-08/06. Keep the refund order's grounds within the RFD-08 notice.
- Source: [LiveLaw Biz - GSTAT Ernakulam on ITC eligibility in refund](https://www.livelawbiz.com/gstat/refund-cannot-be-denied-by-questioning-itc-eligibility-without-section-7374-proceedings-551082); [Taxscan](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/gst-refund-cannot-be-denied-on-ground-of-ineligible-itc-without-s-73-or-74-order-determining-itc-wrongly-availed-or-utilised-gstat-read-order-1451448); [taxo.online](https://taxo.online/?p=551272)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (3+ sources agree; date differs) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Manoranjan Dash v. Commissioner - GSTAT Cuttack Bench - 2026 (reported 8 Jul 2026; matter listed 22 Jul 2026)
- Citation / case no.: not found.
- Holding: The s.73 demand (excess ITC, originally ₹11,34,474) was reduced in first appeal to tax ₹1,02,012, interest ₹98,680 and penalty ₹10,202. The appellant had deposited ₹1,13,447 at the first stage. The Tribunal noted that the s.112(8) deposit is "in addition to" the first-stage deposit. Even so, following Jharkhand HC *Ashirwad Food Industries v. UoI*, it held that no further pre-deposit was needed where the earlier deposit covers the requirement. A court-fee shortfall (₹3,000 paid vs ₹5,000 under rule 110(5)) was to be made good.
- Binding: GSTAT; persuasive.
- Later history: outcome at admission not found.
- Use in orders: verify first-appeal deposits before objecting to GSTAT admission for want of pre-deposit.
- Source: [TaxGuru - no pre-deposit where earlier deposit exceeds requirement](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/no-gst-pre-deposit-earlier-deposit-exceeds-requirement-gstat-cuttack.html); [TaxTMI](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=16964)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2+ sources) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Anti-profiteering decisions of the Principal Bench - 2026 (cinema tickets; finality)
- **ASR Cinema LLP** - 2026 TAXSCAN (GSTAT) 117. Upheld a ₹9,67,589 profiteering demand: a rate cut on cinema tickets must reach consumers through lower prices.
- **Arjun Theatre** - 2026 TAXSCAN (GSTAT) 136. Upheld ₹14,26,344: the benefit cannot be kept by raising the base ticket price.
- **SJP Hotels & Resorts (Migsun Wynn project)** - reported 29 May 2026. A fresh anti-profiteering investigation cannot be started on an issue already decided and affirmed by the High Court; proceedings were dropped.
- **DGAP v. A J Enterprises** - NAPA/2/PB/2025, 20 Feb 2026 (Judicial Member Mayank Kumar Jain); outcome not seen.
- Binding: GSTAT; relevant only to legacy s.171 matters (new complaints sunset 1 Apr 2025; NAA appeals to the PB under s.101A(1A) as amended by Finance Act 2026).
- Source: [Taxscan - ASR Cinema](https://www.taxscan.in/gst/gst-rate-cut-on-cinema-tickets-must-be-passed-to-consumers-by-price-reduction-gstat-upholds-rs-lakh-anti-profiteering-demand-1448170); [Taxscan - Arjun Theatre](https://www.taxscan.in/top-stories/theatres-cannot-retain-benefit-of-gst-reduction-by-raising-base-ticket-price-gstat-upholds-1426-lakh-anti-profiteering-demand-1450318); [StudyCafe - identical allegations cannot be reopened](https://studycafe.in/identical-anti-profiteering-allegations-cannot-be-reopened-again-rules-gstat-422086.html)
- Later history: the same cinemas' earlier writs (*ASR Cinemas LLP* and *Arjun Theatre 70 MM*, Telangana HC, W.P. 26871/2019 batch, P. Sam Koshy and N. Tukaramji JJ., 12.12.2023; https://indiankanoon.org/doc/26064341/) were disposed of by relegating them to the Competition Commission, with a direction to allow virtual hearings.
- Verified: Secondary - headlines and lead paragraphs (one source each); the GSTAT orders themselves were not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026.

#### GSTAT on s.16(4)/(5) relief and s.50 interest - GSTAT (bench and party not found) - reported Sep 2026
- Citation / case no.: not found (reported in the a2ztaxcorp GSTAT E-Journal, 1 Sep 2026 edition).
- Holding: ITC for FY 2017-18 to 2020-21 is protected by s.16(5) where returns were filed by 30.11.2021. But retrospective relief from the s.16(4) time limit does not wipe out a separate s.50(1) interest liability on admitted tax paid late.
- Binding: GSTAT; persuasive. Do not cite by name until identified.
- Use in orders: when dropping s.16(4) demands under s.16(5) (and Circular 237/31/2024), still examine late-payment interest under s.50(1).
- Source: [a2ztaxcorp - GSTAT E-Journal](https://a2ztaxcorp.net/?p=38928)
- Verified: Secondary - single source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

**Hyderabad bench decisions:** none reported in search results by 14 Oct 2026.

### 9. Advance Rulings: conflicting rulings officers meet (persuasive only)

AAR and AAAR rulings bind only the applicant and its jurisdictional officer (s.103). They are not precedent for other taxpayers. Where two States' AARs conflict, follow the statute, CBIC circulars and High Court or SC rulings.

#### ITC on canteen services: Tata Motors (Gujarat AAR) vs Aditya Auto Products (Karnataka AAR)
- Citation / case no.: *Tata Motors Ltd*, GUJ/GAAR/R/39/2021, 30 Jul 2021. *Aditya Auto Products*, Karnataka AAR, ADRG 25/2026, 19 May 2026.
- Holding:
  - Tata Motors: ITC on canteen services was blocked under s.17(5)(b)(i), on a narrow reading of the proviso. GST was payable only on the amount recovered from employees.
  - Aditya Auto: ITC was allowed where the canteen is mandatory under s.46 of the Factories Act, limited to the cost borne by the employer for regular employees. No ITC on the portion recovered from employees or for contract workers.
  - A Gujarat AAR ruling of Sep 2022 (applicant not named) held no GST on employee recoveries.
- Binding: applicants only.
- Later history: Circular 172/04/2022-GST (6 Jul 2022) clarified that the s.17(5)(b) proviso (ITC allowed where the employer must provide the item by law) applies to the whole of clause (b). That overtakes Tata Motors' narrow reading. (Circular from general knowledge; not search-checked this round.)
- Use in orders: allow ITC on statutory factory canteens to the extent of the employer's cost; disallow the employee-recovered share.
- Source: [Taxmann - GST on canteen recovery](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/gst-on-canteen-recovery-from-employees-aar/); [Grant Thornton alert](https://grantthornton.in/link/545e2d0fa0154cd98233cdddb7c15571.aspx)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2-3 sources) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### ITC on demonstration vehicles: WB and Kerala AARs vs MP and Haryana AAR/AAAR
- Citation / case no.: *Toplink Motorcar* (WB AAR, 2022) and *Sai Service* (Kerala AAR, 2024) allowed ITC. MP AAR (2021) and Haryana AAR/AAAR denied it. *Chowgule* (Goa AAR, 2019) denied it under s.17(5)(a).
- Holding: allowing rulings: demo cars are used in furtherance of business, are later sold, and so fall within the "further supply" exclusion. Denying rulings: demonstration is a different first use, so the vehicle is not held "for further supply".
- Binding: applicants only.
- Later history: no HC ruling found (file 02 gap 12 stands).
- Use in orders: the issue is open. If denying ITC, rest the reasoning on the text of s.17(5)(a) and the capitalisation of the vehicle, not on an AAR.
- Source: [Taxwink - ITC on demo cars](https://www.taxwink.com/blog/input-tax-credit-demo-cars-gst); [TaxTMI article](https://www.taxtmi.com/article/detailed?id=13147)
- Verified: Secondary - search summaries (2 sources) (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

#### Maddi Seetha Devi, In re - Telangana AAAR - 20 Feb 2025
- Citation / case no.: Order-in-Appeal No. AAAR/03 of 2025.
- Holding: Neither the AAR nor the AAAR can decide taxability under the pre-GST Finance Act, 1994 (service tax). Jurisdiction under s.97(2) is limited to GST questions.
- Binding: applicant only; useful for Telangana officers responding to AAR applications that mix service-tax periods.
- Later history: none found.
- Source: [TaxGuru - Telangana AAAR](https://taxguru.in/courts/telangana-aaar/); [TaxGuru - AAR/AAAR jurisdiction on Finance Act 1994](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/aar-aaar-jurisdiction-decide-taxability-finance-act-1994.html)
- Verified: Secondary - single main source (not found on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026).

3. **57th Council implementation:** no CGST Amendment Bill, ordinance or notification found. Re-check before 1 Nov 2026 (the proposed date for the input-services refund).
4. **Finance Act 2026 content** is taken from the project's own statute notes (`01-acts.md`), not re-searched. The TaxGuru article claiming a s.54(3) rewrite conflicts with those notes.

Gujarat Jan 2025 cash-ledger case; Calcutta HC 12.01.2026 (r.86A) and 23.01.2026 (ocean freight); Orissa HC 15.05.2026 (pre-deposit); GSTAT s.16(4)/s.50 interest ruling. Do not cite by name.
6. **Nam Estates** date now confirmed (9 Jan 2025). *Emerald Haven* now read: the blog's reading was wrong and the entry is corrected.
7. **Augustan Textile Colours** date: 8 or 10 Sep 2026.
8. **Gupta Battery House** is interim only; final outcome not found.
9. **GSTAT backlog deadline cut-off date** (orders communicated before 1.4.2026 or 1.5.2026) is still reported inconsistently, and the notification number is unconfirmed.
10. **Hyderabad GSTAT** member names and the 5 Oct 2026 sitting notice are from secondary sources (StudyCafe, a2ztaxcorp). No Hyderabad bench decision was found.
11. **Circular 172/04/2022-GST** (canteen proviso) and **Circular 188/20/2022-GST** (unregistered buyer refund on cancellation) are cited from general knowledge and were not search-verified this round.
12. **Microsoft Global Services** (Telangana HC) is now found (with *Qualcom*, 20.03.2024). No Telangana HC GST cash-seizure ruling was found.
13. As of 14 Oct 2026, 21 entries are **primary** (judgment text read on Indian Kanoon); GSTAT orders, AAR/AAAR rulings and the remaining entries are still secondary.

#### M/s SSL Industries Ltd v. Superintendent of Central Tax - Telangana HC (DB: CJ Aparesh Kumar Singh and Vakiti Ramakrishna Reddy J.) - 17.09.2026 - **BINDING IN TELANGANA (procedural)**
- Citation / case no.: W.P. No. 30752 of 2026.
- Holding: The writ challenged a DRC-01 summary (30.09.2025), an order-in-original and DRC-07 (26.12.2025) for 2021-22 as void for want of a DIN and of a valid physical or digital signature; the petitioner said it learned of them only from a demand notice of 01.06.2026. At the petitioner's request the court did not go into the merits (para 6), gave liberty to appeal within two weeks with pre-deposit and a condonation application, and directed the appellate authority to consider the delay and, if satisfied, decide the appeal on merits with all grounds open (paras 7-8).
- Binding: Telangana; procedural only (no ruling on DIN). Decided 13 days before the Full Bench in *Gudla Vanaja* (30.09.2026, file 03), which now holds that an SCN or order without a DIN/RFN is invalid.
- Later history: none.
- Use in orders: (1) the Telangana HC is routing DIN and signature challenges to the s.107 appeal where the taxpayer opts for it, so Commissioner (Appeals) and JC (Appeals) will now meet *Gudla Vanaja* grounds in appeals and must apply it; (2) for the Division: check DIN/RFN on every SCN and order before recovery (as already pending for S1-S10), because a DRC-07 recovery on a DIN-less order will be set aside on appeal.
- Source: [Indian Kanoon - W.P. 30752/2026, 17 Sep 2026](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/183128166/); [TaxGuru (7 Oct 2026)](https://taxguru.in/goods-and-service-tax/telangana-hc-gst-appeal-raising-din-digital-signature-grounds.html)
- Verified: Primary - read on Indian Kanoon 17 Oct 2026 (bench, W.P. number, date, paras 2-8). *Gudla Vanaja* is not cited in it (it predates the Full Bench).

### Verification log (14 Oct 2026)

Checked against judgment text on Indian Kanoon on 14 Oct 2026. The two statutory entries (Finance Act 2026, 57th Council) were not re-checked and were left unchanged.

- **Verified primary: 21.** *Nam Estates*; *Emerald Haven*; *Bansal International*; *Rashmi Agency* (Orissa); *Devi Electronics* (Delhi); *KPIL-JWIL*; *Royale Edible*; *Harbhajan Singh Thukral* (Delhi); *Qualcom / Microsoft Global Services* (Telangana); *Sahil Enterprises* (SC); *Safecon* (SC and Allahabad HC); *Shri Sai Ram Enterprises*; *King Enterprises*; *HCC-SEW-MEIL-AAG JV* (SC notice only); *Rollmet* reference; *PVCON* (SC); *Mohit Minerals* (SC); *Kanishka Matta*; *Smurti Waghdhare*; *Ankit Choudhary*; *STS-KEC JV*.

  - *Devi Electronics*, Delhi W.P.(C) 15557/2024: the entry said a decision to appeal does not stay a refund. In fact the petition was **dismissed** on 1.10.2026 and the s.54(11) withholding upheld on the basis of a pending anti-evasion investigation. Quick answer 4 and the table were fixed to match.
  - *Bansal International*: the facts and interest rule were wrong. The 9% rate runs from day 61 after the post-appeal application, and 6% runs from day 61 after the original application until then.
  - *Emerald Haven*: the party, court, date and holding were wrong. It is a Madras HC DB RERA second appeal (24.01.2025) making the promoter refund the GST component. It does not hold that the buyer must apply under s.54.
  - *Harbhajan Singh Thukral*, Delhi W.P.(C) 3967/2025: this is a fact-specific order (the adjusted demand had already been cancelled), not a general rule against adjustment. Quick answer 5 was fixed.
  - *Microsoft Global Services*: it was listed as "not found". It is the Telangana HC common order with *Qualcom* (20.03.2024).
  - Orissa W.P.(C) 17470/2026: the party is *Rashmi Agency*, the date is 25.06.2026 (not 1.07.2026) and the relief was a remand. Quick answer 4 was fixed.
  - *Smurti Waghdhare*: the claim that the court "rejected *Kanishka Matta*" was removed.
  - *Mohit Minerals*: "recommendations not binding" was qualified. The Government is bound by them when it makes rules.
  - *Rollmet*: the SC's speaking order of 7.11.2025 was in *Mathur Polymers*, not *Ambika Traders*.
  - *Ankit Choudhary*: the date, judge and case number were added, and the reasoning was narrowed.

  - *KPIL-JWIL* and *PVCON*: holdings were narrowed to what the orders say. *STS-KEC*: the reasons were restated.
